# 03_PAPER_DIGESTS — implementation-grade notes on the cornerstone papers and code

Written 2026-09-23. Each digest was written by an agent that read the PDF page by page (the pages read are recorded
per entry) or, for software, the code at a recorded commit, with the instruction to separate what was *measured*
from what was *argued*, to give every number its table, figure or page, and to write "not reported" rather than
guess. They exist so that a coding agent can re-implement a method, and judge the evidence for it, without opening
the paper. Where the user's knowledge base already had a reading of the paper, the digest checked it against the
source and says whether it was accurate (*KB digests checked*).

How to use them: **Mechanism** is exact enough to implement from. **What was not admitted** is the critical read.
**Unspecified** lists what a reconstruction must decide and register (Part B.3 of the master prompt). **Reproduction
relevance** and **Recipe** say what this programme takes from the work. Treat numbers as pointers and re-check them
against the source before citing them in anything public. The machine-readable SMAT specification is
`research-data/infra/smat_spec.json`; the MyoAssist code facts are `research-data/infra/myoassist_facts.json`.

## Contents

- **The method being reconstructed**: SMAT: Staged Multi-Agent Training for Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control
- **The simulator and the throughput lever**: MyoAssist 1.0: An Open-Source Framework for Neuromechanical Simulation of Physical Human-Device Interaction; UniExo: Unified Multi-Skill Policies for Musculoskeletal Locomotion and Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control
- **How to train it**: Where Entropy Is Measured Matters: Policy Geometry in Bounded Continuous-Control PPO; Human Machine Co-adaption Interface via Cooperation Markov Decision Process System
- **What transfers, and what the disputes teach**: Experiment-free exoskeleton assistance via learning in simulation; Learning Hip Exoskeleton Control Policy via Predictive Neuromusculoskeletal Simulation
- **Phase 2: sprinting humans and sprint evidence**: Lambda-Hold Control: Human-Like Movement Emerges from a Minimal Task Reward in Predictive Musculoskeletal Simulation; Reducing sprint time with exosuit assistance in the real world

---

## The method being reconstructed

### SMAT: Staged Multi-Agent Training for Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control

- *SMAT: Staged Multi-Agent Training for Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control* — Yuan, Androwis, Zhou (NJIT; Kessler Foundation), IROS 2026, to appear (per E ref [14]); arXiv v1 8 Mar 2026. arXiv:2603.07618 · code: none in the PDF; O p2: 'The code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.' The official repo github.com/Fitz-Yuan/SMAT-Exo was a placeholder README on 2026-09-22 (request prompt; not re-checked here). Read: pp1-8 (all), page images plus full text cross-check
- *Staged Multi-Agent Training (SMAT) for Hip Exoskeletons: Metabolic and Biomechanical Validation of a Simulation-Trained Co-Adaptive Controller* — Yuan, Wolf, Androwis, Zhou (NJIT; Kessler Foundation), arXiv v1 1 Aug 2026; submitted to IEEE (journal not named). arXiv:2608.00715 · code: no link in the PDF. Read: pp1-14 (all, incl. supplement pp12-14: Table S1, Algorithm S1, Tables S2-S4), page images plus full text cross-check

**Problem.** When a hip exoskeleton assists, the wearer re-organises muscle coordination, so learning the device controller jointly with a simulated human is non-stationary; the authors argue joint training gives oscillatory or mistimed torque and unstable learning. SMAT is a four-stage curriculum in MyoAssist/MuJoCo (a 26-muscle human actor, a bilateral hip-exo actor and one shared PPO critic) that separates gait learning, adaptation to device mass, assistance-timing pre-training against a frozen human, and joint co-adaptation. The Stage 4 exo actor is deployed zero-shot on a 5.94 kg bilateral hip exoskeleton. O reports a 10.1% simulated reduction in four right-side hip-muscle activations plus positive hardware power in 5 people; E reports a 19.7% net-metabolic reduction against the passive device (9.1% against no device) in 8 people. Only the simulated and offline results are reconstruction targets.

**Mechanism (exact).** Agents. Human actor pi_h: MLP [256,128]. Input: a 'musculoskeletal body-state observation' the papers never specify; from Stage 4 it also gets the current normalized exo torques [u_r,u_l], added as 2 input columns with the Stage 2 weights kept and the new weights randomly initialised. Output: 26 muscle excitations in [0,1], 13 per side. Exo actor pi_e: MLP [128,64]. Input (18 dims): 3 consecutive 50 Hz samples of [hip angle R/L, hip angular velocity R/L] plus its own last 3 normalized outputs [u_r,u_l]; no phase variable. Output: u in [-1,1]^2, multiplied by tau_max (0/6/25 Nm by stage) and applied as torque coaxial with the human hip flexion joints. Critic: one shared V_psi, MLP [256,128], on 'the full system state'. Simulation: MyoAssist, a 3D 26-muscle model. Device = pelvis frame plus thigh links parented to the pelvis and thigh bodies, with passive abduction joints. Control dt 0.02 s; target speed v* = 1.25 m/s. Rewards (every term multiplied by dt): r_fwd = exp(-5(v-1.25)^2); r_qpos = sum_i w_i exp(-8(q_i - q_i_ref)^2); r_qvel = sum_i w_i exp(-8(qd_i - rho*qd_i_ref)^2) with rho = v*/qd_pelvis_ref(t); r_muscle = -mean_j a_j; r_delta_a = +mean_j exp(-4(a_jt - a_j,t-1)^2); r_hip_act = -mean_{m in M_hip} a_m^2 over iliopsoas, gluteus maximus, rectus femoris and hamstrings on both legs; r_exo3 = sum_j 0.5 u_j^2 sign(u_j omega_j); r_exo4 = sum_j [0.3 u_j w_j - 0.15 u_j^2 - 2 max(0,|u_j|-0.8)^2] with w = clip(omega/2 rad/s, -1, 1); r_delta_tau = -sum_j (u_jt - u_j,t-1)^2; r_constraint = -max_k |f_k_joint|/mg; r_foot = -max(0, (|f_r|+|f_l|)/mg - 1.2). Weights for Stages 1/2/3/4: r_fwd 0.8/0.8/1.5/1.5; r_muscle 0.01/0.01/0.15/0.15; r_delta_a 0.005/0.005/0.05/0.05; r_hip_act -/-/2/5; r_exo -/-/4 (r_exo3)/4 (r_exo4); r_delta_tau -/-/-/1; imitation 1 throughout, with per-joint weights from E Table S3 (all hip DOFs zeroed in Stages 3-4 per E, hip flexion only per O); r_constraint -/-/0.5/0.5; r_foot -/-/0.3/0.3. Schedule. S1: human alone, no device, about 320M steps, LR 5e-5, entropy 0.001. S2: device mass attached at zero torque; human and critic keep training, about 160M, LR 3e-5. S3: human frozen, exo re-initialised, tau_max 6 Nm, hip imitation off, r_hip_act and r_exo3 on, about 1.1M, entropy 0.003. S4: tau_max 25 Nm; human unfrozen with the 2 added inputs; r_exo4 replaces r_exo3 and r_delta_tau is added; both actors and the critic update jointly, about 66M. PPO in every stage: clip 0.15, 2048 steps/env x 32 envs, minibatch 16384, 20 epochs, gamma 0.99, GAE 0.95, target KL 0.01, grad norm 0.5. Analytic core (derived, not claimed by the authors): r_exo4 on its own is maximised by u = w for |w| <= 0.8, rising to 0.814 at |w| = 1. Adding r_delta_tau turns this exo-only optimum into a first-order low-pass of that command, u_t = 0.529 u*(w_t) + 0.471 u_{t-1}. r_exo3 on its own is maximised by bang-bang u = sign(omega).

**Protocol.** Simulation: four stages on one Xeon W-2145 at about 28 h per 100M steps. Simulated evaluation: Stage 1 left-right symmetry and toe-off; Stage 2 trajectory RMSE; Stage 4 bilateral-average velocity, torque and power profiles, peak normalized torque and negative-power time fraction; mean activation in Stage 4 vs Stage 2, with the device worn in both (right-side four groups in O; bilateral six groups in E). Ablations: Stage 3 only; Stage 4 only from Stage 2 (3 runs); Stage 4 only without r_exo4, from Stage 2. Offline: the frozen exo actor is run on hip kinematics from 10 people at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s (Molinaro et al. 2024, 'GT dataset'), 15 Nm scale, and its lag to the amplitude-scaled biological hip moment is measured by cross-correlation. Hardware, context only. O: 5 healthy adults at 1.25 m/s, fixed order 0 -> 10 -> 15 Nm, 5 min each, encoder inputs, Raspberry Pi 4B; power = commanded torque x One-Euro-filtered velocity; cycles start at peak hip flexion. E: 8 adults; COSMED K5 calorimetry with the Brockway equation, last 2 min of about 6 min trials; order-balanced No-Exo / Passive / {SMAT, Ctrl-B randomised} / Passive / No-Exo; 15 Nm scale (about 12 Nm peak); 3 IMUs, Raspberry Pi 5; paired t-tests with Bonferroni correction; speed test n=4 at 0.76/1.25/1.74 m/s; terrain circuit n=1.

**Headline numbers**
- [sim] Stage 1: left-right symmetry Pearson r > 0.92 for all joints (not hip-only, not vs the reference); toe-off 57.8 +/- 1.1% (right), 58.5 +/- 0.9% (left); about 320M steps (O p5, Fig. 2; E p6 gives the single value 57.8 +/- 1.1%)
- [sim] Stage 2: about 160M steps; RMSE < 3.6 deg for all joints; Pearson r > 0.92 across all joint pairs (O p5; E p6 omits the r)
- [sim] Stage 3 about 1.1M steps; Stage 4 about 66M steps; the exo power reward rises from -0.12 to 0.08 during Stage 4 (O p5, Fig. 3a; logging unit not stated)
- [sim] Stage 4: peak normalized torque 0.83 at 75% of the cycle (late-swing flexion) (0.84 in O p6); negative-power time fraction 10% (O p5, Fig. 3c-d; E p6)
- [sim] Stage 4 vs Stage 2, device worn in both, right side: rectus femoris -13.5%, iliopsoas -10.5%, hamstrings -9.7%, gluteus maximus -6.6%; mean 10.1% (exact mean 10.075) (O p6, Fig. 4)
- [sim] Stage 4 vs Stage 2, both legs averaged, six groups (gluteus maximus, hamstrings, iliopsoas, rectus femoris, gastrocnemius, soleus): -6.1% on average; per-group values not reported (E p6, Fig. 4)
- [sim ablation] Stage 4 only (random exo, from Stage 2): peak 0.14 vs 0.84/0.83 (-83%); the small torque opposes motion over 32% of the cycle; mean of three runs (O p6, Fig. 5; E p7)
- [sim ablation] Stage 4 only without r_exo4 (also from Stage 2, so no Stage 3 either): slight constant extension torque; negative power for 40% of the cycle (O p6; E p7)
- [sim ablation] Stage 3 only: saturated at +/-1 for most of the cycle, reversing abruptly near toe-off (O p6; E p6)
- [offline] 10 subjects at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s, 15 Nm scale: peak 11.6-12.3 Nm; lag of 9-20% of the cycle (~156-210 ms) behind the amplitude-scaled biological hip torque; peak power 38-67 W (O p6, Fig. 6)
- [compute] about 28 h per 100M simulation steps on an 8-core Xeon W-2145 (O p4; E p4)
- [human, context only] O Table IV (n=5, right leg): MPP 13.6 +/- 2.5 W at 10 Nm and 23.8 +/- 4.3 W at 15 Nm; MNP about -0.1 W; tau_MAX 8.1 and 12.2 Nm
- [human, context only] E (n=8): net metabolic rate -19.7 +/- 3.3% vs Passive, -9.1 +/- 6.0% vs No-Exo; Passive +13.1 +/- 4.9% vs No-Exo (Table III, p7). R_pos 0.984 +/- 0.009 and MPP 18.07 +/- 1.77 W per hip (Table II). Hip ROM 44.0 -> 62.0 deg, +40.8% (p7)

**Ablations that matter**
- Stage 4 only (random exo from Stage 2) collapses to near-zero torque: peak 0.14, 32% opposing time, three runs. This is the main evidence for the staging. The authors' explanation (beta*u^2 favours zero torque) ignores that the gradient of r_exo4 at u = 0 is 0.3*w, so the true cause (exploration noise costs, critic bias, human-side terms) is untested. Reproduce it with >= 3 seeds and try warm starts other than Stage 3.
- Stage 4 only without r_exo4 (also from Stage 2) gives a constant extension torque and 40% negative power. So r_exo4 is what aligns torque with velocity. Together with the analytic optimum, this means the reward specifies the assistance pattern rather than training discovering it.
- Stage 3 only gives saturated bang-bang torque, which is the analytic optimum of r_exo3. The Stage 4 saturation penalty (delta 0.8, lambda_s 2) is what brings the peak to about 0.8.
- Missing and decisive: (i) the Stage 4 human reward with tau_max = 0, a reward-matched no-assist control that separates the device's effect from the reward re-weighting; (ii) matched velocity-proportional and phase-based controllers; (iii) an unstaged arm and non-Stage-3 warm starts; (iv) equal budgets and seed statistics for every arm. Stage 2 was never ablated, although O p8 says 'each stage is necessary'.

**Limitations the authors admit**
- Simulation uses anatomical hip angles. Hardware used encoder angles, which soft tissue and strap looseness can inflate, and velocity was noisier; IMUs are proposed (O p7). E switched to IMUs and says this 'helped preserve the assistance timing' (E p10, argued, not shown).
- Mild left-right asymmetry in hip angular velocity; there is no explicit symmetry term (O p7-8; E p10).
- Simulated offloading needed physiological validation, since users may redistribute effort (O p8). E adds calorimetry but no EMG (E p10).
- Small cohorts at one speed: O had 5 healthy people at one treadmill speed (O p8); E had 8 healthy adults with metabolic cost at one speed, and calls for larger cohorts, overground walking and more speeds (E p10).
- Only hip kinematics were recorded, so how the knee and ankle accommodate the larger hip ROM was not quantified (E p10).
- Only one simulation framework (MyoAssist) was used, and the four-stage decomposition is 'not necessarily essential for other frameworks' (Luo et al. trained both controllers simultaneously) (E p10).
- The device is heavy: the passive penalty is +13.1%, against +5.4% for Luo et al., so the benefit vs no device is only 9.1% (E p9).
- The Raspberry Pi 5 has no deterministic real-time scheduling; 50 Hz control was the mitigation (E p4).
- In simulation the added device mass changed muscle recruitment: several hip muscles were less active in Stage 2 than in Stage 1, gastrocnemius rose and soleus fell. Whether real users do the same is unverified (E p9-10).
- Details and results for Ctrl-B are deferred to a future study (E p5).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- No matched simple baseline anywhere: no velocity-proportional, phase-based or transparent zero-torque controller at the same torque budget, in simulation or on hardware. Yet r_exo4 is maximised by u = clip(omega/2) (0.814 at the clip), and the rate penalty turns this into a low-pass with a time constant of about 27 ms (derived).
- The reward design largely fixes the headline outputs in advance. From r_exo4: peak about 0.8 (delta 0.8, lambda_s 2), velocity-aligned positive power (alpha*u*w), a peak torque that does not change with speed (omega_s clip), and the 'emergent' lag behind the biological moment (hip velocity lags the moment). From r_exo3: the Stage 3 saturation.
- The comparator for the activation reduction is confounded. The Stage 4 human was trained 66M more steps with r_muscle 15 times larger, a new r_hip_act (weight 5) on exactly the four reported muscles, hip imitation removed and a larger speed weight. The Stage 2 human never had these terms, and no reward-matched zero-torque control exists, so the 10.1% and 6.1% are not attributable to the device.
- The headline metric is also a reward term: Stages 3-4 directly minimise hip-muscle activation (r_hip_act), the quantity then reported.
- The main pipeline has no seed statistics. Ablations are single runs apart from 'Stage 4 only' (3 runs); their budgets are not stated; checkpoint selection is not described; and 'consistently converges' (O p7) rests on three runs.
- The explanation for the Stage-4-only collapse contradicts the reward algebra (gradient 0.3*w at u = 0) and is not tested, e.g., with a smaller initial log-std, a critic reset or another warm start.
- The gait checks compare the simulation only with itself and ignore amplitude. Stage 1 r > 0.92 is left-right symmetry; Stage 2 RMSE is measured against Stage 1; nothing is compared with the reference motion or human data. On hardware, r = 0.98 coexists with a 40.8% ROM increase, which shows how insensitive Pearson r is to amplitude.
- Simulated rectus femoris, iliopsoas and hamstrings activations sit at or near 1.0 for long stretches of the cycle (O Fig. 4 and E Fig. 4, read approximately). That is not physiological for 1.25 m/s walking and makes relative reductions hard to interpret.
- The simulated device's mass, inertia and actuator are not specified, and the simulation shows no hip-muscle burden from the device (E p9-10) while humans pay +13.1%. The simulation is not validated against the device's real cost.
- Ctrl-B, the only learned comparator run in the same sessions, was 'similar in timing' and most users could not tell it apart, yet its results are withheld. The benefit therefore cannot be attributed to the staged training.
- Hardware power is commanded torque x filtered velocity. No torque sensing or tracking error is reported, so MPP, MNP and R_pos are command-based estimates.
- Order and familiarisation effects. O used a fixed 0 -> 10 -> 15 Nm order, so power scaling is confounded with time on the device. E familiarised participants for 10 min with SMAT only. And the passive condition includes backdriving two geared actuators, so part of the SMAT-vs-Passive benefit may be friction and inertia compensation that any velocity-proportional torque would provide.
- At fixed treadmill speed, stride length = 120*v/cadence (1.360 m from 110.3 steps/min vs 1.366 m reported), so 'unchanged cadence and stride length' is one fact, not two.
- The offline replay is open-loop, the biological moment is amplitude-scaled and per-speed lags are not reported. The pairing '9-20% <-> 156-210 ms' implies cycle lengths of 1.73 s and 1.05 s (derived).
- If the reward is a team reward, r_exo4 pays the human for hip velocity aligned with the torque while hip imitation is off, which could drive the larger hip excursion seen on hardware. Simulated hip ROM in Stage 4 is not reported.
- The papers do not say whether evaluation uses deterministic actions, how many strides or episodes it covers, or how gait events are detected.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- human observation vector, dimension, order and normalisation
- reference motion source, phase handling, per-sample rho(t), imitation units
- activation dynamics and whether rewards use activation or excitation
- action distributions (clip vs squash), log-std parameterisation, network activations and initialisation
- exo observation order, units, velocity scaling and history initialisation
- critic inputs, whether they change across stages, and critic carry-over vs reset
- reward-to-actor assignment (team vs per-agent) and the form of the joint PPO update
- optimizer, LR schedule, target-KL semantics, observation/reward normalisation
- simulated device mass, inertia, attachment and actuator model; passive abduction joint implementation
- physics timestep; episode length, initial state, termination
- step counting (aggregate vs per env; the Stage 3 '1.1M' is 16.8 or 537 PPO updates)
- stopping rule, checkpoint selection, seeds
- frozen-human action mode in Stage 3; evaluation mode and protocol; exact metric formulas
- Stage 4 new-input initialisation scale and whether 'current' exo torque means this step's or last step's command
- M_hip membership (O 'includes' vs E 'such as'); force definitions and body mass in r_constraint and r_foot
- offline-replay processing (dataset release, legs, resampling, action-history feedback, segmentation, lag per speed)
- logging units of Fig. 2 and Fig. 3a
- full list with options: research-data/infra/smat_spec.json -> unspecified_choices (39 items)

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *Copy exactly:*
  - Every reward equation and coefficient: E Table S2 stage weights and E Table S3 per-joint imitation weights; dt 0.02, v* 1.25 m/s, omega_s 2 rad/s, delta 0.8, alpha 0.3, beta 0.15, lambda_s 2.0, alpha_d 0.5
  - Network sizes (human 256/128, exo 128/64, critic 256/128); exo observation (18 dims: 3-sample history at 50 Hz, own past outputs, no phase); exo outputs in [-1,1] scaled by 0/6/25 Nm
  - Stage schedule. S1: human only. S2: device mass at zero torque. S3: human frozen, exo re-initialised, 6 Nm, hip imitation off. S4: 25 Nm, human unfrozen with 2 added inputs (existing weights copied), r_exo4 plus r_delta_tau
  - The per-stage PPO settings in Table S4, and the budgets 320M / 160M / 1.1M / 66M
  - Metric definitions as published, frozen in eval/ before any result: Stage 2 vs Stage 4 at the same device mass; right-side four groups; bilateral six groups; negative-power time fraction; peak of the normalized torque
- *Must test:*
  - B0, the reward-matched no-assist control (Stage 4 human reward at tau_max = 0 for 66M steps); without it no activation reduction can be attributed to the device
  - Velocity baselines B1-B3 (reward-optimal u*(w), its LQ low-pass, and a gain sweep) and a phase profile B4 at equal torque budget, with the human co-adapting under the same Stage 4 reward and budget
  - >= 3 seeds for the main pipeline and for every ablation, at equal budgets, with all failures kept
  - Sweeps over the ambiguous choices: O vs E hip-imitation release; aggregate vs per-env step counting for S3/S4; deterministic vs stochastic frozen human; critic carry-over vs reset; team vs per-agent reward; joint vs per-actor PPO ratio; clip vs tanh; device mass 4.9 vs 5.94 kg
  - Warm starts other than Stage 3 (behaviour-cloned u = w) and an unstaged arm
  - Partner transfer: evaluate each exo policy with held-out human policies
  - Offline replay of SMAT and the baselines once the dataset is identified
- *Do not target:*
  - Any human number: 19.7%, 9.1%, 13.1%, ROM 44 -> 62 deg, 18.07 W, R_pos 0.984, O Table IV, speed and terrain results. These are context only.

**Recipe on our server.** 1) Pin the environment: a new venv with MuJoCo and MyoAssist 1.0.0 (server checkout b1baf69 per the request prompt; re-verify) and model myolegs26. Hash every XML, the reference-motion file and each config. List the model's 13 muscles per side and map the paper's groups (gluteus maximus, hamstrings, iliopsoas, rectus femoris, gastrocnemius, soleus) to actuators before any run.
2) Device: attach the assist_sim Hippo_L1 hip device (per the request prompt, about 4.9 kg with ideal hip-flexion actuators of gain 100). Map ctrl in [-1,1] to +/- tau_max, with tau_max = 0 (S2), 6 (S3) and 25 (S4), applied on the human hip_flexion_l/r joints. Rescale the device mass to 5.94 kg (default; keep 4.9 kg as a sweep value) and record the mass split and inertias. Weld the thigh links to the femurs unless the model already has an abduction hinge. The paper-faithful variant has no motor dynamics; add a 20 ms delay variant later for sim-to-real.
3) Timing: control dt 0.02 s (record the physics dt x frame_skip); gamma 0.99, GAE 0.95.
4) Human actor: MLP [256,128], tanh as the declared default. Outputs are 26 muscle ctrl values (excitations), Gaussian + clip [0,1] by default, with MuJoCo activation dynamics. Observation: the MyoAssist imitation-env default, recorded, with running normalisation frozen at each stage end. In Stage 4, append [u_r, u_l] (the previously applied command by default) and extend the first layer with new columns drawn from N(0, 0.01).
5) Exo actor: MLP [128,64], tanh. Observation: [q_r, q_l, qd_r, qd_l] at t, t-1, t-2 (raw rad and rad/s) plus [u_r, u_l] at t-1, t-2, t-3 (the executed, clipped commands); history zeroed at reset by default. Gaussian + clip [-1,1] by default, tanh as a sweep. Re-initialise at Stage 3.
6) Critic: MLP [256,128] on concat(human obs, exo obs), zero-padded in Stages 1-2 so its input size never changes. Carry it across stages by default, with a reset variant.
7) Rewards: implement the equations exactly, with dt scaling. The team reward is the Table S2 weighted sum; per-joint imitation weights come from E Table S3, with O's flexion-only release as a sweep. Use rho(t) per sample as written, activation = MuJoCo act, and M_hip = 4 groups x 2 sides. Unit tests: signs of the r_exo terms, the u*(w) optimum, the rate penalty, the weight tables and the dt scaling.
8) PPO, with SB3 semantics declared: 32 envs x 2048 steps, minibatch 16384, 20 epochs, clip 0.15, target-KL early stop at 0.01, grad norm 0.5. LR 5e-5 in S1 and 3e-5 in S2-S4; entropy 0.001 in S1-S2 and 0.003 in S3-S4. Default joint update: separate clipped losses per actor sharing one GAE advantage; a product-policy ratio is the sweep. Reset Adam at each stage boundary by default.
9) Stages: S1 320M. S2 160M (load S1, attach the device, tau 0). S3 1.1M (freeze the human with requires_grad False and a weight-hash check; deterministic human actions by default, stochastic as a sweep). S4 66M (unfreeze, expand the input, r_exo4 + r_delta_tau, tau 25). Count budgets as aggregate env steps by default, with per-env counting as a sweep for S3/S4.
10) Branching economy: train a few Stage 1/2 bases (e.g., 3 seeds x the main human-side variants), then branch many Stage 3/4 variants (67.1M each) from each Stage 2 base.
11) Evaluation, frozen in eval/ before any results: deterministic policies, >= 10 episodes at 1.25 m/s, first 2 strides dropped, gait events from a foot-force threshold. Compute: left-right Pearson r per joint; toe-off per side; Stage 2 vs Stage 1 RMSE; peak of the bilateral mean normalized torque; negative-power time fraction (log both the pooled-sample and the mean-profile definitions); per-group relative reduction in mean activation (right-side four, bilateral six); hip ROM. Run ablations and baselines B0-B5 (smat_spec.derived_analysis) with >= 3 seeds each at equal budgets.
12) Offline replay, once the Molinaro/GT dataset is identified: resample hip angles and velocities to 50 Hz, run the exo actor with its outputs fed back into its history, scale by 15 Nm, segment cycles, and cross-correlate against the biological hip moment. Run B1-B3 the same way.
13) Throughput first: measure steps/s and RAM per env on the EPYC before committing budgets. GPU-batched physics (MJX or MuJoCo Warp) is a separate track, gated by parity tests.

**Throughput.** Published: about 28 h per 100M simulation steps on an Intel Xeon W-2145 (8 cores / 16 threads, 3.7 GHz base) (O p4; E p4). That is 992 steps/s: about 124 per core, or about 31 per env with 32 envs. At that rate the 547.1M-step pipeline takes about 153 h (S1 89.6 h, S2 44.8 h, S3 0.3 h, S4 18.5 h). Counting unit: per-env counting would imply about 31,700 aggregate steps/s on 8 cores, implausible for a Python-driven 26-muscle MuJoCo env plus PPO updates, so aggregate control steps is the likely convention. This is argued, not stated; sweep S3/S4 anyway, since Stage 3 is 16.8 PPO updates under aggregate counting and 537 under per-env counting. PPO compute is small (80 minibatch steps of 16,384 samples through ~[256,128] MLPs per 65,536 transitions), so CPU physics dominates. Implication for sail-gpu (EPYC 9535, 64C/128T, 30 GB RAM, one RTX 5090; the experiment lane defaults to <= 48 threads and 12 GB while the forge runs). Suppose per-core env throughput is 1-1.5x the Xeon's (unmeasured) and 24-40 envs fit in RAM. Then aggregate throughput is about 3,000-7,500 steps/s: 100M in about 4-9 h, a full pipeline in about 20-50 h, a Stage 3+4 branch (67.1M) in about 2.5-6 h, and Stage 3 alone in minutes. Hundreds of attempts are therefore feasible only as Stage 3/4 branches off a handful of Stage 1/2 bases (each base is about 480M steps, roughly 18-45 h), plus calibrated short proxies. Hundreds of full pipelines would need GPU-batched physics. The SMAT authors' own UniExo moved to MuJoCo Warp/mjlab at about 37k steps/s (per the request prompt, not these PDFs). RAM (30 GB total) is the likely cap on CPU env count, so measure per-env memory before settling on 32 envs. All server numbers here are estimates to be replaced by measurement.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- Reward identity: SMAT's exo policy is approximately the reward-optimal velocity-proportional controller (u ~ clip(omega/2), capped near 0.81 and low-passed by r_delta_tau). The headline simulated metrics (peak 0.83, 10% negative-power time), hardware R_pos of about 0.98, the speed-invariant peak and the speed-scaled MPP are what any such controller produces.
- Velocity lags moment: the 9-20% lag behind the biological hip moment is simply the phase relation between hip velocity and hip moment, not learned 'optimal phasing'. The resemblance to Molinaro's imposed 125 ms delay is a coincidence.
- Reward re-weighting, not assistance: the Stage 4 human is optimised for lower hip activation (r_hip_act 5.0) and lower overall activation (r_muscle 15 times larger), with hip tracking released. That alone could lower the four hip groups relative to the Stage 2 human.
- Metric-reward coupling: the evaluated muscles are the rewarded muscles. The smaller six-group figure (6.1%) adds distal muscles that only r_muscle touches, which fits an effect concentrated in the rewarded set.
- Warm start, not de-confounding: Stage 3 may help only because it hands Stage 4 an exo policy correlated with sign(omega), from which the r_exo4 gradient leads to u ~ w. Any omega-correlated initialisation might work as well.
- Exploration collapse: Stage 4 alone may fail because a random policy's noise is penalised by beta*u^2, lambda_s and r_delta_tau (and perhaps by a biased carried-over critic), not because the human is non-stationary.
- Hardware: the SMAT-vs-Passive benefit may partly be compensation of the passive device's backdrive friction and inertia (velocity-proportional torque acts as negative damping), helped by 10 min of SMAT-only familiarisation. Ctrl-B, with 'similar timing', could show the same benefit.
- The +40.8% hip ROM could be partly a sensor or strap artefact (IMUs zeroed standing, no optical validation), or a consequence of assistance that pays for hip velocity, rather than offloading at unchanged kinematics.

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Null model first: before training anything, compute every published Stage 4 metric for the analytic reward-optimal controller (B1/B2) paired with the Stage 2 human. If it reproduces 0.83, 10% and the lag, those metrics are reward identities.
- B0, a reward-matched no-assist human (Stage 4 human reward at tau 0 for 66M steps): the cheapest single experiment that decides whether the 10.1% is a device effect.
- Train the Stage 4 human against fixed controllers B1-B4 (exo frozen) and compare activation reductions with SMAT at equal torque and RMS. If B2 matches SMAT, that is a mechanism paper, not a failed reproduction.
- Test whether Stage 3 matters beyond initialisation: replace it with 10k-100k steps of behaviour cloning toward u = w, or with a small-log-std initialisation.
- Diagnose the Stage-4-only collapse with 3 seeds each of: log-std initialised at -1; critic reset at Stage 4; per-agent rewards (exo-only terms routed to the exo).
- Falsify the reward-identity reading by changing coefficients: delta 0.8 -> 0.6 should bring the peak to about 0.6, and omega_s 2 -> 3 rad/s should reduce saturation, with nothing else retuned.
- Partner transfer: evaluate every Stage 4 exo actor with human policies from other seeds and other Stage 2 bases. This is a stricter test of co-adaptation than self-play evaluation.
- Report simulated hip ROM and hip velocity in Stage 4 vs Stage 2, to see whether the simulation predicts the +40.8% hardware ROM.
- Add a muscle-energy proxy to the simulation and calibrate device mass and inertia until the Stage 2 burden is plausible next to the human +13.1%.
- Offline replay of B1-B3 on the same dataset: if they also lag by 9-20% and peak near 12 Nm at 15 Nm, the 'emergent delay' is a property of velocity feedback.
- Budget sweep for S3 (1.1M aggregate vs 35.2M) to test how robust 'Stage 3 in 1.1M steps' is.
- A GPU-batched benchmark of staged vs unstaged human-device co-adaptation with these baselines, publishable as a benchmark and tooling result.

## The simulator and the throughput lever

### MyoAssist 1.0: An Open-Source Framework for Neuromechanical Simulation of Physical Human-Device Interaction

- *MyoAssist 1.0: An Open-Source Framework for Neuromechanical Simulation of Physical Human-Device Interaction* — Robbins*, Son*, Tan, Wang, van Kanten, Sartori, Durandau, Kumar, Caggiano, Song, bioRxiv preprint, posted 2026-08-26 (v1, CC-BY). MJX docs feature-parity table (mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/mjx.html, 2026-09-23)

**Problem.** Simulation research on physical human-device interaction is fragmented across musculoskeletal models, device models, tasks and controller workflows (preprint abstract). MyoAssist 1.0 composes a human MSK from myo_sim, a device from assist_sim and a terrain from myoassist_terrains into one MuJoCo MJCF through a shared pipeline (myoassist_utils/compose.py:335-389; env_spec.py). It runs two controller frameworks on it: RL built on SB3 PPO with separate human and device actors, and reflex controller optimisation with CMA-ES (README.md:9-50). For SMAT, what matters is that it supplies a muscle-actuated walking environment with an imitation reward, a shipped bidirectional hip exoskeleton (Hippo_L1) compatible with myolegs26, and a multi-actor PPO policy with a shared critic.

**Mechanism (exact).** One control step: the SB3 policy computes a mean action (per-actor Tanh MLPs assembled into an nu-wide vector by NetworkIndexHandler) and samples from a diagonal Gaussian with one global log_std vector. Constant slots are overwritten, SB3 clips to [-1,1], and MyoSuite maps linearly to ctrl = mid + a*halfrange (muscle excitation u = 0.5+0.5a; Hippo torque 100*a N*m). The physics holds ctrl for frame_skip = physics_hz // control_hz mj_steps at timestep 1/physics_hz (defaults 1200 // 30 = 40). The imitation env advances the reference index by one resampled sample per control step. The observation is concat(qpos[named joints], qvel[named joints], muscle activations, touch sensors / body weight, target velocity) with no normalisation. The reward is dense = sum_k W_k * r_k, where every r_k is multiplied by the control dt. Terms: forward exp(-5 dv^2); squared-activation penalty; device-effort penalty; smoothness bonus exp(-4 da^2); foot-force above 1.2 BW; max joint-limit force / body weight; heel-strike-based per-step terms; per-joint qpos and qvel imitation exp(-8 d^2) with inner per-joint weights and an outer weight equal to their sum; a constant 'end-effector' term. The episode terminates on pelvis_ty < safe_height or on any imitation joint deviating by more than out_of_trajectory_threshold from the reference. It truncates at custom_max_episode_steps or at the end of the reference. Reset starts at a random reference index in [0, 0.8 N), writes the reference qpos/qvel (velocities scaled by v_target over the reference's instantaneous pelvis speed) into the tracked joints, and keeps all other DOFs from the previous episode. PPO (SB3 2.9.0) optimises one clipped surrogate plus entropy plus value loss with a single Adam optimizer over all actor and critic parameters. MirrorPPO optionally adds a separate optimizer step on a left/right mirror penalty. A 'common_critic' gives one value head for all actors. Citations are in infra/myoassist_facts.json (observation_construction, action_mapping, reward_keys, termination_reset, separated_networks).

**Protocol.** The shipped device-sweep protocol (device_sweep/README.md; the Hippo_L1 config) is: env myoAssistLegImitationExo-v0; myolegs22 (planar 22 muscles) plus the device; flat terrain; control 30 Hz; physics 1200 Hz; 32 SubprocVecEnv; 30M timesteps; target velocity min=max=1.25 m/s (the randomiser bug breaks this in training); safe_height 0.7; out_of_trajectory_threshold 0.2 rad; random reference start; episode cap 1000 steps; reference short_reference_gait.npz tracking 8 joints (ankles, hips, knees, pelvis_tilt, pelvis_ty); reward weights forward 1.0, muscle_activation 10, diff 0.1, joint_constraint 1.0, foot_force 0.5, qpos imitation {pelvis_ty .1, pelvis_tilt 1, hips .2, knees 1, ankles .2}, qvel imitation {pelvis .1/.1, hips .2, knees .1, ankles .1}, exo effort 0. PPO: lr 1e-4, n_steps 512 (16,384 transitions per rollout), batch 8192, 30 epochs (target_kl 0.01 stops after about 7.5 per tools/benchmarks/README.md), gamma 0.99, lambda 0.95, clip 0.2, clip_vf 100, ent 0.001, vf 0.5, max_grad_norm 0.5, device cpu, mirror_coef 0.1. Nets: human_actor [128,128], weight-shared exo_actor_r/l [32,32] (inputs: own-side-first hip angle, velocity and 4 contacts), common_critic [64,64], log_std_init 0. Checkpoints are saved every logging_frequency (8) rollouts. Evaluation: python -m rl_train.run_policy_eval <session> --no-show --steps 1000 --regen, then tools/score_exo_policy.py and tools/plot_kinematics_exo.py.

**Ablations that matter**
- not reported

**Limitations the authors admit**
- The hip assist window used for scoring is 'provisional', and only the ankle path is checked against composed models (tools/score_exo_policy.py:25-27, 41-45).
- The mirror maps assume a planar model with no DOF whose sign must flip (rl_train/train/policies/mirror.py:5-6).
- The mirror penalty is applied as a separate optimizer step, not inside the PPO loss, a deliberate deviation from Yu et al. 2018 (mirror_ppo.py:14-21).
- MirrorPPO is used only when training from scratch; prev_trained_policy_path uses plain PPO (docs 02_configuration.md:156).
- reset_shared_net_after_load is a no-op (02_configuration.md:171).
- 200-step evaluations (about 5 strides) are too few for per-phase statistics; use --steps 1000 (run_policy_eval.py:49-53; device_sweep/README.md:23-24).
- frame_skip 40 bounds throughput, and changing it breaks comparability with existing checkpoints (tools/benchmarks/README.md).
- SubprocVecEnv spawn is slow on Windows (about 132 s for 64 envs) (tools/benchmarks/README.md).
- Auto reward adjustment (ARA) is disabled (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:77-97).
- The imitation reset RNG is unseeded (TODO at rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:362).
- A zero initial pose looks wrong, so 30 settling steps are used (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_base.py:214-216).
- The ExoImitation custom policy params 'actually do nothing' (config_imiatation_exo.py:37).
- Reference pelvis height is corrected only for devices that stand higher than the reference (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:317-359).
- Relative-path loading of segmented.npz: run from the repo root (docs/reinforcement-learning/06_troubleshooting.md:29).
- The motion library is 'planned' (README.md:27-28).
- The Hippo gain and range are 'starting values -- tune per controller' (assist_sim Hippo L1config.yaml:17-19).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- The target-velocity randomiser passes positional args in the wrong slots (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_base.py:407-414), so the training target drifts over roughly [0, 2*pi] m/s whatever min/max say. It has been on main since 4a4cbe3 (2025-08-05). Every shipped 1.25 m/s config trains against random speeds; the fix is only on the unmerged feat/prosthesis-rl branch.
- The RL path cannot run the 3D myolegs26 as shipped. EnvSpec keeps the freejoint and the env reads named pelvis_tx/ty/tilt joints: KeyError pelvis_ty at rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:341 (probe_env). The docs claim the RL path keeps the freejoint 'because a 3D policy controls all six root DOFs' (myoassist.neumove.org/modeling/msk-models.html). No 3D RL config ships.
- The comment that composed myolegs26 has nkey=0 is stale (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_base.py:193-197); assist_sim now injects 5 canonical keyframes.
- reset_policy/value_net_after_load re-create modules outside the optimizer. probe_policy found 18 orphaned tensors, so the reinitialised networks never train. The new modules also get default rather than orthogonal init.
- prev_trained_policy_path ignores the new config's policy_kwargs (commented out at rl_train/envs/environment_handler.py:283) and requires identical spaces, so it cannot carry a policy across stages that change the action dimension, observation layout or architecture.
- Dict-valued reward weights (qpos/qvel imitation) are applied twice: once per joint inside the term and again as their sum outside (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_base.py:158-163; rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:210-215).
- end_effector_imitation_reward is a constant dt per step because the anchor diff is a stub returning [0] (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:179-188, 207).
- 'muscle_activation_diff_penalty' is a positive smoothness bonus, and its previous-activation memory is never reset between episodes (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_base.py:75, 318-324).
- Reset writes only the reference-tracked joints. Every other DOF (3D pelvis/hip DOFs, mtp) carries over from the previous episode's terminal state (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:371-373 with myosuite robot.py:713-761).
- qvel imitation targets are scaled by v_target divided by the reference's instantaneous pelvis speed, which reshapes all joint-velocity targets within each stride (rl_train/envs/myoassist_leg_imitation.py:159-167).
- The reference metadata data_length (449000) is 10x the actual 44,900 samples (probe_ref).
- The scoring and plot tools default to 90.96 kg body mass. That equals the myolegs22 mass under myo-sim 0.2.1's 12.87 kg phantom chest mass (issue #38); with myo_sim 0.2.2 it is 78.09 kg bare and 82.99 kg with Hippo.
- The exo_off config pins the exo to 1.0. That is zero torque only on [-1,0] devices; on Hippo it is +100 N*m.
- The JSON comments label the first activation block 'left'; activations follow actuator order, right side first.
- torch.autograd.set_detect_anomaly(True) is enabled at import in rl_agent_base.py:17 and rl_agent_exo.py:15, which slows every backward pass.
- 'index_mapping' uses the same indices for the network output and the action vector (network_index_handler.py:116-117).
- frame_skip uses integer division, so non-divisible rate pairs silently change the control rate while the reference is resampled for the nominal rate.
- The in-training analysis runs in a blocking fork Pool every evaluate_frequency rollouts (learning_callback.py:148-160) and crashes CUDA runs (issue #36).
- Hippo thigh meshes collide with each other (cuff_r vs cuff_l, thigh_r vs cuff_l in a 1 s passive fall; probe_env). Device collision geometry is unfiltered.
- The freejoint 'stand' keyframe starts the pelvis at 1.0 m, about 9 cm above standing (probe_env).

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Provenance of short_reference_gait.npz and segmented.npz (subject, lab, capture and processing): the files carry only sample_rate/data_length or segments_list, and no doc names a source.
- Hippo_L1 physical calibration: the gain of 100 and the ideal torque source are labelled starting values. The masses come from STL-derived inertials with no documented comparison to the real device.
- Contact model parameters (solref/solimp are defaults; friction (1, 0.005, 0) on all colliding geoms) are undocumented, and device-contact behaviour is not specified.
- No 3D (myolegs26) RL configuration, observation set or reward weights are shipped or documented.
- No seeds, variance or trained checkpoints are published for the shipped RL configs (the KB found no .zip policies in the repo).
- How to hold a device at zero torque is device-specific (constant 1.0 vs 0.0) and not documented as such.
- Muscle parameter provenance sits in myo_sim, outside this repo.

**Reproduction relevance.** MyoAssist is the paper-faithful substrate: SMAT reports building on MyoAssist (0.1 per infra/smat_spec.json). At b1baf69 it supplies the myolegs26 3D human with exactly SMAT's 13 muscles per side, the bilateral hip exoskeleton Hippo_L1 (4.9 kg, bidirectional hip-flexion torque, gain 100 so it needs rescaling to 6 and 25 N*m), the reference walking data (about 1.28 m/s), an imitation reward framework, multi-actor PPO with a shared critic, 50 Hz control with 1000 or 1200 Hz physics through config alone, and evaluation tooling. It does not supply a working 3D RL path (stock env fails on myolegs26; planar_root plus model_path is the workaround), a correct target-velocity randomiser, observation history or previous-action inputs, per-stage device gain, actor freezing, log-prob masking, per-actor std, partial transfer, a stage driver or freeze verification. Two things threaten fidelity whichever way SMAT's original code went: (1) if the SMAT authors used MyoAssist code from after 2025-08-05, their training may have seen random target speeds; (2) the double-weighted imitation terms and the reward dt scaling set the effective reward scale, so reusing MyoAssist's reward code changes the reward the paper describes unless the terms are matched deliberately.

**Recipe on our server.**
- 1. Pin the stack: myoassist b1baf69 (v1.0.0+), assist_sim 1.0.0, myo_sim 0.2.2 (no phantom mass), mujoco 3.4.0, MyoSuite 2.8.4, SB3 2.9.0. Patch the target-velocity randomiser (keyword args as in feat/prosthesis-rl 038f558) and add a test that every reset gives v_target 1.25 when min=max=1.25. Remove torch.autograd.set_detect_anomaly(True).
- 2. Model: xml = compose_env_model('myolegs26', 'Hippo_L1', terrain=None, planar_root=True). Pass it as env_params.model_path with msk_key and device_key None, or patch EnvSpec.compose to forward planar_root. Check nq 46, nv 46, nu 28, mass 82.99 kg, actuator order muscles 0-25 then Exo_R 26 and Exo_L 27.
- 3. Stage-1 human-only model: compose the same XML, then set the 8 Hippo_L1_* body masses to 0 (or a negligible value) and their geom contype/conaffinity to 0 so all layouts match across stages. Alternatively use assist_sim.load_msk plus to_planar_root plus a floor plus keyframes as in the prior smat_integration.create_model. Record the model XML sha256 and total mass per stage (78.09 vs 82.99 kg).
- 4. Rates and episode: control_framerate 50, physics_sim_framerate 1000 or 1200 (frame_skip 20 or 24; both exact), custom_max_episode_steps chosen in 50 Hz steps, safe_height and out_of_trajectory_threshold per SMAT, and flag_random_ref_index true. The reference resamples to 4490 samples.
- 5. Observation: keep qpos, qvel, act (26), sensors (4) and target velocity. Add the 3D DOFs SMAT's human observes (pelvis_list, pelvis_rotation, pelvis_tz, hip_adduction/rotation) and add them to reference_data_keys or an explicit keyframe reset, after checking reference sign conventions by kinematic playback. Add an 'exo_hist' obs block: a ring buffer of 3 x [q_hip_r, q_hip_l, dq_hip_r, dq_hip_l] plus 3 x [a_exo_R, a_exo_L] (actions after clipping), reset on episode start, 18 dims. Extend DEFAULT_OBS_KEYS and get_obs_dict and index it for the exo actor.
- 6. Device torque per stage: add EnvParams.exo_gain_nm and set model.actuator_gainprm[Exo_R/L, 0] = gain at setup. Stages 1-2: exo action pinned to 0.0 (a constant mapping with default_value 0.0, or deterministic 0 in the policy) and gain 0. Stage 3: gain 6.0. Stage 4: gain 25.0. Keep ctrlrange [-1,1] so exo_activation_penalty stays normalised. Wrap the env in a physics audit that asserts |actuator_force[Exo]| <= gain and is 0 in stages 1-2.
- 7. Rewards: implement SMAT's reward terms (infra/smat_spec.json) as explicit rwd_dict keys with one weight each (no dict double weighting). Decide the dt scaling explicitly. Use one reward config per stage.
- 8. Policy: a StagedHumanExoPolicy (port smat_integration.StagedPolicy). Human MLP 256/128 (Tanh) -> 26 Tanh on the human obs indices; exo MLP 128/64 -> 2 Tanh on the 18 exo_hist indices; shared critic 256/128 on the full obs; separate human_log_std (26) and exo_log_std (2). set_stage(k) sets requires_grad (human frozen in stage 3; exo trainable only in stages 3-4; critic always) and rebuilds Adam over trainable params. evaluate_actions sums log-prob and entropy only over the active actors (1-2 human; 3 exo; 4 both).
- 9. Stage driver: for k in 1..4, build a 32-env SubprocVecEnv (start_method='spawn') from the stage config, create PPO(StagedHumanExoPolicy, ...), load_state_dict(previous, strict=True), set_stage(k), hash parameter groups (sha256 over human_*, exo_*, critic, log_std), learn(stage_steps), hash again, and assert the frozen groups are unchanged and the fixed-obs outputs are bitwise equal. Save the checkpoint and a manifest (hashes, gains, masses, XML sha, seeds, steps), then reload and re-hash. Do not use prev_trained_policy_path.
- 10. Training hygiene: set logger_params.evaluate_frequency very large (or patch the Pool to spawn) to avoid issue #36; set MYOASSIST_NUM_THREADS; mirror_coef 0; target speed fixed at 1.25 with the patched randomiser.
- 11. Tests before any long run: obs/act layout (48 + added 3D + 18 obs; 28 actions), zero exo force in stages 1-2, gain scaling (+/-6 and +/-25 N*m at a = +/-1), a stage-3 freeze hash test, a target-velocity test, and a reset-state test (no carry-over of untracked DOFs).
- 12. Evaluation: reuse run_policy_eval/GaitEvaluator with deterministic actions and --steps 1000, and pass --mass 82.99 (or the stage's mass) and --joint hip to tools/score_exo_policy.py. Add abd/add to _MUSCLE_STEMS if muscle RMS matters. Compare against segmented.npz (roll the left reference by 50%).

**Throughput.** Per-env cost is dominated by physics. On one AMD EPYC 9535 core a mj_step costs 62.7 us for planar myolegs26+Hippo against 52.1 us for myolegs22+Hippo, about 1.2x (probe_timing, best of 3). A 50 Hz control step therefore costs about 1.25 ms of physics at 1000 Hz (20 substeps) or 1.5 ms at 1200 Hz (24), against about 2.5 ms for the shipped 30 Hz / 1200 Hz (40 substeps). Measured env.step wall time including frequent resets: 2.36 ms (50/1000), 2.66 ms (50/1200), 3.95 ms (30/1200). That puts the single-core ceiling at roughly 380-420 control steps/s at 50 Hz, before PPO. Upstream benchmark (myolegs22, Threadripper 7985WX, different CPU; not re-measured): 36.2 us per mj_step, env-only 6555 steps/s at 64 envs, end-to-end 2979 steps/s with 8 torch threads. The PPO update, not the env, is their bottleneck, and the thread count changes throughput 3.6x (tools/benchmarks/README.md). torch anomaly detection is enabled at import, slows backward passes and should be disabled. The blocking analysis Pool every 64 rollouts adds wall time. For GPU batching, MJX-Warp's feature table covers every feature in this model; MJX-JAX would need JOINTLIMITFRC sensors removed and ellipsoid-mesh pairs excluded. Neither jax nor warp is installed in the sail-gpu venv, and no parity or throughput was measured.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- not reported

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Treat 'which MyoAssist code SMAT used' as a reproducibility variable. A pre-registered check could train stage 1 twice, with the buggy randomiser and with the fixed one. If the buggy version reproduces SMAT's reported gait metrics better, that is evidence the original was affected.
- Keep one XML topology for all four stages by zero-massing the device in stage 1. Every stage then shares nq, nv, nu and the observation layout, and the freeze and hash proof covers only parameters. The mass change is the only physical difference and is itself hash-recorded.
- Use gainprm scaling (6 then 25 N*m) rather than force clipping or ctrlrange narrowing, so the exo action-to-torque map stays linear and the device-effort penalty stays in normalised units across stages.
- Upstream's unmerged reward-curriculum hook (set_reward_weight_scales through env_method at rollout boundaries) could smooth the stage-4 transition in a single run. That would be an ablation arm against SMAT's hard stage boundaries.
- The prior viewer's security and hash-verification pattern can serve directly as the SMAT sandbox viewer backend once generalised to 26+2 actuators and Hippo stages.
- MJX-Warp looks like the right GPU target (all features listed). A put_model plus 1 s trajectory-parity test on this exact XML is a cheap first gate before any GPU port work.
