### UniExo: Unified Multi-Skill Policies for Musculoskeletal Locomotion and Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control

- *UniExo: Unified Multi-Skill Policies for Musculoskeletal Locomotion and Co-Adaptive Exoskeleton Control* — Yuan, Wolf, Androwis, Zhou (NJIT; Androwis also Kessler Foundation) - the SMAT authors, arXiv preprint cs.RO, v1 2026-09-17 (only version on 2026-09-23); 9 pages, 8 figures. arXiv:2609.19690 · code: not released. p.2: 'The code will be made publicly available.' No UniExo repository by the authors on GitHub (2026-09-23). The first author's probable account github.com/Fitz-Yuan has SMAT-Exo, which holds only a README ('Code coming soon', af659e6, 2026-07-15), and an unmodified fork of MyoHub/mjlab_myosuite.. Read: 1-9 of 9 (all; page images via the Read tool, text layer for exact quotes; PDF md5 d12edac72954a412cbd71b92a5e4d5d1)
- *Towards Embodied AI with MuscleMimic: Unlocking full-body musculoskeletal motor learning at scale* — Li*, Wang*, Ziliotto, Simos, Kovecses, Durandau, Mathis (EPFL, McGill, CRIR, NVIDIA), arXiv preprint cs.RO, v1 2026-03-26; 33 pages. arXiv:2603.25544 · code: https://github.com/amathislab/musclemimic @ 5d4c8af (2026-07-08), Apache-2.0; checkpoints on Hugging Face (amathislab/mm-fullbody-base, mm-bimanual-v0, mm-10m-2; Orbax/JAX). Read: 1-6, 15-23, 28-29 of 33 (Sec. 1-2.2 incl. throughput and on-policy study; Sec. 5.4 implementation, observations, reward, architecture, optimiser; App. A ablations incl. CPU-vs-GPU Tables 5-6; App. C-D; Tables 8-9)
- *mjlab: A Lightweight Framework for GPU-Accelerated Robot Learning* — Zakka, Liao, Yi, Le Lay, Sreenath, Abbeel, arXiv preprint cs.RO, v2 2026-02-25 (v1 2026-01-29); 11 pages. arXiv:2601.22074 · code: https://github.com/mujocolab/mjlab @ c2e1e06 (main, 2026-09-21); latest release v1.6.0 (2026-08-09); Apache-2.0. Read: text layer searched for architecture, managers, actuators, training (Sec. 1-7); docs/source (rsl_rl, faq, changelog, research) and source (rl/config.py, managers/reward_manager.py, envs/manager_based_rl_env.py, sim/sim.py, actuator/xml_actuator.py) read at c2e1e06
- *MyoHub/mjlab_myosuite (myosuite_mjlab): MyoSuite integration for mjlab (software, early prototype)* — MyoHub (Vittorio Caggiano), on a fork of mjlab's history, software; no releases; last push 2026-03-06.  · code: https://github.com/MyoHub/mjlab_myosuite @ main 625640d (2026-02-05), minimal_myoleg ba8b043 (2026-03-06); Apache-2.0. Read: README (main, dev, minimal_myoleg), pyproject.toml, uv.lock, tasks/velocity/myoleg/*, mdp/*, rl/*, tests/* at ba8b043; branch/commit comparison with Fitz-Yuan/mjlab_myosuite

**Problem.** UniExo asks whether one hip-exoskeleton controller, with no skill labels or mode switching, can assist walking, turning, running, backward walking and the transitions between them. The controller is trained in simulation by co-adaptation with one multi-skill, muscle-driven human policy (MyoSuite MyoLeg, 80 muscles) and deployed on a custom bilateral hip exoskeleton (p.1-2). Relative to the authors' SMAT (2603.07618, 2608.00715: one gait, 26 muscles, CPU), it widens the task to four skills, moves training to MuJoCo Warp + mjlab on one GPU, and initialises the exo actor from a supervised hip-moment predictor. It matters to this programme twice. It is the SMAT authors' own GPU implementation of their co-adaptation recipe, the closest template for our mujoco_warp reconstruction. Its human side, the 80-muscle MyoLeg, is the model our sprint phase will use. The software half of this digest records what mjlab, MyoHub/mjlab_myosuite, MuscleMimic and the MuJoCo Warp/MJX documentation offer that port as of 2026-09-23.

**Mechanism (exact).** (A) Simulation (p.2 II-A). MyoLeg: 80 musculotendon units, rigid upper body, OpenSim knee whose auxiliary coordinates are bound to knee flexion by polynomial constraints. Exo modelled from CAD: belt rigidly tied to the pelvis; flexion axes coaxial with the hips; thigh cuffs 'attached through compliant sliding constraints' so hip ab/adduction is not locked; assistance through 'an additional actuator group'. MuJoCo Warp + mjlab; physics 300 Hz, policy 100 Hz. Human action a in [0,1]^80 (excitations mapped to activations); exo action is a 2-D torque command, one per hip, bounded by tau_max. (B) Human observation (p.3 II-C): 1357 values = proprioception 471 [foot contacts 4, root height and tilt 5, body poses and velocities in the root frame 222, tendon lengths and velocities 160, actuator forces 80] + reference 886 [tracking error 84, reference pose in root frame 21, five future frames every 0.20 s 700, motion phase 1, previous action 80]. The unified policy adds a 13x9 height scan at 0.1 m (117 values, all equal to pelvis height on flat ground) (p.3 III-B). (C) Human reward (Eqs. 1-5, p.3): r = sum_k w_k exp(-alpha_k mean(e_k^2)) + 0.1 r_up + 0.05 r_eng + 0.2 r_root. The four imitation terms, as (w, alpha): pelvis-referenced position of 7 tracked bodies (pelvis, L/R tibia, talus, toes) (0.5, 200); their velocity (0.05, 5); 28 joint angles (0.4, 20); joint velocities (0.05, 1). r_up = exp(-3(theta_x^2+theta_y^2)) for pelvis tilt; r_eng = exp(-0.05(||a||_1+||a||_2)); r_root = exp(-5||v_hat_pelvis^xy - v_pelvis^xy||^2) in the world frame, 'added only when fine-tuning the unified policy'. Termination: mean pelvis-referenced deviation of the 7 bodies > 0.3 m. (D) Experts (p.3 III-A): one per skill, PPO (rsl_rl) from scratch. Identical actor-critic: 2048-1536-1024-1024-512-512 MLP, LayerNorm, SiLU ('sized as in' KINESIS). 8192 envs x 50 steps per iteration, 10 epochs, 4 minibatches, lr 5e-5 adapted to KL 0.01, gamma 0.99, lambda 0.95, clip 0.2, entropy 0. 2.9-4.9e9 steps (21.7-36.9 h) each. (E) Unified human policy (Eqs. 6-7, p.3-4). z_t ~ enc(o_ref_t, z_{t-1}); a_t = dec(o_prop_t, o_scan_t, z_t). The encoder is a 1024-512 MLP whose 64 outputs are the mean and std of z in R^32; the decoder has an expert's layer sizes. DAgger loss: E_D[||pi_theta(s) - pi_k(s)||^2 + 0.001 KL(enc(o_ref, z_{t-1}) || N(0.95 z_{t-1}, (1-0.95^2) I))] over 30-step sequences. Round 0 uses beta = 1 (expert acts), later rounds beta = 0. Each round appends 4x8192x30 pairs; there are 3 rounds. Fine-tuning starts from the round-3 student 'because it reaches the highest success rate under action noise'. It uses PPO on 827 clips plus 600 stitched transition sequences: 2-4 clips re-anchored in position and heading and joined by a 1.5 s standing pose, pelvis continuous within 1 cm (1427 sequences, 202.6 min). It runs 8000 iterations in 26.7 h with the expert hyperparameters and the 0.3 m termination (p.4 III-D, Table I). (F) Exo Stage 1, kinematic hip-moment predictor (p.4 IV-A). Roll out the frozen unified policy on the four skills and log hip kinematics and the sagittal hip moment of both legs. Train a 256-128 SiLU MLP by MSE. Input per leg: a 300 ms, 100 Hz window of hip angle and angular velocity of both legs (own leg first), plus each leg's mean |omega| over the window, 122 values. The network has shared weights and runs once per leg. Target: moment low-passed with a 6 Hz Butterworth and divided by the skill's peak moment. Domain randomisation of the inputs only: time warp of about 30%, hip-angle scale and offset, other-leg time shift SD 15 ms, stance velocity bump SD 0.35 rad/s (copying an IMU artefact seen in experiments), Gaussian sensor noise. (G) Exo Stage 2, co-adaptation (p.4-5 IV-B). 'Both policies are actors that share one critic and one PPO update as in [15]' ([15] = SMAT). pi_e sees the same 300 ms window per leg and is initialised from the Stage-1 predictor. tau_exo = tau_max clip(M_hat, -1, 1), then a 6 Hz low-pass gives tau_cmd; tau_max = 15 N m in training (Eq. 8). Reward = human reward (Eq. 5) + r_exo, with r_exo = 1.0 sum_j (0.3 tau_bar_j omega_bar_j - 0.15 tau_bar_j^2) + 3.0 exp(-0.025|tau_muscle|) + 1.0 exp(-4 a_bar_hip) - 4.0 ||tau_bar_t - tau_bar_{t-1}||^2 (Eq. 9). Here tau_bar_j = tau_exo,j / tau_max; omega_bar_j is hip angular velocity 'scaled to [-1, 1]'; tau_muscle = tau_net - tau_exo is the muscles' share of the sagittal hip moment averaged over both legs; a_bar_hip is the mean activation of the 22 sagittal hip muscles. 'Net-moment offload is the main objective.' 2048 envs (p.2). (H) Deployment (p.5 V-A, p.7). 4.23 kg aluminium bilateral hip exo; per side one active sagittal DoF (MyActuator X8-25, 25 N m peak) and one passive ab/adduction DoF. Raspberry Pi 5 at 100 Hz on two CAN buses; three LPMS-B2 IMUs (pelvis, left/right femur) over BLE. Hip angle and velocity come from the thigh IMU relative to the pelvis unit through a One Euro filter. tau_max = 15 N m; command low-passed at 6 Hz. (I) Comparison with SMAT (SMAT arXiv:2603.07618 printed p.4 Eq. 5; research-data/infra/smat_spec.json). The 0.3/0.15 power coefficients are SMAT's Stage-4 alpha = 0.3 and beta = 0.15. UniExo drops SMAT's saturation penalty lambda_s max(0, |u| - 0.8)^2 (lambda_s = 2.0), prints no dt, and weights the term 1.0 instead of 4.0. It adds net-moment offload and replaces SMAT's r_hip_act (-mean a^2 over hip muscles, weights 2.0 / 5.0) with exp(-4 a_bar_hip). Its smoothness weight is 4.0 on the normalised command difference (SMAT r_delta_tau: weight 1.0). Our reading, not the paper's words: the supervised predictor takes the place of SMAT's RL Stage 3 (exo trained against a frozen human), and UniExo Stage 2 corresponds to SMAT Stage 4.

**Protocol.** Motion data (Table I, p.3): walk 428 train / 48 test clips (36.2 / 4.1 min); turn 144 / 19 (16.2 / 2.1); backward 108 / 10 (9.6 / 0.9); run 147 / 16 (10.8 / 1.2); total 827 / 93 (72.8 / 8.3 min). That is 920 clips, 81.1 min, resampled to 100 Hz, split about 9:1. Walk, turn and backward come from AMASS as SMPL joint positions; IK matches the pelvis and four foot bodies, the knee angle is taken directly from SMPL to avoid a crouch, and body targets are recomputed by FK. Run comes from LAFAN1 via the MyoSkeleton retargeting released with LocoMuJoCo (p.2-3 II-B). Human evaluation on the 93 test clips (Table II caption): tracking success = global world-frame tracked-body error stays below 0.5 m; upright = no deviation termination and pelvis above 0.80 m throughout; coverage = fraction of the clip before the global error exceeds 0.3 m; MPJPE after pelvis alignment. Robustness: a 0.25 s horizontal push at 0.90 m height on the pelvis every 2 s at a random gait phase, impulses 0-60 N s (Fig. 4). Exo evaluation in simulation (Table III): Stage 1 vs Stage 2 controllers 'receive the hip kinematics of the unified-policy rollouts as input'. Metrics are mean positive and negative power of the right leg and the positive-work fraction eta+. Hardware (p.5 V-B; IRB 2305033091R003; tau_max 15 N m). Experiment 1: six participants (23.3 +/- 3.3 yr, 176 +/- 4 cm, 77.5 +/- 9.3 kg) walked on a treadmill at 0.76, 1.25 and 1.74 m/s and jogged at 2.25 m/s, with and without assistance ('Zero assist' vs 'Assist on', Fig. 7). IMU kinematics and commanded torque were logged at 100 Hz. eta+ = sum_t max(W_t, 0) / sum_t |W_t| with W_t = tau_exo,t omega_t dt summed over both legs (Eq. 10). The work loops of the Stage-1 predictor with and without DR are computed 'from the same recorded hip kinematics' (Fig. 6 caption). Experiment 2: one participant, clockwise lab route (walk, turn right, run, stop, walk backward, stand), five repetitions (Fig. 8).

**Headline numbers**
- Throughput: '3.7 x 10^4 environment steps per second including the learning update' with rsl_rl PPO on one NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell; 8192 envs (human) and 2048 (co-adaptation); physics 300 Hz, policy 100 Hz (p.2 Sec. II-A).
- Per-expert cost: 2.9-4.9 x 10^9 env steps = 21.7-36.9 h (p.3 III-A). Derived check: 3.7e4 steps/s x 21.7 h = 2.89e9 and x 36.9 h = 4.92e9, consistent with the quoted throughput.
- Unified fine-tuning: 8000 iterations in 26.7 h (p.4 III-D). Derived: 8000 x 8192 x 50 = 3.28e9 steps, about 3.4e4 steps/s.
- Human policies on 93 test clips (Table II, p.5), experts / distilled / unified: tracking success 97.9 / 84.3 / 94.7 %; upright 95.5 / 81.0 / 97.0 %; coverage 99.2 / 92.6 / 93.2 %; MPJPE 27.2 / 36.8 / 31.0 mm. Unified tracking per skill: walk 93.2, turn 99.7, backward 92.1, run 93.8 %.
- Stitched multi-skill sequences: the unified policy 'stays on its feet through 92.0% of episodes' (p.5 VI-A).
- Push robustness (p.6, Fig. 4): with no push, unified 97.3 % upright vs experts 96.9 % and distilled 81.9 %. At 40 N s: unified 68.8 %, experts 43.9 %, distilled 41.2 %.
- Exo in simulation, Stage 1 -> Stage 2 (Table III, p.6): eta+ walk 82.9 -> 94.4, turn 79.7 -> 95.3, backward 57.1 -> 82.3, run 81.8 -> 94.0 %. Right-leg P+/P- (W): walk 7.0/1.4 -> 6.0/0.4, turn 3.4/0.9 -> 3.3/0.2, backward 3.3/2.5 -> 3.7/0.8, run 9.3/2.1 -> 11.3/0.7. Text: negative power falls 65-81 %; positive power changes by -15 to +22 %. The eta+ rises (11.5, 15.6, 25.2, 12.2) are percentage points although printed as '%'.
- Co-adaptation moves the flexion torque peak 10 % of the cycle later in walking and 9 % later in running, into early swing (p.6, Fig. 5).
- Hardware, six participants (p.7 VI-C, Fig. 7): late-stance flexion peak 10.6 N m at 0.76 m/s, 13.4 N m at 1.74 m/s, 11.7 N m at 2.25 m/s. Positive work 96.2 +/- 2.1 % of the total; net work 9.7-15.8 J per leg per stride. Hip range of motion +3 to 5 deg vs zero assist.
- Hardware, one participant on the route (p.7 VI-D, Fig. 8): flexion peak highest when running (14.1 N m) and lowest walking backward (10.2 N m); the extension peak stays near the 15 N m limit in the three forward skills; the torque period shortens from 1.4 to 0.7 s within the first running stride; positive work 94, 96, 98 and 90 % of the total in the four skills.
- Muscle timing against literature EMG, walking (p.6, Fig. 3): GMax, VL and BFlh peak within 5 % of the cycle of the EMG; MGas and soleus 8-11 % later; rectus femoris peaks in swing where the EMG peaks at loading.

**Ablations that matter**
- Distillation vs fine-tuning (Table II): DAgger alone loses 13.6 points of tracking success against the experts (97.9 -> 84.3). PPO fine-tuning on clips plus transitions recovers to 94.7 and raises upright above the experts (97.0 vs 95.5). This is the only ablation of the human pipeline: the KL latent prior, latent size, number of DAgger rounds and the beta schedule are never varied.
- Push robustness (Fig. 4): the fine-tuned unified policy is more robust than both the experts and the distilled policy (68.8 vs 43.9 vs 41.2 % upright at 40 N s).
- Co-adaptation (Table III, Fig. 5): the Stage-1 predictor vs the Stage-2 co-adapted controller, both evaluated on unified-policy rollout kinematics. eta+ rises 11.5-25.2 points and negative power falls 65-81 %. No r_exo term (offload, muscle saving, smoothness), the predictor initialisation (vs random), the shared critic or tau_max is ablated.
- Input domain randomisation (Fig. 6): without DR, work loops are noisy and differ between participants. The comparison was computed offline on recorded kinematics ('from the same recorded hip kinematics'); the no-DR predictor never ran on the device.
- There is no hardware comparison with any other controller; the only hardware contrast is assist-on vs zero assist (Fig. 7).

**Limitations the authors admit**
- The hardware experiments validate the controller output but not its physiological effect; metabolic cost was not measured (p.7 VII).
- Speed generalisation was tested on six participants on a treadmill, and multi-skill assistance on one participant overground (p.7).
- The human policy covers four skills on flat ground; no stairs, slopes or sit-to-stand (p.7).
- The simulated exoskeleton is idealised: the belt is rigidly tied to the pelvis and the flexion axes are coaxial with the hip, whereas the real belt and cuffs move with soft tissue and the axes can be misaligned (p.7).
- Simulated muscle activation does not fully match EMG; rectus femoris, for example, peaks in swing in simulation but at loading in EMG (p.6-7).
- The muscle-saving term covers only the sagittal hip muscles, so co-contraction elsewhere is not penalised and lower hip activation may shift load to other muscles (p.7).
- The unified policy's tracking success is 3.2 points below the single-skill experts (p.5).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- No hardware baseline controller. There is no velocity-proportional (SMAT-like), phase- or time-based, biological-moment (Molinaro, their ref. [5]) or delayed-output-feedback (Lim, their ref. [4]) comparison, and no no-device condition; assistance is compared only with zero assist.
- No physiological outcome on hardware at all: no metabolic cost, EMG or joint moments. eta+ multiplies the commanded torque, not the measured motor torque or current, by the IMU angular velocity (Eq. 10; 'IMU kinematics and the commanded torque were logged'). Actuator tracking error, backlash and cuff compliance are therefore invisible to it.
- eta+ is a phase-alignment score. The assistance-power term the controller was trained on rewards the same alignment, so a high eta+ sits close to the training objective rather than showing benefit. A torque of k*omega would score near 100 %.
- In simulation, Table III compares Stage 1 and Stage 2 on 'the hip kinematics of the unified-policy rollouts'. As written this reads as the same kinematics replayed through two torque profiles, not closed-loop assisted gait; the paper does not say which. No simulated change in muscle activation, metabolic proxy or tau_muscle offload is reported for the co-adapted system, although all three are reward terms.
- Each stage is a single training run: no seeds, variance or confidence intervals for any simulation number, and no statistical tests for the six-participant results.
- The co-adaptation budget (iterations, hours), its PPO settings and the critic's inputs and architecture are not reported, so the 2048-env co-adaptation cannot be reproduced from the text.
- Training includes no actuator model: no motor dynamics, torque-rate (slew) limit, communication latency or IMU filtering, only the 6 Hz low-pass of Eq. 8 and the smoothness penalty. A text-layer search finds no 'rate limit', 'slew', 'latency' or 'seed'; 'delay' appears only in a reference title.
- Human study: participants are young (23.3 +/- 3.3 yr). No familiarisation protocol, condition-order randomisation or blinding is described.
- Tracking success uses a lenient 0.5 m world-frame threshold. The 'unseen clips' are the held-out 10 % of the same datasets, not new subjects.
- Fig. 4's no-push upright rates (unified 97.3 %, experts 96.9 %) differ from Table II (97.0 %, 95.5 %) without explanation; the episode sets probably differ.
- The abstract and conclusion speak of 'user-specific' controllers and policies, but no per-user model or adaptation exists: one controller serves all six participants.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Whether the exoskeleton model (mass, cuffs, sliding constraints) is attached while the experts and the unified policy train, or only during co-adaptation.
- Whether pi_h keeps learning during co-adaptation (Fig. 1 says the two are 'co-adapted'; the text says 'Both policies are actors that share one critic and one PPO update'), and whether r_root stays on then.
- Co-adaptation hyperparameters, iteration count and hours; critic inputs and size; pi_e's initial action std after supervised initialisation.
- The constant that scales omega_bar to [-1, 1] (SMAT used clip(omega / 2.0 rad/s, -1, 1), SMAT p.4).
- How tau_net (the sagittal hip moment) is computed in simulation, and whether the Stage-1 target is the net joint moment or the muscle moment.
- Which 22 sagittal hip muscles are used; the exact 28-joint set; the sliding-constraint parameters of the cuffs; the exo's mass and inertia in simulation (the device weighs 4.23 kg).
- Contact and solver options; MuJoCo, MuJoCo Warp, mjlab and rsl_rl versions; nconmax and njmax; whether MULTICCD was disabled (mujoco_warp 3.14 rejects margins with MULTICCD on).
- How actions become activations ('mapped to activations in [0,1]'), the action-noise parameterisation, and observation normalisation.
- DR magnitudes for scale, offset and sensor noise, and the time-warp distribution beyond 'about 30%'.
- The order and discretisation of the 6 Hz low-pass in Eq. 8, and whether it runs inside the simulated loop during co-adaptation.
- Episode length, reset distribution and reference-state initialisation.

**Reproduction relevance.** UniExo is the SMAT authors' own GPU implementation of SMAT-style co-adaptation: mjlab's manager-based env, rsl_rl PPO, 8192/2048 envs, 300/100 Hz, large MLPs, and two actors trained with 'one critic and one PPO update'. It is therefore the best evidence of how they would build our target system on MuJoCo Warp. Four consequences follow. (1) Reward. The exo assistance-power term keeps SMAT's alpha = 0.3 and beta = 0.15 but loses the saturation penalty and gains a net-moment-offload objective. A SMAT reconstruction must use SMAT's own Eq. 5 (SMAT p.4) and treat UniExo's Eq. 9 as a documented variant, not a correction. (2) Staging. UniExo swaps SMAT's RL Stage 3 (exo against a frozen human) for supervised hip-moment regression. That initialiser is cheap and well defined, and worth registering as an ablation arm. (3) Engineering. mjlab + rsl_rl + MuJoCo Warp at 8192 envs demonstrably works for an 80-muscle MyoLeg with an exo attached. Our probe stepped myolegs26 + Hippo_L1 at 2.35e6 physics steps/s at 8192 worlds, so physics will not bottleneck 50 Hz control. But mjlab currently pins mujoco and mujoco-warp to ~=3.11.0 while the probe validated 3.14.0. MyoHub/mjlab_myosuite proves the muscle-actuator path (XML muscles on tendon transmission, tendon-effort actions) but is a stale prototype with no exo, imitation or myolegs26 task. MuscleMimic supplies a proven large-batch PPO recipe for muscles and a CPU-vs-GPU evaluation precedent, but in JAX on MuJoCo 3.4. (4) Sprint phase. UniExo's run expert (LAFAN1 via LocoMuJoCo MyoSkeleton) and its multi-skill recipe on MyoLeg-80 are the nearest published precedent; nothing faster than 2.25 m/s was tested on hardware. No code is released, so nothing can be copied, and every unspecified item must be registered.

**Recipe on our server.** Two routes, chosen by a one-session gate (step 0). Route A (mjlab) reuses resets, observation history/noise/delay, domain randomisation, the NaN guard and the rsl_rl runner, and is what the SMAT authors used for UniExo. Route B (direct mujoco_warp 3.14 + PyTorch PPO) keeps the probe-validated stack and gives full control over staging. Both must pass the same statistical parity gates (step 6), because the probe showed trajectory-level CPU/GPU parity is unattainable once contacts occur.

0. Stack gate. mjlab HEAD c2e1e06 and v1.6.0 pin mujoco~=3.11.0 and mujoco-warp~=3.11.0 (pyproject). In one uv venv pin mjlab at git rev c2e1e06 (rsl-rl-lib 5.5.1), or 1.6.0 from PyPI (rsl-rl-lib 5.4.2), and re-run the probe's put_model/support, throughput and parity scripts on 3.11.x with MULTICCD disabled. In a second venv, try mjlab with a uv override to mujoco / mujoco-warp 3.14.0 and run mjlab's own pytest. Take Route A if either passes cleanly at >= 100k physics transitions/s at 8192 worlds; otherwise take Route B. Record the lockfile hash.
1. Model (both routes). Compose as the probe did: myolegs26 + Hippo_L1; nq 47, nv 46, nu 28 (26 muscles + Exo_R, Exo_L), na 26, 26 spatial tendons with 96 wraps, 28 joint equalities, 82.99 kg; XML sha256 6a2405b9... (research-data/probe/results_2026-09-23/compose_report.json). Disable MULTICCD, or zero the 0.001 geom margins, and register which: mujoco_warp 3.14 raises NotImplementedError otherwise (io.py:678). Use the sparse Jacobian (dense is rejected for nv > 60, and myosuite_mjlab forces sparse for tendon-heavy models). Precompile to MJB (probe: 153 MB vs 560 MB RSS per process). Build the Stage-1 variant (device absent or massless) as registered in research-data/infra/smat_spec.json.
2A. mjlab entity. EntityCfg(spec_fn=lambda: mujoco.MjSpec.from_file(composed_xml)) with two actuator configs, because one XmlActuatorCfg targets one transmission namespace: XmlActuatorCfg(transmission_type=TENDON) on the 26 muscle tendons, and a second config for the two Hippo actuators (check their transmission in the XML). Set physics through SimulationCfg(mujoco=MujocoCfg(timestep, integrator='implicitfast' or 'euler', jacobian='sparse', disableflags=('multiccd',))), because MjSpec.attach drops the entity's <option> (mjlab FAQ). Check that 'multiccd' is a disable flag in the installed mujoco. Size nconmax and njmax from the probe (about 6.3 contacts/world; constraint rows max 114-130) with headroom, and watch for overflow. decimation = control_dt / timestep (SMAT: 50 Hz control).
3A. MDP. Actions: the action manager slices one policy output into two terms. The muscle term maps a in [-1,1] to ctrl = 0.5 + 0.5a as MyoAssist does (TendonEffortActionCfg or a custom term). The exo-torque term (JointEffortActionCfg or custom) is scaled 6 N m in Stage 3 and 25 N m in Stage 4 per the SMAT spec. Observations: groups 'human', 'exo' and 'critic'. 'exo' is SMAT's 18-D input: 3-step history of bilateral hip angle and velocity plus the last three normalised commands, via history_length. 'critic' is the full state. Rewards: implement SMAT's terms as RewardTermCfg functions without the explicit dt, because mjlab multiplies every term by step_dt by default (reward_manager.py:34-44; scale_rewards_by_dt=True). Alternatively set scale_rewards_by_dt=False and keep SMAT's dt. Never do both. Terminations and resets are unreported by SMAT: register the choices.
4A. Two actors and one critic in rsl_rl 5.5.1, without forking. Set actor class_name='yourpkg.models:SmatTwoActorModel'; resolve_callable accepts 'module.path:Attr'. PPO.construct_algorithm calls actor_class(obs, obs_groups, 'actor', num_actions) and builds one critic (ppo.py:427-463). The custom model therefore receives the whole observation TensorDict, runs the human MLP on 'human' and the exo MLP [128, 64] on 'exo', and emits one diagonal Gaussian over 28 dims (joint log-prob = product policy, one ratio). The critic is an MLPModel [256, 128] on 'critic'. Stage-3 freeze: call requires_grad_(False) on the human sub-network and its std, and override update_normalization so its normaliser stops too (PPO updates normalisers every env step, ppo.py:329-330). Write a PPO subclass for per-stage and per-actor learning rates or SB3-style target-KL early stopping: rsl_rl uses one Adam over actor+critic with one KL-adaptive LR (ppo.py:101, 254-266); use schedule='fixed' to mimic SB3. Re-initialise the exo head at Stage 3. Chain stages with a small runner that loads the previous checkpoint and SHA-256-hashes the frozen parameters.
5A. Batch matching. SMAT used 32 envs x 2048 steps = 65,536 transitions per update, minibatch 16,384, 20 epochs (SMAT spec). Keep transitions per update and minibatch size but avoid tiny rollouts, which MuscleMimic found worst (8 steps; App. A, Tables 5-6). For example use 2048 envs x 32 steps as the SMAT-faithful arm. Register a GPU-native arm (8192 envs, 1 epoch, large minibatches, as in MuscleMimic Table 8) separately.
2B-5B. Direct port. mujoco_warp 3.14: put_model / make_data(nworld, nconmax, njmax). Capture one control step (decimation substeps) as a CUDA graph (docs: 'typically significantly faster'). Share memory zero-copy with torch (wp.to_torch). Compute observations, rewards and terminations in torch. Reset done worlds with masked copies of the initial or keyframe state. Train with a CleanRL-style PPO using the same two-actor model, freezing and stage rules as 4A.
6. Statistical parity gates; pre-register the margins before looking. G0 structure: identical fingerprints on CPU and Warp (counts, mass, actuator order, equality data); put_model passes; flags recorded. G1 contact-free dynamics: contacts disabled or model suspended, identical float32 control traces for 400 steps; muscle activation, tendon length and force must match within float32 error growth (probe: about 2e-6). G2 open-loop distribution parity: at least 1000 worlds per backend from the same initial-state distribution and stochastic excitation process. Compare time-to-fall, contacts per world, constraint rows, peak GRF/BW and CoM height with two-sample tests and TOST equivalence margins. Paths cannot be compared: the probe already sees 10 vs 6 contacts at the seated start. G3 reward parity: per-step rewards from the GPU code equal an independent numpy implementation of SMAT's equations on logged states, to float32; this catches dt double-scaling. G4 freeze proof: SHA-256 of frozen weights and normaliser buffers unchanged across Stage 3; identical actions for identical observations. G5 policy transfer: evaluate every checkpoint in CPU MuJoCo and in Warp, at least 3 seeds x 100 episodes. Success, speed, stride, hip kinematics, mean activations and eta+ must agree within margins (precedent: MuscleMimic 80.76 +/- 5.13 vs 82.20 +/- 8.02 % success, CPU vs GPU, Tables 5-6). G6 training-distribution parity: Stage-1 human on GPU vs the CPU/SB3 reference, at least 5 seeds each; compare final-metric distributions with bootstrap CIs.
7. Seeds. Set seeds, but MJWarp is not deterministic on GPU (docs FAQ; mjlab FAQ; issue #562 open), so report every stage as a distribution over seeds. To debug a single failure, replay it on CPU (wp.set_device('cpu') or native MuJoCo).

**Throughput.** Measured by others. UniExo: 3.7e4 env steps/s including learning for MyoLeg-80 + exo at 300 Hz physics / 100 Hz policy (3 substeps), 8192 envs, 2048-1536-1024-1024-512-512 MLPs, one RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (p.2). MuscleMimic (Fig. 2, p.3-4), end-to-end SPS on one H100 80GB with 416 muscles and full self-collision: 1,263 at 128 envs; 6,105 at 1,024; 11,582 at 4,096; 13,593 at 8,192; 1e9 steps in about 20 h. Its released MyoFullBody checkpoint used 15.87e9 steps (Table 8), about 340 GPU-hours at 1.3e4 SPS (derived). Our probe (cited, not re-run): myolegs26 + Hippo_L1 on MuJoCo Warp 3.14.0, RTX 5090, 8192 worlds, physics only, reached 2,353,437 steps/s = 235,344 control transitions/s at 10 substeps (dt 0.002 s, 50 Hz), 3.6 GB. The CPU reference reached 57.9k transitions/s with 32 processes (research-data/probe/results_2026-09-23/gpu_probe_2026-09-23.json). Implication (derived, not measured). SMAT Stage 4 is about 66 M steps (research-data/infra/smat_spec.json), which is about 5 min of physics at 235k transitions/s. End-to-end speed will be set by the PPO networks and the Python-side observation and reward code; UniExo and MuscleMimic land at 1e4-4e4 steps/s with far larger networks and models. A four-stage SMAT run should therefore take tens of minutes to a few hours of GPU time, not days, which makes 5+ seeds and ablation arms affordable. Caveats: neither external number was measured on an RTX 5090, both include much bigger networks, and the probe excluded learning. Measure end-to-end SPS in the step-0 gate.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- The eta+ gain could come from filtering rather than co-adaptation. The 6 Hz low-pass and the smoothness term push the torque toward a lagged version of the biological moment, and any torque that lags the moment toward the velocity raises eta+. A reviewer would ask for a tau = k*omega_bar or delayed-moment baseline at the same tau_max.
- Table III may be an open-loop artefact. If both controllers see the same unified-policy kinematics, the result shows only that the Stage-2 profile is better phased with those kinematics, not that the co-adapted human-exo system moves with less effort.
- Hardware work loops and eta+ use commanded torque. With actuator lag and cuff compliance, delivered power could change sign in parts of the cycle.
- The 3-5 deg increase in hip range of motion under assistance could reflect motion imposed by the device, or cuff migration, rather than beneficial assistance.
- The unified policy's robustness edge could come from its extra PPO fine-tuning compute (8000 iterations, 26.7 h, with r_root and transitions) rather than from the latent architecture; the experts got no matched extra fine-tuning.
- Speed generalisation of the exo could come from input DR and the |omega| intensity feature rather than co-adaptation. Fig. 6 shows DR matters, and no hardware condition ran the non-co-adapted predictor.

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Register predictor initialisation as a SMAT ablation. Replace SMAT Stage 3 (RL against a frozen human, about 1.1 M steps) with UniExo-style supervised hip-moment regression on Stage-2 rollouts, then run SMAT Stage 4 and compare Stage-4 outcome distributions across seeds.
- Measure closed-loop vs open-loop eta+. Evaluate each controller both on replayed unassisted kinematics (UniExo Table III style) and in closed loop with the co-adapted human; the gap measures how much of the 'improvement' is human adaptation.
- Add the baselines UniExo omitted to every simulated comparison: tau = k*clip(omega/omega_s), the instantaneous optimum of SMAT's power term, and a delayed biological-moment controller. They cost minutes on GPU.
- In the sprint phase, where hip moments are large, try UniExo's net-moment-offload term exp(-0.025|tau_muscle|) as an alternative objective and compare it with SMAT's power term at matched peak torque.
- For the sprint runner, extend UniExo's LAFAN1 run expert with faster AMASS clips retargeted through MuscleMimic's GMR-Fit, screened with its tendon-jump and joint-limit metrics, and train with MuscleMimic's E=1, large-batch PPO recipe.
- Report seed distributions as a contribution. UniExo reports single runs; because MJWarp is nondeterministic, 5+ seeds per stage are needed anyway and supply the missing variance estimate.
- Log motor current or torque on hardware so eta+ can be computed from delivered as well as commanded torque.

**KB digests checked**
- not reported

## How to train it

### Where Entropy Is Measured Matters: Policy Geometry in Bounded Continuous-Control PPO

- *Where Entropy Is Measured Matters: Policy Geometry in Bounded Continuous-Control PPO* — He, Zhou, Wang, Xue, Fei (Lancaster; Beihang; Tsinghua), arXiv preprint v1, 25 Aug 2026 (24 pp, 8 tables). arXiv:2608.24488 · code: an archival package is described (p9 s4.7; p23 D.2), but the PDF gives no URL. Read: pp1-24 (all)
- *What Matters In On-Policy Reinforcement Learning? A Large-Scale Empirical Study* — Andrychowicz, Raichuk, Stanczyk, Orsini, Girgin, Marinier, Hussenot, Geist, Pietquin, Michalski, Gelly, Bachem (Google Brain), arXiv v1, 10 Jun 2020. Published at ICLR 2021 as 'What Matters for On-Policy Deep Actor-Critic Methods? A Large-Scale Study' (per the MAPPO p18 and He et al. p18 reference lists). The ICLR version was not read; all numbers below are from v1.. arXiv:2006.05990 · code: none linked in v1 (built on the SEED RL code base, p2). Read: pp1-48 (all)
- *The 37 Implementation Details of Proximal Policy Optimization* — Huang, Dossa, Raffin, Kanervisto, Wang, ICLR Blog Track, 25 Mar 2022.  · code: https://github.com/vwxyzjn/ppo-implementation-details (CleanRL single-file PPO). Read: full HTML: vectorised architecture, 13 core details, 9 continuous-action details, auxiliary details (early stopping), recommendations, request for research
- *The Surprising Effectiveness of PPO in Cooperative, Multi-Agent Games* — Yu, Velu, Vinitsky, Gao, Wang, Bayen, Wu, NeurIPS 2022 Datasets and Benchmarks (arXiv v4, 4 Nov 2022). arXiv:2103.01955 · code: https://github.com/marlbenchmark/on-policy. Read: pp1-30 (all)
- *DeepMimic: Example-Guided Deep Reinforcement Learning of Physics-Based Character Skills* — Peng, Abbeel, Levine, van de Panne, ACM TOG 37(4) Art. 143, SIGGRAPH 2018 (arXiv v3). arXiv:1804.02717 · code: not linked in the PDF. Read: pp1-18 (all, incl. supplement A-D)
- *Learning to Walk in Minutes Using Massively Parallel Deep Reinforcement Learning* — Rudin, Hoeller, Reist, Hutter (ETH Zurich; NVIDIA), CoRL 2021 (arXiv comment); v3, 19 Aug 2022. arXiv:2109.11978 · code: https://leggedrobotics.github.io/legged_gym/ (p1). Read: pp1-14 (all, incl. appendix A.1-A.5)

**Problem.** SMAT gives its PPO hyperparameters: LR 5e-5/3e-5, clip 0.15, 32 envs x 2048 steps, minibatch 16,384, 20 epochs, gamma 0.99, GAE 0.95, target KL 0.01, grad norm 0.5, entropy 0.001/0.003. It gives none of the implementation choices that decide how a two-actor, shared-critic, staged PPO behaves. Missing are the action distribution and the space in which entropy is measured, how log-std is initialised and shared, freezing semantics and masked log-probs, critic inputs and continuity, reward-to-actor assignment, joint vs per-actor ratio and KL, normalisation, value clipping, time-limit bootstrapping, and the batch geometry for a GPU backend. This digest extracts what each choice does from six primary sources and how much it moved results where it was measured. It turns that into a default plus a ranked sweep list and a trainer spec. The rule for defaults is: where SMAT is silent, copy the behaviour of the code SMAT says it builds on (MyoAssist, which runs on SB3). Deviate only where that behaviour contradicts an explicit SMAT statement (e.g., 'human frozen') or is a known defect. The literature then ranks the alternatives. [ARGUED] The rule rests on SMAT's PPO table using SB3's parameter set (steps per env, minibatch, epochs, target KL, max grad norm) and on MyoAssist being SB3-based; SMAT's own code is unreleased.

**Mechanism (exact).** (1) SB3 PPO, the probable SMAT substrate (master 7cfb4dd). Rollout: a ~ N(mu(s), diag sigma^2) with a state-independent log sigma. The env receives clip(a, low, high) and the buffer keeps the unclipped a (on_policy_algorithm.py:209-216). Vec envs set info['TimeLimit.truncated'] = truncated and not terminated (dummy_vec_env.py:66; subproc_vec_env.py:38); on such steps r += gamma*V(terminal_obs) (on_policy_algorithm.py:234-245). GAE(gamma, lambda) bootstraps from V(next_obs) at the rollout end. Update (ppo.py:204-283): for each epoch, for each shuffled minibatch: advantages normalised per minibatch, (A - mean)/(std + 1e-8); ratio r = exp(logpi - logpi_old) with logpi summed over action dims; policy loss -mean(min(r*A, clip(r, 1-eps, 1+eps)*A)); value loss plain MSE to the TD(lambda) return, clipped only if clip_range_vf is set (default None); entropy loss -mean(H), with H the latent Gaussian entropy summed over dims (distributions.py:180-181); loss = policy + ent_coef*entropy_loss + vf_coef*value. Before the gradient step, approx_kl = mean((r - 1) - log r) (the k3 estimator). If it exceeds 1.5*target_kl, that step is skipped and all remaining epochs end. Otherwise backward, clip the global grad norm over all policy parameters, and take one Adam step. (2) MyoAssist b1baf69 on top of SB3 (sibling myoassist_facts). Actor MLPs end in Linear+Tanh, so the mean is in [-1,1]. One state-independent log_std vector covers all 28 action dims (default -2.0; shipped configs 0.0). Every Linear gets orthogonal gain-1 init with zero bias. Muscle excitation = 0.5 + 0.5a. One joint log-prob over 28 dims, one Adam (eps 1e-8), one target-KL stop. Early termination fires on pelvis height or on any qpos-imitation key deviating more than out_of_trajectory_threshold (shipped 0.2 rad). The env returns truncated at custom_max_episode_steps or at the end of the reference. (3) He et al. geometry. Latent u = mu + sigma*eps; executed a = f(u) (clip, or (tanh u + 1)/2 for muscles). Latent entropy has dH(u)/dmu = 0 and dH(u)/dlog sigma = 1 (eq 11), so the entropy loss adds a constant -c_ent on every log sigma and no force on the mean. Executed entropy H(a) = H(u) + E[log|det J|] (eq 13). For the muscle tanh map, dH(a)/dmu = -2E[tanh u] (eq 14), an inward mean force, and dH(a)/dlog sigma = 1 - 2sigma^2 E[sech^2 u] (eq 15). The PPO ratio is unchanged at stored latent samples because the Jacobian cancels (eq 16). Diagnostic: P11 (actual), P10 (sigma -> 0), P01 (mean centred), P00 (eqs 5-10); the near-bound threshold is |u| >= atanh(0.9) = 1.4722 (eq 4). Andrychowicz B.8 (p15) states the same point: the log tanh' term leaves policy losses and KLs unchanged and only matters for entropy regularisation, where it 'penalizes the policy for taking extreme actions which prevents tanh saturation'. (4) MAPPO (App A p11-12). The actor loss averages per-agent clipped terms with per-agent ratios pi(a_i|o_i)/pi_old(a_i|o_i) and GAE advantages, plus sigma*entropy. The critic loss is max[(V - R)^2, (clip(V, V_old +/- eps) - R)^2] with the same eps as the policy clip. Actor and critic take separate Adam steps; GAE and the reward-to-go use PopArt/value normalisation; init is orthogonal. For continuous actions the actor outputs a Gaussian mean and std. (5) DeepMimic. The state includes the phase phi in [0,1], reset each cycle (p4). The policy is Gaussian with a state-dependent mean and a FIXED diagonal covariance chosen as a hyperparameter, on a ReLU 1024-512 MLP (p4). Imitation reward (p5): r_I = 0.65*exp(-2 sum_j ||q_hat_j (-) q_j||^2) + 0.1*exp(-0.1 sum_j ||qd_hat_j - qd_j||^2) + 0.15*exp(-40 sum_e ||p_hat_e - p_e||^2) + 0.1*exp(-10 ||p_hat_c - p_c||^2). The kernel is applied to the SUM of squared errors, so it is multiplicative across joints, whereas SMAT sums per-joint kernels. RSI samples s0 from the reference; ET terminates when the torso or head touches the ground, with zero reward afterwards (p5-6). PPO settings (p16): 4096 samples per update, minibatch 256, gamma 0.95, lambda 0.95, eps 0.2, SGD momentum 0.9, policy step 5e-5 (humanoid) and value step 1e-2, with the value updated before the policy (Alg. 1). (6) Rudin. Batch = n_robots x n_steps. n_steps must be at least 25 at the 50 Hz policy rate because GAE needs multi-step rewards (p3; A.1.1 p11). Time-out resets are unpredictable to a critic that does not observe time, so the critic target is bootstrapped with its own prediction on time-outs (p3-4). Adaptive LR (Alg. 1 p14): if KL > 2*kl*, lr = max(1e-5, lr/1.5); elif KL < 0.5*kl*, lr = min(1e-2, 1.5*lr). (7) Huang (CleanRL). The vectorised architecture uses fixed-length segments carried across rollouts by next_obs/next_done. Log std is state-independent, initialised to 0, and independent across action dims (log-probs summed). The sample is clipped for the env but stored unclipped. Orthogonal init: sqrt(2) hidden, 0.01 policy output, 1 value output. Adam eps 1e-5; advantages normalised per minibatch; global grad clip 0.5; one optimizer for policy and value. The optional KL early stop stops both policy and value updates (Spinning Up stops only the policy). openai/baselines does not bootstrap truncated episodes.

**Protocol.** He et al.: MyoLeg hybrid model with 80 muscles in [0,1] and 14 motors in [-1,1], 101-D observation, 1 ms physics with frame skip 4 (250 Hz), 1000-step episodes with early termination on pelvis height or orientation, CMU 02_01 walking reference at about 1.187 m/s. The reward has a constant velocity term (r_v = 1), an env quirk (p6 eq 17). Training used SB3 2.7.1 PPO: 12 SubprocVecEnv x 2048 steps, minibatch 128, 10 epochs, lr 1e-4, lambda 0.99, clip 0.2, separate 512-512 ReLU nets, initial log sigma 0, no observation or reward normalisation, no target KL, grad norm 0.5 (Table 7 p22, Table 8 p23). Runs were 145,022,976 steps with seeds 0-2. The canonical evaluator pairs reset and noise seeds. The external replication used Dog-Stand under CleanRL: 3,000,320 steps, 3 seeds, obs and reward normalisation on, value clipping 0.2. Frozen-gradient checks used 10 (MyoLeg) or 30 (Dog) buffers; the common-state audit and a 1-10% margin sweep follow. Hardware: one RTX 5090 with a 32-thread CPU on Windows 10 (p24). Andrychowicz et al.: 5 Gym v1 MuJoCo tasks and more than 250,000 agents. Each thematic experiment samples 2000-4000 configurations uniformly within a group of choices, with the rest at the Table 2 defaults (p17), and trains 3 seeds per configuration for 1M steps (Hopper, HalfCheetah, Walker2d) or 2M (Ant, Humanoid). Score = mean stochastic-policy return over 100 episodes, evaluated every 100k steps, i.e. area under the learning curve; configuration score = median over seeds. Per choice they report the conditional 95th percentile with binomial CIs and the choice's share of the top 5% (p3). Huang et al.: a reproduction of openai/baselines ppo2 as single-file PyTorch, tracked on wandb; it runs no new ablations and borrows effect sizes from Andrychowicz and Engstrom. MAPPO: MPE (10 seeds), SMAC (6 seeds; median win rate over 32 evaluation games; up to 10M steps), GRF (6 seeds; success over 100 rollouts) and Hanabi (at least 3 seeds). Ablations cover selected maps. One 64-core CPU and one RTX 3090 (p4). DeepMimic: Bullet at 1.2 kHz with the policy at 30 Hz and stable PD targets. NR = normalised return over 32 episodes. Most skills got one training run; backflip and run got 3 seeds (Fig. 12 p17). The RSI/ET ablation evaluates with ET disabled from a fixed initial state (p13). Rudin: Isaac Gym, ANYmal C, i9-11900k plus RTX A6000. The Fig. 4 scaling study removes the curriculum and uses 5 runs per point with 1500 updates. Fig. 10 compares training with and without time-out bootstrapping on flat and rough terrain.
