**Ablations that matter**
- *Choice:* action parameterisation and entropy space
  - *Effect size:* near-bound occupancy 15.28% (Beta) to 89.07% (clip) in an 80-muscle walker; matched tanh: 71.42% H(u) vs 29.76% none vs 18.83% H(a); R_det 899 / 9154 / 5001 against R_stoch 13829 / 16011 / 15539
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* He Tables 1-2 pp9, 12
- *Choice:* initial action std
  - *Effect size:* sigma0 0.1 vs 0.5: Humanoid ~800 vs ~2850 (-72%); 1.0 vs 0.5: -6% (Humanoid) to -44% (HalfCheetah); Hopper prefers 1-2
  - *Tag:* MEASURED (read from a crop)
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz Fig. 2 p5
- *Choice:* policy output layer init scale
  - *Effect size:* x0.01 alone gives +66% on Humanoid (area under curve)
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p5, Fig. 24
- *Choice:* entropy bonus
  - *Effect size:* coefficients 1e-3 and 3e-3 (SMAT's values) score lowest on Hopper, Humanoid and Ant (e.g., Ant ~2960 at 1e-5 vs ~1020 at 3e-3); no-entropy gives the best MyoLeg stochastic return (16011 vs 13829 for H(u))
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz Fig. 81 p45; He Table 2 p12
- *Choice:* tanh vs clip
  - *Effect size:* tanh slightly better overall, +30% on HalfCheetah, clip better on Hopper; He et al. do not resolve a matched clip-vs-tanh comparison ('not resolved beyond seed variation')
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p5 Fig. 17; He p10 s5.3
- *Choice:* observation normalisation
  - *Effect size:* 'crucial' on 4/5 Gym tasks at 1-2M steps
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p6 Fig. 33
- *Choice:* value-target normalisation
  - *Effect size:* crucial on 2/5, slight help on 2/5, significant harm on Walker2d (Andrychowicz); 'never hurts... often significantly' improves MAPPO
  - *Tag:* MEASURED / ARGUED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p6 Fig. 37; MAPPO p7 Figs 2, 15
- *Choice:* value-loss clipping and Huber
  - *Effect size:* value clipping hurts at every threshold; Huber worse than MSE on all tasks (MAPPO uses both, unablated)
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p6 Figs 42-43; MAPPO Table 7 p26
- *Choice:* advantage normalisation
  - *Effect size:* per-minibatch normalisation 'seems not to affect the performance too much'
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p6 Fig. 35
- *Choice:* time-limit bootstrapping
  - *Effect size:* +10-20% total reward and lower critic loss with short rollouts (Rudin); no effect with 1000-step limits (Andrychowicz)
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Rudin A.2 p12 Fig. 10; Andrychowicz p7 Fig. 62
- *Choice:* rollout length per env
  - *Effect size:* below ~25 steps at 50 Hz learning fails (Rudin); 6-12 steps per robot collapse at batch <= 98,304 (Fig. 4a); more envs at a fixed iteration size hurt some Gym tasks
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Rudin p3, Fig. 4a p6; Andrychowicz Fig. 55
- *Choice:* epochs and minibatches in multi-agent PPO
  - *Effect size:* 4 minibatches fail every selected SMAC map; 15 epochs suboptimal on hard maps
  - *Tag:* MEASURED (discrete actions only)
  - *Location:* MAPPO p9 s5.3
- *Choice:* centralised critic inputs
  - *Effect size:* concatenated local observations ineffective with many agents; environment state alone subpar on hard maps; agent-specific global state (AS/FP) strong
  - *Tag:* MEASURED (curves)
  - *Location:* MAPPO p7-8 s5.2, Figs 3, 16
- *Choice:* reference state initialisation and early termination
  - *Effect size:* no ET: backflip 0.379 vs 0.791; no RSI: similar return but wrong motion; walking return insensitive (0.974-0.981) but learns slower without ET
  - *Tag:* MEASURED (mostly single runs)
  - *Location:* DeepMimic Table 5 p12, Fig. 11, p13
- *Choice:* separate vs shared actor/critic trunk
  - *Effect size:* separate better on 4/5 tasks
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p5 Fig. 15
- *Choice:* hidden activation
  - *Effect size:* tanh best, ReLU worst (Gym MLPs); He et al.'s ReLU 512-512 still learned MyoLeg walking
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p5 Fig. 30; He Table 8 p23
- *Choice:* PPO clip eps
  - *Effect size:* 0.2-0.3 fine (single-agent); keep below 0.2 in MARL. SMAT's 0.15 is inside both ranges
  - *Tag:* MEASURED
  - *Location:* Andrychowicz p4 Figs 10, 32; MAPPO p10

**Limitations the authors admit**
- He et al.: only PPO with a diagonal Gaussian and state-independent learned log sigma, in MuJoCo-family tasks. State-dependent std, gSDE and dimension scaling are untested. c_ent is fixed at 1e-3, so the relation between effect size and coefficient is not measured. No hardware (p18 s6.6).
- He et al.: n = 3 seeds; return comparisons are descriptive, not population claims (p8 s4.6; p15).
- He et al.: the MyoLeg velocity subreward is constant (r_v = 1), an environment quirk (p6).
- He et al.: the local gradient decomposition constrains but does not explain the long-horizon trajectories (App C p22). The variance-only (P01) comparison does not hold across margins (App A p21).
- He et al.: audit defects were corrected during the work (reward-selected checkpoints, a Beta fallback, replayed buffers, evaluator naming). Two seed-0 runs resume 30M-step pilots and other seeds continue 100M-step runs (App E p24).
- Andrychowicz: choices interact, so single-choice conclusions need care. The losses compared were designed for different settings (p3-4). The time-limit result is 'probably' due to the high 1000-step limit (p7). The MuJoCo version can change behaviour (footnote p2).
- Huang: the blog aims at high-fidelity reproduction, not ablation. It deliberately keeps baselines' lack of truncation bootstrapping, and notes that conclusions from MuJoCo may not transfer to Atari.
- MAPPO: all benchmarks have discrete actions, are cooperative, and mostly have homogeneous agents. Continuous actions and heterogeneous agents are left to future work. No theory (p10). The death-masking justification is intuitive (p15).
- DeepMimic: the phase variable 'advances linearly with time' and limits timing adjustment. Multi-clip is not shown on large libraries. PD gains need insight. Training takes days per skill. The reward metric and weights are hand-defined (p13 s11). Most ablation statistics come from one run (p13 s10.4).
- Rudin: the 25-step threshold is stated 'for our task' (p3). The scaling study removes the curriculum (p6). The aim is fast training, not the best policy (p8). The sim step is limited by the actuator network (A.1.1).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- He et al.: the 89% clipped-Gaussian condition uses SB3's unbounded (linear) mean, and 82% of means sit outside [0,1]. MyoAssist's Tanh-bounded mean plus clip, the configuration SMAT probably used, was never tested, so 89% cannot be carried over. Mean extrusion beyond the interval is impossible there, but saturation at the bound (tanh' -> 0) is not.
- He et al.: Table 7 documents only the tanh experiments. The clipped, Beta, override and symmetric-tau configurations are not fully specified, so Table 1 vs Table 2 is not a matched comparison.
- He et al.: the deterministic-return collapse (R_det 899 vs R_stoch 13829 under H(u)) is reported as geometry, but its practical consequence (a deterministic muscle policy that may not walk) is not analysed. Fall rates are never reported.
- He et al.: MyoLeg runs at 250 Hz with frame skip 4; SMAT and MyoAssist use 30-50 Hz. Boundary behaviour may depend on control rate, which was not tested.
- Andrychowicz: every score is an area under the learning curve at 1-2M steps, so the results measure early learning speed, not the asymptote SMAT reaches after 320M+ steps. Initial-distribution effects (e.g., +66%) may shrink at long budgets.
- Andrychowicz: the defaults differ from SB3/CleanRL: a shared trunk, value normalisation on, per-minibatch advantage normalisation off, and 8-step fragments (2048/256). Advantages are unnormalised, so entropy-coefficient values are not portable to SB3, where advantages are normalised.
- Andrychowicz: the regularisation study ran under the default clip transform. The tanh-entropy Jacobian term they describe in B.8 was never tested with an entropy bonus.
- Andrychowicz: per-choice effects exist only as bar charts without tables, so every number beyond the text is a figure reading.
- Huang: it attributes Andrychowicz's entropy finding to 'decision C13', which is PPO-style value clipping in Andrychowicz (entropy is C33/C46). The MuJoCo defaults it reproduces use num_envs = 1, far from the 32-8192-env regimes here.
- MAPPO: the suggestions on epochs, minibatches and clip come from discrete-action SMAC win-rate curves with large variance (e.g., 3s5z_vs_3s6z 84.4 +/- 34.0) and are tuned per map (Table 17), so 'minimal tuning' is partial. Joint and per-agent ratios are not compared. Value clipping and Huber are used without an ablation, although Andrychowicz found both harmful.
- MAPPO: parameter sharing is shown to be large (Table 4) but is impossible for heterogeneous actors like SMAT's human and exo. The results therefore do not cover the separate-network regime SMAT must use, where MAPPO-Ind did much worse.
- DeepMimic: the fixed covariance value is not reported. The ablation's evaluation disables ET and fixes the start state, which differs from training. The strong RSI/ET claims rest on single runs of acrobatic skills, while walking barely changes. Training uses SGD with momentum, not Adam.
- Rudin: the paper does not name its KL estimator or the adaptive LR's starting value, and does not ablate adaptive vs fixed LR or entropy 0.01. The statement that Stable-Baselines ignores time-outs refers to the 2018 TF library [26]; SB3 now bootstraps them (on_policy_algorithm.py:234-245, checked here).

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- He et al.: full hyperparameters for the Table 1 configurations; Beta parameterisation details; wall-clock times; the code URL.
- Andrychowicz: whether the entropy penalty sums or averages over action dims (B.6 says only that alpha*R is added); per-choice values appear only as bars.
- MAPPO: the continuous-action std parameterisation; whether value normalisation is PopArt (Alg. 1 p11) or running mean/std (s5.1 p7) in each experiment.
- DeepMimic: the fixed Sigma value; the training episode horizon for cyclic skills (20 s is given for evaluation and the ablation); the ET link set beyond 'torso or head'.
- Rudin: the KL estimator; the adaptive LR's initial value; which distribution the entropy bonus uses.
- Huang: CleanRL's exact KL-check placement (per epoch vs per minibatch) is described only qualitatively.
- Across all six sources: none studies two heterogeneous continuous actors with a shared critic, actor freezing or a staged curriculum. Every SMAT-specific interaction below is therefore [ARGUED].

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *Default rule:* Where SMAT is silent, copy SB3/MyoAssist behaviour, the code SMAT says it builds on. Deviate only where that contradicts an explicit SMAT statement (e.g., 'human frozen' requires per-actor log-std) or is a known defect. The literature then orders the sweeps. Record every choice in the run manifest.
- *Choices:*
  - *Rank:* 1
    - *Choice:* Exploration-noise parameterisation: log-std sharing, freezing and initial value (human at S1, exo at its S3 re-init)
    - *Smat says:* entropy coefficient only (0.001 S1-S2, 0.003 S3-S4); human 'frozen' in S3; exo 're-initialised' in S3
    - *Default:* Per-actor, state-independent, learned log-std vectors (human 26, exo 2), initialised to 0.0 in [-1,1] units (sigma 1, i.e. excitation sigma 0.5). An actor's log-std is frozen with its weights. Inactive actors (the exo in S1-S2) are left out of log-prob, entropy and KL.
    - *Why default:* 0.0 matches MyoAssist's shipped configs, the SB3/CleanRL default and He et al.'s setting. Per-actor vectors are needed for 'frozen' to mean frozen. MyoAssist's single 28-dim vector would keep training the frozen human's sigma in S3, and would let inert exo dims in S1-S2 drift upward under the constant entropy force (He eq 12).
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - init -0.69 (sigma 0.5, Andrychowicz's best)
      - init -1.0
      - init -2.0 (MyoAssist code default when the config omits it; equally 'literal')
      - MyoAssist-literal shared vector
      - exo-only lower init (e.g., -1.0) with the human at 0.0
      - fixed non-learned sigma (DeepMimic p4)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] Andrychowicz Fig. 2: sigma0 0.1 vs 0.5 costs up to -72% (Humanoid); 1.0 vs 0.5 costs -6% to -44%. He et al.: with init 0 and c_ent 1e-3 on H(u), 80-muscle latent log sigma rises to +0.80/+0.88 instead of falling to -0.69 (Tables 1-2). [ARGUED] S3 is ~17 updates, so the exo's S3 behaviour is set mostly by its init mean and sigma. At sigma 1 with a zero mean, 31.7% of exo commands are clipped to +/- tau_max ([DERIVED]), consistent with the reported S3 +/-1 saturation.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p5 Fig. 2; He Tables 1-2, eq 12; Huang (log std init 0); myoassist_facts policy_head
  - *Rank:* 2
    - *Choice:* Action distribution and entropy measurement space
    - *Smat says:* human outputs in [0,1] (26 excitations); exo in [-1,1]; nothing on the distribution
    - *Default:* MyoAssist-literal. Linear+Tanh mean in [-1,1] and a diagonal Gaussian; the env gets clip(a,-1,1) and the buffer keeps the unclipped a. Excitation = 0.5 + 0.5*clip(a_h); exo torque = tau_max*clip(a_e). Entropy is the latent H(u) summed over active dims (SB3 distributions.py:180-181).
    - *Why default:* It is the configuration SMAT's substrate implements (myoassist_facts policy_head; SB3 on_policy_algorithm.py:209-216).
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - linear mean + tanh squash, H(u)
      - tanh squash + executed-action entropy H(a) with He's 4-sample antithetic estimator (App D.1 eqs 23-25)
      - no entropy bonus (c_ent 0)
      - Beta policy
      - SB3 MlpPolicy linear mean + clip (He's 'clipped Gaussian')
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] He et al. (80 muscles, 3 seeds): near-bound 89.07% clip, 15.28% Beta; tanh 71.42 / 29.76 / 18.83% for H(u) / none / H(a). Stochastic return varies modestly (12.4k-16.0k); deterministic return varies about 10x (899-9154). Andrychowicz: tanh >= clip, +30% on HalfCheetah. [ARGUED] SMAT's activation metrics (10.1% / 6.1%, r_muscle, r_delta_a, r_hip_act) and the exo peak and saturation are all functions of executed-action geometry. He et al. never tested a tanh-bounded mean plus clip, so measure P10/P01 rather than assume.
    - *Sources:* He Tables 1-2 pp9, 12, eqs 11-16; Andrychowicz p5 Fig. 17, B.8 p15
  - *Rank:* 3
    - *Choice:* Frozen-human action mode in S3, and evaluation mode
    - *Smat says:* nothing
    - *Default:* In S3 the human samples from its frozen distribution (weights and sigma frozen). Evaluation reports deterministic and stochastic modes on paired seeds. Before S3, a gate compares the two modes on the S2 human (falls per 100 strides, imitation reward). The metric of record is declared in eval/ before any result.
    - *Why default:* SB3 rollouts sample every policy dim, and the 2026-09-05 integration test sampled the frozen human (myoassist_facts). He et al. show deterministic muscle policies can do far worse than stochastic ones.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - deterministic frozen human (the sibling smat digest's default; see conflicts)
      - stochastic with sigma scaled by 0.5
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] He Table 2: R_det/R_stoch = 6.5% (H(u)), 57% (none), 32% (H(a)); 'deterministic return is much lower than stochastic return under all three tanh conditions' (p17). [ARGUED] An exo trained against the wrong human mode learns a different assistance, and activation metrics under stochastic evaluation include clip-atom mass that depends on sigma.
    - *Sources:* He Table 2 p12, s6.3 p17; myoassist_facts prior_work
  - *Rank:* 4
    - *Choice:* Reward-to-actor assignment
    - *Smat says:* one weight vector per stage; terms 'can be enabled or disabled per agent' (O p4, per sibling)
    - *Default:* Team reward: one scalar reward and one critic shared by all active actors.
    - *Why default:* It is the literal reading of one V_psi with one weight vector, and it is MAPPO's cooperative setting (p3 s3.1-3.2).
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - per-agent reward subsets (exo: r_exo3/4, r_delta_tau, optionally r_hip_act; human: the rest) with a two-head critic
      - shared reward plus an exo-only auxiliary head
    - *Expected effect:* Not measured in any source (MAPPO studies only shared rewards). [ARGUED] Under a team reward, the human is paid by r_exo4 for hip velocity aligned with the torque, while hip imitation is released. The exo, in turn, is credited with human imitation noise. This can move hip ROM and the exo's learning speed.
    - *Sources:* MAPPO p3; smat_original_and_extended.json limitations_not_admitted
  - *Rank:* 5
    - *Choice:* Target-KL semantics
    - *Smat says:* target KL 0.01
    - *Default:* SB3 semantics (ppo.py:262-270). Before each minibatch step, compute approx_kl = mean((r - 1) - log r) over the active dims (k3). If it exceeds 1.5 x 0.01 = 0.015, skip that step and end all remaining epochs. Log the number of steps actually taken per update.
    - *Why default:* SB3 behaviour (ppo.py:262-270); SMAT's PPO table uses SB3's parameter names ([ARGUED]).
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - CleanRL: check after each epoch against 1.0 x target; stops policy and value (Huang)
      - Spinning Up: policy-only stop
      - Rudin adaptive LR: /1.5 if KL > 0.02, x1.5 if KL < 0.005, bounds [1e-5, 1e-2] (Alg. 1 p14)
      - per-actor KL stops
      - no early stop
    - *Expected effect:* Not measured in any source. [ARGUED] With 4 minibatches x 20 epochs at lr 3e-5 to 5e-5, the stop sets the effective epoch count and therefore the step size. This matters most in S3 (~17 updates) and in S4, where a joint KL over 26+2 dims is dominated by the human. Huang: healthy approx_kl stays below 0.02.
    - *Sources:* SB3 ppo.py:262-270; Huang (early stopping); Rudin Alg. 1 p14
  - *Rank:* 6
    - *Choice:* Joint update form (ratio, clip, KL, optimizer)
    - *Smat says:* 'jointly update (pi_h, pi_e, V_psi) using PPO'
    - *Default:* One ratio over the product of active actors, r = exp(sum over active actors of dlogpi), with one clip (0.15), one normalised advantage, one Adam over trainable params and one KL stop. This is identical to per-actor forms in S1-S3 (one active actor) and differs only in S4.
    - *Why default:* MyoAssist computes one joint Gaussian log-prob over all 28 dims (probe_policy, myoassist_facts training_mechanics). SB3's PPO.train has no per-actor path.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - MAPPO form: per-actor ratios r_h and r_e, each clipped, averaged, with a shared advantage (MAPPO App A p11), plus per-actor KL stops and separate Adam optimisers for the human, the exo and the critic (MAPPO Alg. 1; DeepMimic p16)
    - *Expected effect:* No source compares the forms. [ARGUED] Under the joint form, the 26 human dims dominate the log-ratio variance and the KL, so exo gradients are clipped or stopped by human changes. That is a candidate contributor to weak exo learning when both learn (the Stage-4-only ablation).
    - *Sources:* MAPPO p11; myoassist_facts training_mechanics
  - *Rank:* 7
    - *Choice:* Critic inputs, continuity across stages, value normalisation
    - *Smat says:* one V_psi (256/128) on 'the full system state', initialised once, updated every stage
    - *Default:* V(concat(human obs, exo obs 18)), with the exo block zeroed in S1-S2 so the input size never changes. The critic is carried across stages. No value normalisation; MSE loss without clipping; vf_coef 0.5.
    - *Why default:* It is the literal reading of the paper and matches the sibling spec. It resembles MAPPO's AS state (local plus global).
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - critic-only privileged inputs: reference phase sin/cos, fraction of time to truncation, tau_max/25, stage one-hot (MAPPO Suggestion 2 p8; Rudin p3-4 on unobserved time-outs)
      - FP-style dedup of duplicated hip features
      - running value-target normalisation, reset at each stage (MAPPO p7; Andrychowicz B.9)
      - critic reset, or K value-only warm-up updates, at the S3/S4 start
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] Value normalisation: significant gains in MAPPO; mixed in Andrychowicz (+ on 2/5, - on Walker2d). Critic inputs: concatenated local observations fail, AS/FP strong (MAPPO p8). [ARGUED] S3 changes reward weights (r_fwd 0.8 -> 1.5, r_muscle x15, new r_hip_act and r_exo terms), so a carried critic starts S3 biased, and S3 has only ~17 updates to correct it.
    - *Sources:* MAPPO p7-8, Figs 2-3, 15-16; Andrychowicz p6 Fig. 37; Rudin p3-4
  - *Rank:* 8
    - *Choice:* Early termination and RSI in S3-S4
    - *Smat says:* nothing on termination; hip imitation released in S3-S4
    - *Default:* RSI on (MyoAssist random reference start, with reference qpos/qvel on tracked joints). Always terminate on a fall (pelvis height). Check deviation-based termination only on joints whose imitation weight is non-zero in the current stage.
    - *Why default:* DeepMimic shows ET/RSI are decisive for learning, and that ET 'biases the data distribution' (p6). MyoAssist checks out_of_trajectory over all qpos-imitation keys at 0.2 rad (myoassist_facts), so with zero-weight hip keys still present, episodes would still end on hip deviation although hip imitation is off.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - MyoAssist-literal check on all keys
      - a wider hip threshold (e.g., 0.4 rad)
      - fall-only termination
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] DeepMimic Table 5: no ET drops backflip return to 0.379 from 0.791; walking return barely changes (0.974-0.981) but learns slower. [ARGUED] A hip-deviation termination in S3-S4 would bound the simulated hip-ROM change under assistance. Log terminations by joint.
    - *Sources:* DeepMimic pp5-6, Table 5 p12; myoassist_facts termination
  - *Rank:* 9
    - *Choice:* Observation normalisation
    - *Smat says:* nothing (E p5 says hardware input statistics matched training)
    - *Default:* None (MyoAssist; He et al.'s SB3 MyoLeg learned walking without it, Table 7 p22). Log per-feature mean and std.
    - *Why default:* It is MyoAssist's behaviour.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - running mean/std clipped to +/-10 (VecNormalize), with statistics frozen alongside the actor they feed (human stats frozen in S3; exo stats created at S3; extended at the S4 input expansion)
      - fixed hand scaling (velocities / 2 rad/s)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] Andrychowicz: 'crucial' on 4/5 tasks at 1-2M steps (Fig. 33); MAPPO uses it (Table 7). [ARGUED] It probably matters most for the short S3 budget and least for the 320M-step S1.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p6; MAPPO Table 7; Huang (VecNormalize)
  - *Rank:* 10
    - *Choice:* Time-limit bootstrapping (and rollout-end bootstrapping)
    - *Smat says:* nothing
    - *Default:* Bootstrap on truncation: r += gamma*V(final_obs) when truncated and not terminated (SB3 on_policy_algorithm.py:234-245, with the flag set at dummy_vec_env.py:66 and subproc_vec_env.py:38). Always bootstrap V(next_obs) at the rollout end. Never bootstrap falls or early-termination events.
    - *Why default:* It is SB3 behaviour, and MyoAssist returns truncated at the episode cap and at the end of the reference.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - treat time-outs as terminal (openai/baselines, per Huang)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] Rudin: +10-20% reward and lower critic loss (A.2). Andrychowicz: no effect with 1000-step limits (Fig. 62). [ARGUED] The effect grows as GPU rollouts shorten.
    - *Sources:* Rudin p3-4, A.2 p12; Andrychowicz p7; SB3 source
  - *Rank:* 11
    - *Choice:* GPU batch geometry (our deviation, not a SMAT ambiguity; must be right for GPU runs)
    - *Smat says:* 32 x 2048 on CPU
    - *Default:* At least 32 steps per world per update (0.64 s at 50 Hz). G1 'faithful': 2048 worlds x 32 = 65,536 per update, minibatch 16,384, 20 epochs. G2 'fast': 8192 x 32 = 262,144, 4 minibatches of 65,536, 20 epochs, same KL stop. Budgets are logged in both updates and env steps, and S3 runs at matched updates (~17), not matched steps.
    - *Why default:* Rudin: fewer than 25 steps fails at 50 Hz, and 6-12 steps collapse. G1 keeps SMAT's update geometry exactly; G2 keeps 4 minibatches x 20 epochs.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - 4096 x 24 (Rudin's Table 3)
      - 8192 x 64
      - fewer epochs (5-10) for G2 (Rudin 5; MAPPO <= 10 on hard tasks)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] Rudin Fig. 4a: at batch 98,304, reward ~15 at 48-192 steps per robot, ~13 at 24, ~9 at 12, ~2 at 6. Andrychowicz Fig. 55: more envs at a fixed iteration size hurt some tasks. [ARGUED] G2 does 4x fewer updates per env-step, so compare against CPU at matched updates as well as matched steps.
    - *Sources:* Rudin p3, Fig. 4 p6, Table 3 p13; Andrychowicz p7; Huang (Brax 2048 x 20)
  - *Rank:* 12
    - *Choice:* Advantage normalisation
    - *Smat says:* nothing
    - *Default:* Per minibatch, (A - mean)/(std + 1e-8) (SB3 ppo.py:218-219).
    - *Why default:* SB3 behaviour.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - per batch
      - none
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] Small (Andrychowicz Fig. 35). [ARGUED] Keep it on: with SMAT's dt-scaled rewards, turning it off makes c_ent much stronger relative to the surrogate.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p6; Huang; SB3
  - *Rank:* 13
    - *Choice:* Value clipping
    - *Smat says:* nothing
    - *Default:* None (SB3 clip_range_vf None; MyoAssist clip_vf 100, effectively off).
    - *Why default:* SB3/MyoAssist behaviour, and the evidence says clipping hurts.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - MAPPO-style +/-0.15 with Huber delta 10
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] Andrychowicz: value clipping hurts at every threshold (Fig. 43); Huber is worse (Fig. 42). [ARGUED] +/-0.15 in absolute value units would also slow the critic's re-fit after a stage's reward change.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p6; MAPPO p12; Huang
  - *Rank:* 14
    - *Choice:* Hidden activation and weight init
    - *Smat says:* layer sizes only
    - *Default:* MyoAssist-literal: tanh hidden layers, orthogonal gain 1 on every Linear, zero bias, Tanh actor outputs.
    - *Why default:* myoassist_facts: 'Every Linear gets orthogonal init with gain 1 and zero bias ... not SB3's usual 0.01 output gain'.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - CleanRL/SB3: sqrt(2) hidden, 0.01 actor output, 1.0 critic output
      - ReLU hidden (He et al. 512-512; MAPPO; DeepMimic)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] A 0.01 output gain gives +66% on Humanoid (Andrychowicz p5); tanh beats ReLU in Gym MLPs (Fig. 30). [ARGUED] The output gain matters most for the exo re-init at S3: gain 1 gives random, state-dependent torques, while 0.01 gives a near-zero mean.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p5; Huang; myoassist_facts
  - *Rank:* 15
    - *Choice:* Optimizer and its reset between stages
    - *Smat says:* LR values only
    - *Default:* A new Adam per stage over the trainable params (beta1 0.9, beta2 0.999, eps 1e-8 as in MyoAssist), with constant LR.
    - *Why default:* A new PPO object per stage recreates the optimizer (MyoAssist-style driver); SMAT lists single LR values.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - carry Adam moments across stages
      - separate optimizers per actor and critic
      - eps 1e-5
      - linear LR decay
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED elsewhere] Adam eps has no effect (Andrychowicz Fig. 68); LR decay gains are small except Ant +15% (Fig. 65). The stage reset itself is unmeasured.
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p7-8; MAPPO Alg. 1; myoassist_facts
  - *Rank:* 16
    - *Choice:* Gradient-clipping scope
    - *Smat says:* grad norm 0.5
    - *Default:* One global norm over all trainable params (SB3 ppo.py:277).
    - *Why default:* SB3 behaviour.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - per-module clipping (implied by separate optimizers)
    - *Expected effect:* [MEASURED] Gradient clipping gives a small boost and the threshold matters little (Andrychowicz Fig. 34). [ARGUED] Global clipping couples a critic-gradient spike at a stage change to smaller actor steps (He p16 lists 'shared gradient clipping' among the dynamics that set the final operating point).
    - *Sources:* Andrychowicz p6; He p16 s6.1
  - *Rank:* 17
    - *Choice:* Reward normalisation
    - *Smat says:* nothing
    - *Default:* None (MyoAssist; He et al.'s MyoLeg run).
    - *Why default:* MyoAssist behaviour.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - divide by the running std of the discounted return, clip +/-10 (baselines VecNormalize per Huang; MAPPO Table 7)
    - *Expected effect:* Not measured in these sources. Huang relays Engstrom: reward scaling 'can significantly affect' performance.
    - *Sources:* Huang; MAPPO Table 7
  - *Rank:* 18
    - *Choice:* Phase variable
    - *Smat says:* 'musculoskeletal body-state observation'; no phase
    - *Default:* No phase in actor inputs (MyoAssist has none; myoassist_facts).
    - *Why default:* MyoAssist behaviour.
    - *Alternatives to sweep:*
      - phase sin/cos in the critic only (privileged)
      - phase in both human and critic (DeepMimic p4)
    - *Expected effect:* Not measured. DeepMimic p13 names the phase as a limitation because it ties timing to the clock. [ARGUED] The time-indexed imitation reward is partly unobservable without it, which hurts the critic more than the actor.
    - *Sources:* DeepMimic p4, p13
- *Ranked outcome impact:*
  - 1. Exploration-noise parameterisation (per-actor log-std, freezing, init; especially the exo at S3). S3 lasts ~17 updates, so the init largely defines S3. Andrychowicz Fig. 2 shows up to -72% from the init; He et al. show sigma drifting up under H(u) at c_ent 1e-3.
  - 2. Action distribution and entropy space. He et al.: 15-89% near-bound occupancy and R_det 899-9154. SMAT's activation and saturation metrics are geometry metrics.
  - 3. Frozen-human action mode and evaluation mode. He et al.: R_det/R_stoch 6.5-57% for muscle policies.
  - 4. Reward-to-actor assignment. Unmeasured, but it changes each actor's objective.
  - 5. Target-KL semantics. Sets the effective step size; decisive for the 17-update S3.
  - 6. Joint vs per-actor ratio, KL and optimizer in S4. Unmeasured; the KL is human-dominated.
  - 7. Critic across stages: inputs, carry-over or reset, value normalisation. MAPPO significant; Andrychowicz mixed.
  - 8. Early-termination joint set in S3-S4. DeepMimic ET is decisive; MyoAssist still checks released hips.
  - 9. Observation normalisation. Andrychowicz: crucial on 4/5 tasks at short budgets.
  - 10. Time-limit bootstrapping (Rudin +10-20%) and, on GPU only, rollouts of at least 25-32 steps at 50 Hz (Rudin).
  - Low: advantage normalisation, value clipping, Adam eps and reset, activation and init (except the exo output gain), grad-clip scope, LR schedule, reward normalisation, phase.
- *Conflicts with sibling digests:*
  - Frozen-human action mode. digests/smat_original_and_extended.json (recipe step 9) defaults to deterministic human actions in S3. This digest defaults to stochastic, with a pre-S3 gate, because He Table 2 measured R_det at 6.5-57% of R_stoch for muscle PPO. Coordinator decision; run both.
  - Joint update form. smat_original_and_extended.json (recipe step 8) defaults to separate clipped losses per actor. This digest defaults to one product ratio with one KL stop, because MyoAssist/SB3 implement that and SMAT's PPO table uses SB3 parameters ([ARGUED]); per-actor is sweep 1. The two forms only differ in S4.

**Recipe on our server.**
- 0. Config. Put every choice from reproduction_relevance.choices in one YAML with profile 'smat_sb3' (the defaults) plus named sweep arms. Hash the YAML into the run manifest. Log-std and noise figures are in [-1,1] latent units.
- 1. Networks (PyTorch). HumanActor: Linear(d_h,256)-Tanh-Linear(256,128)-Tanh-Linear(128,26)-Tanh -> mu_h. ExoActor: Linear(18,128)-Tanh-Linear(128,64)-Tanh-Linear(64,2)-Tanh -> mu_e. Critic: Linear(d_c,256)-Tanh-Linear(256,128)-Tanh-Linear(128,1), with d_c = d_h + 18 (+ a privileged block in that arm). log_std_h = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(26)); log_std_e = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(2)), re-created at S3. Init: orthogonal gain 1 and zero bias on every Linear (MyoAssist); sweep arm uses sqrt(2) hidden, 0.01 actor output, 1.0 critic output. S4 input expansion: widen HumanActor layer 0 from d_h to d_h+2, copy the old columns, draw the new columns from N(0, 0.01), and extend any observation normaliser.
- 2. Sampling and env mapping. If the human is sampled: a_h = mu_h + exp(log_std_h)*eps, otherwise a_h = mu_h. Excitation = 0.5 + 0.5*clip(a_h,-1,1). Exo: a_e likewise; torque = tau_max[stage]*clip(a_e,-1,1), with tau_max 0/0/6/25; the exo history stores the clipped command. Store the unclipped a_h and a_e, the per-actor old log-probs logp_h and logp_e (each summed over that actor's dims), V(s), and per-step active and sampled flags.
- 3. Stage table. S1/S2: human trainable and sampled; exo absent (a_e = 0, never sampled, no log-prob, entropy or KL); critic trainable. S3: human frozen (weights and log_std_h requires_grad False) and sampled stochastically by default, excluded from ratio, entropy and KL; exo re-initialised, with a new log_std_e, and trainable; critic carried and trainable. S4: human and exo both trainable and sampled; critic trainable. set_stage(k) sets requires_grad, rebuilds Adam over the trainable params (fresh moments, eps 1e-8), and SHA-256-hashes every frozen group (weights, log-std, normaliser stats) before and after the stage, asserting they are unchanged.
- 4. Rollout. For t in range(n_steps): act; step the vectorised env; capture final_obs BEFORE the auto-reset; if truncated and not terminated, add gamma*V(final_obs) to r_t (SB3 style); done_t = terminated or truncated. After the loop, compute V(next_obs) for the rollout-end bootstrap. Terminations (falls, early termination) get no bootstrap.
- 5. GAE. delta_t = r_t + gamma*V_{t+1}*(1 - done_t) - V_t; A_t = delta_t + gamma*lambda*(1 - done_t)*A_{t+1}; R_t = A_t + V_t; gamma 0.99, lambda 0.95.
- 6. Update, default joint form. For epoch in 1..20, shuffle and split into minibatches (CPU: 16,384). Normalise A per minibatch (+1e-8). d = sum over active actors of (logpi_actor(a|s) - logp_actor_old); r = exp(d); L_pi = -mean(min(r*A, clamp(r, 0.85, 1.15)*A)). H = sum over active actors and dims of (log_std + 0.5*log(2*pi*e)), i.e. latent H(u). L_v = mean((V(s) - R)^2), unclipped. loss = L_pi - c_ent*mean(H) + 0.5*L_v, with c_ent 0.001 (S1-S2) or 0.003 (S3-S4). Before stepping, compute kl = mean(exp(d) - 1 - d); if kl > 0.015, skip this step and end all epochs. Otherwise backward, clip_grad_norm_(trainable, 0.5), Adam step at lr 5e-5 (S1) or 3e-5 (S2-S4).
- 7. Per-actor sweep arm (MAPPO form). Compute r_h and r_e separately; L_pi = mean over active actors of -mean(min(r_i*A, clamp(r_i)*A)) with the same A. Use one Adam each for the human, the exo and the critic. Each actor gets its own k3 KL and stop flag; the critic runs all epochs, or stops with the last actor.
- 8. H(a) sweep arm. For each active actor, H_hat = H(u) + (1/4)*sum_k sum_i l(u_k,i), with antithetic eps {e1, -e1, e2, -e2}. Use l(u) = log 2 - 2u - 2*softplus(-2u) for the [0,1] muscle map, or log 4 - 2u - 2*softplus(-2u) for [-1,1]; this requires the tanh-squash parameterisation (He App D.1 eqs 23-25). The PPO ratio is unchanged (eq 16).
- 9. Logging per update: k3 KL per minibatch and per actor; Adam steps actually taken; clip fraction per actor; entropy and log-std (mean/min/max) per actor; value loss; explained variance; truncation and termination counts, with terminations broken down by joint. At every checkpoint, He et al.'s P11/P10/P01 for the human's 26 dims and the exo's 2, plus R_det vs R_stoch and falls per 100 strides.
- 10. Unit tests (Huang recommendations plus staging): ratio == 1 on the first minibatch of the first epoch for each actor; masked actors add exactly 0 to log-prob, entropy and KL; frozen hashes unchanged after S3; time-out bootstrap applied only when truncated and not terminated; final_obs captured before the reset; save and reload reproduce identical actions; seed everything.
- 11. CPU geometry (SMAT-literal): 32 envs x 2048 = 65,536 per update, 4 minibatches of 16,384, 20 epochs. Budgets S1 320M (4,883 updates), S2 160M (2,441), S3 1.1M (~17), S4 66M (1,007) under aggregate counting.
- 12. GPU geometry (MuJoCo Warp): G1 = 2048 worlds x 32 steps (65,536; minibatch 16,384; 20 epochs), which keeps SMAT's update geometry. G2 = 8192 x 32 (262,144; 4 minibatches of 65,536; 20 epochs plus the KL stop). Never go below 25 steps per world at 50 Hz (Rudin). Keep the buffer, GAE and update on the GPU (Rudin p2-3). Budget S3 in updates (~17) as well as steps. RSI plus early termination desynchronise resets.
- 13. Calibration gate before any sweep. Run S1 for a fixed proxy budget (e.g., 20M steps) under CPU-literal, G1 and G2, 3 seeds each. Compare curves at matched env steps AND matched updates, together with the S1 gates (left-right r, toe-off). Use G2 for sweeps only if it stays within seed variance of G1; otherwise use G1.
- 14. Sweep order: follow reproduction_relevance.ranked_outcome_impact. Use at least 3 seeds per arm and branch the S3/S4 arms from shared S1/S2 bases.

**Throughput.** SMAT: about 28 h per 100M steps on an 8-core Xeon W-2145, i.e. 992 steps/s, or about 153 h for the 547.1M-step pipeline (from smat_original_and_extended.json). DeepMimic: about 60M samples and about 2 days on an 8-core CPU per skill, with no GPU, i.e. about 350 samples/s (p16, derived). He et al.: 145M-step runs with 12 SubprocVecEnvs on an RTX 5090 plus a 32-thread CPU; no wall time is reported (App E p24). Rudin: 4096 robots, 1500 updates of 98,304 samples in under 20 min, i.e. at least 123k samples/s end to end on an RTX A6000 (p7, derived); simulation dominates step time, and learning time does not depend on robot count (Fig. 8 p11); 6 GB of VRAM for 4096 rigid-body robots without rendering. The request states that MuJoCo Warp runs about 235k transitions/s at 8192 worlds on our RTX 5090; this digest did not re-measure that. At that rate simulation alone takes about 7.1 min per 100M steps, about 23 min for S1 and about 39 min for the whole pipeline (derived). [ARGUED] PPO's cost per G2 update (80 passes of 65,536 samples through MLPs with about 100k parameters) should be small next to the ~1.1 s of simulation, but measure it. G1 (2048 worlds) will run below the 8192-world rate; its throughput is unmeasured. Wall-clock, not sample count, is the right comparison across geometries (Huang; Andrychowicz p7: more envs are faster in wall-clock but can cost sample efficiency). Per-world memory for the 26-muscle model with tendon wrapping is unknown; measure VRAM at 2048/4096/8192 worlds before fixing G2.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- SMAT's Stage 3 saturation at +/-1 may be partly an exploration artefact rather than learned bang-bang control. With clipping and sigma near 1 (log-std 0), 31.7% of executed commands sit exactly at the bound even at a zero mean ([DERIVED]), and with only ~17 S3 updates sigma cannot shrink much. This adds to r_exo3's analytic bang-bang optimum.
- If SMAT used MyoAssist's single log-std vector with the exo dims present but inert in S1-S2, the exo's log-std received only the constant entropy force (He eq 12) plus zero-mean surrogate noise, so it drifted upward before S3. Whether 're-initialised' reset it is unknown; a raised exo sigma at S3 would reproduce the saturation. [ARGUED]
- The Stage-4-only collapse could be optimizer coupling rather than non-stationarity. A joint ratio and joint KL over 26 human dims plus 2 exo dims, and global gradient clipping, throttle the exo's steps. The noise in its own actions is also taxed: the expected cost of i.i.d. executed noise under r_exo4 (weight 4) and r_delta_tau (weight 1) is about 4*0.15*V + 2*V per joint per step, where V is the executed-noise variance (V = 0.516 for sigma 1 after clipping, so about 1.34). That is far more than the linear 4*0.3*mu*w gain at small |mu| ([DERIVED] from SMAT's weights in smat_original_and_extended.json). It pushes sigma down before the mean learns. [ARGUED]
- Under a MyoAssist-literal shared log-std, the 'frozen' S3 human's sigma keeps training (entropy 0.003 pushes it up), so the exo learns against a human that differs from the S2 human. [ARGUED]
- If activations are measured under stochastic execution, the mean clipped excitation depends on sigma through the clip atoms. The S2 -> S4 change in entropy coefficient (0.001 -> 0.003) and in r_muscle (x15) shifts sigma, and hence the measured activation, independently of assistance. Deterministic evaluation removes this effect, but a deterministic muscle policy may walk worse (He Table 2). [ARGUED]
- The simulated gait gates (symmetry r > 0.92, toe-off) may have come from stochastic or deterministic rollouts. He et al.'s R_det/R_stoch gap shows the two can differ by an order of magnitude in return for muscle PPO. [ARGUED]
- A MyoAssist-literal out_of_trajectory termination that still checks the released hip keys would end any S3-S4 episode whose hip deviates more than 0.2 rad from the reference. That would make simulated assistance look 'gait-preserving' by construction. [ARGUED]
- Rewards are dt-scaled and SB3 normalises advantages per minibatch, so SMAT's entropy coefficients are relatively strong. He et al. measured latent log sigma rising to +0.8 at c_ent 1e-3 in muscle PPO, so a boundary-dominated human (He: 71-89% near-bound) is a plausible baseline state that the activation metrics inherit. [ARGUED]

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Adopt He et al.'s four-cell diagnostic (P11/P10/P01/P00) for the 26 human dims and 2 exo dims at every stage end. It separates saturation of the mean from noise, costs one pass over a stored buffer, and should be reported beside SMAT's activation metrics.
- Pre-S3 gate: measure the S2 human's R_det/R_stoch and falls per 100 strides. Choose the frozen-human mode from data, and report it.
- Inert-dimension drift test: run S1 once with the exo dims inside the joint log-prob (MyoAssist-literal) and once masked, and plot the exo log-std. If it drifts, that alone may explain S3 saturation.
- A per-actor ratio, KL and optimizer arm in S4 against the joint arm directly tests whether the Stage-4-only collapse is an optimizer-coupling artefact. Pair it with exo log-std init -1 and output gain 0.01.
- Executed-action entropy H(a) for the muscles (He App D.1; four antithetic samples) as a sweep, measuring near-bound occupancy, mean activation and the 10.1% metric. It is the only intervention in the sources shown to pull muscle means inward without killing return.
- Critic-only privileged inputs (reference phase, time to truncation, tau_max, stage id) plus value-target normalisation reset at each stage boundary, and optionally K value-only warm-up updates at the S3/S4 start. These are cheap, leave deployment untouched, and target the stage-boundary critic bias.
- Entropy-coefficient check at SMAT's values vs 0. Andrychowicz Fig. 81 puts 1e-3 and 3e-3 at the bottom on 3/5 tasks, and He et al.'s no-entropy arm had the best MyoLeg stochastic return.
- GPU calibration protocol (recipe step 13) before any GPU sweep. Report budgets in updates and env steps. Run S3 at matched updates (~17) and at the per-env-counting alternative (~537 updates; about 141M steps in G2, minutes on GPU).
- Early-termination audit: count S3-S4 terminations by joint. If released hips dominate, the termination rule is shaping the assisted gait.
- A small benchmark note: 'SB3-literal vs MAPPO-style staged two-actor PPO for human-exo co-adaptation'. With GPU throughput, a 3-seed x 10-arm table of these choices costs hours, and it would itself be publishable tooling evidence.
