That file ends mid-Results: abstract, Introduction, Fig. 1 protocol, group definitions and the first results paragraph. The Methods, the expertise criterion, Figs 2+ and the supplement were NOT read

**Problem.** The question is what 'co-adaptation' can honestly mean when the wearer is simulated, and which update schedules a SMAT reconstruction should compare. The seven sources cover four areas. (1) Finite cooperative-MDP theory (G, S): a 'patient' agent and a 'robot' agent improve their policies. The update rule (simultaneous, alternating or threshold-gated) decides whether learning converges and which Nash equilibrium it reaches; patient policy switches are treated as costly. (2) Human experiments on who adapts how fast. C finds that a machine's adaptation rate, and whether it adapts actions or policies, selects which game-theoretic equilibrium people reach. People respond near-optimally to the machine's rule (1-D visual cost task, n = 20 per experiment). PC finds that wearer training supplies about half of an ankle exoskeleton's metabolic benefit and takes about 109 min. (3) HILO optimisers that treat the wearer as a drifting objective: KS in simulation, KiS with 16 people. (4) Dual control (J, simulation), which trades immediate effect against identifying the person. None of the seven trains a neural device policy against a neural human policy (SMAT's setting). None involves sprinting, and none validates a simulated wearer's adaptation rate against a real person.

**Mechanism (exact).** G (arXiv 2305.02058v1). Agent0 = patient, Agent1 = robot; 'the less switching of [the patient's] policy the better' (p2). CaMDP (Definition 1, p3): S = S0 x Ss x S1 (patient-only, shared, robot-only states). P(s0'|s,a0,a1) = P(s0'|s0,a0); P(ss'|s,a0,a1) = P(ss'|ss,a0,a1); P(s1'|s,a0,a1) = P(s1'|s1,a1). Rewards factor the same way: P = P0 (x) Ps (x) P1 and R = R0 (x) Rs (x) R1 (Kronecker products). States and actions are finite and transitions stationary; the CaMDP is only the high layer, which switches among pre-designed low-level controllers (pp3-4). Lemmas 1-3 (p4): for ergodic P, V = [I - gamma P]^-1 diag(P R'), and the time-normalised value becomes state-independent as gamma -> 1. Procedure 1, Revised Policy Improvement (p5): for each state, J_k = sum p(s',r|s,a)[r + gamma V(s')] under the current pi_k(s); switch pi(s) to the argmax only if max_a Q(s,a) - J_k >= eta; stop when no state switches. Theorem 1 (pp5-6): if the CaMDP is ergodic under all policies, Agent0 follows Procedure 1 and Agent1 is HELD FIXED at pi1^j, then the value loss is <= eta [I - gamma P^pi*]^-1 1 = eta/(1-gamma). Note 1 (p6): an overall bound must cover every j. Procedure 2, 'PI-alike' (p7): I_k = max_a Q - J_k; switch if kappa_I * sum_{j=0..M} I_{k-j} >= eta. The printed condition compares only the kappa_I-weighted window sum with eta. There is no separate proportional term, although the text calls eta the 'proportional-alike' parameter. Section 4.2 (p7, ARGUED) uses a second-order system's damping ratio as an analogy: if one agent responds much faster ('normally, we anticipate Agent1 (robot) has a faster response than Agent0 (patient)'), non-stationarity is less likely. There is no convergence proof for joint learning or for Procedure 2. S (arXiv 2503.07319v1). Same CaMDP (Definition 1, p2), with positive rewards (Note 1, p2) and states 'directly accessible to either agent via sensors or designed observers' (p2). Fig. 1 (p2) draws the team reward as a weighted sum k0 r0 + k1 r1, not the Kronecker product. Policy order, Eq. (1) (p3): V_ij(s_k) - V_i'j'(s_k') >= eps > 0 for every pair of states; in practice V(i,j) = max_s or mean_s (p3). Update rules (p4). Definition 2, simultaneous: pi0^{k+1} = argmax E_{pi0,pi1^k}[V0] and pi1^{k+1} = argmax E_{pi0^k,pi1}[V1]. Definition 3, alternating: Agent0 updates, then Agent1 best-responds to pi0^{k+1}. Only alternation is analysed; the paper cites [5] for more oscillation under simultaneous updates (p4). Theorem 1 (pp4-5): alternating standard policy iteration converges to one of the Nash equilibria if Agent0's improvement depends only on (S0,Ss) and is unaffected by S1, and Agent1's only on (S1,Ss). The proof holds the partner fixed and shows dV = [I - gamma P^pin]^-1 g >= 0 (Eqs 3-6). Lemma 2 (p5): with distinct V(i,j), #NE <= min{N_DC, N_DR} (Eq 7). Theorem 2 (pp5-6): if one agent has a policy that is best against every partner policy (Eq 8 or 9), alternation reaches the global optimum 'within at most two round'. Section V (p6): Agent0 uses Procedure 1 (threshold eta) to cut patient switching; Agent1 uses 'less greedy' epsilon-greedy improvement; Theorem 3 restates eta/(1-gamma) with Agent1 fixed. Algorithm 1 (p7): gamma 0.9, theta 1e-6, max_iterations 1000, epsilon 0.1. Its Agent0 evaluation block maximises over actions1, and convergence_criteria_met is undefined (KB 06). Complexity reduction (pp6-8): prune policies whose best value is <= eps (Approach 1), or restrict Agent0 to a subset of states and compare best values (Approach 2, Eqs 10-12). J (IEEE L-CSS 2023). Model: x_{k+1} = f(x,u,theta) + w, y = h(x,u,theta) + v, exercise effect z = phi(x,u), with theta unknown (Eqs 1-3, p2). The OC objective E[G_N(Z_N) + sum G_i] is conditioned on the state estimate (Eqs 4-6). OED uses the Fisher information (Eqs 8-14, p3); the control-oriented OED minimises tr(grad_theta H0 M^-1 grad_theta H0^T) (Eqs 15-20, pp3-4). Dual control: W_b = min E[OC cost] + alpha * tr(grad H0 M~^-1 grad H0^T) (Eq 21, p4). Algorithm 1 (p4) is a batch design: estimate theta from the previous session, then design the next session's input. The case study (p5) is a Kegel fatigue compartment model (active, fatigued and resting units; Eq 22). The input is normalised voluntary effort ('brain force'), not device torque; z is the discounted accumulated count of active units, gamma = 0.999 (Eq 23). The person is modelled as a plant with unknown parameters, not as a learner. KS (bioRxiv 2024). No co-adaptation model. A surrogate f(assistance parameters) -> normalised metabolic cost is fit to 5 subjects' HILO data (GP, absolute-exponential kernel chosen by 0.632 bootstrap; pp2, 4). Simulated HILO queries the surrogate plus Gaussian noise with SD 4.6% (pp3-4). 'Adaptation' (Scenario 2, p4) is a linear interpolation of the landscape from one subject's model to another's over the first 80 evaluations, then held constant; it depends only on the evaluation count, not on the assistance applied. Optimisers (pp2-3): CMA-ES (4 + floor(3 ln N) evaluations per generation, initial step about 30% of range); cross-entropy (twice CMA-ES's population and elite counts); BO (Matern GP, UCB with exploration constant 2.6); exploitative BO (constant 0.93; package [25]). KiS (bioRxiv 2026). DBO is BO with a spatio-temporal GP: k((u,t),(u',t')) = k_u(u,u') * k_t(t,t') (Eq 2), with k_t = alpha^|t-t'| and alpha in (0,1] (Eq 3). Here t is the iteration index (Fig. 1b). k_u is an ARD squared-exponential over torque amplitude T and duration D, with a periodic distance min(|u-u'|, 100-|u-u'|) for timing P (Eq 4). The noise term sigma_eps^2 delta_{u,u'} is also multiplied by alpha^|t-t'| (Eq 6), so repeated identical inputs get time-correlated noise. BO is the same model without k_t. Acquisition is expected improvement with an 'exploration and exploitation ratio' of 0.1 (p4). The objective is to minimise -v, where v is the mean treadmill speed over strides 36-40 of each 40-stride torque block (p4). Validation iterations 10/20/30/40 apply the input with the lowest predicted cost, and their observations feed back into the GP (p4). Prediction error: e_pred(i) = |f(u*,i) - m(u*)| (Eq 7); e_std = e_pred / sigma(u*) (Eq 8). C (Sci Rep 2025). Scalar actions: h (human, mouse or touch) and m (machine). Costs: c_H = 1/2 h^2 + 7/30 m^2 - 1/3 hm + 2/15 h - 22/75 m and c_M = 1/2 m^2 + h^2 - hm (Eqs 1-2, p7). Equilibria (Table 1, p3): NE (-0.2,-0.2); human-led Stackelberg SE (+0.2,+0.2) with slopes L_H -0.2, L_M 1; CCVE about (0.276,0.373); machine optimum = reverse Stackelberg RSE (0,0); human optimum (0.1,0.7). Exp 1 (p8): m+ = m - alpha * dc_M/dm (Eq 3), alpha in {0, 0.003, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1}. At alpha = 0 the machine holds m = -0.2; at alpha = 1 the update IS the policy m = h. Exp 2 (p8): trial pairs play m = L_M h and m' = L_M h' + delta. The conjecture L_H~ = (h~' - h~)/(m~' - m~) comes from median actions (Eq 5), then L_M+ = (1 - 2 L_H~)/(1 - L_H~) (Eq 6a); K = 10 iterations starting from L_H~ = 0. Exp 3 (p8): pairs play m = L_M h and m' = (L_M + Delta) h'. A finite-difference gradient of c_M~(L_M) = c_M(h, L_M(h - h*_M) + m*_M) (Eqs 8-9) drives L_M+ = L_M - gamma * dc_M~/dL_M (Eq 10), with gamma = 2. The human is not modelled as a learner; the claim is that people approximately solve their own constrained optimisation given the machine's rule (p6). PC (Sci Robot 2021; preprint pp1-6). 15 naive participants were randomly sorted into three training regimens (p5). Static training: a daily 72-min adaptation trial with a fixed generic profile, 'intended to isolate the effects of training time on user expertise' (p6). Continued optimization: HILO throughout, each day seeded from the previous day's estimate, so variation shrinks as confidence grows; the day-n customised profile reflects 72n min of optimisation (p5). Re-optimization: HILO re-seeded identically every day, giving the highest variation (p6). The optimiser samples profiles around its estimate for 2 min each and ranks them by energy cost (p5). The generic profile is the mean optimised profile from a 2-day, 10-person pilot. It is re-applied periodically inside adaptation trials 'to track motor adaptation' (pp5-6).

**Protocol.** G: numerical only. One 2x2x2-state CaMDP with printed P and R (pp7-8): gamma 0.5 vs 0.98 (Fig. 1, p9); a 'non-stationary' run of 50 iterations and a PI-alike run of about 1200 iterations (Fig. 2, p9); an eta sweep (Table 1, p10). No people and no robot. S: numerical only. One random 8-state CaMDP at four gammas (Table I, Fig. 4, pp8-9); 1000 random CaMDPs checked against three conditions (p8); one hand-picked CaMDP with printed matrices for the epsilon-greedy and pruning examples (pp8-10). J: simulation only. A 2-min session (T = 0.1 s, N = 1201) with 10 runs per condition: OC, half OED/half OC, and DC with alpha 0.1/1/10. G_N = G_z = 10, G_u = 1, w ~ N(0, 0.25); the simulated plant is a more complex cramping model (p5). KS: simulation on surrogates of data from PC's HILO experiment: 5 subjects, CMA-ES over six 2-h sessions, about 180 two-minute metabolic evaluations each. Four parameters (p2): peak torque <= 1 Nm/kg; peak time 35-55%, rise 10-40% and fall 5-20% of the gait cycle. Scenario 1, expert: 40 simulations x 180 evaluations. Scenario 2, novice with a drifting landscape: 95 x 160. Scenario 3, 4/12/20 parameters: 40 x 180, 50 x 260, 25 x 260 (p4). KiS: 16 healthy adults (9 F; 24.4 +/- 3.4 y; 170.3 +/- 8.5 cm; 70.1 +/- 9.2 kg). APO bilateral hip exoskeleton (IUVO; 10 Nm peak; adaptive-oscillator phase). Bertec split-belt treadmill with user-driven speed (force plates at 400 Hz; cap 1.6 m/s; harness). Two visits 1-7 days apart, DBO or BO in pseudo-randomised, balanced order. Familiarisation: >= 3 min at zero torque, then three fixed pulses ramped over <= 14 strides (pp3-4). Main session: 4 trials of <= 15 min with >= 5 min of seated rest. Each trial has 10 iterations of 40 torque strides + 20 washout strides. Trial 1 opens with a 100-stride baseline, and each later trial restarts at the previous trial's final speed (p4). Input ranges: T in [-9, 9] Nm, P in [0, 100]%, D in [10, 40]%. The first three inputs are fixed: (-9,75,30), (9,50,40), (-5,50,20). Sham visit (n = 3): iterations 4-40 use 37 random inputs with the covariance of the higher-variance optimiser session (p4). Statistics: a linear mixed model with fixed optimiser, iteration and interaction, and random participant and participant x optimiser x iteration terms; post hoc paired t-tests with Cohen's dz (pp4-5). C: Prolific crowd workers under UW IRB STUDY00013524, a separate n = 20 per experiment, paid $2, data collected in 2020 (p7). Exp 1 had 14 trials of 40 s; Exps 2-3 had 20 trials of 20 s. Input sampled at 60 Hz; cost shown as rectangle height (square-root transformed); a 30-s rest offered every three trials; about 10-14 min in total. Exp 1 randomises a mirror symmetry s in {-1,+1} per trial and initialises the joint action uniformly in [-0.4,0.4]^2 (p8). Tests: Student's t (Exp 1) and Hotelling's T^2 (Exps 2-3), P <= 0.05 (p9). PC: a pre-test day of six 6-min validation trials (2 in normal shoes, 2 at zero torque, 2 with generic assistance). Then 5 training days, each a 72-min group-specific adaptation trial followed by the same validation trials, plus 2 customised-profile trials for the two HILO groups. Validation order was randomised in a double-reversal ABCDDCBA sequence (Fig. 1C, p4). Measures: respirometry, EMG, ground reaction forces, kinematics (Fig. 1B). Each participant walked 'nearly ten hours' in the exoskeleton (p3).

**Headline numbers**
- [theory] G Theorem 1 (pp5-6): with Agent1 held fixed, threshold-gated improvement for Agent0 loses at most eta [I - gamma P^pi*]^-1 1 = eta/(1-gamma) of value.
- [sim] G Table 1 (p10), an eta sweep with Agent0 on Procedure 1 and Agent1 on standard PI: eta > 1.0625e-4 -> [1111][1111] No.256, value 0.180; eta = 1.0000e-4 -> [0111][1100] No.125, 0.187; eta = 8.1250e-5 -> [0001][1100] No.29, 0.207; eta = 6.2500e-5 -> [0000][1100] No.13, 0.210; eta < 3.1250e-5 -> alternates between No.13 and No.175 (0.210 vs 0.196).
- [sim] G p9 and Fig. 2 (p9): the optimum is pi0 = [0000], pi1 = [1100] (value 0.2101); the start is [1111][1100] (0.1736). The PI-alike rule with kappa_I = 1 and eta = 0.1 converges to policy 13 (Fig. 2b datatips X180 Y157, X374 Y141, X701 Y13; x-axis to 1200 iterations). The 'non-stationary' run keeps alternating between indices 175 and 13 (Fig. 2a datatips X16 Y175, X25 Y13; 50 iterations).
- [sim] S Section VI-B (p8): of 1000 random CaMDPs, 150 satisfy dominance (Condition 1), 516 the information condition (2), and 652 reach the global NE from the tested initial conditions (3). 'All the systems which satisfy both Conditions (1) and (2) satisfy Condition (3)'; the size of that intersection is not reported.
- [sim] S Section VI-C (p9): standard PI from Agent0 {0,0,0,0}, Agent1 {1,0,0,0} ends at a local NE, printed as {1,1,0,0} and {1,0,1,0}, value 9.81 (second highest). Epsilon-greedy for Agent1 reaches the global NE, 9.99, 'after the several round'.
- [sim] S Section VI-D (p10): restricting Agent0 to pi0(ss) cuts its policies from 16 to 4 and the best value from 9.99 to 9.05; restricting it to pi0(s0) also leaves 4 policies, with best value 9.81.
- [sim] S Table I (p9): two policies over 8 states at gamma 0.5/0.75/0.95/0.998 (P1 ranges 0.3340-0.4409 at 0.5 and 99.2792-99.4081 at 0.998). Re-verified from KB 06: within-policy spreads do not shrink with gamma (P1 0.1069 -> 0.1289; P2 0.1490 -> 0.1570).
- [theory] S Theorem 2 (pp5-6): with a dominant policy and distinct V(i,j), alternating exact best responses reach the global optimum within at most two rounds.
- [sim] J Table I (p6), 10 runs, mean (SD), columns effect / loss of optimality / effort / estimation error: OC (without noise) 19.57 (1.49), 3.34 (1.14), 3.38 (0.769), 9.27 (3.12); half OED then half OC 20.08 (2.31), 7.21 (16.02), 4.16 (0.695), 8.36 (3.25); DC alpha 0.1: 12.79 (2.26), 0.866 (0.321), 2.10 (0.16), 3.26 (1.92); DC alpha 1: 9.31 (1.00), 0.338 (0.073), 1.85 (0.549), 1.40 (0.578); DC alpha 10: 8.11 (2.29), 0.219 (0.184), 2.72 (1.06), 0.766 (0.732). Session length N = 1201 at T = 0.1 s (p5).
- [sim] KS Fig. 3 and p6: the chosen GP surrogate has about 10% mean absolute percent error, against about 5% SD noise in first-order metabolic estimates.
- [sim] KS Scenario 1 (p4, Fig. 5): BO and exploitative BO converge near the optimum after about 60 evaluations; CMA-ES improves more slowly and asymptotically; cross-entropy never reaches the optimum (40 simulations x 180 evaluations per algorithm).
- [sim] KS Scenario 2 (pp4-6, Fig. 6): the landscape drifts over the first 80 evaluations. This equals 160 min at 2 min per evaluation, which the authors argue is within the range the dataset's subjects took to become expert. Exploitative BO reaches the optimum at about 100 evaluations, BO is slower, and CMA-ES matches BO after 80 evaluations (95 simulations x 160 evaluations).
- [sim] KS Scenario 3 (pp4, 6, Fig. 7): with 4/12/20 parameters, BO reaches the lowest cost at 12 and 20 parameters 'in the 250 evaluations' (the methods say 260), and exploitative BO stalls at a suboptimum.
- [human, n=16] KiS p5, LMM on walking speed: iteration p < 0.0001; optimiser x iteration p = 0.015; optimiser main effect p = 0.150.
- [human] KiS p5, change from baseline at iterations 1/2/30/40 (m/s; '+/-' is unlabelled, [derived] consistent with SE). DBO: 0.08 +/- 0.03 (p 0.03, dz 0.615), 0.11 +/- 0.03 (p 0.005, dz 0.841), 0.30 +/- 0.04 (p < .0001, dz 1.94), 0.30 +/- 0.04 (p < 0.0001, dz 1.90). BO: 0.08 +/- 0.03 (p 0.012, dz 0.741), 0.11 +/- 0.03 (p 0.001, dz 1.011), 0.15 +/- 0.03 (p < 0.001, dz 1.124), 0.18 +/- 0.06 (p 0.007, dz 0.803).
- [human] KiS p5, late vs early within DBO: iteration 30 vs 1, 0.23 +/- 0.05 (p < 0.001, dz 1.171); 40 vs 1, 0.22 +/- 0.05 (p < 0.001, dz 1.161); 30 vs 2, 0.20 +/- 0.04 (p < 0.001, dz 1.249); 40 vs 2, 0.19 +/- 0.05 (p 0.001, dz 1.014). Within BO none is significant: 30 vs 1, 0.07 +/- 0.03 (p 0.06); 40 vs 1, 0.11 +/- 0.06 (p 0.09); 30 vs 2, p 0.37; 40 vs 2, p 0.24.
- [human] KiS pp5-6: DBO minus BO in change from baseline at iterations 20/30/40 is 0.11 +/- 0.04 (p 0.013), 0.16 +/- 0.04 (p 0.002) and 0.12 +/- 0.05 (p 0.04). Absolute speed differs only at iteration 30: DBO 1.38 +/- 0.04 vs BO 1.26 +/- 0.05 m/s (p 0.02). At iteration 40 the difference is 0.08 +/- 0.06 (p 0.21).
- [human, n=3] KiS p6: in the sham condition (random torques with matched variability), speed was 1.1 +/- 0.1, 1.1 +/- 0.1, 1.2 +/- 0.1 and 1.1 +/- 0.1 m/s at iterations 10-40, not different from baseline.
- [human] KiS p6: prediction error at iterations 10-40 was 0.10 +/- 0.02, 0.07 +/- 0.01, 0.05 +/- 0.01 and 0.06 +/- 0.01 m/s for DBO, and 0.07 +/- 0.02, 0.05 +/- 0.01, 0.09 +/- 0.02 and 0.10 +/- 0.02 for BO. DBO is lower at iteration 30 (0.04 +/- 0.02, p 0.048) but not at 40 (p 0.159). Standardised error: DBO 10.78, 6.29, 4.74, 1.49 vs BO 16.79, 5.27, 17.88, 9.93, with no significant between-optimiser differences.
- [human] KiS p8, Table I: normalised distance of the tested inputs from the iteration 1-2 inputs at iterations 10/20/30/40: DBO 0.26/0.26/0.23/0.23; BO 0.26/0.36/0.28/0.28. KiS p5: iterations 30-40 correspond to about 1800-2400 strides of exposure.
- [human, n=20] C Exp 1 (Fig. 1, pp2-3): median actions shift from NE at machine rate alpha = 0 to the human-led SE at alpha = 1. At alpha = 0: P < 0.001 vs SE, P = 0.2 vs NE. At alpha = 1: P < 0.001 vs NE, P = 0.5 vs SE.
- [human, n=20] C Exp 2 (Fig. 2, pp3-4): conjectural-variation play moves from SE toward CCVE by k = 9 (Hotelling T^2: P < 0.001 vs SE, P = 0.06 vs CCVE).
- [human, n=20] C Exp 3 (Fig. 3, pp4-5): policy-gradient play moves from SE to the machine's optimum RSE (P < 0.001 vs SE, P = 0.11 vs RSE), raising the human's cost toward the RSE cost.
- [human] C p7: each experiment took about 10-14 min; trials lasted 40 s (Exp 1) or 20 s (Exps 2-3) at 60 Hz, i.e. T = 1200 samples per 20-s trial (p8).
- [human] PC preprint p6: after 12 min of lifetime exposure, generic assistance lowered metabolic cost by 10.0 +/- 11.0% (mean +/- SD) vs zero torque (P = 0.009). After >= 4 h of training, the generic profile vs zero torque gave 30.6 +/- 8.1% for continued optimization (P = 0.005), 28.2 +/- 4.0% for static training (P = 2.74e-5) and 17.5 +/- 9.2% for re-optimization (P = 0.09).
- [human] PC abstract: after moderate-variation training, customised assistance lowered metabolic rate by 39% vs the exoskeleton turned off. Training contributed about half of this and customisation about one quarter; a generic controller gave 10% before training and 31% after. Training needed about 109 min of assisted walking. The low-variation group took twice as long as the moderate-variation group to become expert, and the high-variation group never did. All users came to draw less mechanical power from the device. Optimised peak torque 'grew slowly over the study, suggesting a longer time-scale adaptation in the person'.
- [cited] PC p2: earlier estimates of the time to reach steady-state minimum energy cost ranged from 18 min (ref 17) to 90 min (ref 15).
- [derived] G and S reconstruction. Convention: Kronecker-product reward, gamma = 0.98, V = max over the 8 joint states, policy vector order [(x0,ss0),(x0,ss1),(x1,ss0),(x1,ss1)]. G's printed values reproduce: [0000][1100] 0.210127 (printed 0.2101); [1111][1100] 0.173602 (0.1736); [0111][1100] 0.187532 (0.187); [0001][1100] 0.207822 (0.207); [1010][1110] 0.196426 (0.196). G's 3-decimal values are therefore truncated, not rounded. G's Table 1 row [1111][1111] computes to 0.171243, not 0.180. The nearest candidate, [1111][1110] (Agent1's best response to [1111]) at 0.179480, does not truncate to 0.180 either, so that row is unresolved. S reproduces under the same convention: best 9.988937 (9.99), pi0(ss)-only best 9.046 (9.05), pi0(s0)-only best 9.811 (9.81). Algorithm 1's gamma = 0.9 gives values of about 2.0, and additive rewards give about 18 or more, so neither fits. Policy No. = 16*bin(pi0) + bin(pi1) + 1 matches all five labels in G's Table 1.
- [derived] Update order and simultaneity, using S's scalar policy-pair game V(i,j) (the Lemma 2 abstraction) under the convention above. G has exactly two pure NE: No.13 (0.2101) and No.175 (0.1964), the pair in its Table 1 last row and Fig. 2a. From G's stated start (No.253), simultaneous best responses cycle between No.15 and No.173 forever; alternation reaches No.13 if the patient moves first and No.175 if the robot moves first. S has two pure NE: ([0100],[0000]) at 9.989 and ([1100],[1000]) at 9.811. From S's stated start, simultaneous responses cycle between 9.573 and 9.803; patient-first alternation reaches the local NE and robot-first the global NE. S's printed local NE policy {1,0,1,0} for Agent1 computes as [1000], its unchanged initial policy; ([1100],[1010]) scores 9.711.
- [derived] KiS: the '+/-' values match SE at n = 16 (e.g. 0.30 / dz 1.94 = SD 0.155, so SE 0.039). One printed test is internally inconsistent: 'iteration 10: 0.06 +/- 0.02, p = 0.023, dz = 0.357' (p6). dz = 0.357 at n = 16 implies t of about 1.43 and p of about 0.17.
- [derived] PC abstract decomposition: immediate 10/39 = about 26%, training (31-10)/39 = about 54%, customisation (39-31)/39 = about 21%. This is consistent with 'about half' and 'about one quarter'.

**Ablations that matter**
- G Table 1 (p10) is the threshold ablation: raising eta from 6.25e-5 to >1.06e-4 trades value for fewer patient switches (0.210 -> 0.207 -> 0.187 -> 0.180), while eta < 3.125e-5 alternates between No.13 and No.175. For SMAT: any threshold-gated human update needs an eta sweep reported as update fraction vs final value, not a single eta.
- S does not re-run simultaneous vs alternating updates; the comparison is asserted from [5] (p4). Its own ablations are greedy vs epsilon-greedy for Agent1 on one CaMDP (9.81 -> 9.99, p9) and policy-set restriction (16 -> 4 policies, best value 9.05 or 9.81, p10). There are no seeds or distributions.
- [derived] Update order is an ablation that neither G nor S runs. In both printed examples the first mover decides which NE alternation reaches, and simultaneous exact best responses cycle. SMAT's Stage 3 is exo-first by construction, so human-first order and a simultaneous start are the missing arms.
- J Table I (p6) compares a staged arm (half OED, then half OC) with blended dual control (alpha 0.1/1/10) and pure OC at the same session length N = 1201. It is the only matched-budget staged-vs-blended comparison among these sources. The staged arm gave the largest effect (20.08) but the most variable loss of optimality (7.21 +/- 16.02). Simulation only, 10 runs.
- KS Scenario 1 vs 2 (Figs 5-6): exogenous drift erodes plain BO's advantage. Exploitative BO (exploration constant 0.93) handles drift best, while plain BO (2.6) slows. Under non-stationarity, the exploration constant is the ablation that matters.
- KiS: DBO vs BO (temporal kernel on or off) in a crossover of 16 people over 2 days with balanced order, plus a sham of random inputs with matched variability (n = 3). The sham is the only human arm among these sources aimed at separating device learning from wearer familiarisation, and it is underpowered.
- C: a sweep of machine adaptation rate (7 alphas, within-subject, randomised, each twice; Exp 1) and of adaptation space (actions vs policies; Exps 1-3, separate groups of 20). The rate selects NE vs SE; policy-space learning selects CCVE or the machine's optimum.
- PC: static vs continued vs re-optimization (15 people), plus generic vs customised assistance compared on the same training day. Static training isolates exposure (p6), and the generic probe inside adaptation trials tracks wearer adaptation (pp5-6). This is the reference design for separating device learning from wearer familiarisation.

**Limitations the authors admit**
- G assumes an observer and full observability 'at the early stage' (p2). The CaMDP covers only the high layer, with finite states and actions (p4). A pre-designed optimal switching law 'could be time-consuming' (p5). The loss bound covers a fixed Agent1 policy only, so an overall bound must consider every Agent1 policy (Note 1, p6). Pre-selecting eta is 'challenging' (p9). Real and clinical experiments are left to future work (p10).
- S: the conditions are sufficient only (Note 3, p5). The proof assumes centralised simulation, and 'in real clinical trial scenarios ... the previous proof may not hold' under partial observability (p5). A CaMDP satisfying Eq. (1) is hard to construct (Note 2, p3). Complex environments, patient-specific factors, real-world use and deep RL are future work (p10).
- J: the fatigue model was validated only at zero or maximal effort (p5). The design is batch and offline; recursive identification and receding horizon are extensions (p6). An adaptive alpha is a 'non-trivial extension' (p6). There are no physiological input constraints (p6).
- KS: the surrogate 'does not capture the full complexity', and experimental pilots remain essential (pp2, 7-8). The CMA-ES-collected data are non-uniform, so the surrogate is inaccurate where sampling was sparse; a grid search is recommended (Fig. 4 caption p5; p6). 'We do not fully understand how this metabolic landscape changes during adaptation' (p7). Only five experimental subjects (p7). More simulations are needed at high dimension (pp6, 8).
- KiS: 40 iterations may be too few. Strides per iteration were traded against fatigue. The 1.6 m/s cap produced near-constant responses (Participant 1 with DBO, Participant 7 with BO). The analysis tracks trends against iteration, not the specific inputs tested (pp8-10). Earlier DBO work used only simulations or artificial non-stationarity (p2).
- C: experiments lasted only about 10-14 min, and longer runs might shift outcomes through boredom (p7). The infinite regress of reasoning levels was not observed, possibly because computation is bounded (pp6-7). Misaligned machine goals may need regulation (p7).
- PC (pages read): earlier estimates of time to steady state vary widely (p2); the abstract says training deserves more attention.

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- G Section 4 says the self-adjusting methods 'ensure the convergence of the mutual learning process' (p5). Theorem 1 fixes Agent1, and there is no proof for Procedure 2 or for joint learning.
- G p2 assumes all states are 'directly observable by either of the two agents'. p3 says states, rewards and actions 'can only be available for the patient and robot separately'.
- G never states the update rule behind Fig. 2a ('Non-stationary'): simultaneous updates, or Procedure 1 with a small eta?
- [derived] G's printed values are max-over-states discounted values at gamma 0.98 with product rewards, not the 'asymptotic' state-independent value that p9 implies. Table 1's first row does not reproduce (see headline_numbers).
- G's PI-alike example uses eta = 0.1, three orders of magnitude above Table 1's eta values, so the accumulation window M must span hundreds of iterations. M is never given (p9).
- S: the example values 9.81 and 9.99 are inconsistent with Algorithm 1's gamma = 0.9 (KB 06 bound: 9.0565). [derived] They fit gamma 0.98 with max over states. The printed local NE {1,0,1,0} for Agent1 does not reproduce.
- S claims 'improved patient outcomes' in its contribution list (p2) without any patient data. The '1000 random CaMDPs' have no stated sampling distribution, seeds or initial-condition protocol, and the reported counts overlap (p8; KB 06).
- J's text says control effort 'is higher for a higher weight alpha' (p6), but Table I gives 2.10, 1.85 and 2.72. The OC comparator is 'without noise', unlike the other arms.
- KS models adaptation as exogenous (interpolation driven by evaluation count), so it cannot represent a wearer adapting to the assistance itself, i.e. co-adaptation. The surrogate was fit to CMA-ES-sampled data, so the reported sim-vs-experiment agreement for CMA-ES may be partly circular. Noise is i.i.d. Gaussian, with no drift or autocorrelation. There is no statistical comparison of algorithms, only mean +/- SD bands. Scenario 3 has 260 evaluations in the methods but '250 evaluations' in the results.
- KiS: the sham is n = 3 on an extra visit whose timing relative to the main visits is not given, so it cannot statistically exclude familiarisation. The abstract's 'DBO outperformed BO' is not qualified by the optimiser main effect (p = 0.150) or the final-iteration difference (p = 0.21). The DBO alpha and GP hyperparameters are not reported, so DBO cannot be rebuilt. There is no multiple-comparison correction across many post hoc t-tests. Speed carries over between iterations and trials through the user-driven treadmill. The same p = 0.374 is printed for two different effects (p6). One test is internally inconsistent ([derived], dz 0.357).
- C reads P > 0.05 as the median being 'not significantly different, consistent with' the equilibrium (p9), inferring equivalence from non-significance with n = 20. The perturbation sizes delta and Delta are not in the main text. The task is a 1-D visual cost game in which people see their own cost; exoskeleton users do not perceive metabolic cost directly, and physical interaction was not tested.
- PC (pages read): the generic probe used to measure 'expertise' is the controller the static group trained on, so that group's expertise may be controller-specific. Groups are small (15 people across 3 groups).

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- G: the Procedure 2 window M; accumulator reset after a switch; initialisation; stopping while sub-threshold improvements are still accumulating (p7; KB 05). Also the update rule for Fig. 2a, which agent moves first, the tie rule, and whether 'value' means max, mean or average reward ([derived] max at gamma 0.98 fits).
- S: gamma and the value scalarisation for the Section VI-C/D example ([derived] gamma 0.98 with max fits all three numbers). Agent1's policy-vector ordering: assumed [(s10,ss0),(s10,ss1),(s11,ss0),(s11,ss1)], mirroring Agent0's (p7). Also the sampling distribution for the 1000 CaMDPs, 'several rounds', convergence_criteria_met, and any epsilon decay (KB 06).
- G/S never define policy indexing. [derived] No. = 16*bin(pi0) + bin(pi1) + 1, with the first listed state as the most significant bit, reproduces every G Table 1 label.
- J: the definition of z (the printed sum repeats index k, and Eq. 23 uses a different convention); the Jacobian terms in Eq. 26; the gradient-outer-product surrogate in Eqs 16-18 (KB 07); the noise in the 'OC (without noise)' arm.
- KS: which subject pairs were interpolated in Scenario 2 and in which order; seeds; cross-entropy hyperparameters (swept on one landscape, p3); per-subject normalisation. The public code [26] may resolve these (not inspected).
- KiS: the DBO temporal alpha; the GP hyperparameters and whether they are refit each iteration; the semantics of the EI 'exploration and exploitation ratio'; when the sham visit happened relative to the main visits; whether '+/-' is SE ([derived] yes); which optimiser each participant received first (Fig. 4 colour-codes it but no table).
- C: delta (Exp 2), Delta (Exp 3), and how often the machine updates within an Exp 1 trial (data are recorded at 60 Hz, but per-sample updating is not stated). The supplementary non-quadratic variants were not read.
- PC: the expertise criterion, per-group n, the CMA-ES settings, and any final numbers beyond the abstract (only preprint pp1-6 were obtained).
- Across all seven: no source gives a measured rate of human motor-policy change that could be mapped onto an RL learning rate or update frequency. The simulated human's adaptation rate in SMAT has no empirical anchor.

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *What simulated coadaptation models:* Two learning policies in one simulated body adjust to each other under an update schedule the designer chooses: freeze, alternate, gate, or set relative learning rates. The simulated 'human' re-optimises its neural policy's muscle excitations for the stage reward given the device, while the device policy learns against a partner that is itself changing. In the finite theory (G, S) and in this digest's derived checks, the schedule decides whether learning converges or cycles and which equilibrium it reaches.
- *What it does not model:*
  - A real nervous system's learning timescale and mechanisms. PC measured about 109 min of assisted walking to expertise and slower adaptation for peak torque; C's people settled within 20-40 s trials. A PPO learning rate x batch size is an algorithm setting, not a measured human rate.
  - The deployment game. Deployed device weights are fixed (G p2 already notes that medical policies are fixed, approved, and updated only after long data aggregation), while the wearer keeps adapting. The Stage 4 joint solution need not be a fixed point of 'device frozen, human adapting'.
  - The human's own objective. The simulated reward (imitation, activation, forward speed) is a designer's proxy, and C shows that the equilibrium reached depends on each side's cost.
  - Anticipation. C's people behaved as if optimising against the machine's rule (SE at alpha = 1). For simultaneous gradient learners, 'merely changing adaptation rates in simultaneous gradient play does not change stationary points' (C p3, citing [25]). A PPO human is that kind of learner. Rate schedules in SMAT can therefore change the basin reached and its stability, but should not be presented as producing leader/follower outcomes [argued].
  - Differences between individuals. KS Fig. 4 shows subject-specific optima, while SMAT has one simulated body.
  - Fatigue, boredom and attention (C p7). J's fatigue model is a plant, not a learner.
- *Claim phrasing:*
  - Say 'trained against a co-adapting simulated musculoskeletal policy under schedule S, deployed as a fixed policy'. Never say 'the controller co-adapts with the wearer'.
  - Say 'schedule S reached [metric] at matched environment steps (n seeds, 95% CI) versus joint-throughout training'. Do not say 'co-adaptation improves assistance' unless the schedule effect survives the curriculum-matched arms (A0-A3).
  - Say 'the simulated human's adaptation rate is an algorithmic setting (learning rate, update frequency), not a measured human rate'.
  - Do not cite S Theorems 1-2 as convergence guarantees for PPO. They cover exact, finite, alternating policy iteration under an information condition.
  - Qualify with C: which equilibrium people reach depends on the device's adaptation rate and on whether it adapts actions or policies. A wearer adapting against a fixed device may settle somewhere other than the simulated pair.
  - Keep MEASURED (human: PC, KiS, C; simulation: G, S, J, KS) separate from ARGUED (G's damping-ratio argument, KS's 160-min equivalence, KiS's attribution based on a 3-person sham).
- *Copy exactly:*
  - G Procedures 1 and 2 as printed (instantaneous and accumulated thresholds), with declared M, reset and tie rules, as the baselines for threshold-triggered human updates.
  - S Definitions 2-3 (simultaneous, alternating) as the named schedule families.
  - For any later HILO: the KiS DBO kernel (Eqs 2-6), validation iterations every 10th iteration, and a sham with matched input covariance.
  - PC's validation block (shoes / zero torque / generic probe / customised, in ABCDDCBA order) and its three-group logic (static, continued, re-optimised).
  - C's equilibrium bookkeeping: before running, compute the analogues of NE, SE, CCVE and RSE and state which one each update rule should reach.
- *Must test:*
  - The 2x2 of schedule (frozen-then-joint vs joint-throughout) x curriculum (C3->C4 vs C4 only) at matched budget (A0-A3 in reimplementation_recipe).
  - Alternation in both orders (A4h, A4e); timescale ratio (A5); threshold-gated human updates with yoked and random-schedule controls (A6, A6b, A7); the exo-only frozen-human arm (A8).
  - Deployment probes D1-D4 on every arm's final checkpoints.
- *Later human study must control:*
  - Wearer familiarisation vs device learning: (a) a static-device arm with matched exposure (PC's static training); (b) a fixed probe controller, e.g. the frozen SMAT policy or a generic profile, re-tested at fixed intervals in every arm (PC pp5-6); (c) a sham/yoked-variability arm in which the device changes on the same schedule with matched variability but independently of the wearer's data (KiS sham, properly powered, not n = 3); (d) daily validation blocks (no device, zero torque, probe, current) in ABCDDCBA order (PC Fig. 1C).
  - Exposure dosing: log lifetime exposure minutes. For walking with ankle exoskeletons, expertise took about 109 min and twice as long under low variation (PC abstract). No source measures sprinting, and sprint exposure is limited by fatigue, so plan several days.
  - Order and carry-over: counterbalance device or optimiser order across days (KiS: separate days, 1-7 days apart); use washout blocks; log carry-over (KiS p8: some participants needed more washout strides).
  - Non-stationarity in any HILO: use a time-aware surrogate (KiS DBO with alpha declared) or a forgetting optimiser (CMA-ES, KS p7), and choose the optimiser and evaluation count in a simulated rehearsal first (KS).
  - Equilibrium bookkeeping (C): preregister the outcome each device update rule predicts, and test equivalence with margins rather than P > 0.05.
  - Never automated: device parameters change only between blocks, after the researcher reviews the candidate and its safety envelope. No online weight updates on hardware. Keep hard caps (torque; treadmill speed, with KiS's 1.6 m/s cap and harness as the walking precedent; KS peak torque <= 1 Nm/kg) and fatigue stop rules. A person, not an agent, runs every session.
  - Measurement noise: 2-min first-order respirometry estimates have about 5% SD (KS p6). For sprint outcomes, establish timing repeatability before any optimisation (no source here).
  - Report three effects separately: the device effect at a fixed wearer state (randomised A/B of old vs new setting within a session), the wearer effect at a fixed device (probe drift across sessions), and their interaction.
  - Speed objectives are hazardous: speed caps and carry-over biased the optimiser in KiS (pp8-10).
- *Measured vs argued:*
  - *Measured human:*
    - PC: training vs customisation shares, 109 min, variation effects (abstract; p6)
    - KiS: DBO vs BO speed changes and prediction errors; 3-person sham (pp5-8)
    - C: equilibrium shifts with machine rate and adaptation space (Figs 1-3)
  - *Measured simulation:*
    - G Table 1 and Fig. 2
    - S Section VI
    - J Table I
    - KS Figs 3, 5-7
  - *Theory:*
    - G Theorem 1 (partner fixed)
    - S Theorems 1-3 and Lemma 2 (finite, exact, alternating)
  - *Argued:*
    - G p7: a faster robot reduces non-stationarity
    - KS p4: 80 evaluations = 160 min lies within the experts' range
    - KiS p7: the sham shows the gains come from optimisation, not familiarisation
    - J p6: an adaptive alpha should front-load OED
    - C p6: the results may generalise to settings where people optimise their own utility
  - *Derived here:*
    - the CaMDP value convention, update-order and cycling results
    - KiS SE labelling and the dz inconsistency
    - PC share decomposition
    - C toy algebra in reimplementation_recipe step 8

**Recipe on our server.** 0) Scope. Arms differ only in how the two actors are updated after Stage 2; everything else follows smat_original_and_extended.json and research-data/infra/smat_spec.json. Train Stages 1-2 ONCE per seed and branch every arm from those same Stage 2 checkpoints. Declare the freeze semantics: a frozen actor takes no gradient steps, and its action sampling (stochastic with the checkpoint's log-std, recommended, or deterministic mean) is the same in every arm. The shared critic updates every PPO iteration in every arm and is never scheduled. 1) Budgets. Log four quantities: (a) environment steps, the PRIMARY match (B = 67.1M at full scale, i.e. SMAT's 1.1M + 66M, or a screening B such as 20M); (b) PPO iterations (65,536 transitions each at 32 envs x 2048); (c) per-actor gradient steps; (d) wall-clock and core-hours. Alternation and threshold arms give each actor fewer updates at equal (a), so rerun the best such arm matched on (c) as a sensitivity check. 2) Config blocks. C3 = Stage 3 settings: tau_max 6 Nm, r_exo3, r_hip_act 2, hip imitation off, no r_delta_tau. C4 = Stage 4 settings: tau_max 25 Nm, r_exo4 + r_delta_tau, r_hip_act 5, human observation + [u_r, u_l]. 'Curriculum' means switching C3 -> C4 at step B3 = 1.1M; 'no curriculum' means C4 from step 0, observation augmentation included. 3) Core 2x2, which separates scheduling from the reward and torque curricula. A0 SMAT-faithful: human frozen while the exo learns under C3 for B3, then joint training under C4. A1 schedule only: human frozen for B3, then joint, all under C4. A2 curriculum only: joint from step 0, C3 for B3, then C4. A3 neither: joint from step 0 under C4 (SMAT's 'Stage 4 only' ablation, now at matched budget with >= 5 seeds). Schedule effect = mean(A0, A1) - mean(A2, A3); curriculum effect = mean(A0, A2) - mean(A1, A3); report the interaction too. If the curriculum matters, split it into A2t, torque only (6 -> 25 Nm with r_exo4 throughout), and A2r, reward only (r_exo3 -> r_exo4 at 25 Nm). Freeze-length dose: run A1 with B3 in {0 (= A3), 1.1M, 5M, 20M} and B4 = B - B3. 4) Schedule family, all at C4 with the same B. A4 alternation: blocks of K PPO iterations in which only one actor updates, K in {1, 8, 64}, in both orders (A4h human-first, A4e exo-first); the derived CaMDP checks show the first mover can pick the equilibrium. A5 timescale ratio: both actors update every iteration with lr_human = rho x lr_exo, rho in {0.1, 0.3, 1, 3}; alternatively the human updates every n-th iteration, n in {1, 4, 16}. This is the simulated analogue of C's machine rate and G's 'robot faster than patient' (p7), a modelling choice with no human anchor. A6 threshold-triggered human updates (G Procedure 1): every iteration computes the candidate human update but applies it only if I_k >= eta. Fix I_k before any run, e.g. the importance-weighted advantage gain of the candidate policy on a held-out batch. A6b accumulated (G Procedure 2): apply when kappa_I * sum_{j=0..M} I_{k-j} >= eta, with M declared and the accumulator reset after each applied update. Sweep eta so the human update fraction is about 0.1, 0.3 and 0.6, log every update time, and let the exo update every iteration. A7 yoked controls: replay A6/A6b's realised human-update times on fresh seeds without the trigger, plus a random schedule with the same update count; A6 must beat both before 'state-informed triggering' is claimed. A8 exo-only: the human stays frozen for all of B under C4, so the exo learns a best response to a fixed partner. This tests whether human co-adaptation helps at all. 5) Outcomes per arm and seed, from frozen checkpoints with fixed evaluation seeds and episodes. Team reward and its components. The SMAT metrics: peak normalised torque, negative-power time fraction, hip-muscle activation vs the Stage 2 checkpoint with the device worn, and joint RMSE. Stability: KL between successive checkpoints, torque-profile drift over the last 10% of B, and the count of human policy 'switches' (checkpoint-to-checkpoint KL above a declared threshold). Equilibrium identity: cluster final behaviour on torque timing relative to hip velocity and on the human's hip-activation pattern, and report basin frequencies per arm, not just means. Compute cost per arm. 6) Deployment probes, the honest co-adaptation test. D1 deployment consistency: freeze each final exo, continue training its co-trained human for 10% of B, and report drift in activation, kinematics and delivered torque; large drift means the joint solution is not a fixed point of the deployment game (device fixed, wearer adapting). D2 fresh partner: from held-out Stage 2 seeds, train a new human against the frozen exo and compare with D1. D3 cross-play: pair each exo with the frozen humans of every other seed and arm, evaluation only. D4 no-assist control: train the C4 human reward at tau_max = 0 (per the SMAT digest), so reward re-weighting is not credited to the device. 7) Statistics. Use >= 5 seeds per arm at the confirmatory tier and 3 at screening. Preregister the primary outcome and B. Report means with bootstrap 95% CIs and per-seed points. Use equivalence margins (TOST) for 'no difference' claims instead of C's P > 0.05 convention. Report arms that cycle or diverge as results, not failures to rerun. 8) Toy tier FIRST (hours, CPU). (i) Rebuild G's and S's example CaMDPs with the reconstructed convention: joint state index s0*4 + ss*2 + s1; Agent0 acts on (s0, ss) and Agent1 on (s1, ss); P = P0 (x) Ps (x) P1; per-transition reward = the product R0*Rs*R1; gamma 0.98; V = max over the 8 states; policy No. = 16*bin(pi0) + bin(pi1) + 1. Unit-test against 0.210127 / 0.173602 / 0.187532 / 0.207822 / 0.196426 (G) and 9.988937 / 9.046 / 9.811 (S), and against the order, cycling and NE results in headline_numbers. Then run Procedures 1 and 2 at declared M and eta. (ii) C's quadratic game: a machine adapting at rate alpha against a simulated human that is either a gradient learner at rate beta or a best-responder. By algebra, simultaneous gradient play has its stationary point at the NE for every alpha and beta (h - m/3 + 2/15 = 0 and m = h give h = m = -0.2), while a best-responder facing m = h reaches the SE (0.2, 0.2) by minimising 0.4 h^2 - 0.16 h. This calibrates the analysis code and the claim language before any SMAT compute is spent.

**Throughput.** Simulation. The toy tier is negligible: exact 8-state CaMDP value solves take milliseconds per policy pair, and C's quadratic game with simulated humans takes seconds. For SMAT, the sibling digest (smat_original_and_extended.json, throughput_notes) gives about 28 h per 100M steps on one Xeon W-2145, about 992 aggregate steps/s (as claimed there). Branching every arm from one shared Stage 2 checkpoint avoids re-running the 480M steps of Stages 1-2 (about 134 h at that rate) per arm. A full-budget arm (67.1M) costs about 18.8 h per seed at that rate, so 12 arms x 5 seeds is about 1,130 h, too much for one CPU box. A screening pass at 20M steps x 3 seeds x 8 arms is 480M steps, about 134 h; confirm only the top 2-3 arms at full budget with 5 seeds. D1 and D2 at 10% of B add about 10-20%; D3 is evaluation only. Human time (later study, never automated): PC used about 10 h of exoskeleton walking per participant over 6 days, and walking expertise took about 109 min. KiS used 2 visits, each with familiarisation plus 4 trials of <= 15 min, i.e. 40 iterations of 60 strides. KS assumes 2 min per metabolic evaluation, so 80 evaluations take about 160 min. C's online sessions lasted 10-14 min. Sprint trials are short but fatiguing, so far fewer HILO evaluations fit into a session than for walking [argued; no source measures sprinting].

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- SMAT level: any staged benefit may come from the reward curriculum (r_exo3 -> r_exo4, r_hip_act 2 -> 5), the torque curriculum (6 -> 25 Nm), the exo warm start, critic carry-over, the human-observation augmentation, or unequal budgets and seeds, rather than from freezing the human. The 2x2 and dose arms target these.
- The derived CaMDP results come from exact best-response dynamics on tiny 16x16 policy games under one value scalarisation (max over states). State-wise policy improvement, other scalarisations, or stochastic PPO updates may behave differently.
- KiS (DBO > BO): day order and cross-day learning; carry-over through the user-driven treadmill; the 1.6 m/s ceiling; uncorrected post hoc tests. BO's validation inputs lay further from the initial inputs (Table I: 0.36 vs 0.26 at iteration 20), so BO may have explored more rather than modelled worse.
- C (the NE -> SE shift): at alpha = 1 the machine literally implements m = h within a trial, which confines people to a line. Visual hill-climbing on the displayed square-root cost along that line reaches the same point without game-theoretic reasoning. Equivalence was inferred from P > 0.05 with n = 20.
- PC (training effect): expertise with the generic profile may be specific to the profile the static group practised. Groups are small, and respirometry noise (about 5% SD, KS p6) is large next to the 10 +/- 11% pre-test effect.
- KS (CMA-ES sim-vs-experiment agreement): the surrogate was fit to CMA-ES-sampled data, and the exogenous drift model favours forgetting optimisers by construction.
- J (the staged half/half arm wins on effect): one model family and noise setting, 10 runs, and a noise-free OC comparator, so the comparison is not matched.

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- [idea] Order as an equilibrium selector in SMAT: human-first vs exo-first alternation from the same Stage 2 checkpoint, >= 10 seeds each, with basin frequencies as the outcome. The derived CaMDP result predicts order sensitivity, and SMAT's Stage 3 is exo-first by construction.
- [idea] Make deployment consistency (D1/D2) a primary co-adaptation endpoint: a schedule 'wins' only if its joint solution survives continued human-only training against the frozen exo.
- [idea] Toy-tier timescale map: in C's quadratic game and the G/S CaMDPs, sweep the human model (gradient learner at rate beta vs best-responder) against machine rate alpha. The analysis pipeline should recover C's NE -> SE shift only for best-responders. This is a cheap, falsifiable calibration of claim language before SMAT runs.
- [idea] An anticipating device: in the toy tier, give the exo a conjecture of the human's response (C Exp 2 conjectural variation, Exp 3 policy gradient). In SMAT, approximate it with an inner loop in which the human re-converges before each exo update (a two-timescale limit). Compare with naive joint training.
- [idea] Population training: train the exo against a population of simulated humans (different seeds, learning rates and reward weights) and test cross-play on held-out humans. This tackles partner specificity (KS Fig. 4 shows subject-specific optima).
- [idea] A simulated HILO rehearsal for sprint, using the KS method: build a surrogate landscape from SMAT-sprint simulation plus pilot data, add an adaptation model that depends on the applied assistance (unlike KS's exogenous drift), and choose the optimiser (DBO or CMA-ES) and evaluation budget before any human session.
- [idea] A wearer-rate estimator as a research question (KB 05/06): predict the next response from observations available before the decision, beyond recent error, error slope and practice time, and compare against yoked schedules.
- [idea] Dual-control budgeting for sprint HILO (J): explicitly split each session's few sprints into identification probes and exploitation trials, front-loading identification (J p6 argues for an adaptive alpha but does not test it).
- [idea] Device-side threshold gating in human studies (G Theorem 1 logic, with its partner-fixed caveat): propose a new device setting between sessions only when the predicted improvement exceeds eta, which bounds the loss per switch. A human approves every change.
- [idea] Measure two curves per human session: the device effect at a fixed wearer state (randomised A/B of old vs new setting) and wearer drift at a fixed device (PC's probe). Report their interaction.
