## What transfers, and what the disputes teach

### Experiment-free exoskeleton assistance via learning in simulation

- *Experiment-free exoskeleton assistance via learning in simulation* — Luo, Jiang, Zhang S., Zhu, Yu, Dominguez Silva, Wang, Rouse, Zhou B., Yuk, Zhou X. (NJIT; also SMAT senior author), Su (corresponding). Author contributions: code 'on the basis of a framework created by S.L. and X.Z.' (N p10), Nature 630:353-359; received 4 Sep 2023, accepted 3 Apr 2024, published 12 Jun 2024; Editor's Note added 27 Jul 2026. arXiv:none · code: Code availability (N p10): pseudocode only, github.com/IntelligentRobotLearning/pseudocode_learning_in_simulation: 4 files (Closed_loop_training.py, Environment_simulator.py, Human_exoskeleton_model.py, README.md), HEAD 422a9c12e288598a66a08e8259d18cd12147195a dated 2025-06-03 (README edit; code files unchanged since 2024-03-01), no license. Not runnable (stub environment, undefined names, no reward functions). Data availability (N p10): 'in the Article and its Supplementary Information' plus Source Data. The Source Data holds only group mean/SD for metabolic rate, per-participant torque curves (Fig. 3b,c) and one 28.2-s torque/power trace (Fig. 4b,c). SI Table 7 gives per-participant assist-off and no-exo metabolic values, but per-participant assist-on values are not public.. Read: pp1-18 (all); N-SI pp1-30 (text) with pp18-26 as images; N-SD all sheets
- *Experiment-free learning of exoskeleton assistance remains an unsolved problem* — Collins, De Groote, Gregg, Huang, Lenzi, Sartori, Sawicki, Si, Slade, Young (alphabetical; replication run and analysed by P. Slade at Harvard, C p5), bioRxiv v1 posted 6 Apr 2026, CC-BY-NC-ND 4.0. Per R p11, submitted to Nature as Matters Arising in Dec 2025. The bioRxiv API (2026-09-23) shows v1 only and 'published: NA'.. arXiv:none · code: Analysis code for Fig. 1 and the Supplement is 'provided as Supplementary Data 1'; study data 'in the Source Data' (C p5). The bioRxiv supplementary files were not downloaded or checked here.. Read: pp1-26 (all)
- *Experiment-free learning of exoskeleton assistance is not an unsolved problem* — Luo, Jiang, Zhang S., Zhu, Yu, Dominguez Silva, Zhou B., Yuk, Zhou X., Su (Wang and Rouse from the 2024 author list are not authors), bioRxiv v1 posted 17 Jun 2026, CC-BY-NC-ND 4.0; v1 only, unpublished (bioRxiv API 2026-09-23). arXiv:none · code: Claims 'executable code, raw experimental data, and simulation files' were released (R p11) and refers to a 'released extended codebase' (R p15). No URL is given, and nothing could be located on 2026-09-23 (see availability_audit_2026_09_23). A new video, youtube.com/watch?v=0CHMQ6bModE (R p19), exists (oEmbed title 'Torque_Angle_Ground reaction force', channel 'Robot Learning'); its content was not viewed and it is not data.. Read: pp1-24 (all)

**Problem.** Luo et al. claim that a hip-exoskeleton controller learned only in simulation (three MLPs trained jointly in a DART/MASS-derived 208-muscle human model, about 8 h on one RTX3090) transferred zero-shot to a 3.2 kg portable hip exoskeleton. Against no exoskeleton, they report net metabolic reductions of 24.3% walking (1.25 m/s), 13.1% running (2.0 m/s) and 15.4% stair climbing (65 steps/min), n=8 per activity, and call these the largest reported for portable hip exoskeletons. Collins et al. (10 exoskeleton researchers) make three arguments. (i) The implied energy ratios (metabolic savings per unit of positive exo work: 1:5.5 walking, 1:6.6 stairs) exceed a ceiling of about 1:4 set by muscle efficiency. (ii) A 10-person hardware replication that applied Luo's average walking torque profile found no significant saving (best about 1%). (iii) The learning method cannot be reproduced: model, code, reward constants, seeds and trained policies were never released, and the supplementary movies show non-physical simulation. Luo et al. reply as follows. The value of 4 is an average efficiency, not a ceiling. The critique mis-timed the torque (their 0% gait phase is the torque peak, not heel strike) and so underestimated power; their own estimates of 17.9-24.9 W give ratios of 2.4-3.4. The replication tested a fixed curve on a 50% heavier device, without a zero-torque baseline and without first reproducing a known result. Other groups have since used the paradigm. Nature added an Editor's Note on data and code availability on 27 Jul 2026. For this programme, the dispute is a worked example of how a simulation-trained-controller claim fails review: no positive control, power and metabolic cost not measured together, undefined phase references, and a pseudocode-only release. It is also relevant that SMAT's senior author co-built Luo's framework and co-signed the response.

**Mechanism (exact).** THREE NETWORKS trained 'simultaneously' in simulation (N p5; Methods pp8-10; ED Figs 1-4 pp11-14; N-SI Table 1 p18). (1) Motion-imitation network pi_phi(a_m|s_m). PPO actor MLP [256,256], critic [256,256]; ReLU with a Normal output and state-independent log-std per the pseudocode. Input (50,2): angle and angular velocity of 50 joint DoF. Output (50,1): target joint angles (Methods p8), converted by PD tau = 50*(p_des - p) - 14.14*pdot (Eq. 10, N p9); ED Fig. 2 instead calls the output a 'joint torque command'. Reward r_human = w_p*r_p + w_root*r_root + w_cop*r_cop, where r_p = exp(-sigma_p * sum_j ||p_des - p||^2) (Eq. 7), r_root = exp(-sigma_root * sum_j ||p_root_des - p_root||^2) (Eq. 8), and r_cop = exp(-10*||D(c_cop,S)||^2) if the CoP is inside S, else 0 (Eq. 9; S = a 9 x 16 cm rectangle per foot, N-SI Fig. 7 p17). Weights by grid search (w_p in {0.25,0.5,0.75,1}, w_root in {0.1,...,1}): w_p=0.75, w_root=0.5, w_cop=0.2 (N p9). sigma_p and sigma_root are NOT reported. Reference data: CMU mocap, 10 s (120 Hz x 1,200 frames) per activity (walk, run, stairs) from one representative subject (N p8); subject and trial IDs are not reported. (2) Muscle-coordination network pi_theta(a). 50 desired joint torques -> [512,256,256] -> 208 activations (N-SI Table 1; ED Fig. 3), trained by supervised regression with loss = E||pddot_des - M^-1(J_M^T F_M + J_ext^T F_ext - c)||^2 + w_a*||a||^2 (Eq. 11; w_a not reported). Only the 108 lower-limb muscles enter 'the reward function equation (11)' (N p9); which 108 is not listed. The public pseudocode instead copies MASS: input = muscle-Jacobian torques + desired torques, hidden 1024/512/512 with LeakyReLU(0.2), Tanh then ReLU, and a torque-space loss 0.01*mean(a^2) + mean(((tau - tau_des)/100)^2). (3) Exoskeleton control network pi_varphi(a_e|s_e). Actor 18 -> [128,64] -> 2; critic 18 -> [128,64] -> 1; ReLU; Normal with state-independent log-std (pseudocode). Input: 3 consecutive 100 Hz samples of [L/R hip (thigh) angle, L/R angular velocity, L/R previous action]. Dimension 18 per ED Fig. 4 '(9x2)x1' and N-SI Table 1, but '16 x 1' in Methods p9. Output: 2 target joint positions -> second-order low-pass filter (cutoff not reported) -> 'preprocess action' (not described) -> PD tau_exo = 50*(a_e - p_exo) - 14.14*pdot_exo (Eq. 15, N p9). Reward r_t = w_m*r_m + w_tau*r_tau + w_p*r_p + w_root*r_root + w_cop*r_cop + w_as*r_as (Eq. 13), with w_m=0.5, w_as=0.2, w_tau=0.1. The weights on r_p, r_root and r_cop inside Eq. 13 are not stated. r_m ('muscle work') and r_tau ('exoskeleton energy consumption') are named with NO formulas. r_as = exp(-sigma_as*||a_t - 2a_{t-1} + a_{t-2}||^2) (Eq. 14; sigma_as not reported). Because r_p, r_root and r_cop are reused, the exo scores well only if the simulated human keeps tracking the reference (N p9). SIMULATOR: DART (N-SI Text 4 p5; author replies in GitHub issues #2 and #3). MASS-derived (issue #10 author reply 2025-07-01; R p16: 'adopt the same human walking control network and muscle coordination network as the established MASS model ... augmented only with an additional exoskeleton control network'). Model: 170 cm, 72 kg, 50 DoF (8 revolute + 14 ball-and-socket joints), 208 Hill-type musculotendon units (Eqs 1-3). Passive force-length is given; active force-length, force-velocity, tendon and foot-contact models are not. The public MASS repo ships a 284-muscle model (lsw9021/MASS data/muscle284.xml; last commit e44c2ef, 2021-12-08); the 208-muscle model is not public. Integration 600 Hz; control 100 Hz for both human and exo (N-SI Text 4). HUMAN-EXO COUPLING: linear bushings F = k*d + c*ddot and tau = alpha*theta + beta*thetadot (Eq. 5, N p8). Pelvis-belt: k_y=8000, c_y=10. Leg straps: k=[1500,500,1500], c=[10,1,10], alpha=[10,3,10], beta=[1,0.1,1] (N-SI Table 1; units N/m, N s/m, N m/rad, N m s/rad are given only in R p15). TRAINING: PPO with gamma 0.99, lr 1e-4, batch 128, 10 epochs (3 for the muscle net), clip 0.2, memory buffer 2048, max iterations 120,000 (N-SI Table 2 p19; identical to the MASS python/main.py defaults). GAE lambda 0.99 and 16 parallel environments appear only in the pseudocode/MASS. Xavier-uniform initialisation; about 20 M samples (N-SI p5). Training stopped when the exo reward 'converged and stopped increasing' at about iteration 3,500, at about 8 s per iteration: under 8 h on one RTX3090 plus a Xeon E5-1660 v3 (N p10; N-SI p5). The response adds a rule: converged when the reward changes by less than 5% across 10 consecutive validation evaluations (R p14). DOMAIN RANDOMISATION, sampled uniformly per episode (N p10): exo mass, inertia and motor strength x[0.8,1.2]; control latency x[0.5,1.5]; friction coefficient x[0.9,1.6]; muscle maximum isometric force x[0.5,1.5] (N-SI Table 3 p20) but x[0.5,1.0] in N-SI Text 7 p7. Nominal values are not reported. DEPLOYMENT: weights copied from simulation. Inputs: thigh IMU (LPMS-B2, 100 Hz) angle relative to standing, plus angular velocity; the 'hip joint angle' is a thigh-segment angle because the pelvis is not measured (N-SI Text 6). Treadmill runs used Simulink Real-Time; outdoor runs used a Raspberry Pi 4 (Python) -> Teensy 4.1 (N p10). Torque is estimated from motor current. A hand-coded rule zeroes assistance if hip range of motion deviates more than 50% from the reference (N p6).

**Protocol.**
- *Luo 2024 human:* 11 participants (8 M, 3 F; 69.4 +/- 17.7 kg; 172.8 +/- 8.4 cm; 28.4 +/- 3.3 y), 8 per activity: P1-8 walking; P1-5 and P9-11 running and stairs. 9/11 (6/8 walkers) are marked 'Exoskeleton experience: YES' (ED Table 1 p17); prior exposure is not quantified. Metabolic protocol: 5-min standing rest; no-exo; a 5-min warm-up/adaptation with assistance; then Assist On, Assist Off and No Exo, 5 min each, last 2 min averaged, breaks of at least 5 min, order randomised. N-SI p9 says no-exo was run twice (start and end) and averaged, whereas ED Fig. 6 (p16) shows one randomised no-exo block. Respirometry: VO2 Master Analyzer, O2 only; VCO2 set to 0.85*VO2; Brockway equation; net = minus standing (N-SI p9). Not blinded (N-SI p10). Statistics: paired t-tests, one-sided per the Fig. 5 caption (N p5); mean +/- SEM in the text, SD in Fig. 1e. Torque-profile protocol: 8 participants at 0.75/1.25/1.75 m/s walking and 2.0 m/s running, 1 min each, assist on only, about 30 strides averaged (N pp3-4; N-SI p9). Transition trial: 1 participant outdoors, about 0.8 m/s walk -> about 2 m/s run -> 7 stairs; power = motor torque x thigh-IMU angular velocity (N p4 Fig. 4; ED Table 2 p18; N-SI p9). CONTROLS PRESENT: device worn at zero torque (Assist Off) and No Exo. CONTROLS ABSENT: any conventional controller on the same device; any simulated-vs-measured check of kinematics, GRF or EMG; exo power measured during the metabolic trials.
- *Collins replication:* Harvard (run by P. Slade). n=10 (23 +/- 3 y, 1.69 +/- 0.07 m, 70 +/- 16 kg), mostly novice exo users; treadmill at 1.25 m/s. 5 conditions: No Exo, plus Luo's published mean 1.25 m/s torque profile played against stride-normalised time, starting at IMU-estimated heel strike (sign change of thigh sagittal velocity; Quintero 2017) and delayed 0/5/10/15% of the gait cycle (GC). Double-reversal order ABCDEEDCBA, 5 min each, last 2 min averaged over both presentations; 5-min warm-up walk and 5-min quiet standing. COSMED K5 (O2 and CO2), Brockway equation (C pp13-14). Device: custom 4.8 kg including batteries, derived from M-BLUE, AK10-9 motors, load-cell torque sensing (C pp14-15, Fig. S3). Pilots (n=1 each): the mean and mean +/- 1 SD profiles were within 2% of each other in metabolic cost; shifts of -10% to +30% GC in 5% steps were tried and 0-15% chosen as lowest cost (C p14). No zero-torque condition: the 1.6 kg mass difference was corrected with Browning 2007 (0.045 W/kg per kg), subtracting 0.072 W/kg (C p15). Power = load-cell torque x thigh sagittal angular velocity (C p16). The statistical test, p-values, confidence intervals and power analysis are NOT reported.
- *Collins calculations:* Walking power: per-participant torques from N-SD Fig. 3b multiplied by the subject-averaged hip velocity from Camargo 2021 (N=22, 1.2 m/s). Assumes Luo's 0% GC = heel strike and that assistance barely changes kinematics; negative power zeroed; x2 for bilateral (C pp8-9, Fig. S1); sensitivity checked with +/-5% GC shifts (C p10). Running and stairs: positive work over time from steady strides of Luo Fig. 4, i.e. the n=1 outdoor trial (4 running strides, 6 stair strides) (C p10). Numerator = assist-off minus assist-on metabolic rate in W (definitions C p7, p12). Model and compute comparison with Simos et al. 2025 (C pp21-22). Frame-by-frame review of Luo Supplementary Movies 1-2 (C pp22-24).
- *Luo response analyses:* (1) Redefines the gait phase: 0% of Fig. 3b is the torque peak, which can fall up to about 30% GC after heel strike (citing Young 2017); an 18% shift is used as representative (R p4 Fig. 1a; p12). (2) Direct power estimate from the Fig. 4 transition trial (R p5; Fig. S2 p12). (3) Collins' own code rerun with an 18% shift and Lim 2019 exo-on hip velocity scaled to 1.25 m/s (R p13, Fig. S3). (4) A literature table of energy ratios (R p6, Fig. 2) and a cross-device torque-power-metabolic table (R pp6-7, Table 1). (5) Methodological objections to the replication (R pp3-4, 8-9). (6) A list of 13 learning-in-simulation works (R p10, Table 2). (7) Clarifications: bushing units and frames (R p15); convergence rule and seeds (R p14); Movie 1 is a post-edited illustration (R p17); DART LCP contact artefacts (R pp17-18); a new unedited video (R p19). The PDF presents no new human-subject or simulation data tables.

**Headline numbers**
- [measured, Luo] Walking 1.25 m/s, n=8: net metabolic rate 2.91 +/- 0.26 W/kg (no exo) -> 2.19 +/- 0.19 W/kg (assist on), mean +/- SEM. Per-participant reduction 19.9-30.8%, mean 24.3% (N p4 text; Fig. 5a p5; ED Table 2 p18). [re-derived] Reduction computed from the group means = 24.7%.
- [measured, Luo] Running 2.0 m/s, n=8: 8.25 +/- 0.92 -> 7.19 +/- 0.85 W/kg; per-participant 7.6-20.8%, mean 13.1% (N p4). [re-derived] From the group means = 12.8%.
- [measured, Luo] Stairs, 65 steps/min on a stepmill, n=8: 5.54 +/- 0.24 -> 4.66 +/- 0.22 W/kg; per-participant 8.7-25.7%, mean 15.4% (N p4). N-SD Fig. 1E/5A list 5.5 and 4.67. [re-derived] From the group means = 15.9%.
- [measured, Luo] Assist Off vs No Exo: +5.4% walking, +6.7% running, +10.3% stairs. Fig. 5a marks both Assist Off vs No Exo and No Exo vs Assist On with ** (P <= 0.01, one-sided paired t-test) in all three activities (N p5). N-SI Table 7 (p25): means 3.07/2.91, 8.73/8.26 and 6.09/5.55 W/kg; penalties 5.42, 7.49 and 10.28%. [re-derived] The table's own running rows give 6.70% (mean of per-participant ratios), not 7.49%.
- [measured, Luo] Device 3.2 kg (N p10); 3.226 kg with 81% at the waist (N-SI Table 6 p24). Its mass model predicts a penalty of 14.53 W walking and 22.35 W running (N-SI Table 6). Peak torque 18 N m (N p10) but 36 Nm in N-SI Text 6 (p6); 'This work' is listed at 14, 18 and 16 Nm (N-SI Table 4 p21).
- [measured, Luo] Torque profiles: max flexion torque 6.0-7.7 Nm (mean 6.8) at 1.25 m/s and 12.9-17.8 Nm (mean 15.4) running (N-SD Fig3C; N Fig. 3c). [re-derived] At 1.25 m/s the normalised extension peak is 0.166 Nm/kg (mean). Mapping participants to ED Table 1 masses (an assumed mapping) gives peaks of 8.3-14.1 Nm, mean 11.1 Nm.
- [measured, Luo] Negative exo work averaged 3.47% of delivered work per gait cycle, in bursts of 0.04-0.08 s (6-8% GC) (N p4). [re-derived] From the N-SD Fig. 4c trace, peak-to-peak stride segmentation gives 1.74% per stride (1.94% over the whole trial); the paper's definition is not given.
- [measured, Luo] Torque tracking RMSE 0.83 Nm (4.92% of peak), 1 participant running at 2.0 m/s, with torque estimated from motor current (N-SI Text 6 p6; SI Fig. 6).
- [simulated, Luo] Robustness: with 0-80% input perturbation over 200 simulation tests, success falls from 100% to about 50% (SI Fig. 5 caption) or 'merely above 40%' (N-SI Text 5 p6).
- [reported, Luo] Training converged at about iteration 3,500 of a 120,000 cap, about 8 s per iteration, under 8 h on 1 RTX3090 (N p10; N-SI Table 2 p19); about 20 M samples (N-SI p5). [re-derived] 3,500 x 8 s = 7.8 h. 3,500 iterations x a 2048-sample buffer = 7.2 M samples, which does not match 20 M.
- [calculated, Collins] Luo walking positive exo power 11.2 +/- 2.0 W (mean +/- SD over 8 torque curves; C p9, Fig. S1). Shifting the timing +/-5% GC gives 9.4-13.8 W and ratios 1:4.5 to 1:6.5 (C p10).
- [calculated, Collins] Energy ratios for Luo (mechanical:metabolic): walking 1:5.5 (11.2 W : 61.6 W); running 1:1.9 in the text (C p10) but 1:1.8 in Table S1 (C p12) (63.5 W : 112.7 W); stairs 1:6.6 (15.5 W : 101.5 W) (C pp9-10, p12). [re-derived] All three numerators are reproduced exactly as (no-exo mean x (1 + Fig. 5a penalty), rounded, minus assist-on mean) x 70 kg. The 70 kg is unstated; the cohort means are 68.4 kg walking and 76.5 kg running/stairs.
- [argued/cited, Collins] Muscle does 1 J of positive work for about 4 J of metabolic energy (efficiency 0.25); the apparent efficiency of joint work in gait is 25-50%; no prior exo exceeded 2.3 J saved per J (C p1, pp7-8). Table S1 (C p12): Seo 2016 1:1.7, Franks 2021 1:1.8, Kim 2022 1:2.3, Molinaro 2024 1:0.8, Park 2025 (stairs) 1:2.3.
- [measured, Collins replication] No assisted condition significantly reduced metabolic cost. Mean change vs no exo at 0/5/10/15% shift: +10/+7/+1/+3%; mass-compensated +8/+5/-1/+1% (C Fig. 1b labels p2). Best: 10% shift, about -1% (C p3, p15). Best single participant about 14% (C p15). Excluding 2 non-responders: 6% (C p16; the authors do not endorse the exclusion). Exo power 11.1-13.0 W, average 11.7 W (C p10; Fig. S4 p16). Energy ratio 1:1.1 (11.7 W : 12.7 W; C p12), where the 12.7 W is relative to a MODELLED zero-torque condition. Summary: 13 W vs 62 W saved, 1% vs 24% vs normal walking (C p17).
- [measured, Collins pilots, n=1] Mean vs mean +/- 1 SD torque profiles: within 2% metabolic. Timing shifts of -10% to +30% GC: 0-15% had the lowest cost (C p14).
- [calculated, Collins] Network size: Luo's networks total 494,662 parameters vs 22,676,723 for the Simos 2025 MoE (about 46x). Training data 3 x 10 s vs 6,840 s (1,084 trials, 21 people). Training time 8 h on an RTX3090 vs 240 h on an A100 (30x) (C pp21-22). [re-derived] 494,662 is reproduced exactly assuming a 100-dim imitation input and an 18-dim exo input; the exo actor alone has 10,818 parameters (R p1 says about 10,000).
- [argued, Collins] From the movies: pose discontinuities (Movie 1 'MOESM2', frames 22:26-22:29); GRFs with large horizontal and negligible vertical components plus foot-ground penetration (Fig. S5; Movie 2 at 19:15); abrupt torque jumps in transitions (Movie 2 'MOESM3') (C pp22-24).
- [record, Collins] Code requests: in person 29 May 2024; Zoom 18 Jun 2024 and 24 Jun 2025; e-mails 5 Jun 2024, 28 May 2025, and 2, 5 and 24 Jun 2025 (C p5 ref. 12). GitHub issue #5 by pslade2 (2024-09-16) has no reply. [argued, response] Collins requested and received metabolic-rate data in 2024; the specific technical issues were not raised with the authors before posting; Luo and Su received no communication from Gregg (R p11).
- [calculated, response] Walking power 24.9 W from Fig. 4 'cycle 3' (6.0-7.0 s), giving a ratio of 60.17/24.9 = 2.4 (R p5; Fig. S2 p12), where 60.17 W = (3.07 - 2.19) x 68.375 kg. [re-derived] 24.9 W = 2 x 12.5 W, the one-trace mean positive power over the stride 5.96-6.96 s in N-SD Fig. 4c. That is the FIRST ACCELERATING stride (1.00 s, after about 1.25 s strides; the next strides are 0.90 and 0.88 s) of an outdoor trial whose speed was not measured. The 0.8 m/s 'cycles' at 10.4 and 11.3 W (R p12) match 2 x one-trace values of 10.3 and 11.2 W.
- [calculated, response] Collins' method with an 18% shift and Lim 2019 exo-on kinematics gives 17.9 W, ratio about 3.4 (R p13 Fig. S3; also R p3, p5). Running: 57.3 W and 107.8 W, ratio 1.9 (R p6). [re-derived] 57.3 W = 2 x one-trace power of a single running stride (10.90-11.59 s); 107.8 W = (8.73 - 7.19) x 70 kg, although the running cohort's mean mass is 76.5 kg.
- [cited, response; not verified here] Energy ratios above 4 in the literature: Collins 2015 4.3 (unpowered ankle), Young 2017 5.0 (range 2.5-5), Ishmael & Lenzi 2021 5.4 (above-knee amputees), Malcolm 2013 4.8 (ankle), Seo 2017 up to 6.7 (individual maximum; median 5.0 at a 5 deg incline, 3.9 level), Seo 2016 corrected maximum 4.4 (median 3.7), Lee 2017 maximum 4.8 (mean 3.3), Barati 2026 individual values 4.95/4.63/4.52/4.35 (4 of 8) (R p3, R p6 table).
- [cited, response; not verified here] Barati et al. 2026 (RAL): 15.2% mean and 22.5% maximum reduction; peak torque 9.8 Nm mean (up to 10.5); 17.3 W mean positive power; 2.9 kg device. Lim 2019: 19.8% mean, 24.9% max, 10.9 Nm. Lee 2017: 21%, 10.9 Nm (R p5, Table 1 pp6-7).
- [argued, response] Samsung data: Collins' '24.1 Nm' vs 10.9 Nm in Seo 2016 against a 12 Nm hardware limit, and a Seo 2016 ratio of 1.7 vs published individual values up to 6.7 (R p3, p7, S1 pp11-12). 24.1 Nm is not stated in Collins' text; it is the response's reading of C Fig. S2 (about 0.35 Nm/kg). The response's corrected Seo 2016 power (10.8 W) is exactly half of Collins' 21.6 W, which suggests a bilateral x2 or torque-scaling disagreement. Neither is adjudicated here.
- [argued, response] Metabolic signal-to-noise ratio: 2.8 in Collins 2015 vs about 1000 for IMU angles (R p8). A hypothetical 16% benefit against a 15% mass penalty would net about 1% (R p9). [re-derived] With Luo's own N-SI Table 6 coefficients, the extra 1.6 kg costs 6.4 W at the waist to 10.7 W on the thighs, i.e. 2.9-4.8% of 3.2 W/kg. Luo's own 3.2 kg device measured 5.42% (N-SI Table 7).
- [record, Nature] Editor's Note dated 27 Jul 2026 (live page fetched 2026-09-23): 'Readers are alerted that concerns have been raised regarding the availability of data and code required to support the results and conclusions. Appropriate editorial action will be taken once this matter is resolved. In the meantime, readers are advised to use caution when using results reported therein.'

**Ablations that matter**
- Zero-torque arm (device mass). Luo measured it (Assist Off: +5.4/+6.7/+10.3%, N Fig. 5a; N-SI Table 7). Collins did not and modelled it instead (0.072 W/kg). This gap was the response's strongest counter-argument (R pp4, 9). A zero-torque arm is mandatory in any assistance comparison, simulated or human.
- Learned feedback policy vs its own mean open-loop profile. Luo never ran this, and the policy was never released. Collins could only run the mean profile, with a timing sweep. Whether state feedback adds anything in steady treadmill walking is untested. In simulation this ablation is cheap and decisive.
- Timing sweep of a fixed profile. Collins' main study used 0/5/10/15% GC; an n=1 pilot covered -10% to +30% (C p14). The response's 18% alignment (R p4) lies inside the pilot range but outside the main study. [re-derived] In N-SD Fig. 3b, the torque maximum sits at exactly 0% or 100% phase in all 32 participant-speed curves. So the cycles were cut at the torque peak, consistent with the response's definition, which the Nature paper never states.
- Individual vs mean profile. The n=1 pilot found <2% metabolic difference between the mean and mean +/- 1 SD profiles (C p14): weak evidence against the response's 'averaging removes individualisation' objection (R p8).
- Reward-term ablations. Luo reports none. Collins cites Zhong et al. 2025 (ICML): removing the kinematic-error term broke normative walking in an RL exosuit controller (C p19). The response says the rewards are 'fully specified' (R p10), but sigma_p, sigma_root, sigma_as, r_m and r_tau remain undefined.
- Domain randomisation. Only a simulated input-perturbation robustness test exists (N-SI Text 5, Fig. 5). There is no ablation of DR on human outcomes.
- Post-hoc exclusion. Collins shows that dropping 2 of 10 non-responders moves the best condition from 1% to 6% savings, and explicitly does not endorse doing so (C p16). This is a model for reporting a post-hoc sensitivity analysis.
- Mass-correction models. Collins uses Browning 2007 (0.045 W/kg per added kg at the hips); Luo's N-SI Table 6 uses 4.005 (waist) and 6.674 (thighs) W per added kg at 1.5 m/s. [re-derived] Both predict about 2-5% for 1.6 kg, well below the response's hypothetical 15% whole-device penalty.

**Limitations the authors admit**
- Luo 2024: trained for able-bodied users; not directly applicable to gait impairments (N p5).
- Luo 2024: gaits far from the training data (one subject's walk/run/stairs) may be assisted less well; if hip range of motion deviates more than 50% from the reference, assistance is zeroed (N p6; N-SI Fig. 5).
- Luo 2024: soft straps mean IMU readings do not reflect true thigh motion, causing small negative power peaks (N p4).
- Luo 2024: metabolic rate was kept out of the reward because metabolic simulation needs calibration of hundreds of muscles and is non-smooth (N p6).
- Luo 2024: only pseudocode was released (N p10). GitHub replies cite collaboration and IP discussions (issue #6, 2025-03-06; #10, 2025-07-01).
- Collins: only the average torque against normalised time could be tested, because the trained policy was unpublished and refused on request (C p17).
- Collins: no zero-torque condition; the mass effect was modelled (C p17).
- Collins: timing may differ between studies; addressed by testing several shifts and reporting the best (C p17).
- Collins: Luo power estimates rely on dataset kinematics because Luo published none, and ignore negative work (C p10, p13).
- Collins: post-hoc exclusion of non-responders is not endorsed (C p16).
- Response: the 24.9 W estimate is 'not presented as a new full-cohort power measurement' (R p5), and the 18% shift is 'representative', not participant-specific (R p4).
- Response: Supplementary Movie 1 is post-edited, shows a subset of muscles in fixed colours plus outdoor scenes not used in training, and 'cannot be used to read off muscle activity' (R p17).
- Response: brief friction-cone violations occur in the simulation, attributed to DART's LCP contact solver (R pp17-18).
- Response: with no concurrent exo power measurement on stairs, a stair energy ratio 'cannot be derived' (R p8).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- Luo: exo power was never measured during the n=8 metabolic trials. The only published power trace is one participant outdoors (N Fig. 4), so both sides estimate cohort power from mismatched data.
- Luo: Fig. 4 does not say whether power is one side or both. [re-derived] Collins and the response both silently doubled the single N-SD Fig. 4c trace: the response's 10.4/11.3/24.9 W equal 2 x the one-trace stride means (10.3/11.2/24.9 W), and Collins' 63.5 W running and 15.5 W stairs are also about 2x the trace.
- Response: the '1.25 m/s' 24.9 W is a single accelerating stride of an outdoor trial whose speed was not measured [re-derived].
- Luo: the Nature paper never defines the gait-phase reference for Fig. 3b; it first appears in R p4.
- Luo: on hardware the 'hip angle' is a thigh-segment angle relative to standing (N-SI Text 6), while the simulation observes hip joint angles. This observation mismatch is not analysed.
- Luo: no validation of the simulated human (kinematics, GRF, EMG vs data), no learning curves, one training run shown, no seeds. R p14 asserts consistency across seeds without showing data.
- Luo: the public pseudocode contradicts the paper. It has muscle-net widths of 1024/512/512 (from MASS) vs 512/256/256 in the paper, and a torque-space rather than acceleration-space muscle loss. Its PPO actor loss, (ratio*gae).mean(), has neither the clip nor the minus sign of MASS's -min(surr1, surr2). MAX_ITERATION is 100,000 vs 120,000 in N-SI Table 2.
- Luo: internal inconsistencies. Exo input 16 (N p9) vs 18 (ED Fig. 4; N-SI Table 1). Muscle-force DR range [0.5,1.5] (N-SI Table 3) vs [0.5,1.0] (N-SI Text 7). Peak torque 18 Nm (N p10) vs 36 Nm (N-SI Text 6). No-exo run once (ED Fig. 6) vs twice (N-SI p9). Running penalty 7.49% (N-SI Table 7) vs 6.7% (Fig. 5a and the table's own rows [re-derived]). Negative work 3.47% (N p4) vs 1.7-1.9% recomputed [re-derived]. About 20 M samples vs 7.2 M implied [re-derived]. Robustness success 'above 40%' vs '50%' (N-SI Text 5 vs its Fig. 5).
- Luo: respirometry is O2-only with RQ fixed at 0.85 (N-SI p9). [re-derived] For true RQ between 0.75 and 0.95 the Brockway bias is at most about +/-2.2%. That is too small to explain 24%, but it is below the field standard (C p14).
- Response: 'reproducibility' is redefined as the paradigm working elsewhere (R p9, p14). The works in Table 2 use other models and devices, and several share author X. Zhou (SMAT, R refs 38, 48, 49; the ERAU/NJIT/NYU JNER 2026 paper). None replicates the 24.3% result, and they are not independent.
- Response: its peak torque of 14.1 Nm equals the largest single participant's extension peak under the assumed mass mapping (the mean is 11.1 Nm) [re-derived], yet it is compared with Barati's MEAN of 9.8 Nm (R p5).
- Response: it scales savings by different body masses across activities: x68.375 kg for walking but x70 kg for running, although the running cohort's mean mass is 76.5 kg [re-derived].
- Response: its 15% mass-penalty scenario (R p9) exceeds what Luo's own N-SI Table 6 coefficients predict for the extra 1.6 kg (2.9-4.8%) [re-derived].
- Response: the claimed release (R p11, p15) has no location. Neither author GitHub account has pushed anything since 2025-06-03.
- Collins: treats an energy ratio of 4 as a physiological law. Individual and some cohort-level values above 4 are reported (R p6; not verified here), and assistance may lower force-related (isometric or co-contraction) costs that positive work does not capture. C p13 argues the opposite direction without data.
- Collins: assumes Luo's 0% GC = heel strike without evidence. The N-SD torque peaks sit at exactly 0% or 100% [re-derived], so the +/-5% sensitivity window may not bracket the true alignment.
- Collins: the running and stair ratios combine power from the n=1 outdoor trial with n=8 treadmill and stepmill metabolic data. [re-derived] The outdoor stair stride is about 1.5 s (about 80 steps/min) vs 65 steps/min on the stepmill.
- Collins: the numerators use an unstated 70 kg body mass for all activities [re-derived].
- Collins: factual slips. HyFyDy is named as Luo's engine (C p21), but Luo used DART (N-SI Text 4; GitHub issue #2). Participant experience is called 'not stated' (C p13), but ED Table 1 has an experience column. Actor activations and output distribution are called 'not provided' (C p19), but the pseudocode defines them. The Table S1 caption defines apparent efficiency as the inverse of its tabulated numbers (C p12). The running ratio is 1:1.9 (C p10) vs 1:1.8 (C p12). Fig. 1a is labelled 'Shim et al. (2016)' while Table S1 says 'Seo et al. (2016)'.
- Collins: the replication has no positive control on its own device and no zero-torque arm. With no test named, no confidence intervals and no power analysis, the size of effect it can exclude is unknown.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Reward: sigma_p, sigma_root, sigma_as, w_a; formulas for r_m ('muscle work') and r_tau ('exo energy consumption'); the weights of r_p, r_root and r_cop inside Eq. 13; which 108 muscles.
- Human model: segment inertias, joint axes and centres, muscle parameters (F_max, optimal fibre length, tendon slack length, pennation), tendon model, path waypoints, foot-ground contact and friction. The 208-muscle list (the public MASS model has 284).
- Reference motion: CMU subject and trial IDs, retargeting pipeline, looping and phase handling of the 10-s clips (asked in GitHub issue #11, no reply).
- Episodes: length, early termination, initial-state distribution; whether DR stays on at evaluation.
- Observation and action normalisation; low-pass filter order and cutoff; the 'preprocess action' step; hardware PD gains.
- PPO: GAE lambda, number of parallel environments, gradient clipping, value and entropy coefficients, log-std initialisation, seeds (the pseudocode/MASS values are lambda 0.99, 16 envs, entropy weight -0.001).
- DR nominal values (e.g. nominal control latency) and the meaning of the friction range.
- Checkpoint selection: 'stopped increasing' near iteration 3,500 (N p10); R p14 adds a rule of <5% change over 10 validation evaluations.
- Figures: the gait-phase reference of Fig. 3b (stated only in R p4); speeds during the Fig. 4 windows; whether Fig. 4 power is one side or bilateral.
- Hardware: thigh-angle vs hip-angle mapping, IMU calibration, commanded vs delivered torque (motor-current estimate only).
- Human data: per-participant assist-on values (absent from Source Data), per-participant condition order, acclimation beyond the 5-min warm-up, extent of prior exoskeleton experience.
- Collins: the statistical test; the M-BLUE modifications and mass distribution of the 4.8 kg device; heel-strike estimator parameters; per-participant data (claimed to be in the Source Data, not checked here).

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *Context:* This programme reconstructs SMAT, not Luo. SMAT's senior author X. Zhou co-created Luo's framework (N p10) and co-signed the response, and the response cites SMAT as support (R p10 Table 2; R p15). SMAT's human results are therefore not independent confirmation of Luo. Our reconstruction must be run and reported as an independent test with its own controls. Any author-supplied material must be disclosed.
- *Controls reviewers will demand:*
  - POSITIVE CONTROL on the same pipeline (R pp3-4 makes this the price of a credible negative). Before any 'does not reproduce' verdict: (a) reproduce a known published result on our stack, e.g. MyoAssist or SMAT Stage-1 unassisted gait metrics within a tolerance fixed in advance; (b) show that a known-effective simple hip controller (e.g. delayed-output-feedback per Lim 2019, or a fixed phase-locked flexion/extension profile) gives a non-zero simulated effort reduction. If either fails, the verdict is 'inconclusive (pipeline)'.
  - ZERO-TORQUE ARM: report no device / device worn at zero torque / assist on for every simulated effect. SMAT E itself reports 19.7% vs the passive device and 9.1% vs no device.
  - AVERAGE-POLICY REPLAY (Collins' test, done in simulation): replay the trained exo policy's mean torque vs phase open-loop, phase-locked to heel strike detected from simulated GRF. Sweep timing from -10% to +30% GC and include per-seed profiles. If it matches the learned policy within the equivalence margin, the verdict is 'a simpler controller does the same'.
  - REWARD-ONLY CONTROL: at matched sample budget, train a joint single-stage policy with the same reward terms, and one with the hip-activation term removed, to separate staging from reward shaping.
  - SEEDS AND CURVES: at least 5 seeds per arm, learning curves for every stage, deterministic evaluation episodes, and a checkpoint rule fixed in advance. Report per-seed values plus mean +/- SD (C p3 cites Henderson 2018).
  - HUMAN-MODEL VALIDITY before interpreting any assistance effect: simulated joint angles vs reference (RMSE), GRF shape, friction-cone residual statistics, and activation timing vs EMG norms (C pp22-24).
  - POWER ACCOUNTING in the same evaluation episodes that produce the effort metric: per-side and bilateral positive and negative exo power, labelled as such, from hip joint velocity. Compute delta(modelled metabolic cost)/positive exo power per seed as a plausibility flag: a value above 4 needs an explicit mechanism, but is not automatic rejection.
- *Reporting sim vs human:*
  - Keep simulated effects (activation or modelled-metabolic proxies) and measured human effects (the authors' respirometry) in separate tables with different column names. Never convert a simulated activation reduction into a predicted metabolic saving.
  - Label every SMAT human number 'reported by the authors (n, device, baseline); not reproduced'.
  - State each percentage's definition: the mean of per-seed or per-subject changes vs the change of the means (Luo: 24.3% vs 24.7% [re-derived]); SD vs SEM (Luo's text uses SEM, Fig. 1e uses SD); and the baseline (no device vs zero torque).
  - Define the gait-phase origin on every phase plot (heel strike from a GRF threshold) and publish the time series, not only phase averages.
  - Give the speed and condition for every torque or power number. Never use a transition or accelerating stride as a steady-state value, and never mix trials or cadences within one ratio.
  - Label montage videos as illustration and publish unedited evaluation rollouts as data (R p17).
- *Phrasing verdicts:*
  - REPRODUCES: 'Under reconstruction R (deviations D1..Dn listed), the SMAT-staged policy reduced <metric> by X% (mean +/- SD, k seeds) vs Y% reported, within the pre-registered tolerance T; the positive control passed.'
  - DOES NOT REPRODUCE: allowed only if (a) the positive control passed, (b) the unassisted baseline matched the reported metrics, (c) budget and seeds are at least those reported, and (d) each deviation is argued not to explain the gap. Wording: 'We did not obtain X under reconstruction R; the unreleased implementation may differ in U1..Um (listed).' Never write 'the method does not work'.
  - A SIMPLER CONTROLLER OR THE REWARD CHANGE DOES THE SAME: requires an equivalence test (TOST) with a margin fixed before running. 'Not significantly different' is not equivalence.
  - INCONCLUSIVE: when the positive control fails, or when listed unspecified details could plausibly flip the result.
  - Report documentation findings ('cannot be reproduced from the paper: A, B missing') separately from empirical findings. Keep a dated log of material requests to the authors and their answers; C p5 ref. 12 is the model.
- *Release package:*
  - Executable code at a tagged commit, archived with a DOI (e.g. Zenodo), with a license. Never pseudocode alone: Luo's pseudocode differed from the paper (PPO loss, muscle-net widths), and its release was the subject of the Editor's Note.
  - Environment lockfile or container, simulator versions, and the hardware used.
  - Model files: human MJCF, device model, muscle list and parameters, attachment parameters with units and frames.
  - Reference motions with source IDs and preprocessing scripts.
  - Every reward term with its constants; observation and action normalisation; all PPO hyperparameters (GAE lambda, n_envs, gradient clipping, entropy weight, log-std initialisation); DR ranges with nominal values.
  - Seeds, per-stage checkpoints for every seed, normalisation statistics, the checkpoint rule, and training logs.
  - Evaluation scripts that regenerate every figure and table from raw per-step rollout logs (torque, kinematics, GRF, activations), including the positive-control and ablation runs.
  - A deviations register (inferred choice vs paper) and the author-correspondence log.
- *Gate changes:*
  - Add the positive control as a hard precondition of any negative verdict.
  - Add a zero-torque device arm and an average-policy replay arm with a timing sweep to the core evaluation.
  - Pre-register tolerance, equivalence margin and seed count; add 'inconclusive' as a formal outcome.
  - Add power accounting and energy-ratio flags plus a human-model validity report to every evaluation run.
  - Report SMAT human results only as authors' claims, and disclose the Zhou link when citing SMAT or Luo as support.

**Recipe on our server.** Not applicable: this programme does NOT reconstruct Luo et al. If a Luo-style baseline were ever needed, the only honest version is an 'inspired-by' reimplementation. Start from MASS (DART) at lsw9021/MASS commit e44c2ef (its 284-muscle model, not Luo's 208). Add a 2-DoF hip exo with bushings per N-SI Table 1. Use an exo actor 18 -> 128 -> 64 -> 2 (ReLU, Normal) and the MASS human networks. Train with PPO per N-SI Table 2, using MASS's clipped surrogate, and DR per N-SI Table 3, on CMU 10-s clips (IDs unknown). Label every inferred item, and never call the result a reproduction. For this programme, use this digest as a checklist of what the SMAT reconstruction must specify, control and release.

**Throughput.**
- Luo: about 3,500 PPO iterations x about 8 s = about 7.8 h on 1 RTX3090 plus a 16-thread Xeon E5-1660 v3; DART at 600 Hz physics and 100 Hz control (N p10; N-SI p5). The 16 parallel environments appear only in the pseudocode/MASS. The stated '~20 M samples' is inconsistent with 3,500 x 2048 = 7.2 M [re-derived]. Training stopped at about 3% of the 120,000-iteration cap.
- Collins' compute comparison: the Simos 2025 imitation-only MoE has 22.7 M parameters, used 6,840 s of KIT data and trained for 240 h on an A100 (C pp21-22). The response counters with Zuo et al. 2026 at about 8 h (R pp16-17; not verified). Model scales differ, so neither side's timing comparison transfers.
- Luo's networks total 494,662 parameters, and the deployed exo actor has 10,818 [re-derived]; the controller is tiny, so wall-clock is dominated by musculoskeletal simulation, not networks.
- For this programme: budget from our own measured env-steps/s per stage, and report wall-clock time, samples, hardware and seeds per stage. Do not import the '8 h' figure.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- Genuine effect of well-timed bilateral flexion and extension assistance, with Collins underestimating power through timing and kinematics assumptions (the response's position; R p5, p13). Settling it requires synchronized per-participant power, which neither side has published.
- A benefit specific to the learned feedback policy that its average profile does not capture. Untested either way.
- Participant familiarity: 9/11 Luo participants had exoskeleton experience (ED Table 1) vs mostly novices in Collins (C p13). Adaptation and training can change benefits substantially (Poggensee & Collins 2021, C ref. 37).
- Protocol and measurement: O2-only respirometry with fixed RQ (bias bounded at about +/-2% [re-derived]); a single 5-min bout per condition vs Collins' double reversal; unblinded; order description inconsistent (ED Fig. 6 vs N-SI p9).
- Device differences: 4.8 kg vs 3.2 kg (mass effect modelled, not measured); different actuators, cuffs and attachment compliance; different phase estimators.
- Timing mismatch: the replication's heel-strike-referenced playback vs Luo's torque-peak-referenced cycles (N-SD peaks at 0%/100% [re-derived]). Collins' n=1 pilot over -10% to +30% only partly mitigates this.
- Statistics: n=8, one-sided paired tests, and every walker between 19.9% and 30.8% (N p4). This is not evidence of error, but the per-participant assist-on data are not public, so the distribution cannot be checked.

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Make the positive control gate item 1: reproduce MyoAssist or SMAT Stage-1 unassisted gait metrics and a simple hip-assist effect before training any SMAT stage.
- Build a 'Collins-in-simulation' harness: log the trained exo policy's torques, compute the per-seed mean profile against GRF-defined phase, then replay it open-loop with a timing sweep. It answers 'does a simpler controller do the same?' directly and cheaply.
- Add an energetic-plausibility module to evaluation: a modelled metabolic cost (e.g. Umberger or Bhargava on logged muscle states), exo positive power per side and bilateral, and an energy ratio per seed.
- Plot a timing-robustness curve: assistance benefit vs phase offset of the replayed profile. It turns the Luo-Collins timing argument into a measured curve.
- Start a deviations register and a dated author-request log on day 1.
- Pre-register verdict thresholds: tolerance for 'reproduces', a TOST margin for 'same as the simpler controller', and triggers for 'inconclusive'.
- Run the zero-torque device arm in every evaluation, and publish unedited rollouts with GRF and activation overlays.
- When citing SMAT or Luo as support for each other, disclose the shared authorship (X. Zhou).

ADDED here beyond the KB audit: the PPO loss and muscle-net discrepancies vs MASS and the paper; the contents of N-SI Tables 1-3 and 6-7; the Source Data re-derivations (Fig. 4 doubling, 0% phase = torque peak, numerator reconstruction); Collins' factual slips; the response's mass and peak-torque inconsistencies.

### Learning Hip Exoskeleton Control Policy via Predictive Neuromusculoskeletal Simulation

- *Learning Hip Exoskeleton Control Policy via Predictive Neuromusculoskeletal Simulation* — Park, Song, Kang (Carnegie Mellon University, MetaMobility Lab; NIH R21 1R21EB037268-01), arXiv preprint v1 only, 4 Mar 2026 (cs.RO; cs.LG). The abs page has no comments and no journal reference (checked 2026-09-23). One web search found no venue.. arXiv:2603.04166 · code: Paper: no code statement. Project page repo IlseungPark/Learning-Hip-Exo @ b8ec147 (2026-03-09) is website source only; its Code link reads 'Code (Coming Soon)'. Nearest public code: github.com/IlseungPark/Walk-RL. Branch main = e5c55db10b114a54ec4a7a7cc5a28b9b4b6d724a (commit 2025-12-19); branch NMSM = a092f8d48b9f28668c4a23a94ad02e54f52b702d (2025-12-18); branch Neuron = main. Created 2025-11-26, pushed_at 2026-06-26, no licence, no releases or tags, 2 stars. Walk-RL has the H2190 SCONE/Hyfydy human with SAC and synergies only: no exoskeleton actuator, reward terms, TCN, distillation data, checkpoints or deployment code. Its content matches the companion synergy paper arXiv 2603.10474, not this paper (see reproduction_relevance.code_and_assets).. Read: pp1-11 (all), page images plus text layer; Figs 9 and 10 re-read at 4-5x zoom
- *Musculoskeletal Motion Imitation for Learning Personalized Exoskeleton Control Policy in Impaired Gait* — Choi*, Park* (equal contribution), Halilaj, Kang (Carnegie Mellon University; NIH R21 1R21EB037268-01), arXiv preprint v1 only, 10 Apr 2026 (cs.RO); abs comments '9 pages, 7 figures'; no journal reference (checked 2026-09-23). Title verified on arxiv.org/abs/2604.09431 before reading.. arXiv:2604.09431 · code: None. The paper has no code or data statement: text search for code/github/available found nothing. Project page ichoi02.github.io/Motion-Imitation-Exo (repo ichoi02/Motion-Imitation-Exo @ 5676160c, 2026-04-14) holds HTML, figures and videos and links only to arXiv.. Read: pp1-9 (all), page images plus text layer; Tables I-III and Figs 3-4 re-read at 4x zoom

**Problem.**
- *Park:* Hip-exoskeleton controllers that generalise across speeds and slopes usually need motion-capture data and biomechanical labels. Park et al. train a hip-exo policy entirely in a predictive neuromusculoskeletal simulation, without motion imitation. The teacher sees privileged simulator state and learns muscle excitations and hip torques jointly. It is distilled into a student that uses only a thigh gyroscope and runs on hardware. The paper evaluates (i) simulation fidelity against open human data, (ii) simulated effort reduction against a matched no-exo policy, and (iii) whether the torque waveform survives transfer to hardware (pp1-3).
- *Choi:* The question is whether a musculoskeletal imitation policy can be fine-tuned with RL into 'device-agnostic' hip or ankle exo policies. The policy tracks one subject's walking and running kinematics and kinetics. The resulting policies should cut simulated metabolic cost for able-bodied gait and give deficit-specific (asymmetric) assistance for simulated unilateral muscle weakness, with no human trials (pp1-2). 'Personalised' means specific to a simulated deficit. The policy is not fitted to an individual wearer's data. Simulation only; no hardware.

**Mechanism (exact).**
- *Park teacher:*
  - *Simulator:* [config] Hyfydy physics inside SCONE. The control policy runs at 40 Hz and forward dynamics are 'integrated at 200 Hz' (p3 Sec II-A). Walk-RL implements this as fixed_control_step_size = 0.005 in every .scone file plus a wrapper step_size of 0.025 s, so excitations are held for 5 substeps.
  - *Human model:* [config] H2190 full body with the upper limbs removed: 21 DoF and 90 Hill-type muscles with first-order activation dynamics. 80 muscles act on the legs (40 per leg; 6 DoF per leg: hip 3, knee 1, ankle 2) and 10 on the pelvis and torso (torso 3 DoF) (p3 Sec II-A-1, Fig 2 p2). Model mass 74.5 kg (p5 Eq 4). [derived-ours] The body masses in Walk-RL model/H2190v2k.hfd sum to 74.53 kg.
  - *Device model:* [config] Bilateral hip flexion-extension torque actuators, added in curriculum stage 2. Added masses: 0.5 kg per thigh, 2.0 kg pelvis and 1.3 kg torso, 4.3 kg in total. Segment inertia is scaled in proportion to the added mass. Result: 90 muscle actuators plus 2 exo actuators (p3 Sec II-A-1).
  - *Torque path:* [config] Raw command u_cmd goes through a causal first-order low-pass filter: u_k = u_{k-1} + alpha*(u_cmd,k - u_{k-1}) with alpha = dt/tau_LPF (Eqs 1-2, p3). The paper defines dt as 'the simulation time step' and sets tau_LPF = 0.1 s. Peak actuator torque magnitude is capped at 12 Nm (p3). Reward term r_sm also penalises the squared finite difference of successive left and right exo commands (p4). The order of clip and filter is not stated.
  - *Action space:* [config] 32 inputs: 10 synergy coefficients per leg (20), 10 direct torso and pelvis activations, and 2 hip torque commands. In the no-exo branch the torque commands are clamped to zero (p3-4 Sec II-A-3). Synergies come from human overground walking data (kinematics plus GRFs). OpenSim Moco (MocoInverse) on an OpenSim-converted H2190 estimates activations, and NMF on the right-leg activation matrix gives a fixed synergy weight matrix (p3). The synergy data came from Jooeun Ahn's group (Acknowledgment p9). [code, Walk-RL] Actions in [-1,1] map to [0,1] via 0.5*(a+1). Leg excitations = a_syn . (1.5*W), with W a 10x40 non-negative matrix (max entry 1.537 after the 1.5 factor). The same W serves both legs, and everything is clipped to [0,1]. Optional 'use_symmetry' swaps the left and right action and observation blocks when the last foot strike was left (contact force y > 1 N). The paper never mentions this swap.
  - *Observation:* [config] Muscle fibre length, velocity, force and excitation for all muscles; joint positions and velocities; head orientation and angular velocity; relative positions of both feet; GRF under each foot, all normalised. Also the current bilateral hip-exo torques (normalised by max torque), whole-body COM velocity and target speed (p4 Sec II-A-4). [derived-ours from Walk-RL] The public layout has 515 dims: 5x90 muscle arrays (fibre length, fibre velocity, force, excitation, activation), head quaternion 4, head angular velocity 3, feet relative to pelvis 6, DoF positions 21 (pelvis translations zeroed), DoF velocities 21, GRF/BW 6, COM velocity 3, target speed 1. SB3 VecNormalize(norm_obs=True, norm_reward=False).
  - *Reward:* [config] Eq 3 (p4): r = w_vel*r_vel + w_eff*r_eff + w_rom*r_rom + w_sm*r_sm + w_fall*r_fall + w_knee*r_knee + w_front*r_front. The terms are defined in the paper (p4) but no weight is reported. (a) r_vel: product of Gaussians of forward and vertical COM velocity, head orientation and head angular velocity, with a flat band around target speed. (b) r_eff: sum of squared muscle activations. (c) r_rom: knee beyond 0 deg and lumbar extension outside [-30, 2.5] deg. (d) r_knee: stance knee load above 3 body weights. (e) r_front: squared hip adduction angles on both sides. (f) r_sm: squared difference of successive exo torque commands. (g) r_fall: one-time large penalty when COM height falls below a relative threshold. [code, Walk-RL; no exo, so a precursor only] Only four terms exist, with weights gaussian_vel 10, effort -0.1, rom -25, fall -250. r_vel = r_vx * r_head_angvel * r_vz, each term exp(-0.06*x^2/sigma^2). sigma_vx = 0.07 m/s measured from the edge of a +/-0.05 m/s flat band; sigma_vz = 0.1; head angular velocity sigma = (0.6, 0.65, 1.4) rad/s. Head orientation is computed but left out of the product. rom = knee_r + knee_l violations above 0 rad + lumbar violation outside [-20, +10] deg. fall = 1 if COM y < 3 (absolute; terrain sits at y about 5) or COM height above the lower calcaneus < 0.5 m. The knee-load, frontal and smoothness terms are absent.
  - *Curriculum and randomisation:* [config] Each episode draws one slope from 11 values (-5 to +5 deg in 1-deg steps), via a categorical distribution over per-slope difficulty scores. Scores start uniform; a fall raises the score, a success lowers it, with a floor. Target speed follows a cyclic list 0.7 -> 1.5 -> 0.7 m/s across episodes (ref [35], MetaMobilityLabCMU/speed-adaptive-agent) (p4 Sec II-A-5). [code, Walk-RL] Different: 10,000 pre-generated terrains of 50 tiles, each 1 m long with an integer slope uniform in -6..+6 deg per tile; a speed list of 20 entries 0.7..1.6..0.7 m/s, advanced at every reset; episode cap 1000 steps (25 s at 40 Hz).
  - *Training:* [config] Table I (p2): SB3 SAC, MlpPolicy, actor and critic [512, 512, 256], 30 parallel envs. Stage 1: steps 0-5e7, exo zero-clamped, LR linear 1e-3 -> 0. Stage 2: steps 5e7-1e8 run as two parallel branches: (i) exo policy-controlled and (ii) exo zero-clamped for the same number of steps. The LR schedule is reset to linear 5e-4 -> 0 for both. gamma 0.99, replay 3e6, batch 512, target tau 0.005, ent_coef 'auto_0.1' (SB3 syntax: auto-tuned from 0.1). [derived-ours] Total env steps = 5e7 + 2*5e7 = 1.5e8. [code, Walk-RL main] ReLU; gSDE on (use_sde=True, sde_sample_freq=1); learning_starts 1e4; train_freq 4; gradient_steps 4; target_update_interval 1; ent_coef 'auto'; 20 SubprocVecEnv envs; 75M steps; batch 256; tau 0.02; LR 1e-3 linear. [code, NMSM branch] Batch 512, tau 0.01, LR 5e-4, 6 synergies ('251204_NMSM_Inv_6'), symmetry off. Neither branch matches Table I.
- *Park student:*
  - *Sensor selection:* [measured-hw vs sim] Simulated femur angular velocity in the femur frame is compared with a thigh-bar IMU while 'a participant' walked for 1 min at 1.2 m/s on level ground. Axes are defined in upright standing: x anteroposterior, y vertical, z mediolateral. z: r 0.55, RMSE 96.0 deg/s. x: r -0.19, RMSE 41.2 deg/s. y: r -0.25, RMSE 60.9 deg/s. z was chosen on correlation alone, although it had the largest RMSE (p4 Sec II-B, Fig 3).
  - *Data:* [config] Teacher rollouts on level ground, +5 deg and -5 deg over a range of target speeds, with constant-speed trials and smoothly varying-speed trials. 'During rollouts, the controller operated at 100 Hz' (p4). Each sample pairs the teacher's instantaneous right-hip torque command with a 0.95 s (95-sample) history of right femur mediolateral angular velocity (p4-5). Dataset size, split unit and normalisation are not reported.
  - *Model and training:* [config] Temporal convolutional network (TCN), single channel in, right-hip torque out. MSE loss, 5 epochs, with a held-out validation set. Training loss 7.45e-2 -> 4.99e-2 and validation loss 8.23e-2 -> 6.54e-2, in normalised torque units [-1,1] (p5). The TCN architecture, optimiser and batch size are not reported.
  - *Open loop check:* [measured-sim] Fig 4 (p5) shows one level-ground trial at 0.7/1.1/1.5 m/s (text: 4 s each; caption and axis: 5 s each). The student tracks the teacher with R^2 = 0.93, and nothing is output for the first 0.95 s while the history fills. No closed-loop student-in-simulation result is reported.
  - *Inconsistency:* Sec II intro (p3) says the student receives 'bilateral femur mediolateral angular velocity'. Sec II-B (p4-5) trains a right-only, unilateral mapping. The left-hip mapping (mirroring, sign) is not described.
- *Park deployment:*
  - *Hardware:* [config] Robotic hip exo, 4.5 kg, joint torque up to 18 Nm in the sagittal plane. Two quasi-direct-drive AK80-9 motors (CubeMars), ICM20948 IMUs on the distal thigh bars, 22.2 V 3300 mAh LiPo, Jetson Orin Nano. The student is converted to TensorRT and runs at 100 Hz, with separate I/O and inference processes (IMUs on I2C, actuators on CAN; Fig 5b) (p5 Sec II-C).
  - *Command chain:* [config] u_scaled = u_cmd * m_subject/74.5 (Eq 4). tau_LPF = 0.15 s on hardware vs 0.1 s in simulation, 'to better attenuate measurement noise and inter-subject gait variability'. Rate limiter u'_t = clip(u_t, u_{t-1} - c, u_{t-1} + c), c = 0.5 (p5). Units of c and the order of scaling, clip, rate limit and filter are not stated. If c is in Nm per 10 ms step it caps slew at 50 Nm/s; if it is in normalised units it is essentially non-binding [derived-ours].
- *Choi base imitation:*
  - *Simulator:* [config] Hyfydy with the SCONE Python API: physics 200 Hz, policy 25 Hz, contacts with nonlinear damping and dynamic plus viscous friction (p2 Sec II-A-1). No MyoSuite or MuJoCo model is used anywhere.
  - *Human model:* [config] Hyfydy 3D H2190: 21 DoF, 90 muscles, with tendon elasticity, pennation and fibre damping (p2 Sec II-A-2, ref [29] Schumacher et al. 2025). This is the same body as Park.
  - *Reference data:* [config] One subject from the Scherpereel et al. 2023 Sci Data dataset (12 participants, 31 activities): a treadmill clip of about 2 minutes covering five speeds from 0.6 to 2.2 m/s. Fig 2 shows walking at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s and running at 2.0/2.2 m/s. Belt velocity, estimated from toe markers during stance, is added to convert to overground. OpenSim IK/ID give reference joint angles and moments plus head, pelvis and feet positions. The clip is duplicated and mirrored across the sagittal plane (p3 Sec II-B). Which subject is not stated.
  - *Rl formulation:* [config] DeepMimic-style (ref [31]). A = [-1,1]^96: 90 muscle excitations mapped linearly to [0,1], plus 6 normalised sagittal torques at bilateral hip, knee and ankle. The torques are clamped to 0 except during exo fine-tuning. Excitation -> activation uses first-order forward Euler inside Hyfydy (p3 Sec II-C-1). The observation is in R^839: S_c (fibre length, force, activation; GRFs; joint angles; root and feet linear velocities) plus S_f = (Theta_{t+k} - Theta_t) for k = 1..5. The text says 'next four reference frames', inconsistent with k = 1..5 (p3 Sec II-C-2).
  - *Reward:* [config] Eq 1 (p3): r = w_pos r_pos + w_vel r_vel + w_root r_root + w_ee r_ee + w_torq r_torq - w_eff r_eff - w_smt r_smt - w_exo r_exo. The tracking terms (pos, vel, root, ee, torq) are each exp(k * squared error): pos over all 21 DoF including root orientation; vel on joint velocities; root on the pelvis position; ee on feet and head positions; torq against inverse-dynamics joint torques (Eqs 2-6). r_eff = Edot = hdot_AM + hdot_SL + wdot_CE is a metabolic probe after Umberger 2003 and Uchida 2016, with fast-twitch fractions from refs [34-36] and other parameters at Hyfydy defaults (Eq 7). r_smt = (1/N)*sum_m (e_t - e_{t-1})^2 on excitations (Eq 8). r_exo = 0.5*sum_{L,R} |tau_exo|/tau_max (Eq 9). Table II (p4), base / fine-tune: k_pos -2.0/-0.4, k_vel -0.05/-0.01, k_root -500/-500, k_ee -80/-16, k_torq -2.0/-0.4. Weights are identical in both columns: w_pos 0.25, w_vel 0.1, w_root 0.15, w_ee 0.25, w_torq 0.25, w_smo -1.0. Effort weight w_eff -3e-5 (base) vs -3e-4 (fine-tune); w_exo absent (base) vs -0.2 (fine-tune). The table lists penalty weights as negative while Eq 1 subtracts them, a sign ambiguity; the intended reading is surely a penalty.
  - *Training:* [config] Table III (p4): SB3 SAC, actor and twin-Q [512, 512, 256], batch 256, LR linear decay from 3e-4, soft update 0.02, entropy auto, gamma 0.95, train freq 4, gradient steps 4, target update interval 1. Base policy 600M steps; each fine-tune 150M. 96 parallel envs on an RTX 5090 + Intel (Core) Ultra 9 285K workstation, about 48 h for the base policy (p4 Sec II-D-1). Episodes use reference-state initialisation at a uniformly sampled clip time, with the vertical position adjusted so net GRF equals body weight. Episodes are truncated at 250 steps (10 s) and terminate early when the root is more than 0.4 m from the reference root (p4-5).
- *Choi exo finetune:* [config] Each device condition fine-tunes the base policy for another 150M steps, with the exo action space enabled; hip and ankle policies are trained separately. The four tracking gains are loosened 5x and w_eff is raised 10x (Table II). Device mass and inertia are added: hip exo 2.9 kg (pelvis 1.5, 0.7 per thigh); ankle exo 3.9 kg (pelvis 1.5, 1.0 per shank, 0.2 per foot). Totals come from OpenExo (ref [30]), split 'using a heuristic approach', with inertia scaled proportionally (Table I p3; p2 and p5). Output in [-1,1] is mapped to [-tau_max, tau_max]. The rate of change is clipped at tau_max per step, then a first-order LPF is applied with cutoff 1 Hz for the hip (tau = 0.159 s) or 2 Hz for the ankle (tau = 0.080 s) (p5 Sec II-D-2). tau_max is never reported. The knee motor exists in the action space, but no knee policy is reported.
- *Choi impaired:* [config] Unilateral plantarflexor weakness or hip-flexor weakness: the maximum excitation of the affected group is set to 5% of baseline. The policy is fine-tuned with the same reward changes as the exo fine-tune. Weakness plus the exo action space is then trained jointly, still tracking the healthy reference (p5 Sec II-D-3). The muscle lists per group are not given. Stated hypothesis: imitating healthy gait with a weakened model plus exo yields compensating torque profiles (p5).

**Protocol.**
- *Park sim fidelity:* [measured-sim] The stage-2 no-exo policy (Fig 6 caption, p6) is compared with Reznick et al. 2021 open data on sagittal hip, knee and ankle angles and moments, at 3 slopes and 3 speeds (0.8, 1.0, 1.2 m/s) (p6-7 Sec III-A). The exo-assisted gait is never validated against human data.
- *Park sim effect:* [measured-sim] Exo branch vs no-exo branch at 3 slopes x 9 speeds (0.7-1.5 m/s in 0.1 steps, per the Fig 9 axis). Metrics: mean activation over all muscles in a 15 s segment, and mean positive joint power. The Fig 9 caption says hip, knee and ankle bilateral, sagittal; the text says 'hip-knee'. Effects are reported as % change 'related to no-exoskeleton', averaged over speed (p7 Sec III-C). Seeds, episodes per condition and the definition of joint power (biological vs total) are not reported.
- *Park profile timing:* [measured-sim] The learned level-ground profile at 1.2 m/s is compared on peak timing only with a spline controller at 1.25 m/s (ref [37]) and a delayed bio-torque profile (ref [15]; mean of 20 cycles x 20 participants with a 125 ms delay) (p7 Sec III-B, Fig 7). No performance comparison is made.
- *Park human:*
  - *Design:* [measured-hw] N=5 able-bodied (4 M, 1 F; 71.62 +/- 8.89 kg), CMU IRB STUDY2024_00000506 (p5 Sec II-D). Five minutes of acclimation at 1.2 m/s with the exo powered, on an instrumented split-belt treadmill (Bertec FIT5). Slopes 0, +5 and -5 deg; within each, speed rises 0.7 -> 1.5 m/s in 0.2 steps at 30 s per speed. The paper says 3 min 30 s per condition, but 5 x 30 s = 2 min 30 s. GRFs are used only for gait events. Onboard exo 'hip-torque commands were logged'. Five consecutive cycles per speed-slope condition are normalised to 101 points (p6). r and RMSE against the simulated profile are computed per speed-slope condition (p7 Sec III-D).
  - *Shows:* [measured-hw] With the IMU-only TCN running on a Jetson at 100 Hz, right-hip torque waveforms normalised to the gait cycle keep the simulated shape. r = 0.82 +/- 0.19 overall; 0.98 uphill, 0.83 level, 0.66 downhill. RMSE is about 0.02-0.04 Nm/kg against peaks of about 0.1 Nm/kg (Fig 10, p8).
  - *Does not show:* Delivered torque: the protocol logs commands, even though Results says 'measured', and no torque sensor or calibration is reported. Any effect on the wearer: no metabolic, EMG, kinematics, comfort or preference data, and no zero-torque or no-device condition. Per-participant results and statistics. The left hip. Overground, transient or non-cyclic tasks. Latency. Robustness to sensor placement. Retention of the simulated effort savings. The authors' own project page says the controller 'fails to provide effective assistance during ramp descent' (IlseungPark/Learning-Hip-Exo index.html @ b8ec147).
- *Choi eval:* [measured-sim] (1) Tracking against the same single-subject reference used in training: angles, moments and GRF averaged over 10 cycles; moments low-pass filtered (4th-order zero-lag Butterworth, 4 Hz) (Fig 2 caption p5). This is in-sample fit, not held-out prediction. (2) Metabolic-probe cost for no-exo, hip exo and ankle exo at 5 speeds (Fig 4a), and averages over the walking speeds (0.6/1.2/1.8) and running speeds (2.0/2.2) (Fig 4b). (3) Assistance profiles compared visually with experimentally optimised splines (refs [13], [39], [40]) (Fig 3). (4) Impaired models at 1.2 m/s: kinematics, asymmetry of assistance, metabolic cost and bilateral symmetry RMSE (Figs 5-7). How the no-exo baseline was trained, and whether it carries device mass, is not stated. [derived-ours] Fig 4b draws one shared No-Exo bar for both devices, although the devices differ in mass (2.9 vs 3.9 kg), so the baseline most likely carries no device mass.

**Headline numbers**
- *Paper:* Park
  - *Tag:* measured-sim
  - *What:* Mean muscle activation, exo vs matched no-exo, averaged over speed
  - *Value:* -1.1% level, -3.4% ramp ascent (reductions); ramp descent 'changing by -2.2%'
  - *Where:* p7 Sec III-C; Fig 9 p7; abstract p1 ('up to 3.4%')
  - *Caveat:* [derived-ours] In Fig 9, positive values mean reduction (level ground averages about +1 and the text calls it a 1.1% reduction). The ramp-descent -2.2% is therefore an increase in activation. Activation rose with assistance at 7/9 descent speeds, 3/9 level and 2/9 ascent speeds, mostly the slow ones (figure reading, approximate). The % basis (relative?) is unspecified; one policy per branch; no variance reported.

  - *Tag:* measured-sim

  - *Value:* -6.3% level, -7.0% ramp ascent, -4.8% ramp descent
  - *Where:* p7 Sec III-C; Fig 9
  - *Caveat:* The text says 'hip-knee' power; the Fig 9 caption says hip, knee and ankle. Whether this is biological or total joint power is not defined.
