**Ablations that matter**
- Park reports no ablations. There is no synergy vs direct-muscle exo teacher, filter tau or torque-cap sweep, reward-term ablation, curriculum ablation, seed replication, student window, architecture or sensor ablation beyond the axis correlation, or sensor-noise randomisation. The matched no-exo branch (Table I; p3) is a control, not an ablation. It is the design element most worth copying.
- Park's Fig 7 compares spline and delayed bio-torque references on peak timing only. The ablation that matters, the effort benefit of the same references run through the same simulated device, is missing (p7 Sec III-B).
- [code evidence] Walk-RL has 'ind' (all 90 muscles) vs 'syn' (synergy) configs and 10-seed scripts (commit f9042a9, seeds 10-19). That comparison belongs to the companion paper arXiv 2603.10474, not to the exo paper; no exo-condition synergy ablation exists.
- Choi reports no ablations: no reward-only control, seeds, tau_max, filter or device-mass variation. The most important missing control is a no-exo fine-tune with the identical reward change (5x looser tracking, 10x effort weight), the same 150M steps and the same device mass. Without it, part of the 4.6-13.8% probe reduction may come from the reward change rather than the torque (see rival_explanations).
- Choi's hip vs ankle and able-bodied vs impaired comparisons are condition comparisons, not ablations. Knee motors exist in the action space, but no knee policy is reported.
- For our programme, these ablations are cheap and decisive. (1) Paired seeds per arm (exo vs matched zero-torque). (2) A filter-tau sweep, to test whether the 'learned' lag follows the filter. (3) A closed-loop student vs teacher run in simulation. (4) Command vs delivered torque on the bench. (5) A reward-change-only fine-tune whenever the reward changes between stages.

**Limitations the authors admit**
- Park p9: 'the human evaluation was not intended as a rigorous efficacy study'.
- Park p9: the controller targets cyclic locomotion and has not been validated for non-cyclic or unstructured behaviour.
- Park p9: a single-axis thigh gyro may be insufficient for broader contexts and sensitive to sensor-placement variability.
- Park p9: 'we did not explicitly optimize domain randomization and adaptation strategies'. Future work: match sensor noise and mounting dynamics, add richer sensing, use sim-to-real robustness objectives during distillation.
- Park p8: minimal benefit in simulation on ramp descent (explained by negative-work biomechanics). Project page (b8ec147): the controller 'fails to provide effective assistance during ramp descent, suggesting that a sim-to-real gap still remains'.
- Choi p7: policies 'have not yet been deployed or validated on physical hardware'; distillation (ref [20] = Park) is proposed.
- Choi p7-8: the impaired models may not capture the full complexity of real pathological gait; patient experiments are required.
- Choi p7: peak torques applied to the impaired-gait models 'may exceed the current mechanical capabilities of lightweight portable devices'.
- Choi p8: the study covers only level-ground walking and running.

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- Park: a single training run per branch, apparently (no seeds or variance). The 1.1-3.4% activation effects compare two separately trained policies, so seed-to-seed variance is confounded with the exo effect.
- Park: at slow speeds, assistance increased activation on all three slopes, and ramp descent shows a net increase (Fig 9, sign convention derived by us). The text calls descent a 'modest benefit'.
- Park: the student is evaluated only open-loop against teacher rollouts. The closed-loop effect of the student on simulated effort is never reported, and hardware measures no effort. So there is no evidence that the savings survive distillation or transfer.
- Park: the hardware comparison uses logged commands (p6), although Results calls them 'measured'. r ignores amplitude. Nm/kg normalisation cancels the mass scaling. The hardware filter (0.15 s) and rate limiter differ from simulation, so 'without additional hardware tuning' (abstract) holds only narrowly.
- Park: the validation loss (6.54e-2 normalised) implies aggregate distillation error far larger than the showcased R^2 0.93 (derived). Dataset size and split unit are unknown, so window leakage cannot be ruled out.
- Park: the gyro axis was chosen on one participant and 1 minute of walking. r = 0.55 is moderate, and the chosen axis has the largest RMSE (96 deg/s).
- Park: the reported 103-166 ms lag is interpreted as learned, phase-dependent timing (p9). A first-order LPF with tau = 0.1 s produces about 90 ms of lag at walking stride frequency by itself (derived), and the paper does not discuss this.
- Park: effort is scored as mean activation, and the reward directly penalises summed squared activation, so the metric is not independent. No metabolic model is used.
- Park: the paper does not say whether the device mass is present in the no-exo branch, or whether the replay buffer carries over between stages.
- Park: text vs figure inconsistencies. 4 s vs 5 s per speed (Fig 4); '3 min 30 s' vs 5 x 30 s (p6); 'hip-knee' vs hip-knee-ankle power (p7 vs Fig 9); bilateral vs unilateral student input (p3 vs p4-5); 4.3 kg simulated vs 4.5 kg hardware mass.
- Choi: the no-exo baseline's training (base policy or matched fine-tune) and its device mass are unspecified (a shared bar in Fig 4b suggests no device mass). The reward change (10x effort, 5x looser tracking) and 150M extra steps are therefore confounded with assistance.
- Choi: tau_max is never reported. Fig 3 hip torques reach about 0.2 Nm/kg in running (about 15 Nm at 74.5 kg; derived by eye), above Park's 12 Nm simulation cap. The 1 Hz hip LPF sits below the running stride frequency.
- Choi: 'align with state-of-the-art profiles' (abstract) is only partly true. Running hip profiles carry about +0.2 Nm/kg of flexion torque where the extension-only reference is zero. Ankle running profiles carry dorsiflexion-direction torque absent from the plantarflexion-only reference (Fig 3 p6, visual reading).
- Choi: tracking and GRF accuracy are in-sample on the single training clip. GRF agreement is largely implied by tracking ID moments and kinematics, though it is presented as emergent.
- Choi: 'personalised' rests on one reference subject. Improved symmetry and affected-side-biased assistance follow from tracking a mirrored healthy reference, so they are expected rather than emergent.
- Choi: the metabolic probe is both the reward term and the evaluation metric. No seeds or variance are reported. Ref [40] (Kim et al. 2019, an experimental exosuit study) is cited as 'musculoskeletal simulation' (p6). 'Next four reference frames' conflicts with k = 1..5 (p3). Table II weight signs conflict with the minus signs in Eq 1.
- Both: 'running' reaches only 2.2 m/s (Choi) and walking only 1.5 m/s (Park). The synergy prior (Park) comes from walking data. Neither paper supports sprint-speed claims.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Park reward: every weight w_(.), the Gaussian widths and flat-band width, the fall threshold, and the scaling of the knee-load and frontal terms. (Walk-RL gives precursor values for vel/effort/rom/fall only.)
- Park curriculum: the difficulty-score increments, floor and sampling temperature; the speed-list granularity; episode length and termination; the initial state.
- Park SAC: learning_starts, train_freq/gradient_steps, gSDE (used in Walk-RL), activation function, seeds, training hardware and wall-clock, and whether the replay buffer is reused at the stage switch.
- Park filter: whether dt in alpha = dt/tau is 5 ms (dynamics) or 25 ms (control); the order of clip and filter; whether the observed 'current exo torque' is raw or filtered.
- Park synergies: source subjects, number of strides, normalisation, the NMF rank-selection criterion, and whether the published W equals Walk-RL's 1.5x10x40 matrix.
- Park distillation: how a 40 Hz teacher was run or resampled at 100 Hz; dataset size; the split unit (trial vs window); input normalisation; TCN channels, kernel, dilations and receptive field; optimiser, LR and batch; the left-side policy (mirroring, sign conventions).
- Park deployment: units of c; order of mass scaling, rate limit, LPF and clip; the low-level motor torque control (current loop, torque constant); IMU sampling and filtering; end-to-end latency; start-up behaviour during the 0.95 s window.
- Park evaluation: the % basis (relative to no-exo?); the joint-power definition (biological vs total); episodes per condition; whether Fig 10's simulated curves are teacher or student-in-sim; what the +/- in r and RMSE is taken across; per-participant results.
- Choi: tau_max per joint; the breakdown of the 839-dim observation; reference frame rate and interpolation; which of the 12 subjects; the muscles in each weakened group; SAC buffer size and learning_starts; the no-exo baseline definition and its device mass; evaluation cycles, episodes and seeds; gross vs net metabolic (basal term); wall-clock for fine-tunes; whether knee motors were ever enabled.

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *A matched simple controllers and actuator model:*
  - Copy Park's control design exactly: fork the exo arm and a zero-torque arm from the same stage-1 checkpoint, with identical reward, curriculum, LR reset, step budget and device mass (Table I; p3). Unlike Park, run at least 3 paired seeds per arm. Every simple controller (spline, delayed biological moment, delayed output feedback) should be its own arm under the same rules, with the human policy co-adapting for the same steps.
  - Park compared the spline and delayed bio-torque references on timing only (Fig 7). Comparing them on the simulated objective through the same actuator model is the missing experiment, and exactly what 'matched simple controllers' should deliver. Their timing anchors: extension peak 9-17% GC, flexion peak 61-71% GC, bio-torque delay 125 ms (p7).
  - Actuator realism in these papers is minimal. Park: an ideal torque source, 12 Nm clip, first-order LPF tau = 0.1 s and a smoothness penalty; hardware uses tau = 0.15 s and a rate limiter with c = 0.5. Choi: ideal motors, rate clip tau_max per step, and LPF cutoffs of 1 Hz (hip) and 2 Hz (ankle). For a cable-driven hip exosuit, add tension-only channels, pretension or slack, series (textile) compliance, the motor torque-speed and power envelope through the pulley, and a pure transport delay, all calibrated on the lab bench. Neither paper supplies these.
  - [derived-ours] Filters copied from walking cripple sprint assistance. For a first-order LPF at an assumed ~2.2 Hz sprint stride frequency: tau = 0.1 s gives gain 0.59 and 68 ms lag (15% of the stride); tau = 0.15 s gives gain 0.43 and 81 ms (18%); Choi's 1 Hz hip cutoff gives gain 0.41 and 83 ms (18%). At a ~0.9 Hz walking stride, tau = 0.1 s gives gain 0.87 and 91 ms lag. Model the delay and bandwidth explicitly and sweep them. Do not inherit these constants.
  - Torque scale: Park's 12 Nm cap is 0.161 Nm/kg at 74.5 kg. Scale by model mass (MyoAssist human about 78.09 kg per the myoassist digest), or set it from the exosuit motor spec. Choi's running hip torques of about 0.2 Nm/kg (derived by eye) exceed that cap.
- *B teacher student sprint transfer:*
  - Park's template is: privileged SAC teacher -> teacher rollouts at the deploy rate (100 Hz) -> 95-sample history of one thigh mediolateral gyro -> TCN -> unilateral torque -> mass scale -> rate limit -> LPF -> motor. This is the only published end-to-end pipeline in these papers, and it validates waveform shape only.
  - Sprint-specific problems. (1) The 0.95 s history yields no output for the first 0.95 s (Fig 4 caption). That is fatal for block starts, where the largest measured sprint-exosuit benefit sits (0-100 m; see sprint_evidence_and_baselines digest). Use a shorter window, a start-phase prior or a padded history. (2) The student has no speed or phase input, so it must infer acceleration vs max-velocity phase from gyro history alone. Test this closed-loop in simulation. (3) The gyro agreement (r 0.55 at 1.2 m/s walking) must be re-measured at sprint speeds, where thigh angular velocities and soft-tissue artefacts are far larger. (4) Evaluate the student closed-loop in simulation against the teacher on the actual objective (200 m time), not just R^2. Use DAgger if covariate shift appears. (5) Split by whole trials, phases and seeds to prevent window leakage.
  - SMAT's exo actor already observes only hip angles and velocities plus past commands (smat_original_and_extended digest, recipe step 5), so a SMAT-style exo policy may deploy without distillation where hip encoders exist. A cable exosuit with thigh IMUs measures thigh, not hip, angle unless a pelvis IMU is added. Decide the sensor set before training the sprint teacher, and log simulated sensor signals at the IMU site in every rollout.
  - Do not carry Park's walking synergies (NMF of walking MocoInverse activations, scaled 1.5x in Walk-RL) into a sprint human. They constrain the action space to walking coordination.
  - Choi's route to a running human is imitation: a DeepMimic reward, then a fine-tune with loosened tracking and a stronger effort term. It reaches only 2.2 m/s. If used for a sprinter, give the matched no-exo fine-tune the identical reward change.
- *C throughput planning:*
  - Choi's 600M steps in about 48 h used 96 Hyfydy CPU envs on a 24-core desktop CPU, so it anchors a CPU physics stack, not our GPU. It does not transfer to MuJoCo MyoAssist on a 64-core EPYC. See throughput_notes for the arithmetic.
  - Park's budget is 1.5e8 env steps per full two-branch pipeline (derived), with no wall-clock reported. It is small next to SMAT's 547M, so Park-style matched-arm experiments are the cheap ones to multiply over seeds.
  - Hyfydy reference runs (Walk-RL, Choi-style) need a licence, and the EUR 995/yr personal non-commercial tier is capped at 24 CPU cores (hyfydy.com/purchase, fetched 2026-09-23). Using the full server costs EUR 2,000/yr (personal HPC) or EUR 4,000/yr (academic lab).
- *Code and assets:*
  - Walk-RL (main e5c55db, 2025-12-19): SCONE/Hyfydy gym wrappers (H2190UnevenVelocityWrapper for training on random terrain, H2190RampVelocityWrapper for fixed-slope evaluation), SB3 SAC train.py, rollout.py, TensorBoard analysis scripts, and a 10x40 synergy matrix (synergy/synergy.py, x1.5). Also H2190v2k .hfd variants (plain/S2/uphill/downhill), .scone scenarios, a terrain generator (terrains themselves absent), and vendored sconegym 0.2.0 (pyproject says MIT, LICENSE file is Apache-2.0). No exo actuator, TCN, datasets, checkpoints or README.
  - Walk-RL NMSM branch (a092f8d, 2025-12-18): synergy/synergy_results_arr.py with the 10x40 matrix and a '251204_NMSM_Inv_6' 6-synergy matrix, described as NMF of MocoInverse activations via an unreleased sto2synergy_mat.py. Also a hangman test script and an install-order note: gym 0.13 via sconegym requirements, stable-baselines3[extra]==2.6.0, torch 2.7.0 cu128, numpy 1.26.*, scikit-learn 1.6.1, shimmy 2.0.0, pyyaml.
  - The model/H2190_S2.scone and H2190v2k_S2.hfd files are upstream sconegym variants (identical copies ship in sconegym-main/sconegym/data-v1). They set enable_dof_actuators = 0, add a lumbar joint motor (stiffness 10, damping 10) and muscle_force_multiplier = 2. 'S2' is not Park's curriculum stage 2.
  - [argued-ours] Walk-RL matches the companion paper arXiv 2603.10474 (Park, E. Choi, J. Ahn, J. Ahn; v2 2026-05-28): synergy vs unconstrained control, 0.7-1.8 m/s, +/-6 deg grades, uneven terrain. That paper's project page is named 'WebHomepage__Walk-RL' and says 'Code (Coming Soon)', 'Dataset (Coming Soon)'. So Walk-RL is the precursor human controller, not a partial exo release.
  - Related lab repos (MetaMobilityLabCMU): speed-adaptive-agent (MIT; Park ref [35], cyclic speed curriculum source) and MeMo-Hyfydy-Workshop (2026-03, H0918 sconegym workshop). Neither contains exo or TCN code.
  - Choi: nothing public beyond the project page.

**Recipe on our server.**
- 0. Scope: this is an independent reconstruction in MuJoCo/MyoAssist. H2190/Hyfydy numbers (90 muscles, compliant tendons, 200 Hz) are not numeric targets; the targets are directions, relative effects and the transfer procedure. Label every inferred choice.
- 1. Environment: MyoAssist myolegs26 + Hippo_L1 composed per the myoassist_framework digest (planar root; actuators: 26 muscles, then Exo_R, Exo_L). Control at 40 Hz as the Park variant, next to SMAT's 50 Hz. Physics at 1000 or 1200 Hz; record frame_skip.
- 2. Device mass: add the measured exosuit mass split (Park's hip-exo split was thigh 0.5/0.5, pelvis 2.0, torso 1.3 kg; Choi's was pelvis 1.5, thigh 0.7/0.7 kg) with inertia scaled proportionally. Add it to every arm, including the zero-torque arm.
- 3. One actuator module shared by every controller. Commanded torque at control rate -> optional pure delay d -> clip to +/-T_max (Park: 12 Nm * m_model/74.5) -> slew limit c per 10 ms (Park hardware c = 0.5, read literally as Nm per step, i.e. 50 Nm/s; log the choice) -> first-order LPF with alpha = dt/tau, or exact 1 - exp(-dt/tau), at physics dt (tau 0.1 s sim-faithful, 0.15 s hardware-faithful; sweep 0-0.15 s) -> hip joint torque. Exosuit variant: split into unidirectional flexion and extension channels (tension >= 0), add pretension/slack, series stiffness and a motor torque-speed envelope from the lab bench. Choi variant: clip [-1,1] -> +/-tau_max, rate clip tau_max per step, 1 Hz hip LPF.
- 4. Park teacher (single joint policy, one variant beside SMAT's two agents): SB3 SAC MlpPolicy [512,512,256] for actor and critic. gamma 0.99, buffer 3e6 (see RAM note), batch 512, tau 0.005, ent_coef 'auto_0.1', LR linear 1e-3->0 in stage 1, reset to 5e-4->0 in stage 2. Park used 30 envs; optional gSDE as in Walk-RL. Actions: 26 excitations ([-1,1] -> 0.5*(a+1)) plus 2 normalised hip torques. Synergies are optional at 13 muscles per leg; treat NMF synergies as a variant. Observation: the Walk-RL layout adapted to MyoAssist (fibre length, velocity, force, excitation and activation; head orientation and angular velocity; feet relative to pelvis; joint positions with pelvis translation zeroed; joint velocities; GRF/BW; COM velocity; target speed) plus normalised current exo torques. VecNormalize on observations only.
- 5. Park reward: start from the Walk-RL weights: 10*r_vel - 0.1*sum(a^2) - 25*rom - 250*fall. r_vel = exp(-0.06*(v_x - band edge)^2/0.07^2) with a +/-0.05 m/s flat band, times exp(-0.06*v_z^2/0.1^2), times head-angular-velocity Gaussians with sigma (0.6, 0.65, 1.4) rad/s. Add w_sm*sum((u_t - u_{t-1})^2) and w_knee*max(0, F_knee - 3 BW). Both weights are unknown, so sweep them over at least 3 log-spaced values chosen before the sweep. The frontal term does not apply in a planar model. rom: knee > 0 plus lumbar outside [-30, 2.5] deg (paper) or [-20, 10] deg (code); record which.
- 6. Park curriculum: stage 1 with the exo clamped (50M steps in Park). Stage 2 forks two runs from the same checkpoint (exo vs zero-torque; 50M each in Park) with the same seeds and the same LR reset. Slopes -5..+5 deg (11 values) via difficulty scores: +delta on fall, -delta on success, floor s_min, p proportional to score; delta and s_min are unspecified, so choose and record them. Speeds follow the cyclic list 0.7->1.5->0.7 in 0.1 steps, advanced per episode. Implement slope by tilting the floor or rotating gravity in the planar model. Run at least 3 paired seeds per arm; report paired differences with CIs.
- 7. Park metrics, defined before running: mean activation over all muscles in a 15 s steady segment, at 9 speeds x 3 slopes. Mean positive biological joint power = positive part of (net moment - exo torque) x joint velocity, averaged over bilateral hip, knee and ankle. % change = 100*(noexo - exo)/noexo, with sign stated. Also sagittal angle and moment RMSE and r against Reznick et al. 2021 at 0.8/1.0/1.2 m/s.
- 8. Matched simple controllers, each through the step-3 module with the human co-adapted for equal steps and equal seeds: (i) zero-torque plus mass; (ii) gait-phase spline with extension peak at 9-17% GC and flexion peak at 61-71% GC, amplitude matched to the RL controller's RMS torque; (iii) delayed biological hip moment, k*M_hip(t - 125 ms), clipped to T_max; (iv) delayed output feedback on thigh angles as a sensor-realistic baseline. Tune (ii)-(iv) with a fixed, pre-declared budget (e.g. CMA-ES) on tuning seeds, then evaluate on fresh seeds.
- 9. Student (Park): roll out the teacher at 100 Hz (zero-order-hold the 40/50 Hz command; log raw and filtered torque). Log femur mediolateral angular velocity in the femur frame at the IMU site, plus a noise, bias and misalignment model. Use 95-sample windows with target = raw right-hip command / T_max. Split by whole trials (speed x slope x seed blocks). TCN, our default (not Park's): causal dilated Conv1d, kernel 3, dilations 1-32, 32-64 channels, Adam 1e-3, batch 512, 5-20 epochs with early stopping. Mirror for the left side after checking signs. Report open-loop R^2 and RMSE per condition and closed-loop retention of the teacher's effect with the student driving the device. Include the 0.95 s start-up gap.
- 10. Choi-style imitation (optional route to running or sprinting humans): MyoAssist imitation env with the DeepMimic terms and Choi Table II gains (base k: pos -2.0, vel -0.05, root -500, ee -80, torq -2.0; w: 0.25/0.1/0.15/0.25/0.25). Effort: Umberger/Uchida-type probe with w 3e-5 (base) and 3e-4 (fine-tune). Excitation smoothness weight 1.0. Reference-state init; 10 s (250-step) episodes at 25 Hz; terminate at 0.4 m root error. SAC per Table III (gamma 0.95, batch 256, LR 3e-4 linear, tau 0.02, train_freq 4, gradient_steps 4). Exo fine-tune: 150M steps with tracking k/5, w_eff x10 and w_exo 0.2*0.5*sum|tau|/tau_max. Always run the matched no-exo fine-tune with the identical reward change, steps and mass.
- 11. RAM guard: an SB3 replay buffer stores obs and next_obs as float32. At 3e6 transitions, 515-dim observations need about 12.4 GB, over the 12 GB experiment lane. Use optimize_memory_usage=True, a 1e6 buffer, or a smaller observation, and measure per-env memory before choosing the env count.
- 12. Provenance: log seeds, repo commits, MJCF/XML sha256, the actuator-module parameters, filter discretisation, metric definitions and the Walk-RL/paper choice made for every unspecified item above.

**Throughput.**
- [reported] Choi: 96 parallel envs, 600M steps, about 48 h on an NVIDIA RTX 5090 + Intel Ultra 9 285K (p4 Sec II-D-1). [derived-ours] That is about 3,470 env-steps/s aggregate, about 36 steps/s per env, or about 1.45x real time per env at 25 Hz. With 200 Hz physics that is about 28k physics steps/s across the machine. Hyfydy physics runs on the CPU; the 285K has 24 cores (8P+16E per Intel's spec, not stated in the paper), and the GPU only runs SAC updates.
- [derived-ours] SB3 counts train_freq in vectorised-env steps. With 96 envs, train_freq 4 and gradient_steps 4 give one gradient update per 96 transitions (UTD about 0.0104), about 6.25M updates over 600M steps at batch 256. This low UTD explains how 600M steps fit in 48 h. A higher UTD would cut env throughput.
- [reported] Choi fine-tunes: 150M steps per device condition (Table III), wall-clock not reported. [derived-ours] At the same rate each takes about 12 h, and the hip, ankle and impaired variants add 25% or more per model.
- [reported] Park: 30 envs, 1e8 steps per branch, 40 Hz control, 200 Hz dynamics; no hardware or wall-clock reported. [derived-ours] 1.5e8 env steps per full pipeline is about 1,042 h of simulated walking.
- [from sibling digests, not re-measured here] Server sail-gpu: EPYC 9535 (64C/128T), 30 GB RAM, one RTX 5090; the experiment lane defaults to at most 48 threads and 12 GB. MyoAssist myolegs26+Hippo env.step measured at 2.36 ms (50 Hz control / 1000 Hz physics), about 420 steps/s per core env-only (myoassist_framework.json). [derived-ours] 40 CPU envs could therefore supply about 10-17k steps/s before learner and IPC overhead. A Park-style 1.5e8-step two-arm pipeline would take about 3-8 h, and hundreds of seeds or arms become feasible only as stage-2 forks off shared stage-1 checkpoints. All of this must be measured.
- [derived-ours] Replay memory: 3e6 transitions x obs_dim x 4 B x 2 is 12.4 GB for 515 dims (Walk-RL layout) and 20.1 GB for 839 dims (Choi). SAC at Park or Choi buffer sizes collides with the 30 GB RAM cap once 40-60 env processes are running.
- [vendor page, fetched 2026-09-23] Hyfydy licences: personal non-commercial EUR 995/yr (max 24 CPU cores); personal non-commercial HPC EUR 2,000/yr (no core limit); academic lab EUR 4,000/yr; academic department EUR 8,000/yr. Running Walk-RL or Choi-style Hyfydy baselines on all 64 cores needs the HPC or lab tier.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- Park timing: the 103/166 ms lags may be mostly the tau = 0.1 s filter (about 91 ms phase delay at a 0.9 Hz stride; derived) rather than a learned phase-dependent strategy. Test: retrain the teacher at tau in {0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.15} s and see whether the lag tracks tau.
- Park effort effects (1.1-3.4% activation): these may lie within the seed-to-seed variance of two separately trained SAC policies. Test: at least 3 paired seeds per arm.
- Park speed trend (r 0.98): it holds on 9 slope-averaged points (derived). It may partly reflect larger absolute effort, and larger torque use, at higher speed rather than better-matched assistance. The effect is not robust once slopes are pooled (r about 0.69 over 27 points, derived).
- Park sim-to-real agreement: both signals are smooth, filtered, phase-locked functions of thigh motion. Similar walking kinematics plus low-pass filtering can give high r whatever the assistance quality. r ignores amplitude, and the signals are commands, not delivered torque. Uphill r 0.98 vs downhill 0.66 may reflect signal size (large, simple ascent profiles vs small descent profiles) rather than 'training-distribution representation', for which no evidence is shown.
- Park 'without hardware tuning': the hardware filter (0.15 s) and rate limiter change the waveform, and could have been chosen after pilot testing (not stated).
- Choi metabolic reductions: if the no-exo baseline is the base policy, the fine-tuned exo policies differ in reward (10x effort weight, looser tracking), in training (+150M steps) and in mass, not only in assistance. The reward change alone could lower probe cost by freeing the gait from the reference. Test: a matched no-exo fine-tune.
- Choi larger savings at higher speeds: consistent with larger absolute torques (tau_max unknown; hip up to about 0.2 Nm/kg in running) and a 1 Hz filter that interacts differently with stride frequency. Not necessarily better timing.
- Choi impaired results: asymmetric assistance and improved symmetry are expected from tracking a symmetric healthy reference with a weakened side, so they are not independent evidence of clinical validity.
- Choi GRF fidelity: largely implied by tracking kinematics plus inverse-dynamics joint moments, so it is not an independent emergent validation.

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Make the Park-style matched zero-torque fork the default control for every stage transition in our SMAT reconstruction, with paired seeds. Use the same fork rule for each simple controller, so co-adaptation is matched.
- Run a 'filter-attribution' mini-study: sweep the actuator time constant and delay, and measure whether the learned timing and benefit move. This is cheap, directly tests a published interpretation, and sets the realistic-actuator requirements for the exosuit.
- Add a distillability gate for any sprint teacher: after training, fit a single-thigh-gyro student and require closed-loop retention of at least X% of the teacher's 200 m time gain in simulation before calling the controller deployable (X declared in advance).
- Report both effort proxies on identical rollouts: Park-style mean activation and positive power, and a Choi-style Umberger/Uchida probe (as 01_PAPERS.md line 310 already proposes). Exo benefit can depend on the proxy.
- Adopt Park's gyro-axis agreement check as an acceptance test for the simulated sensor model: per-axis r and RMSE of simulated vs real thigh gyro. Extend it to sprint phases (start, acceleration, max velocity) once the lab records IMU data.
- For a cable exosuit, compare a SMAT-style exo actor on deployable inputs (hip or thigh kinematics plus past commands) with a Park-style privileged teacher plus a distilled student. If the deployable actor matches the teacher, distillation is unnecessary.
- If a Hyfydy licence is obtained, use Walk-RL (H2190, compliant tendons) as a comparator for key Phase-1/2 conclusions, as 01_PAPERS.md suggests. Use 24 cores under the EUR 995 tier.

## Phase 2: sprinting humans and sprint evidence

### Lambda-Hold Control: Human-Like Movement Emerges from a Minimal Task Reward in Predictive Musculoskeletal Simulation

- *Lambda-Hold Control: Human-Like Movement Emerges from a Minimal Task Reward in Predictive Musculoskeletal Simulation* — Jun Hyuk Lee, Chihyeong Lee, Jooeun Ahn (Seoul National University), arXiv preprint (cs.RO). v1 2026-08-17 is the only version per the arXiv API on 2026-09-23. Arxiv comment: '19 pages, 8 figures, 1 table'.. arXiv:2608.17030 · code: NOT RELEASED (checked 2026-09-23). (1) The project page source (_projects/lambda-hold.md in github.com/Lee-Jun-Hyuk-37/Lee-Jun-Hyuk-37.github.io, last commit 4d7aadc on 2026-08-21) shows 'Code (TBA)'. (2) `gh search repos "Lambda-Hold"` and `"lambda-hold"` return only unrelated AWS-Lambda repositories. (3) The first author has 13 public repos and none holds Lambda-Hold code. His 'myoassist' fork is an upstream snapshot whose latest commit (c108837, 2025-10-15) is by the MyoAssist authors. Only demo videos are public: images/lambda-hold/*.mp4 for lambda-hold, the four baselines and early/mid/final stages.. - *KINESIS: Motion Imitation for Human Musculoskeletal Locomotion* — Merkourios Simos, Alberto Silvio Chiappa, Alexander Mathis (EPFL), ICRA 2026. The arXiv v3 comment reads 'Accepted to ICRA. Here we include an appendix'. v1 2025-03-18, v3 2026-03-25. The README keeps the older title 'Reinforcement Learning-Based Motion Imitation for Physiologically Plausible Musculoskeletal Motor Control'.. arXiv:2503.14637 · code: https://github.com/amathislab/Kinesis at HEAD cddab3c3d6 (tag and release v2.0, 2026-02-03). No later commits as of 2026-09-23. The GitHub API shows licence NOASSERTION. It holds 7 files: legs/{kinesis-moe-imitation,target-reach}, legs_abs/{kinesis-moe-imitation,target-reach} and legs_back/{kinesis-moe-imitation,target-reach,ball-kick}/model.pth. PDF v3 sha256 135f8d635ce50913c1338a37d63f41eb448fca0b44bbc03411bd62f30b60007a (scratchpad/pdfs/kinesis_2503.14637v3.pdf). Code read at cddab3c: README.md; src/env/myolegs_env.py lines 100-330 and 488-575 (observations, PD/direct actuation); src/utils/convert_kit.py (full); src/env/myolegs_directional_control.py (grep); cfg/config_legs.yaml, cfg/env/env_im.yaml, cfg/run/train_run_legs.yaml, cfg/run/eval_run_legs.yaml, cfg/run/train_run_legs_back.yaml, cfg/learning/im_mlp.yaml; data/kit_train_keys.txt and data/kit_test_keys.txt (all keys); issues #11 and #12 with all comments.

**Problem.** LAMBDA-HOLD. The paper argues that RL on muscle-level action spaces explores inefficiently for two reasons. First, independent per-muscle excitation noise largely cancels in the many-to-one excitation-to-joint map. Second, the policy must emit a full excitation vector 'hundreds of times per second' (p.2, sections 1.2-1.3). It groups prior remedies into fixed reflex rules with tuned gains, correlated noise (OU/pink/gSDE), DEP-RL, and fixed synergy bases, and says all of them impose coordination from outside (pp.2-3, section 1.4). Lambda-Hold changes the control variable to per-muscle equilibrium-point threshold lengths (lambda). A fixed stretch-reflex law turns lambda into excitations, and lambda is held between gait events. The paper claims 'to our knowledge, the first coordinated, human-like sprinting' in predictive musculoskeletal simulation from a forward-velocity reward alone (p.3, section 1.5). KINESIS. The paper says torque humanoids miss joint constraints and overactuated muscle control, and that muscle-driven locomotion work is narrow or lacks comparison with human data (p.1). Kinesis trains a model-free PPO motion-imitation policy on 1.8 h of KIT locomotion (walk, gradual turn, turn in place, walk backwards, short runs) for MyoLeg variants with 80, 86 and 290 muscles. It reuses the result as a locomotion prior for downstream tasks and compares its muscle patterns with EMG (pp.1-2). FOR THIS PROGRAMME the question is whether either method can supply an unassisted MuJoCo sprinting human that meets the replication gate: median maximum speed >= 4.0 m/s over 5 seeds, with the published reward unchanged and no increase in muscle strength or shortening velocity.

**Mechanism (exact).** LAMBDA-HOLD, from the paper unless marked. PLANT: H2190, a 3D model with 21 mechanical DOFs and 90 musculotendon units, simulated in SCONE with the Hyfydy engine. The simulation step is 0.01 s and every method shares it. At each step the engine exposes each muscle's length l_i and velocity v_i and takes excitation e_i in [0,1]. Both l_i and v_i are normalised by optimal fibre length, which is Hyfydy's default output format. Activation follows from e_i through the engine's activation dynamics, and force from activation plus force-length and force-velocity (p.3 section 2.1; p.4). RECRUITMENT LAW (Eq. 1, p.4): e_i = clip_[0,1]( G_tonic*(l_i - lambda_i) + G_phasic*max(v_i, 0) ). G_tonic = 50 and G_phasic = 0.1 are shared by all 90 muscles and fixed throughout; lambda_i is the policy command, bounded to [0.6, 1.2]. The tonic term is length feedback about the threshold (static stretch reflex). The phasic term is rectified velocity feedback (dynamic stretch reflex), so it only adds drive while the muscle is stretching. No neural delay appears anywhere. The law contains no coupling term; coordination comes from mechanically coupled lengths (p.4). HOLD: the policy emits lambda only at decision points and holds it until the next one, while the law keeps producing e from the evolving l and v at every 0.01 s step. The reward is accumulated over the hold. Each hold is one SAC transition, so exploration noise perturbs lambda once per decision and stays constant across the interval (pp.4 and 6). The paper stresses that this is not action repeat because the excitation keeps varying through the reflex loop (p.4). DECISION TIMING (p.4 section 2.3; Fig. 2 p.5): a decision fires whenever either foot changes contact state, i.e. when that foot's vertical GRF crosses 0.05 body weight rising (strike) or falling (toe-off). One sub-point is inserted between consecutive events at t_last_event + 0.5*T_est, where T_est is the gap between the third- and second-most-recent events. That gap is the most recent interval of the same type, because stance and flight intervals alternate. Every hold is bounded to [0.05, 0.15] s. Fixed-interval triggering was rejected because 'in our experience the stride period then settles at multiples of the hold' (p.4), which is anecdotal and not shown. Lambda is therefore updated 'only eight times per gait cycle' (p.12): four GRF events plus four sub-points per stride. LEARNING (p.5 section 2.4; Table 1 p.18): SAC with gSDE on Stable-Baselines3. Actor and critic are MLPs with two hidden layers of 256. gSDE noise is resampled every 4 decision steps. Learning rate 3e-4, gamma 0.99, target smoothing tau 0.005, batch 256, replay 5e5, 2 gradient steps per train_freq 1, entropy coefficient 'auto', 24 parallel environments, online mean/variance normalisation of observations and rewards. OBSERVATION (p.6): 432 dims covering muscle length and velocity (spindle Ia/II), MTU force (Golgi Ib), foot contact and GRF, joint angles and angular velocities, vestibular signals, and an efference copy of the held lambda. Excitation and activation are excluded. [COMPUTED here: the per-muscle blocks l, v, F and lambda give 4 x 90 = 360. If joints means the 21 DOFs, angles plus velocities give 42. That leaves 30 dims for contact/GRF plus vestibular, which the paper does not itemise.] ACTION: 90-dim lambda in [0.6,1.2]; the mapping from the squashed SAC output is not stated. REWARD (Eq. 2, p.6): r = exp(v_x) * exp(-(v_z/sigma_lat)^2) per 0.01 s step, with sigma_lat = 0.1 m/s. v_x is forward and v_z mediolateral centre-of-mass velocity. The velocity term is uncapped, and the lateral factor lies in [0,1]. TERMINATION (p.6): centre-of-mass height < 0.5 m or head height < 0.9 m. START: standing (p.7 'accelerates from a standing start'; p.17 'default standing posture'). EXTENSIONS that are 'not part of the controller evaluated in the main text' (App. B, p.17): (a) RSI with a Go-Explore-style self-generated archive of the highest-return episodes; after a 3e6-decision-step warm-up, 'a fraction' of resets draw their initial state from it (p.18). (b) A vmax curriculum that holds 10 L_opt/s until 3e6 decision steps, then raises vmax in unit increments to 20 over the next 150e6 simulation steps, on top of RSI (p.19). BASELINES (p.6 section 2.6), all with the same task and model: Plain SAC on 90 excitations every step; Excitation-hold, which holds excitations between the same decision points without a reflex; DEP-RL, the previous study's DEP+MPO implementation with its step-counted quantities rescaled from 0.025 s to 0.01 s (time windows and delays x2.5, per-step rates /2.5, gamma -> gamma^(1/2.5)); Synergy, a 30-dim latent (10 NMF synergy coefficients per leg with one basis shared by both legs, plus 10 independent trunk excitations), with the basis taken from NMF of lambda-hold's own best rollouts and mirroring omitted. KINESIS. PLANT: MyoSuite MyoLeg in MuJoCo, in three variants (p.2 section III.A): Legs with 80 MTUs and the upper body 'a rigid volume with mass'; Legs+Abs with 86 (six abdominal muscles for basic postural control); Legs+Back with 290. Body dimensions 'closely match the mean human body shape of the SMPL model'. DATA (p.2 section III.B; p.5; App. p.9): KIT-Locomotion is 1054 AMASS/KIT sequences (1.8 h), split 946 train and 108 test, covering five skills. The run skill is described as 'the subject breaks into a short running stride'. Clips are downsampled from 100 to 30 fps, mapped from SMPL onto a 24-joint MuJoCo template skeleton, and IK is run at several frames per trajectory to give random start states (p.3). POMDP (p.3): the observation holds pelvis height, tilt and velocity; each joint's position, velocity, angular position and angular velocity; feet contact forces; and the target pose at t+1 (absolute and relative). N = 309, 363 and 453 for the three models. The action is M muscle signals. REWARD (Eqs. 1-4 p.3; Table II p.5): a weighted sum of r_pos = exp(-k_pos * sum_i ||p_i - p_hat_i||^2) over pelvis, knees, ankles and toes (plus head for Abs/Back), r_vel = exp(-k_vel * sum ||v_i - v_hat_i||^2), r_e = -||m||_1 - ||m||_2 and r_up = exp(-k_up(theta_f^2 + theta_s^2)). Values: k_pos 200, k_vel 5, k_up 3; w_pos 0.6, w_vel 0.2; w_e 0.1/0.2/0.2 and w_up 0.1/0.0/0.0 for Legs/Abs/Back. ACTUATION (pp.3-4): in PD mode the action is a desired next-step muscle length, F_i = F_peak*(k_p(a_i - l_i) - k_d v_i), converted to activation through m_i = (F_i - F_P(l_i)) / (F_L(l_i) F_V(v_i)) (Eq. 5). In direct mode m = (a+1)/2. The paper states that direct control trained faster and scored better, and it was used for Abs/Back and downstream tasks. EPISODES (p.4): random motion and start frame; terminate if any tracked keypoint is > 0.15 m from the reference, otherwise stop at clip end. POLICY (pp.4-5): each expert is a Gaussian MLP with layers [2048,1536,1024,1024,512,512] and SiLU, trained with PPO plus Lattice exploration. Hard negative mining trains a copy of the current expert on the motions it failed, and repeats; 3 experts sufficed. An MoE gating MLP [1024,512,256] with softmax chooses among the frozen experts. TRAINING (p.5): one A100 with 128 CPU threads, 128 envs, 51,200 samples per epoch, 10 PPO rounds, lr 5e-5, gamma 0.99, epsilon 0.2, tau (GAE) 0.95, Adam; simulation at 150 Hz and control at 30 Hz. CODE AT cddab3c, differing from or adding to the paper: (1) The PD helper uses kp = 5*F0 and kd = 0.1*kp, clips force to [-F0, 0], and ignores the cfg kp_scale/kd_scale (src/env/myolegs_env.py:529-540); Table II lists k_p = k_d = 1. (2) Target lengths map a in [-1,1] to [0, lengthrange_max] (myolegs_env.py:558-575). (3) The PD law is recomputed every physics substep within a 30 Hz hold (myolegs_env.py:296-320). (4) The released Legs imitation checkpoint is evaluated in PD mode (cfg/run/eval_run_legs.yaml:10), while legs_back trains in direct mode (cfg/run/train_run_legs_back.yaml:10). (5) Legs proprioception = root_height, root_tilt, local body pos/rot/vel/ang_vel and 4 feet_contacts; task inputs = diff_local_body_pos, diff_local_vel, local_ref_body_pos (cfg/run/train_run_legs.yaml). (6) Value lr is 3e-4 and policy gradient clip 25 (cfg/learning/im_mlp.yaml). CROSS-LINK: Kinesis Legs-PD is structurally a lambda-like length-target controller: the muscle pulls when longer than its target, and the target is held over a fixed 1/30 s interval. It differs from lambda-hold in three ways: it inverts force through the F-L-V curves instead of using a reflex excitation law, it holds on a fixed clock instead of GRF events, and it tracks a reference.

**Protocol.** LAMBDA-HOLD. Task: sprint from a standing start under the Eq. 2 reward. Each method is trained under an identical simulation-step budget; the Fig. 3/7 axes run to about 150e6 simulation steps and about 15e6 decision steps for the hold methods. The mean over 2 random seeds is plotted with a min-max band (Fig. 3 p.8; Fig. 7 p.18). Two axes are reported: simulation steps (equal environment experience) and decision steps (policy queries) (p.7 section 2.7). EXPLORATION (p.7 section 2.8): 15 anatomical joint velocities (the 6 pelvis DOFs excluded). (a) Kozachenko-Leonenko k-NN entropy with k = 4 on per-method standardised velocities at an equal sample size n = 150. (b) Grid coverage with 4 equal bins per dimension between the pooled 1st and 99th percentiles, 4^15 (about 1.1e9) cells, counted cumulatively. HUMAN-LIKENESS (p.7 section 2.9): cycles are aligned at right initial contact (model segmented at the rising edge of the right vertical GRF) and time-normalised to 101 points. Pearson r is computed between model and human mean waveforms. Kinematics and vGRF are compared with Fukuchi et al. 2017 (PeerJ 5:e3298) treadmill running at 2.5, 3.5 and 4.5 m/s, grouping model cycles by mean centre-of-mass speed within +/-0.35 m/s. EMG uses Van Hooren and Meijer 2024 (Data in Brief 54:110312), 19 participants at 5.0 m/s, filtered 20-450 Hz band-pass, rectified and 10 Hz low-pass, then peak-normalised; it is compared with model activations from cycles of mean speed >= 4.3 m/s (129 cycles, Fig. 6). HARDWARE: 'a single NVIDIA RTX 5070 GPU with a 24-thread Intel Core Ultra 9 CPU' (p.8). No head-to-head wall-clock comparison is reported. KINESIS. Train on the 946 KIT-Locomotion clips and evaluate on the 108 unseen test clips. Metrics (p.5): frame coverage (% of dataset frames imitated), success (tracked markers within 0.5 m of the reference on average over the trial) and global MPJPE in mm, as mean and SEM over N = 3 evaluation runs (Table I p.4). Downstream (pp.5-6): text-to-control with MDM motions, judged qualitatively. Target reaching uses a 2x2 m area, 5 s (150 frames) per episode, termination at root height < 0.7 m, success within 0.1 m and 1000 random trials; it is fine-tuned from expert 2 for 5,000-10,000 more epochs, and the from-scratch baseline gets 5,000 epochs (App. p.9). The Legs+Back penalty kick is tested against 3 goalkeeper strategies. EMG (p.6-7): gait-averaged correlations with the Wang et al. 2023 and Scherpereel et al. 2023 datasets, compared against DynSyn and DEP-RL on the Legs model, plus MPJAE over 4 left-leg joints (Fig. 6).

**Headline numbers**
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: speed reached 'in roughly an hour of wall-clock training'
  - *Value:* about 4.0 m/s (RTX 5070 + 24-thread Intel Core Ultra 9; Hyfydy CPU physics)
  - *Location:* p.8 section 3.1
  - *Evidence:* REPORTED single approximate number; no seeds, variance or speed definition
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: top sprint speed
  - *Value:* about 4.7 m/s 'at its fastest'; 'tops out near 4.7 m/s' also with RSI
  - *Location:* p.9 section 3.3; p.10 section 3.4; p.19 App. B.2
  - *Evidence:* REPORTED (2 seeds in the figures; per-seed speeds, evaluation protocol and training time to 4.7 not reported)
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: top speed with the vmax curriculum 10 -> 20 L_opt/s (plus RSI)
  - *Value:* about 6.5 m/s
  - *Location:* p.14 section 4.3; p.19 App. B.2; Fig. 7 p.18
  - *Evidence:* REPORTED as an uncalibrated DIAGNOSTIC by the authors. It violates our gate (shortening velocity doubled). COMPUTED here: the stated schedule starts at 3e6 decisions (about 30e6 simulation steps at Fig. 3's ~10:1 ratio) and runs 150e6 more simulation steps, so it would end near 180e6, beyond the ~150e6 plotted. The vmax actually reached is therefore unclear, about 18 by the stated schedule.
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: hip-flexor force during the concentric swing drive at top speed
  - *Value:* Activation saturates at 1; normalised velocity reaches -2 to -3 (shortening). Mean normalised force at activation > 0.9 is 0.95 (psoas) and 0.94 (iliacus) near isometric, but 0.26 and 0.33 at the fastest shortening ('a quarter to a third of isometric').
  - *Location:* Fig. 8 p.19; p.13 section 4.3
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED in simulation (one top-speed episode, right side)
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: EMG agreement (model activations vs surface EMG at 5.0 m/s)
  - *Value:* Mean r = +0.42 over 11 muscles: soleus 0.80, gastrocnemius medialis 0.87, gastrocnemius lateralis 0.83, biceps femoris 0.62, semitendinosus 0.52, rectus femoris 0.49, gluteus maximus 0.50, gluteus medius 0.19, vastus medialis 0.00, vastus lateralis 0.27, tibialis anterior -0.44. Model cycles pooled: 129 at >= 4.3 m/s.
  - *Location:* p.10 section 3.4; Fig. 6 p.11
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. RE-DERIVED here: the mean of the 11 rounded values is 4.65/11 = 0.423, consistent. The text says 'six of eleven achieve r > 0.5', but only 5 rounded values strictly exceed 0.5; gluteus maximus is printed as 0.50.
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: kinematic/vGRF waveform correlations (hip, knee, ankle, vGRF)
  - *Value:* 2.5 m/s: 0.94, 0.83, 0.90, 0.97. 3.5 m/s: 0.97, 0.92, 0.86, 0.83. 4.5 m/s: 0.95, 0.95, 0.93, 0.83. Simulated bands pool 32, 54 and 218 cycles; measured bands pool 31, 39 and 31 runners.
  - *Location:* Fig. 5 p.10
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED (shape correlation only; the amplitude gap grows with speed)
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: knee swing-flexion peak
  - *Value:* model 85 deg vs human 93 deg at 2.5 m/s. Human rises 93 -> 108 -> 117 deg across 2.5/3.5/4.5 m/s; the model does not rise. Peak hip flexion falls below the human band above 2.5 m/s.
  - *Location:* p.9 section 3.3
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: DEP-RL baseline final gait
  - *Value:* balances and moves forward without falling at about 2.6 m/s; far forward trunk lean, markedly asymmetric, 'awkward fast walk'
  - *Location:* p.8 section 3.1
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED/qualitative
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: return gaps (Fig. 3, log axis)
  - *Value:* Lambda-hold separates from all baselines within the first 10e6 simulation steps. Excitation-hold settles about an order of magnitude above Plain SAC, and lambda-hold is more than an order of magnitude above Excitation-hold. On the decision axis, both hold methods need about 10x fewer queries. Figure-read at 150e6 steps (approximate): lambda-hold oscillates up to ~5e4; DEP-RL ~5e3; Excitation-hold ~1.3e3; Synergy ~2.5e2; Plain SAC ~1e2.
  - *Location:* p.8 section 3.1; Fig. 3 p.8
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED (2 seeds). NOTE (computed here): with r = exp(v_x), a 10x return ratio at equal episode length corresponds to a mean-speed gap of only ln(10) = 2.3 m/s.
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: exploration measures at ~150e6 steps
  - *Value:* Coverage (figure-read, approximate): lambda-hold ~76k cells, DEP-RL ~30k, Plain SAC ~13k, Excitation-hold ~11k, Synergy near 0. Entropy: lambda-hold ~21 nats, DEP-RL ~18.5, Excitation-hold ~16, Plain SAC ~6.5, Synergy ~9.5. The text claims ~2.5x DEP-RL and ~7x Plain SAC and Excitation-hold.
  - *Location:* p.9 section 3.2; Fig. 4 p.9
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. RE-DERIVED from the figure: the ratio vs Plain SAC is ~5.8x, not ~7x; ~7x holds for Excitation-hold.
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: decisions per gait cycle and budget ratio
  - *Value:* 8 per cycle (4 GRF events + 4 sub-points). Fig. 3/7 axes give ~150e6 simulation steps against ~15e6 decision steps, i.e. a mean hold of ~0.1 s.
  - *Location:* p.12 section 4.2.1; Fig. 2 p.5; Figs. 3 and 7
  - *Evidence:* REPORTED (8/cycle); ratio COMPUTED from the axes
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: simulated experience per run
  - *Value:* 150e6 x 0.01 s = 1.5e6 s (about 417 h) of simulated time per seed
  - *Location:* computed from Fig. 3 axes and p.3
  - *Evidence:* COMPUTED here
- *Quantity:* Lambda-Hold: effective discount horizons implied by Table 1
  - *Value:* SAC gamma 0.99 per transition. That is a ~100-decision (~10 s) horizon for the hold methods but ~100 x 0.01 s = 1 s for Plain SAC and Synergy. DEP-RL was rescaled to gamma^(1/2.5) ~ 0.996 per 0.01 s, about 2.5 s.
  - *Location:* Table 1 p.18; p.6 section 2.6
  - *Evidence:* COMPUTED here (the paper does not discuss horizon)
- *Quantity:* H2190v2k (sconegym db2d2c1, KB copy) body and muscle totals
  - *Value:* 8 bodies with total mass 74.53 kg (pelvis 11.777, femur 9.3014 x2, tibia 3.7075 x2, calcn 1.25 x2, torso 34.2366; no articulated arms or head). 21 DOFs: pelvis 6; per leg hip x3, knee, ankle, subtalar; lumbar x3. 90 point_path muscles: 40 leg + 5 trunk per side. Sum Fmax 71,860 N (leg muscles 61,646 N). No toes. Contact: 3 spheres per foot (heel r 0.03, two toes r 0.02); material static friction 0.9, dynamic 0.6, stiffness 11006.4, damping 1. No max_contraction_velocity is set, so the Hyfydy default applies; the paper says 10 L_opt/s.
  - *Location:* research-rebuild-2026-09-05/simulation/sprint-reference/H2190v2k.hfd; per-muscle table in scratchpad lh/h2190_muscles.csv
  - *Evidence:* CODE/MODEL FILE (whether Lambda-Hold used this exact file is not reported)
- *Quantity:* MyoLeg-80 (Kinesis Legs @cddab3c) muscle totals
  - *Value:* 80 muscles (40 per side), no trunk muscles. Sum F0 92,428 N, i.e. 1.50x H2190's leg muscles at multiplier 1 and 0.75x at multiplier 2. vmax = 10 L0/s and fvmax = 1.4 for all 80. fpmax 0.81-2.0; lmin median 0.088 and lmax median 3.48 (very wide active F-L); tau_act/deact 0.01/0.04 s; rigid tendons (springlength, no stiffness). XML timestep 0.001 s, overridden to 1/150 s by the env. Joints per side: hip x3, knee (with coupled translations and patella), ankle, subtalar, mtp; free root; no lumbar joint.
  - *Location:* KB kinesis-model-parameters-2026-09-09.json/.md; recomputed in scratchpad lh/myoleg80_muscles.csv; myolegs.xml joint list
  - *Evidence:* CODE/MODEL FILE, COMPUTED here
- *Quantity:* MyoLeg-80 normalised-length mapping relevant to lambda bounds
  - *Value:* Under MuJoCo semantics L0 = (lr1-lr0)/(range1-range0) and LT = lr0 - range0*L0. Unclamped muscles have L0 equal to the OpenSim optimal fibre length (soleus 0.044, iliacus 0.1066, psoas 0.1169, tibialis anterior 0.0683 m, all matching H2190). 26 of 80 muscles have range ends clamped at 0.1 or 1.8, which inflates L0: recfem 0.171 vs lopt 0.0759, vaslat 0.173 vs 0.0994, semimem 0.121 vs 0.069, gasmed 0.075 vs 0.051. glmax3_r/l have range [1.367, 1.8] and LT = -0.26 m, so their l never drops below 1.367. Across full length ranges, 70/80 muscles can exceed l = 1.2 and 48/80 can fall below 0.6.
  - *Location:* computed from gainprm/lengthrange in the KB ledger; formula verified in MuJoCo src/engine/engine_util_misc.c mju_muscleGain (main @ a7e365917a, 2026-09-07)
  - *Evidence:* COMPUTED here
- *Quantity:* MuJoCo vs Hill force-velocity at equal vmax = 10 L0/s
  - *Value:* MuJoCo shortening FV = (1+V)^2 with V = vel/(L0*vmax). At 1/2/3/4/5 L0/s it gives 0.81/0.64/0.49/0.36/0.25, against a Hill hyperbola (a/F0 = 0.25) of 0.64/0.44/0.32/0.23/0.17, i.e. 1.26-1.56x more force. Lambda-Hold's Hyfydy plant shows 0.26-0.33 at the fastest shortening (Fig. 8).
  - *Location:* MuJoCo source (above); Hill values computed here; Lambda-Hold Fig. 8 p.19
  - *Evidence:* COMPUTED here. The Hyfydy F-V formula is not given in the paper.
- *Quantity:* Kinesis: KIT-Locomotion imitation (Table I, mean +/- SEM, N = 3 evaluation runs)
  - *Value:* Train (946): Legs-PD 96.98% coverage / 90.63% success / 48.8 mm MPJPE; Legs-Direct 98.10 / 93.27 / 42.72; Legs+Abs 98.99 / 96.48 / 44.01; Legs+Back 99.22 / 97.04 / 42.26. Test (108): Legs-PD 96.38 / 91.36 / 48.76; Legs-Direct 97.99 / 94.14 / 43.04; Legs+Abs 98.63 / 95.68 / 44.17; Legs+Back 99.43 / 96.91 / 42.84.
  - *Location:* Table I p.4
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED (no training-seed variance reported)
- *Quantity:* Kinesis: running content and speed
  - *Value:* The run skill is 'a short running stride'; no speed is reported anywhere in the paper. COUNTED here from the key files: 90 of 1054 clips are KIT 'walking_run' (79 train, 11 test); the rest are walking_slow/medium/fast, turns, circles, backwards and handrail variants. MuscleMimic validated its KIT/317 walking_run01-05 playback against 1.8 m/s treadmill running (MuscleMimic arXiv 2603.25544 pp.6 and 9).
  - *Location:* Kinesis p.2 section III.B; data/kit_train_keys.txt, data/kit_test_keys.txt @cddab3c

- *Quantity:* Kinesis: reference playback-speed distortion
  - *Value:* convert_kit.py keeps every int(100/30) = 3rd frame of the 100 fps KIT data (the author confirms 100 fps in issue #11) but labels it fps = 30. The effective rate is 33.3 fps, so references play ~10% slower than recorded (x0.9 speed). The author's own totals (1.66 h train + 0.19 h test) are the stretched durations; real capture time is ~1.66 h.
  - *Location:* src/utils/convert_kit.py:94-96,137; GitHub issue #11 (merc-s comment 2026-03-24)

- *Quantity:* Kinesis: downstream success

  - *Location:* Fig. 4 p.6; p.6 text

- *Quantity:* Kinesis: EMG (forward walking, average correlation)

  - *Location:* Figs. 5-6 p.7

MuscleMimic states ~10 days on 128 CPU envs + A100 for the 290-actuator model. Target-reach fine-tuning of 5,000-10,000 epochs is 256-512e6 samples (computed).
