**Ablations that matter**
- *Ablation:* Excitation-hold vs Plain SAC (holding without lambda)
  - *Result:* Holding alone lifts return by about an order of magnitude on the simulation-step axis.
  - *Location:* p.8 section 3.1, Fig. 3
  - *Caveat:* Confounded with discount horizon: gamma 0.99 per transition gives Excitation-hold ~10 s and Plain SAC ~1 s (computed here).
- *Ablation:* Lambda-hold vs Excitation-hold (control variable at equal decision points)
  - *Result:* Lambda-hold return is more than an order of magnitude higher. The authors attribute 'the larger part of the advantage' to lambda plus the reflex law rather than to holding.
  - *Location:* p.8 section 3.1
  - *Caveat:* This is the paper's cleanest comparison (matched decision points and horizon), but it has only 2 seeds, and exp(v) returns compress to about a 2.3 m/s mean-speed gap per decade.
- *Ablation:* Synergy (fixed NMF basis from lambda-hold's own best rollouts) vs lambda-hold
  - *Result:* Synergy stalls at rudimentary locomotion, with the lowest coverage and entropy that is higher than Plain SAC. This supports the claim that lambda's state-dependent coordination beats a fixed basis.
  - *Location:* pp.8-9, Figs. 3-4; p.11 section 4.1
  - *Caveat:* The Synergy baseline has a 1 s horizon (no hold) and omits the mirroring used by its original study.
- *Ablation:* DEP-RL (DEP+MPO, prior implementation, rescaled to 0.01 s)
  - *Result:* Best baseline late in training: about 2.6 m/s with a non-human-like gait. Coverage is ~2.5x lower than lambda-hold.
  - *Location:* p.8, Fig. 3-4
  - *Caveat:* Hyper-parameters come from the author's H2190 running config (they match the sconegym/DEP-RL sconerun_h2190 YAML), and DEP-RL was trained under a reward it was not tuned for.
- *Ablation:* RSI from a self-generated archive (App. B.1)
  - *Result:* The late-training oscillation, in which the fast sprint is lost and relearned, disappears; return becomes smooth at ~1e5 (figure-read). Top speed stays near 4.7 m/s.
  - *Location:* pp.17-19, Fig. 7
  - *Caveat:* The fraction of RSI resets and the archive size are not reported. RSI is allowed under our gate because it changes neither reward nor body.
- *Ablation:* vmax curriculum 10 -> 20 L_opt/s (on top of RSI)
  - *Result:* Top speed ~6.5 m/s, which identifies the H2190 force-velocity relation as the ceiling.
  - *Location:* p.19 App. B.2; Fig. 7
  - *Caveat:* Explicitly diagnostic, and forbidden by our gate. The schedule end lies beyond the plotted budget.
- *Ablation:* MISSING in the paper: lambda without holding (lambda at every 0.01 s)
  - *Result:* Not tested, so the 2x2 factorial (lambda vs excitation) x (hold vs no-hold) is incomplete.
  - *Location:* section 2.6 lists the baselines
  - *Caveat:* Cheap to run in a reimplementation; it separates reflex-law benefits from temporal abstraction.
- *Ablation:* MISSING: fixed-interval holds vs GRF-event holds
  - *Result:* The paper asserts, 'in our experience', that fixed holds lock stride period to multiples of the hold. No data shown.
  - *Location:* p.4 section 2.3
  - *Caveat:* Matters for the exosuit, whose controller will likely run on a fixed clock.
- *Ablation:* MISSING: sensitivity to G_tonic/G_phasic, the lambda bounds, the sub-point, the lateral term, the efference copy, neural delays and the muscle force multiplier
  - *Result:* Not reported.
  - *Location:* section 2.2 fixes the gains 'throughout this work'
  - *Caveat:* Reimplementation on MyoLeg will have to probe the lambda bounds at least (see recipe).
- *Ablation:* Kinesis: PD target-length vs direct control (Legs)
  - *Result:* Direct gives higher success (train 93.27 vs 90.63%) and lower MPJPE (42.72 vs 48.8 mm), and trains faster.
  - *Location:* Table I p.4; p.4 and p.6
  - *Caveat:* The released Legs checkpoint is nevertheless evaluated in PD mode (cfg/run/eval_run_legs.yaml:10).
- *Ablation:* Kinesis: imitation prior vs from-scratch target reaching
  - *Result:* The from-scratch baseline (5,000 epochs) learns an unrealistic gait: one leg stiff, the other propelling. The prior gives symmetric hip flexion.
  - *Location:* App. p.9, Fig. S1 p.10
  - *Caveat:* Qualitative. The prior's value for speed is untested.
- *Ablation:* Kinesis: model complexity (Legs / +Abs / +Back)
  - *Result:* Imitation improves slightly with more muscles. EMG is best for Legs+Back and worst for Legs+Abs, which the authors attribute to 'an unrealistic increase in upper body weight' in that embodiment.
  - *Location:* Table I; p.7
  - *Caveat:* Shows that embodiment mass errors change EMG conclusions.

**Limitations the authors admit**
- Lambda-Hold: top speed is capped near 4.7 m/s by the H2190 force-velocity relation. Fully active hip flexors deliver only 1/4 to 1/3 of isometric force while shortening fast in swing; 'a force-velocity property of the H2190 plant, not of the lambda-hold controller' (p.13 section 4.3; p.19 App. B.2).
- Lambda-Hold: the model has no arms, which removes a contributor to angular-momentum balance at high speed (p.14 section 4.3).
- Lambda-Hold: sagittal amplitudes depart from human data, and the gap widens with speed (knee and hip swing flexion too small) (p.9 section 3.3).
- Lambda-Hold: EMG agreement is poor for gluteus medius, the vasti and tibialis anterior, with disagreement concentrated in swing (p.10 section 3.4).
- Lambda-Hold: return oscillates late in training as the fast sprint is forgotten and relearned. The authors attribute this to the multi-task nature of acceleration plus steady sprint, and add RSI (p.8; pp.17-18 App. B.1).
- Lambda-Hold: the GRF trigger is 'one task-specific solution', relies on cyclic gait, and does not generalise to tasks without clear events (p.4-5 section 2.3; p.13 section 4.2.2).
- Lambda-Hold: no head-to-head wall-clock comparison, because simulation-to-decision ratio, optimiser, parallel environments and throughput all differ (pp.8-9).
- Lambda-Hold: the 6.5 m/s run uses an uncalibrated vmax and is 'a diagnostic rather than a performance result' (p.19).
- Lambda-Hold: the architecture is 'a modeling demonstration', not a claim about how the CNS is organised; what the policy learns is not interpretable from the controller (p.12 section 4.2.1).
- Lambda-Hold: decision updates are more frequent than intervals reported for human intermittent control (p.13 section 4.2.2).
- Lambda-Hold: the lower Fukuchi speed bins contain accelerating rather than steady-state gaits (p.7 section 2.9).
- Lambda-Hold: the Synergy basis from lambda-hold's own rollouts 'favours the baseline' (p.6 section 2.6).
- Lambda-Hold: generalisation to balance, jumping, walking and perturbations is left as future work (p.14 section 4.4).
- Kinesis: the Legs+Abs embodiment had 'an unrealistic increase in upper body weight' that may explain its lower EMG correlation (p.7).
- Kinesis: KINESIS MPJAE is 'significantly higher compared to inter-subject values' (p.7).
- Kinesis: the Legs+Abs target-reaching model struggles for targets right behind the start (about 60%) (p.6).
- Kinesis: more comprehensive training data and refined musculoskeletal models are needed (p.7 conclusion).

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- Lambda-Hold: every curve uses 2 seeds (Figs. 3, 7). The 4.0 and 4.7 m/s figures are single approximate numbers with no per-seed values, no dispersion and no definition of 'speed' (peak? per-cycle mean?). Our gate needs 5 seeds and a pre-declared metric.
- Lambda-Hold: the muscle strength scenario is undisclosed. The only public H2190 running scenario (sconegym db2d2c1 data-v1/H2190_run.scone) sets muscle_force_multiplier = 2 with a 0.005 s fixed control step; the walking scenario H2190.scone does not. The paper's DEP-RL hyper-parameters (1024-unit MLP, actor lr 3.53e-5, critic 6.08e-5, dual 2.13e-3, 0.025 s original step) match the author YAML for sconerun_h2190-v1 exactly (KB sconerun-h2190-config.yaml). The termination thresholds (0.5 m COM, 0.9 m head) and 0.01 s step also equal sconegym defaults (gaitgym.py). A doubled-strength plant therefore cannot be excluded. This is inference, not proof.
- Lambda-Hold: the reflex has zero delay and G_tonic = 50 saturates excitation 0.02 L_opt above threshold. It is essentially a stiff, delay-free length servo, whereas real stretch reflexes have 30-50 ms latencies and much lower gains. 'Physiologically grounded' rests on the form of the law only. SCONE's own neural_delays_FEA_v4.zml (hip 10 ms, knee 20 ms, ankle 40 ms) is not mentioned.
- Lambda-Hold: a horizon confound in the baseline comparison. With gamma = 0.99 per transition, Plain SAC and Synergy (0.01 s decisions) have a ~1 s effective horizon against ~10 s for the hold methods. Only DEP-RL was rescaled. Part of the Excitation-hold vs Plain SAC gap may be horizon rather than intermittency.
- Lambda-Hold: 'orders of magnitude faster' (section 3.1 title) is read off log-scale returns of an exp(v) reward. One decade of return equals about 2.3 m/s of mean speed at equal episode length, so the plots overstate the performance gap as a learning-speed ratio.
- Lambda-Hold: human-likeness (kinematics, GRF, EMG) is reported only for lambda-hold, never for a baseline. No excitation-level controller reached comparable speed, so the claim that the representation (and not the speed or the plant) makes the gait human-like is untested.
- Lambda-Hold: validation is limited to sagittal hip/knee/ankle angles and vertical GRF shape. Horizontal GRF, step length and frequency, contact time, pelvis/trunk and frontal-plane motion are not reported. The 4.5 m/s bin (4.15-4.85 m/s, possibly accelerating) is compared with steady treadmill running, and EMG uses 5.0 m/s data against model cycles >= 4.3 m/s.
- Lambda-Hold: the exploration metrics (joint-velocity entropy and coverage) measure diversity, which also rises with flailing and falling. They are correlational evidence for the 'efficient exploration' mechanism.
- Lambda-Hold: baselines share one SAC configuration presumably chosen for lambda-hold (no per-baseline tuning is reported). DEP-RL used its original running hyper-parameters under a different reward.
- Lambda-Hold: the 6.5 m/s diagnostic changes two things at once (RSI plus the vmax curriculum), and the curriculum as described would not finish inside the plotted budget.
- Lambda-Hold: nothing addresses sustained sprint distance, 200 m, pacing or fatigue. If episodes use sconegym's 1000-step default (an inference consistent with the Fig. 3/7 return magnitudes), training covers only ~10 s, about 35-45 m.
- Lambda-Hold: 'within an hour' refers to ~4.0 m/s on the authors' workstation. The wall-clock time to reach 4.7 m/s and the total compute for 150e6 steps are not given.
- Lambda-Hold: 'six of eleven muscles achieve r > 0.5', but only five rounded values exceed 0.5. The coverage claim of ~7x over Plain SAC reads as ~5.8x in Fig. 4b.
- Kinesis: the 'run' skill is 90 short KIT walking_run clips of unreported speed. Given the frame-skip labelling the references play at ~0.9x recorded speed. There is no running kinematic or GRF validation, and running EMG is shown only qualitatively.
- Kinesis: Table II lists PD k_p = k_d = 1, while the code uses kp = 5*F0 and kd = 0.5*F0 and ignores the configured scales.
- Kinesis: Table I variance is over N = 3 evaluation runs, not independent training seeds.
- Kinesis: the 1.8 h dataset duration is the stretched 30-fps-label duration (1.85 h by the author's own numbers); real capture time is ~1.66 h.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Lambda-Hold: which SCONE scenario and H2190 file version were used, and the muscle_force_multiplier (1 or 2). Hyfydy and SCONE versions.
- Lambda-Hold: whether l and v are fibre-level (compliant tendon) or MTU-derived; the paper says only 'normalized by the optimal fiber length' per Hyfydy's default output. The Hyfydy tendon model and activation time constants are not stated.
- Lambda-Hold: sensor or actuator delays. None is mentioned. sconegym has use_delayed_sensors/actuators flags that default to false.
- Lambda-Hold: the exact 432-dim observation layout: order, GRF encoding (per foot, 3D or vertical only), contact flags, the vestibular channels (sconegym uses head quaternion + angular velocity), force normalisation, and whether pelvis translations are zeroed.
- Lambda-Hold: how the SAC tanh output maps to lambda in [0.6, 1.2] (presumably linear).
- Lambda-Hold: scheduler edge cases. What happens to an event arriving < 0.05 s after the previous decision (deferred? dropped?); whether a decision is forced at 0.15 s; sub-point handling before three events exist (e.g. from standing, where both feet are loaded); GRF filtering or hysteresis; simultaneous events.
- Lambda-Hold: episode time limit, the standing initial posture (sconegym's H2190 scenario instead initialises from InitStateGait10.zml, a mid-gait state at 1 m/s), reset noise, and initial activations.
- Lambda-Hold: discounting over variable-length holds (a flat gamma per decision is implied by Table 1 but not stated).
- Lambda-Hold: SAC learning_starts, gSDE log_std_init/full_std, network activation, target entropy, SB3 version, seed values.
- Lambda-Hold: the RSI reset fraction, archive size and which states are stored or sampled.
- Lambda-Hold: the top-speed definition and evaluation protocol (episodes, deterministic vs stochastic policy), and which seed or run gave 4.7 m/s.
- Lambda-Hold: contact model parameters. The sconegym H2190v2k.hfd gives friction 0.9/0.6, stiffness 11006.4, damping 1 and 3 contact spheres per foot, but the paper states none.
- Kinesis: total training samples and time per expert and for the MoE; number of training seeds.
- Kinesis: speeds of the KIT walking_run clips (the data require AMASS access to measure).
- Kinesis: whether target-reach or directional use (reference-free at run time) still requires SMPL assets at import. The KB notes these tasks inherit the imitation environment. Not verified here.

**Reproduction relevance.** Lambda-Hold is the only published muscle-driven RL result that clears our replication gate's speed on its own plant (4.0 m/s in ~1 h, top ~4.7 m/s), and it does so with the minimal reward our gate freezes. It is also the Phase-2 starting design. It cannot be reproduced as published: the code is TBA, SCONE/Hyfydy leaves the lab after 2026-09-25, only 2 seeds are shown, and the strength multiplier is undisclosed. Any MuJoCo version is therefore an independent reimplementation on a different plant. MyoLeg-80 differs from H2190 in ways that cut both ways. (1) Its leg muscles total 1.5x H2190's F0 at multiplier 1. (2) MuJoCo's quadratic force-velocity curve gives 1.4-1.5x more force than a Hill hyperbola at 2-3 L0/s shortening, which is exactly where the paper locates its 4.7 m/s ceiling. (3) Its active F-L curves are very wide (median lmax 3.5). (4) It has rigid tendons with no elastic recoil. (5) It has no lumbar DOF or trunk muscles, but it does have mtp toes. (6) Its contact model differs. (7) Its activations match EMG worse: Beyond Kinematics reports r 0.11 for MuJoCo vs 0.40 for HyFyDy. Items 1-3 favour reaching >= 4.0 m/s without strength or vmax changes; items 4-7 threaten realism and stability. Porting the lambda law also needs a normalisation audit. MyoLeg's normalised lengths are MTU-derived, 26 muscles have clamped ranges, and glmax3 would sit permanently saturated under the published bounds. Kinesis supplies the open MyoLeg-80 MJCF with pinned parameters, a working MuJoCo length-target muscle controller (the PD mode, a close cousin of lambda-control) and pretrained locomotion priors. It does not supply a sprint-capable human: its checkpoints are reference-conditioned imitation or target-reach policies trained on walking-dominated KIT data whose 90 short 'run' clips have unreported speed, and imitation needs SMPL plus AMASS licences. For SMAT staging, three points follow. (a) A lambda-hold human is an SMDP SAC agent acting 8 times per stride, whereas SMAT's exo actor is 50 Hz PPO with a shared critic; staging must either freeze the SAC human (stage 3) or define a joint SMDP update (stage 4). (b) GRF events are also natural exo triggers, since Moon et al.'s sprint suit is gait-event-timed. (c) The exo observations must exclude lambda, l and v, which a real device cannot sense. The paper's missing 'lambda without hold' and fixed-interval-hold arms map onto our matrix conditions G and H (06-architecture note).

**Recipe on our server.**
- 0. Label and pre-register. Call it 'independent MuJoCo/MyoLeg reimplementation of Lambda-Hold (arXiv 2608.17030v1)'. Before any run, freeze the gate metric: per seed, the maximum over complete right-foot gait cycles of per-cycle mean COM forward speed, from 10 deterministic 20 s episodes started from standing, using the last checkpoint. PASS if the median over 5 seeds is >= 4.0 m/s. Also report the best 1 s window speed and the fall rate. Forbidden in gate runs: any change to F0, vmax, fvmax, F-L curves, mass, the Eq. 2 reward, or the vmax curriculum.
- 1. Model. Use MyoLeg-80 'Legs' from amathislab/Kinesis@cddab3c data/xml/legs/myolegs.xml + myolegs_assets.xml, which needs no SMPL for the model itself, or the matching MyoSuite myo_sim myolegs; record the file sha256 either way. Keep all actuator parameters: vmax 10, fvmax 1.4, dynprm (0.01, 0.04), per-muscle F0 summing to 92,428 N. Compile once and record total mass, inertia, nq/nv/nu = 80 and the joint list. Note in the report that it has no arms (like H2190) and no lumbar DOF or trunk muscles (unlike H2190), so the action dimension is 80, not 90.
- 2. Physics. Use native MuJoCo on CPU. Set opt.timestep = 0.002 s (verify against 0.001 s on a 2 s rollout) and keep the XML contact and solver settings. The lambda-law/controller step is 0.01 s, i.e. 5 substeps, matching the paper's 0.01 s step. Do not use Kinesis's 1/150 s for sprint impacts without a parity check.
- 3. Muscle state, vectorised over 80 actuators, using MuJoCo's own normalisation (engine_util_misc.c mju_muscleGain): L0 = (lr1 - lr0)/(range1 - range0) from model.actuator_lengthrange and actuator_gainprm[:,0:2]; l = range0 + (data.actuator_length - lr0)/L0; v = data.actuator_velocity/L0 in L0/s, with positive meaning lengthening, the same sign convention as the paper's rectified phasic term. Unit test: recompute FL*FV from (l, v) with MuJoCo's formulas and check that it reproduces (data.actuator_force - mju_muscleBias(...))/(-F0*act) to 1e-6. The bias term is the passive force.
- 4. Recruitment law exactly as Eq. 1: e = clip(50*(l - lambda) + 0.1*max(v, 0), 0, 1), written to data.ctrl once per 0.01 s. Use zero delay, as the paper implies. Keep e for logging only.
- 5. Lambda-range pre-flight audit (blocking). Log each muscle's l range over (i) its lengthrange, (ii) the standing keyframe and (iii) a kinematic playback of a running clip if available. Expected from the ledger: glmax3_r/l have l >= 1.367 everywhere (LT = -0.26 m), so under lambda <= 1.2 they are permanently fully excited. 26 muscles have clamped range ends with inflated L0. Primary arm: the published bounds [0.6, 1.2] for all muscles. Pre-declared fallback arm (a controller-side deviation that leaves the body unchanged): raise the upper lambda bound only for muscles whose l cannot fall below 1.2 (glmax3) to range0 + 0.05. Report both arms if the fallback is used.
- 6. GRF events. Every 0.01 s, compute per-foot vertical GRF as the world-z component of the mj_contactForce normals summed over contacts between the floor and that foot's bodies (calcn, talus, toes). Threshold 0.05 x BW, where BW = compiled total mass x 9.81. Contact state = GRF > threshold. Events are rising edges (strike) and falling edges (toe-off) of either foot.
- 7. Decision scheduler, a per-env state machine on the 0.01 s grid. Keep the last three event times. The candidate next decision is the earlier of the next event and the sub-point t_e1 + 0.5*(t_e2 - t_e3), where t_e1 is the most recent event and t_e2, t_e3 the second- and third-most recent. Enforce a minimum hold of 0.05 s (an event inside it defers the decision to t_dec + 0.05: assumption A1) and a maximum of 0.15 s (forced decision: A2). With fewer than 3 events (standing start) use events plus the 0.15 s cap only (A3). Unit-test on a synthetic running GRF trace: expect 8 decisions per stride, as in Fig. 2.
- 8. Observation (all per 0.01 s state at decision time). l (80), v (80), actuator_force/F0 (80; normalisation A4), held lambda (80); independent joint angles and velocities (root orientation plus hip x3, knee, ankle, subtalar and mtp per side; no root x/y position; coupled knee/patella DOFs omitted); per-foot contact flag (2) and GRF/BW 3-vector (6); vestibular = torso/head orientation quaternion (4) + angular velocity (3). This is about 360-380 dims, not 432, because of the 80 muscles and different DOFs. Wrap in VecNormalize (observations and rewards, running mean/var) as in Table 1. Exclude excitations and activations.
- 9. Action. 80-dim SAC tanh output a in [-1,1] -> lambda = 0.9 + 0.3*a (linear map: A5).
- 10. Reward, unchanged. Per 0.01 s step r = exp(v_x)*exp(-(v_lat/0.1)^2), with COM velocity from mujoco.mj_subtreeVel then data.subtree_linvel[root] (or mass-weighted cvel). First identify the model's forward axis (the Kinesis directional task places its default goal at -1.265 m along x). SMDP transition reward = sum over the hold. gamma = 0.99 per decision (A6, per Table 1).
- 11. Termination and horizon. Terminate if COM z < 0.5 m or head z < 0.9 m (the head is a fixed body in the rigid torso). Training time limit 10 s (A7, consistent with sconegym's 1000-step default and the Fig. 3/7 return magnitudes). Treat truncation as time-limit, not terminal. Reset to the standing keyframe with small noise (A8: joint position sigma 0.05 rad, velocity sigma 0.1 rad/s, as in sconegym).
- 12. SAC per Table 1. SB3 SAC('MlpPolicy', use_sde=True, sde_sample_freq=4, learning_rate=3e-4, gamma=0.99, tau=0.005, batch_size=256, buffer_size=500_000, train_freq=1, gradient_steps=2, ent_coef='auto', policy_kwargs=dict(net_arch=[256,256])) on 24 SubprocVecEnv envs, where one env step equals one hold. Unspecified choices take SB3 defaults and are logged (A9): learning_starts, activation, log_std_init, target entropy.
- 13. Budget: 150e6 simulation steps (about 15e6 decisions) per seed. Evaluate every 5e6 simulation steps with deterministic actions. Log speed, fall rate, decisions per stride and wall-clock. Early smoke check: per the paper, H2190 reached about 4.0 m/s in about an hour. If MyoLeg is below 2 m/s after 30e6 simulation steps, stop and debug normalisation and scheduler before spending seeds.
- 14. Optional RSI (App. B.1) as a separately named arm 'lambda-hold+RSI'. After 3e6 decisions, draw a fraction p of resets from an archive of states taken from the top-K return episodes (A10: p = 0.5, K = 20 episodes, states sampled uniformly from their second half). The gate permits it because it changes neither body nor reward. Never enable the vmax curriculum in gate runs.
- 15. Gate run: 5 seeds x 150e6 simulation steps, then the frozen evaluation from step 0. Report the per-seed maxima, the median, and a bootstrap CI over seeds.
- 16. Fidelity checks from the paper's section 2.9, on cycles grouped by per-cycle mean COM speed within +/-0.35 m/s. Sagittal hip/knee/ankle + vGRF against Fukuchi et al. 2017 at 2.5/3.5/4.5 m/s. EMG against Van Hooren and Meijer 2024 at 5.0 m/s for the MyoLeg analogues of the 11 muscles (soleus, gasmed, gaslat, tibant, vasmed, vaslat, recfem, bflh, semiten, glmax1-3 pooled, glmed1-3 pooled), with 20-450 Hz band-pass, rectification, 10 Hz low-pass and peak normalisation. Compare with the paper's r values.
- 17. Plant diagnostics: replicate Fig. 8 for iliacus, psoas and recfem (activation, normalised velocity, FV factor (1+V)^2, force/F0 at activation > 0.9). This shows whether MyoLeg's ceiling is force-velocity-limited like H2190 or limited elsewhere (rigid tendon, contact, balance).
- 18. Matched-budget controls (these become matrix arms). Plain SAC on 80 excitations at 0.01 s; the same with horizon-matched gamma = 0.99^(1/10) per step; Excitation-hold at the same decision points; lambda every 0.01 s with no hold (missing in the paper); lambda with fixed 0.08 s holds (the paper's untested claim).
- 19. Throughput before scaling. Measure mj_step for MyoLeg-80 at 2 ms on one EPYC core first (not measured yet; the 26-muscle myolegs26+Hippo costs 62.7 us per step on an EPYC 9535 per myoassist_framework.json). Size concurrent seeds so that envs across concurrent seeds stay within about 60 cores and replay buffers plus env processes fit in 30 GB (see throughput_notes).
- 20. Only after the gate passes, run separate 200 m evaluation episodes of at least 50 s to test stability beyond the ~10 s training horizon. Train on 200 m only as a new, labelled condition.
- K1. Kinesis route (fallback or comparator, not gate-ready). Obtain SMPL_NEUTRAL.pkl and AMASS KIT (SMPL+H G) under their own licences and run convert_kit.py. The KB has an untested py3.12 lock with MuJoCo 3.1.2 and NumPy 1.26.4. Evaluate legs/kinesis-moe-imitation (PD mode, 30 Hz) on the 11 test walking_run clips and measure their actual speeds. For sprinting, either (a) supply sprint references (not in KIT) and retrain or fine-tune imitation, or (b) fine-tune legs/target-reach with the lambda-hold reward (a new artefact; state that the Kinesis reward was replaced). Correct the x0.9 playback-speed labelling first, or document it.

**Throughput.** Lambda-Hold (reported). About 4.0 m/s emerges in about 1 h on an RTX 5070 + 24-thread Core Ultra 9 with Hyfydy (CPU physics), SB3 SAC and 24 envs (p.8; Table 1). Curves run to about 150e6 simulation steps (about 15e6 decisions) per seed; wall-clock for the full run is not reported. Because each hold is one transition, the learner sees about 10x fewer transitions than simulation steps, so the policy-update cost is small and CPU physics dominates. Kinesis (reported). 1 A100 + 128 CPU threads, 128 envs, 51,200 samples per epoch at 30 Hz control and 150 Hz physics; total training time not reported. MuscleMimic says Kinesis needs about 10 days on 128 CPU envs + A100 for the 290-actuator model (MuscleMimic p.3). Our server (estimates, ARGUED, to be measured). MyoLeg-80 mj_step cost at 2 ms is unmeasured; the 26-muscle myolegs26+Hippo costs 62.7 us per step on an EPYC 9535 core. If MyoLeg-80 costs 100-200 us per step, one 0.01 s lambda step (5 substeps) is 0.5-1 ms, and 150e6 simulation steps is about 21-42 core-hours of physics per seed, i.e. under an hour of physics at 48 cores if parallelised. With 24 envs per seed (the paper's setting) a seed is learner-bound, not physics-bound: 15e6 decisions / 24 envs x 2 gradient steps is about 1.25e6 SAC updates of batch 256 on 2x256 MLPs, likely 1-3 h on the RTX 5090 (an unmeasured assumption). RAM: a float32 replay of 5e5 x (2 x ~370 obs + 80 act) is about 1.6 GB per seed, and 24 MuJoCo subprocesses are roughly 1-2.5 GB, so 2-3 concurrent seeds fit in 30 GB with headroom (a 5-seed gate is then about 2 batches). GPU physics (MJX-Warp) is not needed for the published SAC design. It would pay off for sweeps over the lambda bounds and scheduler assumptions. The lambda law is elementwise and ports trivially, but the per-env GRF-event scheduler needs a vectorised state machine, and variable-length holds complicate fixed-step GPU batching unless every env is stepped every 0.01 s with masked policy queries.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- Stiff length servo rather than EP physiology: with G_tonic = 50 and no delay, each muscle is almost a bang-bang switch at lambda that holds joint configurations stiffly. Any held length-target controller could provide the same stability and effective action compression, e.g. Kinesis's PD target-length mode or joint-space PD targets. The paper never compares against one.
- Temporal abstraction and horizon: holds put decisions about 0.1 s apart. With gamma = 0.99 per decision that is a ~10 s horizon, against ~1 s for the per-step SAC baselines. Credit assignment over a sprint's acceleration phase may explain much of the gap between hold methods and Plain SAC/Synergy, independent of lambda.
- Plant strength: if the H2190 run scenario's muscle_force_multiplier = 2 was in effect, both 'sprint emerges' and '4.7 m/s' reflect a doubled-strength body. The F-V ceiling analysis would then describe an unrealistically strong model.
- Reward shape and log-plot reading: exp(v) makes return exponential in speed, so large return ratios correspond to modest speed differences, and 'orders of magnitude faster learning' partly restates a different speed level.
- Human-likeness from plant and speed, not controller: kinematic shape correlations (r 0.83-0.97) may mostly come from musculoskeletal geometry and running at speed (any running gait has similar sagittal shapes). No baseline reached comparable speed for a controlled comparison.
- Kinesis EMG advantage from imitation constraints: better EMG correlation than DEP-RL/DynSyn may come from tracking human kinematics (which constrains activation timing) rather than muscle-level plausibility. The authors test this only partially via MPJAE, and MuscleMimic reports that 'strong kinematic imitation does not automatically guarantee physiologically faithful muscle activations' (MuscleMimic arXiv 2603.25544 p.10).

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- Plant-fidelity axis as a finding: run the identical lambda-hold port on MyoLeg with MuJoCo's native quadratic F-V and with a Hill-hyperbolic F-V (via a CPU gain callback or a custom actuator). If the MuJoCo curve alone moves the speed ceiling past 4.7 m/s, that quantifies how much 'sprint capability' is a muscle-model artefact. This is relevant to the Exo-Plore and Beyond Kinematics caveats in the brief.
- Event-synchronous exosuit: query the hip-exo actor at the same 8 GRF-event decision points per stride, with a small per-hold torque-profile parameterisation (e.g. peak time and magnitude within the hold). This mirrors gait-event-timed sprint suits (Moon et al.) and shrinks exo exploration exactly as holds shrink human exploration.
- Complete the 2x2 factorial the paper left open (lambda vs excitation x hold vs no-hold) plus fixed-interval holds, all at matched gamma-horizon. This is cheap, it maps onto matrix arms G/H, and it would be publishable as a clarification of Lambda-Hold.
- Use Kinesis's PD target-length code (target_length_to_force + force_to_activation) as a ready MuJoCo implementation of the rival 'length servo' control law. Hold it on the same GRF events for a controller-form ablation (reflex excitation law vs force-inversion law).
- Muscle-normalisation audit as a test: a unit test that flags any muscle whose l range cannot cross the lambda bounds (glmax3 today) or whose L0 deviates from the OpenSim lopt by more than 20% (26 clamped muscles). It prevents silent saturation in both lambda and exo experiments.
- Strength-matched cross-check: if a Hyfydy licence becomes available again, H2190 at muscle_force_multiplier 1 vs 2 under lambda-hold directly measures how much the published speed depends on strength. Record this as an open question for the authors (e.g. an email asking which scenario was used).
- Long-horizon sprint: the published objective has a ~10 s effective horizon. For 200 m, add a separately labelled condition with longer episodes and gamma per unit time. A fatigue model (MyoSuite 3CC-r, present in Kinesis but uncalibrated for sprint) could come later as its own condition, so that assistance effects on late-race deceleration become testable.
