### Reducing sprint time with exosuit assistance in the real world

- *Reducing sprint time with exosuit assistance in the real world* — Moon, Nam, Ryu, Kim, Yun, Yang, Yang, Lee (Chung-Ang Univ.; HUROTICS Inc.), Science Robotics 8(82), eadf5611 (Focus article), 27 Sep 2023. arXiv:none · code: None released. The data statement (main p3) points to Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.8273070 (CC-BY-4.0, published 2023-09-27). It holds only 2 files: the supplement PDF (665,149 B, md5 1cbaaa742cb403e0fa188c25f5b62027, byte-identical to the KB copy) and Movie S1 (88,610,225 B). There are no raw force, IMU or timing data. Movie S2 is listed in main p2 but is not deposited. The Science-hosted supplement (science.org .../scirobotics.adf5611_sm.pdf) returned HTTP 403 (Cloudflare) to both curl and WebFetch on 2026-09-23, so the KB/Zenodo supplement is the one read.. Read: Main PDF pp1-4 of 4 (3 article pages + download cover). Supplement pp1-7 of 7. All read as page images.
- *Task-agnostic exoskeleton control via biological joint moment estimation* — Molinaro, Scherpereel, Schonhaut, Evangelopoulos, Shepherd, Young (Georgia Tech; X, The Moonshot Factory), Nature 635(8038):337-344, 2024 (online 13 Nov 2024). arXiv:none · code: Code Ocean capsule 10.24433/CO.7641031.v2 (Python package to deploy and test the trained models; not opened). Dataset 10.35090/gatech/75759; see reimplementation_recipe.E.. Read: Main PDF pp1-21 of 21 (article pp337-344, Methods, ED Figs 1-5, ED Tables 1-3). Supplement PDF pp1-12 of 12 (cover + numbered pp1-11).
- *A human lower-limb biomechanics and wearable sensors dataset during cyclic and non-cyclic activities* — Scherpereel, Molinaro, Inan, Shepherd, Young (Georgia Tech), Scientific Data 10:924, 21 Dec 2023 (article CC BY 4.0). arXiv:none · code: Code.zip (9,338 B; MATLAB helpers) inside dataset 10.35090/gatech/70296 (CC BY 4.0). Read: No PDF read. The publisher HTML was parsed for the abstract, participants, GRF processing, Data Records and licence. The dataset readme.txt (277 lines) was read in full.
- *Simulating Ideal Assistive Devices to Reduce the Metabolic Cost of Running* — Uchida, Seth, Pouya, Dembia, Hicks, Delp (Stanford), PLOS ONE 11(9):e0163417, 22 Sep 2016 (CC BY 4.0; DOI verified on Crossref). arXiv:none · code: Models, simulations and software at simtk.org/home/idealassist_run. Base simulations: Hamner and Delp 2013, simtk.org/home/nmbl_running. Metabolics probe in OpenSim 3.3, plug-in at github.com/opensim-org/opensim-metabolicsprobes. None were downloaded.. Read: pp1-19 of 19 (PLOS printable PDF, 3,806,420 B), read as page images
- *Muscular strategy shift in human running: dependence of running speed on hip and ankle muscle performance* — Dorn, Schache, Pandy (Univ. Melbourne; Australian Institute of Sport), Journal of Experimental Biology 2012. arXiv:none · code: none. Read: The PDF was NOT read. journals.biologists.com returned a Cloudflare 403 to curl; the Wayback snapshots contain only the JS challenge page; Unpaywall lists the article as closed, not open access. Text used: the Europe PMC abstract (PMID 22573774) and two WebFetch extractions of the publisher HTML (Methods, Results, Discussion). Table 2 values were not transcribed. Erratum content comes from the KB ledger (Fig. 2B right axis should read Impulse (BW s)).
- *Design of a Quasi-Direct Drive Actuator with Embedded Pulley for a Compact, Lightweight, and High-Bandwidth Exosuit (supporting)* — J. Yang, J. Moon, Ryu, J. Kim, Nam, Park, Y. Kim, G. Lee (Chung-Ang Univ.), Actuators 12(1):21, 2023 (CC BY 4.0). arXiv:none · code: none. Read: Abstract only (Crossref/OpenAlex). The MDPI PDF and HTML returned 403. [argued] It is probably the Moon sprint actuator: same authors, the 'embedded pulley' wording and the 'high control bandwidth' wording in Moon Fig. 1B. The sprint paper does not state this link.
- *Improved assistive profile tracking of soft exosuits for walking and jogging with off-board actuation (Moon ref. 9: the admittance controller and the 0.15 m arm)* — G. Lee, Ding, Bujanda, Karavas, Zhou, Walsh (Harvard), IEEE/RSJ IROS 2017, pp. 1699-1706. arXiv:none · code: none. Read: Not read. The Harvard-hosted PDF returned 403 to both curl and WebFetch. Only the abstract, as returned by one web search, was seen.
- *MyoAssist assist_sim HMEDI_L1 cable hip device (L1model.xml, L1config.yaml)* — assist_sim 1.0.0 installed on sail-gpu at /home/aurnab/projects/sail-sprint-20260905/.venv, software, read 2026-09-23. arXiv:n/a · code: assist_sim/models/HMEDI/L1model.xml (6,239 B) and L1config.yaml (2,904 B). Read: Both files read in full. One read-only probe (MuJoCo 3.4.0) composed myolegs26+HMEDI_L1 in memory; its temp dir was deleted.

**Problem.** Programme question: can a tension-only, cable-driven hip exosuit reduce SIMULATED 200 m time from a common standing start (secondary endpoints: 100 m split and max speed) under realistic device constraints? And does a SMAT-style co-adapted controller beat matched non-learning baselines? What each source contributes: (1) Moon 2023 is the only direct human evidence that hip-extension exosuit assistance reduced 200 m time (n=9 non-elite men, outdoors). It also supplies the numbers for a fixed Moon-style profile and for a realistic cable model. (2) Molinaro 2024 supplies a biological-moment estimator and a walking-tuned scale/delay/filter mapping, which define the biological-moment-mapping baseline. It also supplies the Georgia Tech dataset used in SMAT's offline replay at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s. (3) Uchida 2016 simulates ideal (massless, unlimited) devices at 2 and 5 m/s. It shows where hip assistance should act and that the ideal hip torque is not the net joint moment. (4) Dorn 2012 gives model-based evidence that above ~7 m/s, speed gains come from faster swing driven by hip muscles (iliopsoas, gluteus maximus, hamstrings) while plantarflexor force capacity collapses. The gap: none of these sources simulates or measures an assisted 200 m sprint with a realistic cable model.

**Mechanism (exact).**
- *Moon device:* [measured/reported] Hip-extension soft exosuit (supp p2, Fig. S1 p4). Actuation: a two-degree-of-freedom actuation system mounted on the vest (supp p2), 'a customized actuator with electrical motors with embedded pulleys' with 'high control bandwidth performance' (main p1 Fig. 1B caption). Transmission: Bowden cables with two anchoring points, the bottom of the waist belt and the posterior part of the thigh brace. The actuator shortens the distance between the anchors, which produces tension 'parallel to the hip extensor' (supp p2; main p2). Sensing: two MTi-630 AHRS IMUs (Xsens) on the anterior thigh detect maximum hip flexion (MHF); two FUTEK LSB205 load cells on the posterior thigh brace measure cable force; I2C bus (supp p3). Fig. S1 also labels an 'abdomen IMU' whose role is not described. Component masses (supp p2): waist belt 250 g, spandex base layer 166 g, two thigh braces 2x94 g, soft exosuit vest 177 g, actuator system 2818 g, two sensor module systems 2x123 g, four battery packs 4x180 g mounted on the front of the vest. Controlled tops 145 g, bottoms 88 g and shoes (Adidas Alphalava) were worn in both conditions. [derived] Listed exosuit components total 4,565 g. The mass added over the no-suit condition is 4,399 g, because the base layer was worn in both conditions (supp p3).
- *Moon profile and control:* [reported] 'A admittance force controller was used to generate the hip extension assistive force profiles with desired onset, peak, and release timings. An IMU-based algorithm was used to detect the MHF as a gait event and calculate the gait cycle percentage based on the gait event (2, 9)' (supp p3). Gait detection follows Kim et al. Science 2019 (ref 2); the admittance controller follows Lee et al. IROS 2017 (ref 9) (main p2). Target force (supp p3): starts 2%, peaks 30% and ends 45% of the gait cycle measured from MHF. Peak magnitude was chosen by each participant within 20-35% of body weight in a preliminary experiment. Profile origin is stated two ways: 'mimicking the optimal assistive trajectory derived from the musculoskeletal simulation result of running presented by Uchida et al.' (main p2, Fig. 1C) versus 'mimicked from the biological hip joint moment (8)' (supp p3). Uchida shows these two differ at the hip (Uchida p7, Fig. 3 p9). The ADMITTANCE LAW AND ALL ITS PARAMETERS (virtual mass or damping, gains, switching thresholds, loop rate, delay, force limit, slack handling) ARE NOT REPORTED. Lee 2017 abstract (search snippet only): 'switching admittance-position control' for force tracking and cable-slack management, with feedforward models compensating individual kinematics, nonlinear hip-exosuit stiffness and actuator transmission loss. Moment and power reporting (supp pp3-4): hip moment = force x 0.15 m (the 0.15 m arm is cited to ref 9, not measured); power = moment x measured hip angular velocity (source of that velocity unspecified); positive and negative 'delivered power' = positive or negative work / stride time; unilateral.
- *Molinaro estimator:* [reported] A temporal convolutional network (TCN) estimates TOTAL hip and knee flexion/extension moments (N m/kg). Biological moment = estimate minus the measured actuator torque from the previous loop (Methods p9). Inputs: unilateral hip and knee encoders; thigh, shank and foot 6-axis IMUs; insole vertical GRF and COP. Exo data run at 55 Hz and are upsampled in real time to 200 Hz. Inputs are normalized with training mean/std. Labels are scaled by body mass. Hip labels were delayed 50 ms relative to the input sequence, which gave +5% hip validation MAE improvement (Methods p9). Selected hyperparameters (ED Table 1 p19): 80 filters, 5 residual blocks of 2 conv layers, kernel 5, ReLU, weight normalization, spatial dropout 0.15, LR 5e-5, no L1/L2; input sequences > 250 samples were excluded. Two-stage optimization: Google Vizier 11-D (>10,000 models), then a 6-D grid (1,440 models), for an 8% MAE improvement (Methods p10). The final model used 19 of 28 tasks from 15 training participants (p339; Fig. 2c p340). Compute: Raspberry Pi 4B runs the loop at 55 Hz; the TCN runs on a Jetson Nano over TCP/IP (Methods p9). [derived] With exponential dilations 1-16 (dilation schedule not reported), receptive field = 1+2*4*31 = 249 samples, about 1.25 s at 200 Hz, consistent with the 250-sample cap.
- *Molinaro mapping:* [reported] Desired torque (Methods p9; p339): (1) scale the biological moment by 20% at the hip and 15% at the knee. (2) Delay the hip by 100 ms: 50 ms is the minimum loop-consistent delay, plus 50 ms extra 'to maximize the positive work done by the exoskeleton during walking' (refs 14, 44; ref 44 is a loaded-walking soft-exosuit timing study). Delay the knee by 50 ms. (3) Apply a 2nd-order 10 Hz Butterworth low-pass, which adds ~25 ms, so the total delay is 125 ms at the hip and 75 ms at the knee. (4) Clamp at the 15 N m peak. Actuators: T-Motor AK80-9 quasi-direct drive; encoder velocity low-passed with a 2nd-order 10 Hz Butterworth. The exo 'added roughly 7 kg'. Power: two 20 V 3 Ah drill batteries (Methods p9). Phase-2 variants from the dataset readme: BT13 day 1 used 10% hip / 8% knee of the NET torque; BT13 day 2 and BT14-BT17 used 20% hip / 10% knee of the bio torque; all used 100 ms hip + lpf and 50 ms knee + lpf.
- *Uchida method:* [sim] OpenSim 3.2 Computed Muscle Control (CMC) on 10 male long-distance runners (Hamner and Delp data) at 2 and 5 m/s, 3 gait cycles each. Model: 29 DOF, 92 muscles, arms driven by torque actuators (pp4-5). Ideal devices were created by raising the bilateral ankle, knee and/or hip reserve-actuator weight from 1 N m to 1 MN m in J = sum a_i^2 + sum (tau_j/w_j)^2 (Eq. 1, p5), with ground reaction forces and kinematics held at their unassisted values. No device mass and no magnitude or rate limit; 660 simulations (p5). Metabolic power comes from a modified Umberger model. Unidirectional savings were approximated by partitioning each simulation into flexion-only and extension-only segments rather than by simulating one-way devices (p6).
- *Dorn method:* [model, via WebFetch of the publisher HTML] 3-D 12-segment, 31-DOF skeleton with 92 Hill-type musculotendon units. Static optimisation minimises the sum of squared activations, constrained to each muscle's force-length-velocity surface. Peak isometric forces were NOT scaled (same values for all subjects and speeds). Muscle contributions come from pseudo-inverse induced-acceleration analysis. Data: 22 cameras at 250 Hz over an 11.5 m capture volume, 8 force plates at 1500 Hz, EMG of 11 muscle groups.
- *Smat offline replay:* [reported in SMAT 2603.07618 p6 Sec. III.B, Fig. 6] The frozen SMAT exo actor receives 'only hip kinematics' from the open GT dataset ([19] = Molinaro 2024): 10 subjects at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s, outputs scaled to 15 N m. Its torque is compared with the biological hip torque ('no-exo condition', amplitude-scaled to the exo peak for timing), with the lag measured by cross-correlation. The replay is open loop on kinematics, and the subject subset is not specified (see reimplementation_recipe.E).
- *Hmedi l1 as shipped:* [probe, assist_sim 1.0.0 / MuJoCo 3.4.0, myolegs26] Two spatial tendons (cable_r/l), each through 3 sites: hmedi_torso, then femur-flap 'MM03 cobra', then thigh 'MM03 cobra 150'. Tendon length limit [0, 2] m. The actuators are general tendon actuators with fixed gain 100, ctrlrange [-1, 0], gear 1, dyntype none and no forcerange, so they give tension 0-100 N, instantaneously, with no slack, spool, stiffness or delay model. Device mass is 4.50 kg, of which 4.2 kg is one torso block; the composed model mass is 82.59 kg. Moment arm r = dL/dq_hip = 0.070 m at -30 deg, 0.086 at -20, 0.097 at -10, 0.104 at 0, 0.107 at 10, 0.106 at 20, 0.102 at 30, 0.093 at 45, 0.079 at 60, 0.062 at 75, 0.042 at 90, 0.022 at 105, and ~0.000 m at 120 deg flexion. Length rises with flexion, so pulling EXTENDS the hip: full pull at the default pose gave actuator force -100 N and qfrc on hip_flexion_r = -10.22 N m (negative = extension). This contradicts the L1config.yaml comment calling it 'hip-flexion assistance'; the geometry says posterior routing, i.e. hip extension.

**Protocol.**
- *Moon:* [reported] 9 healthy male non-elite runners: age 26.4 +/- 3.2 y, mass 71.7 +/- 7.4 kg, height 1.77 +/- 0.04 m (mean +/- SD). Sprint ability was 'equivalent to the average 50 m sprint time' of the Korean Statistical Information Service. IRB 1041078-202107-HR-214-01C (supp p2). Familiarization: 4 x 45 s treadmill bouts at 4.5 m/s, with suit and assistance only, free rests, and an extra 3 min on request (main p2; supp p3). Main session: 4 trials on a 200 m straight asphalt road without traffic (Hwaseong-si; Fig. S3). Two trials suit-on (S) and two no-suit (N), double-reversal NSSN or SNNS (supp p3). Rest is stated two ways: 'a minimum of 30 min of rest between trials' (main p2) versus 'rested for 30 min to prevent the effect of fatigue' before participation (supp p3). THERE IS NO SUIT-WORN, ASSISTANCE-OFF (ZERO-FORCE) CONDITION, NO MASS-ONLY CONDITION AND NO SHAM: N = controlled tops, bottoms, shoes and base layer; S = N plus thigh braces, waist belt, vest with batteries, and actuator (supp p3). Timing: Freelap. A beacon on the abdomen pairs with transceivers at the start line, 100 m and the finish, triggering within 0.8 m (supp p3). Start posture, run-up and starting command are not reported. Weather (Table S4 p7): participants 1-4 at 9.1 C, wind 1.5 m/s, RH 53.8%; participants 5-9 at 13.1 C, 1 m/s, 80.3%; wind 'did not exceed +2 m/s', direction not given. Statistics (supp p4): repeated-measures ANOVA over the 2 conditions; paired t-tests 'with a Sidak-Holm correction'; alpha 0.05; mean +/- SEM; Pearson correlation for step frequency vs time reduction. Step metrics came from video (Fujifilm) plus Freelap (supp p4).
- *Molinaro:* [reported] 22 able-bodied participants in 3 phases (Methods pp10-11). Phase 1: 10 people (6 M / 4 F; 23.7 +/- 2.0 y; 176.3 +/- 8.7 cm; 76.5 +/- 13.3 kg). All 28 tasks / 66 conditions were done with the exo unpowered, plus heuristic assistance for 46 of 66 conditions: spline assistance with 8-node (hip) and 6-node (knee) cubic Hermite curves, phase from the previous two strides' insole-measured durations; impedance stance control at 5 N m/rad with 0 deg equilibrium. Phase 2: 5 people (4 M / 1 F; 23.4 +/- 4.9 y; 171.4 +/- 7.4 cm; 68.0 +/- 12.7 kg) with a preliminary estimator in the loop. Phase 3: 10 people (7 M / 3 F; 21.8 +/- 2.5 y; 174.8 +/- 8.5 cm; 71.7 +/- 10.2 kg) over three sessions: accuracy (28 tasks, exo on), metabolic, and joint work. Mocap: Vicon at 200 Hz (120 Hz for 2 phase-1 people, upsampled). GRF: force plates and a Bertec instrumented treadmill at 1000 Hz. Sync: three right-leg kicks with the exo off. Metabolic tasks: level walking 1.25 m/s, lifting 25 lb, 5 deg incline at 1.25 m/s, level running 2.5 m/s. Walking-type tasks used ABCCBA x 6 min with the last 3 min averaged; running used ABC x 3.5 min with a first-order fit. Habituation: 1.25 m/s walking with 4 ramp steps every 2 min, then 5 min at full assistance.
- *Uchida:* [sim] See mechanism_exact.uchida_method. Speeds 2 and 5 m/s, steady state, 10 male long-distance runners.
- *Dorn:* [model, WebFetch] 9 subjects (5 M / 4 F; 27.7 +/- 8.0 y; 73.1 +/- 8.6 kg; 176 +/- 7 cm; leg length 93 +/- 5 cm). Target speeds and n: 3.5 m/s (n=9), 5.0 (n=9), 7.0 (n=8), maximal sprint >= 8.0 (n=7). Achieved: 3.5 +/- 0.1, 5.2 +/- 0.1, 7.0 +/- 0.1, 9.0 +/- 0.7 m/s. Steady-speed passes through an 11.5 m capture volume; no acceleration phase.

**Headline numbers**
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon 200 m time, suit-on minus no-suit (paired)
  - *Value:* -0.97 +/- 0.41 s (mean +/- SEM), n=9, P=0.047
  - *Where:* Moon supp p2 'Result / Sprint time'; main p2 ('decreased by 0.97 s on average'); Fig. 1D p1 (error bars SEM)
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon 0-100 m and 100-200 m segments
  - *Value:* 0-100 m: -0.68 +/- 0.23 s, P=0.017. 100-200 m: -0.30 +/- 0.25 s, P=0.269 (not significant). The hypothesized larger second-half benefit was not observed.
  - *Where:* supp p2; main p2; Fig. 1D
- *Type:* derived
  - *What:* Recomputation from Table S1 (supp p6)
  - *Value:* Paired differences, mean / SD / SEM: -0.97333 / 1.24264 / 0.41421 s (200 m); -0.67889 / 0.68206 / 0.22735 (0-100); -0.29667 / 0.74409 / 0.24803 (100-200). Two-sided paired t(8) = -2.350 / -2.986 / -1.196, p = 0.0467 / 0.0174 / 0.2659. Wilcoxon signed-rank p = 0.055 / 0.027 / 0.43. dz = -0.78 / -1.00 / -0.40. The printed P values equal UNCORRECTED paired t-tests. A Holm-Sidak adjustment across the three time endpoints would give 0.091 (200 m), 0.051 (0-100 m) and 0.266 (100-200 m). The stated 'Sidak-Holm correction' family is undefined.
  - *Where:* computed from supp p6 Table S1
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon group means (Table S1)
  - *Value:* 200 m: no-suit 32.79 +/- 1.15 s vs suit-on 31.82 +/- 0.86 s (SEM). 0-100 m: 16.01 +/- 0.57 vs 15.33 +/- 0.38. 100-200 m: 16.79 +/- 0.60 vs 16.49 +/- 0.49. [derived] Relative change -2.97% / -4.24% / -1.77%; mean 200 m speed 6.10 -> 6.29 m/s. Segment times sum to the 200 m times within 0.01 s.
  - *Where:* supp p6 Table S1
- *Type:* derived
  - *What:* Moon individual responses
  - *Value:* 7/9 were faster over 200 m. Participant 5 was +0.62 s and participant 9 +0.19 s slower. Over 0-100 m, participant 9 was +0.45 s slower. Over 100-200 m, participants 1 (+0.77) and 5 (+0.80) were slower.
  - *Where:* supp p6 Table S1
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon delivered power decline
  - *Value:* Positive delivered power per stride: 0.52 +/- 0.02 W/kg (0-100 m) -> 0.45 +/- 0.03 W/kg (100-200 m), i.e. 13.3 +/- 1.9% lower (P<0.001). Hip extension angular velocity fell 6.7 +/- 0.5% after 100 m (P<0.001), which the authors attribute to 'accumulated fatigue' (not measured).
  - *Where:* supp p2 'Mechanical power'; Table S3 p6
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon Table S3, actuation and exosuit parameters (mean +/- SEM, unilateral; columns 0-200 / 0-100 / 100-200 m)
  - *Value:* Start 2.57+/-0.20 / 2.76+/-0.29 / 2.39+/-0.12 %GC_MHF. Peak 28.18+/-0.97 / 28.09+/-1.09 / 28.26+/-0.85. Release 47.02+/-0.21 / 47.42+/-0.24 / 46.62+/-0.19. Peak moment 0.38+/-0.02 / 0.36+/-0.02 / 0.41+/-0.02 N m/kg. Peak power 3.12+/-0.19 / 3.24+/-0.18 / 2.99+/-0.19 W/kg. Positive delivered power 0.49+/-0.03 / 0.52+/-0.02 / 0.45+/-0.03 W/kg. Negative delivered power -0.03+/-0.01 in every column. Positive angular velocity 11.86+/-0.47 / 12.70+/-0.47 / 11.86+/-0.47 rad/s. Negative angular velocity -11.70+/-0.41 / -12.26+/-0.41 / -11.70+/-0.41 rad/s. Caveat: the 0-200 m angular-velocity entries duplicate the 100-200 m column (unresolved).
  - *Where:* supp p6 Table S3
- *Type:* derived
  - *What:* Moon numbers converted for a 71.7 kg runner with the 0.15 m arm
  - *Value:* Peak moment 27.2 N m, i.e. peak cable force 182 N = 25.8% BW (by half: 172 N / 24.5% BW for 0-100 m, 196 N / 27.9% BW for 100-200 m). Peak power 214-232 W per leg. Mean positive power 32-37 W per leg. The 20-35% BW selection range = 141-246 N = 21.1-36.9 N m. Cable speed needed to follow 11.9-12.7 rad/s of hip extension at 0.15 m: 1.78-1.90 m/s; payout during flexion at 12.3 rad/s: 1.84 m/s. Delivered peak moment is ~11% of Uchida's ideal hip extension peak at 5 m/s (0.38 vs 3.5 N m/kg).
  - *Where:* from supp p3 and p6 Table S3; Uchida Fig. 3 p9
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon force tracking
  - *Value:* RMSE 17.49 N on average: preliminary test, 3 participants, treadmill at 4.5 m/s (Fig. S2A). RMSE 12.04 N: representative participant who reached the highest speed, 7.3 m/s (Fig. S2B). Different tests, not a speed comparison. Visual only: reference peaks ~200-225 N near 28-30 %GC_MHF; actual force lags the reference at onset by a few % of the cycle.
  - *Where:* supp p5 Fig. S2 and caption
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Moon 'step frequency' (Table S2)
  - *Value:* Pearson R = 0.908, P = 0.033, n = 5: the rate of increase in step frequency correlated with the rate of sprint-time decrease. Data for participants 1-4 were lost. Table S2 values (e.g., P5 no-suit 116.5 = 57 + 59.5) are unitless, and the half-distance values sum to the whole. The row labelled SEM equals the sample SD. [derived] Read as step counts, they give 3.05-3.78 steps/s and 1.59-2.02 m/step, which is plausible for sprinting; read as strides, 1.6-2.0 m would be implausibly short. They are almost certainly COUNTS, not a frequency, and the dispersion row is SD, not SEM.
  - *Where:* supp p2 'Step frequency'; p6 Table S2
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Molinaro online estimator accuracy (n=10; run n=9)
  - *Value:* R2 hip / knee: cyclic 0.79 / 0.86; impedance-like 0.81 / 0.87; unstructured 0.80 / 0.82; overall average 0.83. RMSE: cyclic 0.15 / 0.13 N m/kg; impedance-like 0.21 / 0.16. Normalized MAE: cyclic 7.3 / 5.5%; impedance-like 7.1 / 6.0%. Representative run stride: hip R2 0.93 (baseline 0.91), knee 0.98 (baseline 0.97). Normalized error distribution: hip mean -2.7% (SD 7.8%), knee -1.3% (SD 6.6%). Accuracy statistics were computed AFTER time-aligning estimates to ground truth, so they do not describe causal delay.
  - *Where:* abstract p337; p339; Fig. 3b,d p341; ED Figs 4-5 pp17-18; supp p5 lines 1-3
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Molinaro improvement over the perfect-classifier baseline and generalization
  - *Value:* Hip R2 +0.13 +/- 0.04 and knee +0.25 +/- 0.04 on cyclic tasks; hip +0.31 +/- 0.05 and knee +0.32 +/- 0.02 on impedance-like tasks. The 9 held-out tasks reached R2 0.83 (hip) and 0.85 (knee). Eight novel tasks with 3 users: hip R2 0.24-0.92, knee 0.32-0.91; layup, burpees and mountain climbers failed on magnitude or shape.
  - *Where:* Fig. 3 caption p341; p339-340; supp pp1-2, Supp Fig. 1 p7
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Molinaro user outcomes
  - *Value:* Metabolic cost, exo on vs zero torque: level walk -14.3%, lift weight -8.0%, 5 deg incline -19.7%, run at 2.5 m/s -13.7%. Exo on vs no exo: level walk +8.0% (worse, attributed to the ~7 kg mass), lift -7.5%, run -4.8% (n=9 each). Varying speed/incline circuit (n=3): -12.9% vs zero torque, -1.6% vs no exo. Positive biological joint work: -5.3 to -15.7% vs zero torque; -8.5 to -22.3% vs no exo in 4 of 6 tasks.
  - *Where:* Fig. 4 p342; text pp341-342
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Molinaro sensor ablation and dropout (hip R2 loss vs all sensors)
  - *Value:* Ablation: -insole 0.026; -insole-foot IMU 0.034; IMUs only 0.070; encoders only 0.213; insole only 0.313. Dropout: thigh IMU 0.155; shank IMU 0.096; foot IMU 0.137; hip encoder 0.330; knee encoder 0.101; insole 0.319. Knee dropout losses: thigh IMU 0.338, knee encoder 0.589.
  - *Where:* ED Fig. 1 p14
- *Type:* measured
  - *What:* Molinaro task selection
  - *Value:* Generalization saturated (95% of total improvement) after 7 tasks. 19 tasks gave 0.133 N m/kg RMSE, within 5% of training on all tasks. Standing poses were the most useful task after the level-walking seed. Peak-to-peak hip/knee moments were 2-4 N m/kg for most tasks.
  - *Where:* Fig. 2c p340; Methods p10; ED Fig. 3 p16
- *Type:* sim
  - *What:* Uchida ideal-device metabolic savings (n=10, bilateral, % of unassisted)
  - *Value:* 2 m/s: ankle 26, knee 26, hip 25, A+K 52, A+H 50, K+H 41, A+K+H 66. 5 m/s: ankle 19, knee 27, hip 33, A+K 44, A+H 50, K+H 51, A+K+H 69. Hip: 33 +/- 4% at 5 m/s vs 25 +/- 2% at 2 m/s; ankle 19 +/- 4% vs 26 +/- 4%. At 5 m/s the hip saved significantly more than the ankle or knee (p < 0.002, matched-pairs t-test). At 2 m/s each single joint saved ~1.5 W/kg. For one-way (unidirectional) assistance, extension-only beats flexion-only at the ankle and hip.
  - *Where:* Fig. 1 p7; text p6; p11
- *Type:* sim
  - *What:* Uchida ideal hip torque at 5 m/s
  - *Value:* Peak mean actuator torque -3.5 +/- 0.5 N m/kg (extension) in early stance (circle at ~10% of the foot-strike-to-foot-strike cycle), the largest of the three joints (knee -2.2 +/- 0.3, ankle -2.8 +/- 0.6). Toe-off is ~38% (dashed line). The ideal hip torque 'deviated substantially' from the net joint moment: muscles produced a large stance hip-flexion moment that opposed the actuator's extension torque. The between-subject SD of actuator torque was substantially larger at the hip. Visual reading of Fig. 3/6b: extension from foot strike to ~30%, flexion ~+1 N m/kg in mid-swing, return toward zero/extension by ~90-100%.
  - *Where:* Fig. 3 p9; text p7; Fig. 6b p12; p13
- *Type:* sim
  - *What:* Uchida cross-joint effects at 5 m/s
  - *Value:* Knee-extension metabolic power fell 72% with the knee device and 58% with the hip device. The hip device raised rectus femoris activity in stance and reduced the vasti; gluteus maximus activity fell in stance. Predicted savings (25-33%) exceed an experimental hip device (8-10%, Sugar et al.: 10.2% at 3.6 m/s, 8.0% at 2.7 m/s).
  - *Where:* p7; Figs 4, 6 pp10-12; p11; p15
- *Type:* model
  - *What:* Dorn speed strategy (WebFetch of publisher HTML; abstract via Europe PMC)
  - *Value:* From 3.5 to 7.0 m/s stride length rose proportionally more than stride frequency; above 7.0 m/s the opposite. Contact time fell monotonically. Aerial time and effective vertical impulse peaked at 7.0 m/s. Peak vertical GRF rose from 2.7 BW (3.5 m/s) to 3.6 BW (7.0 m/s), then plateaued. SOL, GAS and VAS supplied ~75% of the vertical support impulse (SOL up to 50%).
  - *Where:* Dorn 2012 Results 'Dependence of stride length and stride frequency', 'Muscle function during stance'; Table 2 (values not transcribed)
- *Type:* model
  - *What:* Dorn plantarflexor limit vs hip-muscle drive
  - *Value:* At sprinting, SOL and GAS contracted at 37% and 23% of max shortening velocity at their force peaks, so their potential peak forces were only 30% and 40% of peak isometric. From 3.5 to 9.0 m/s, force capacity fell from 100% to 30% (SOL) and from 140% to 40% (GAS). SOL peak force fell from 7.0 to 9.0 m/s. GMAX and HAMS terminal-swing peak forces went from 1.0 and 4.6 BW at 7.0 m/s to 2.2 and 9.0 BW at 9.0 m/s. Hip-muscle swing work rose with speed: ILPSO generates in the 1st half of swing (linear, R2 0.9457); GMAX generates in the 2nd half (quadratic, R2 0.9432); RF absorbs in the 1st half (R2 0.9288); HAMS absorbs in the 2nd half (R2 0.9274). Between 7.0 and 9.0 m/s, ILPSO, HAMS and GMAX produced an almost twofold increase in hip and knee accelerations.
  - *Where:* Dorn 2012 Results 'Lower-limb muscle forces', 'Muscle function during swing'; Discussion 'Hypothesis 1/2'; Figs 3, 6 (plantarflexors), 7-8 (hip swing) per WebFetch
- *Type:* reported
  - *What:* SMAT offline replay (context for the replay test)
  - *Value:* 10 subjects at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s with a 15 N m scale. Peak exo torque 11.6-12.3 N m. Exo torque lagged the amplitude-scaled biological hip torque by 9-20% of the cycle (~156-210 ms, cross-correlation). Peak exo power 38-67 W in early-to-mid swing. Toe-off ~62%. Per-speed lags not given.
  - *Where:* SMAT arXiv 2603.07618 p6 Sec. III.B, Fig. 6
- *Type:* metadata
  - *What:* GT dataset used by SMAT (Molinaro 2024)
  - *Value:* 'Dataset for Task-Agnostic Exoskeleton Control via Biological Joint Moment Estimation', Georgia Tech, date 2024-08, DOI 10.35090/gatech/75759. 72,989,207,373 B total (72.99 GB): readme.txt 27,050; Phase1And2_Complete.zip 27,212,182,942; Phase1And2_Parsed.zip 20,147,014,181; Phase3_Complete.zip 12,604,936,224; Phase3_Parsed.zip 9,588,432,261; Phase3_Segmented.zip 3,436,614,715. Licence: rights statement 'In Copyright' (rightsstatements.org InC/1.0, 'all rights reserved unless otherwise noted') plus the GT deposit licence allowing distribution for non-commercial research and educational purposes only. It is NOT a CC licence. Paper: >22 million ground-truth moment labels per joint across both legs.
  - *Where:* DataCite + GT DSpace REST item a9341fbd-33c4-43e8-878d-a44cf9e99c75; license.txt; Nature Data availability (PDF p12)
- *Type:* metadata
  - *What:* Scherpereel 2023 dataset (the 12-participant no-exo dataset used for Molinaro's stage-1 optimization)
  - *Value:* DOI 10.35090/gatech/70296, CC BY 4.0 (license_rdf). 12 participants (7 M / 5 F; 21.8 +/- 3.2 y; 176.7 +/- 8.6 cm; 76.9 +/- 14.4 kg). 20 non-cyclic + 11 cyclic activities; mocap, IMUs, EMG and force plates, but NO exoskeleton. 79,406,239,198 B total. ProcessedData.zip 13,378,286,332 B; Segmentation.zip 1,150,878,319 B.
  - *Where:* Sci Data HTML 'Participants', 'Data Records'; GT DSpace item 20860ffb-71fd-4049-a033-cd0ff308339e; readme
- *Type:* probe
  - *What:* HMEDI_L1 cable device as composed on myolegs26
  - *Value:* Mass 4.50 kg (4.2 kg torso block). Tension 0-100 N, instantaneous. Moment arm 0.070-0.107 m over -30..+30 deg, 0.042 m at 90 deg, ~0 at 120 deg. Full pull -> -10.2 N m (extension). Maximum hip torque ~10.7 N m vs Moon's ~27 N m delivered peak.
  - *Where:* this digest's probe, 2026-09-23 (mechanism_exact.hmedi_l1_as_shipped)
- *Type:* reported
  - *What:* Moon-group actuator (PEQDD) bandwidth
  - *Value:* 'Control bandwidth ... improved from 6.25 Hz [Harvard exosuit motor] to 20 Hz.' Preliminary walking and running tests. The KB (research-rebuild 04) notes this is a position-response bandwidth, not on-body force bandwidth.
  - *Where:* Yang et al. 2023 Actuators abstract (Crossref); KB research-rebuild-2026-09-05/research/04 line 89

**Ablations that matter**
- Moon ran NO device or controller ablation. There was no suit-worn zero-force arm, no mass-only arm, no sham, and no profile timing or force-level comparison; peak force was self-selected between 20 and 35% BW. The only decompositions are the 0-100 / 100-200 m split and a 5-person correlation. Assistance, suit mass, garments, expectation and cadence effects are therefore confounded (supp p3; main p2).
- Molinaro: exo-on vs zero-torque vs no-exo shows the mass penalty (+8.0% level-walking cost vs no exo). Sensor ablation and dropout (ED Fig. 1): hip-encoder dropout cost 0.330 hip R2. Delayed-label training gave +5% hip MAE improvement. Task-set size: 7 tasks saturate and 19 are within 5%. Best-case baseline with a perfect 28-class classifier and ideal phase (supp pp3-4).
- Uchida: single vs combined joints; flexion-only vs extension-only partition (extension-only better at hip and ankle); extra hip DOFs (Fig. 7); 2 vs 5 m/s. There was no mass ablation, and device mass was deliberately excluded (p15).
- Dorn: speed is the only manipulated variable (3.5 to 9.0 m/s).
- [argued] Arms the programme needs that no source ran: ideal torque vs cable model (slack, force cap, delay, geometric arm); zero-force suit worn; mass-only; delay sweep at sprint cadence; fixed 0.15 m arm vs geometric r(q); force-cap sweep across 20-35% BW; hip-extension-only vs bidirectional hip vs ankle (Uchida and Dorn prioritise the hip at speed).

**Limitations the authors admit**
- Moon: the mechanism is unexplained ('sophisticated biomechanical studies must be performed to clarify the mechanism', main p2). The expected larger second-half benefit did not appear (main p2; supp p2). Assistive power fell after 100 m as hip-extension velocity dropped, attributed to fatigue (supp p2). Step-frequency data for participants 1-4 were lost (Table S2 caption).
- Molinaro: the hip-knee exo's mass made level walking costlier than no exo (+8.0%) (p341). Some users found knee-extension help unhelpful or uncomfortable in level walking (Methods p12). The estimator mis-scales or fails on extreme novel tasks (supp pp1-2). The mid-level controller may not be optimal for all tasks or populations (p343). Upper-body kinematics were not measured (supp p3). Some backpack and seam discomfort (Methods p9).
- Uchida: CMC (activation floor 0.02, not always globally optimal); GRF and kinematics fixed at unassisted values; ideal massless actuators with instantaneous, arbitrarily large torque; no fatigue; predictions exceed experimental savings (pp14-15).
- Scherpereel dataset: running capped at 2.5 m/s because participants were not active runners; young able-bodied adults only (Sci Data Discussion).
- Dorn: no explicit limitations section in the fetched text. The KB ledger treats the ~7 m/s transition as specific to that sample and model.

**What was not admitted (critical read)**
- Moon [derived]: the printed P values match uncorrected paired t-tests. With Holm-Sidak across the 3 time endpoints the 200 m result would be p=0.091. Two of nine runners were slower.
- Moon [argued]: unblinded, with no sham or zero-force arm; familiarization happened only in the assisted condition, so expectation or motivation cannot be excluded.
- Moon [argued]: start procedure and start posture are unreported. The clock starts when the abdomen beacon passes the start transceiver (<= 0.8 m), and the largest gain was in the segment that contains the start.
- Moon [argued]: wind was reported per session (1.0-1.5 m/s) with no direction and no per-trial values. The two sessions differed in temperature and humidity (Table S4). The allocation to NSSN vs SNNS is unreported, and rest is described inconsistently (main p2 vs supp p3).
- Moon: the 0.15 m moment arm is assumed (ref 9), not measured, so every moment and power in Table S3 inherits it. The hip angular-velocity source is unstated (Fig. S1 shows an abdomen IMU that the methods do not mention).
- Moon: individual peak forces are unreported (only the 20-35% BW range). Group force tracking at sprint speed is not given; only one representative participant is shown (Fig. S2B).
- Moon: Table S2 is labelled 'frequency' but holds counts, and its SEM row is SD. The 0-200 m angular-velocity entries in Table S3 duplicate the 100-200 m column. Supp p2 says the time 'reduced to 0.97 s' (it means by 0.97 s). Movie S2 is listed but not deposited. No raw data.
- Moon: familiarization was at 4.5 m/s on a treadmill, far below the 7.3 m/s peak sprint speed. The fatigue explanation is untested (no biomarker or EMG).
- Molinaro [argued]: the 100 ms hip delay was chosen for walking positive work (ref 44 is a walking study) but applied to every task, including running at 2.5 m/s. At sprint stride periods of ~0.50-0.55 s, 125 ms is 23-25% of the cycle vs ~11% of a 1.1 s walking stride [derived].
- Molinaro dataset: the highest speed is 2.5 m/s; there is no sprint data. The exo is worn in every trial (no true no-exo biomechanics). The readme lists 11 phase-1 IDs (BT01-BT12 without BT05) while the paper reports 10 phase-1 participants [derived]. It is not openly licensed.
- Uchida [argued]: only 3 cycles per subject; steady speed only (no acceleration or 200 m dynamics); male distance runners; savings depend on the metabolic model; the ideal torque minimises squared activation, not metabolic cost; unidirectional savings are approximated by partitioning, not simulated.
- Dorn [argued]: n=7 at maximal sprint (9.0 +/- 0.7 m/s); steady-speed capture only; muscle forces are model estimates with unscaled strength across subjects of both sexes.

**Unspecified details a reconstruction must decide**
- Moon admittance or force-control law, gains, loop and sensor rates, command-to-force delay, force cap, cable pretension and slack management: not reported. Lee 2017 full text not obtained.
- Moon MHF detection (filtering, thresholds, debounce), the stride-period estimate used to turn time into %GC_MHF, and how the first strides after the start were handled: not reported.
- Moon profile curve shape between onset, peak and release (Fig. S2 looks smooth), and whether force was set to exactly zero between pulses or held at a pretension: not reported.
- Moon per-participant peak force, the rule used in the preliminary selection, and whether %BW excludes suit mass: not reported.
- Moon motor model, gear ratio, pulley radius, Bowden cable type, length and friction, torque-speed-power envelope, battery capacity and actuator efficiency: not reported (PEQDD abstract gives only the 6.25 -> 20 Hz bandwidth).
- Moon: whether each Table S1 value averages both trials per condition, and which strides entered Table S3: not reported.
- Moon start type (standing, crouch or rolling) and distance behind the start transceiver: not reported.
- SMAT replay: which GT subset (phase-1 unpowered 'off' trials vs phase-3 powered trials), which leg or legs, the angle source (OpenSim vs exo encoder), resampling, deterministic vs sampled actions, sign convention and per-speed lags: not reported.
- GT dataset: which mass (with or without exo; both are listed) normalises N m/kg: not stated in the readme.
- Molinaro TCN dilation schedule and input window length: not reported (receptive field 249 is inferred).
- Uchida per-subject device torque time series: on SimTK, not read here. Moon's MHF-to-foot-strike offset needed to map Uchida's foot-strike cycle onto %GC_MHF: not reported.
- Dorn Table 2 (stride length, frequency and contact times per speed): not transcribed.

**Reproduction relevance.**
- *Copy exactly:*
  - Moon profile timing: onset 2, peak 30, release 45 %GC_MHF (commanded). Measured delivered timing 2.57 / 28.18 / 47.02 as a variant (supp p3; Table S3).
  - Moon peak-force range 20-35% BW. The measured-mean equivalent 25.8% BW (0.38 N m/kg / 0.15 m) is the primary level.
  - Moon endpoint definitions (0-200, 0-100, 100-200 m) and the reporting of paired differences as mean +/- SEM with n, plus individual differences.
  - Molinaro mapping as the walking-tuned starting point: scale 0.20 (hip), delay 100 ms, 2nd-order 10 Hz Butterworth (~25 ms), total ~125 ms, clamp at the device limit.
  - SMAT replay conditions: 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s, 10 subjects, 15 N m scale, cross-correlation lag against the amplitude-scaled biological hip moment.
- *Reconstruct with disclosure:*
  - MHF detector and stride-period estimator; profile curve shape; admittance or force controller; slack and pretension; actuator dynamics and delay; suit stiffness; mass distribution; start handling; replay input source and subset. Record each as a named setting with its chosen value.
- *Validation checks not targets:*
  - Moon Table S3 per-leg values: peak moment 0.36-0.41 N m/kg, positive power 0.45-0.52 W/kg, timing ~2.6/28/47%, hip extension velocity ~12 rad/s. Force RMSE of 12-17 N as a tracking-realism reference.
  - Dorn plausibility for the simulated sprinter: peak vGRF plateau ~3.6 BW above 7 m/s; stride frequency, not length, carries speed gains above ~7 m/s; large GMAX and HAMS terminal-swing demand.
  - Uchida sign pattern for hip torque at 5 m/s: extension in early stance, flexion in swing.
- *Do not target:*
  - Moon's -0.97 s: a cohort-specific, unblinded, no-sham human result. Do not tune the simulator to reproduce it.
  - Uchida and Molinaro metabolic percentages as sprint-time proxies.
  - Dorn's 7 m/s as a universal threshold.

**Recipe on our server.**
- *0 conventions:*
  - Signs: MyoAssist and OpenSim use hip_flexion + = flexion. A posterior cable produces a negative (extension) generalized force [probe]. GT dataset: OpenSim angles and moments are flexion-positive; exo.csv is extension-positive, flexion-negative (readme 'General notes').
  - Cycle origins: Moon uses %GC_MHF (0 = that leg's maximum hip flexion). Uchida, Dorn and the GT segmentation use foot strike. Never mix them without a measured per-subject MHF-to-foot-strike offset.
  - BW: use the human-only mass (myolegs26 = 78.09 kg [probe]) and state it. Moon says '% of the participant's body weight' without saying whether suit mass is included.
  - Report per leg: cable force; geometric hip moment F*r(q); Moon-convention moment F*0.15 m; positive and negative hip work per stride; actuator mechanical and electrical work. Label every Moon-comparable number with its convention.
- *A fixed moon profile baseline:*
  - *Sensing:* Per-leg thigh orientation, emulating the anterior-thigh MTi-630: thigh segment pitch in the world frame. The hip flexion angle is an alternative; record which is used.
  - *Gait event:* MHF = local maximum of thigh flexion. Use a minimum inter-event interval and hysteresis (Moon's values not reported; choose, e.g., 0.25 s and 2 deg, and sweep) [argued].
  - *Phase:* p = 100*(t - t_MHF_last)/T_hat, clamped to <= 100, where T_hat = mean of the last k MHF-to-MHF intervals (k not reported; default 3, sweep 1-5) [argued]. Assistance stays off until 2 valid MHF events per leg after the standing start [argued; not reported by Moon].
  - *Profile:* F_d(p) = 0 for p < 2 or p >= 45. Rise: F_pk*0.5*(1 - cos(pi*(p - 2)/28)) for 2 <= p < 30. Fall: F_pk*0.5*(1 + cos(pi*(p - 30)/15)) for 30 <= p < 45. Knots (2, 30, 45) are from supp p3; the half-cosine shape is a disclosed choice [argued]. [derived] At 3.6 steps/s (stride ~0.556 s): onset 11 ms, peak 167 ms, release 250 ms after MHF.
  - *Peak force:* Primary F_pk = 0.258*m_human*g (= 197.6 N for 78.09 kg), the Table S3 mean equivalent. Sweep {0.20, 0.275, 0.35}*m*g (153 / 211 / 268 N for 78.09 kg), which is Moon's self-selection range [derived].
  - *Variants:* Commanded knots 2/30/45 (primary) vs measured delivered knots 2.57/28.18/47.02 (use the latter only with an ideal-force actuator, to emulate what Moon delivered).
  - *Output:* Command F_d to the realistic cable model (D). The ideal-torque control arm uses tau_hip = -F_d*0.15 m (extension), labelled 'Moon convention'.
  - *Tuning:* None beyond the F_pk sweep: this is the 'as published' reference. Log seeds and the sweep.
- *B phase delay compensated baseline:*
  - *Idea:* Same profile family, but timed to be right at every cadence, including the acceleration phase where the previous-stride T_hat lags. The KB programme doc 01 requires this baseline.
  - *Phase:* Continuous phase from the thigh angle-velocity phase portrait, phi = atan2(-omega_normalized, theta_normalized), or from an adaptive oscillator. MHF is phase zero. Map phi to %GC_MHF [argued].
  - *Delay compensation:* Identify the command-to-force delay Delta of model D (step or chirp response; group delay near 2-10 Hz). Command F_d(p + 100*Delta/T_hat), i.e. lead the profile by Delta [argued].
  - *Tuning:* Optimise (onset, peak time, release, F_pk, Delta_extra) on 200 m time with a recorded budget (e.g., 5-D CMA-ES). Use the same sensors, force cap, energy budget and evaluation seeds as RL (KB doc 01).
- *C optimised bio moment mapping baseline:*
  - *Law:* Cable force command F = clip(max(0, -s*LPF_fc(M_hat_hip_bio(t - d)))/r_hat(q), 0, F_max), with M in the flexion-positive convention. Only extension lobes are deliverable by a tension cable [argued].
  - *Start values:* s = 0.20, d = 100 ms, fc = 10 Hz 2nd-order Butterworth (+~25 ms), total ~125 ms (Molinaro Methods p9). Also try d = 50 ms (knee-style minimum) and d = 0.
  - *Estimator:* The deployable version needs an estimator using the permitted sensors, e.g. a TCN like Molinaro's (ED Table 1) trained on simulated sprint data with encoder, thigh IMU and optionally foot contact. Ground-truth simulated M_bio (net inverse-dynamics hip moment minus device torque, as in the GT dataset; or the sum of muscle moments) is allowed only as a separately labelled diagnostic comparator (KB doc 01).
  - *Tuning:* Optimise (s, d, fc and optionally phase-specific gains) on 200 m time under the same budget as B. Powell et al. (preprint; TMRB 2026 per the KB) optimise delay, scaling and shaping for walking.
  - *Caution:* [derived] At sprint stride ~0.50-0.55 s, the walking 125 ms equals 23-25% of the cycle, so the walking settings are a starting point only.
- *D realistic cable actuator model:*
  - *Geometry:* Per leg, a spatial tendon from a posterior-inferior waist-belt anchor (pelvis) to a posterior thigh anchor (femur), matching Moon's anchors (supp p2). Take the moment arm r(q) = dL/dq from MuJoCo (ten_J), not a constant. Either place the sites so r is about 0.15 m near neutral (Moon's assumption) or keep anatomical sites and report r(q). HMEDI_L1's posterior routing gives 0.07-0.107 m around neutral, falling to ~0 at 120 deg [probe]. HMEDI_L1 is a usable starting geometry: it already extends the hip.
  - *Tension only and slack:* Suggested model: spool take-up x_s; stretch delta = L(q) - (L_free - x_s); F = k_s*max(delta, 0) + b_s*d(delta)/dt while delta > 0, else 0. This gives tension only, slack when the spool pays out faster than the hip flexes, and resistance when the spool cannot pay out fast enough [argued]. Suit and cable stiffness is not reported by any source: sweep k_s and calibrate so that tracking a Moon profile gives ~12-17 N RMSE at ~200 N peaks (Fig. S2). The simpler alternative (HMEDI style): tendon actuator, ctrlrange [-1, 0], gain = F_max, with no slack.
  - *Force speed power limits:* F_max >= 0.35 BW (268 N at 78.09 kg; Moon's upper selection; Fig. S2 peaks ~200-225 N). HMEDI's 100 N gain is too low: set gainprm = F_max and forcerange [-F_max, 0]. Cable speed capability >= 1.9 m/s for reel-in and ~1.85 m/s for payout at r = 0.15 m (Table S3 velocities x 0.15) [derived]; scale by r(q) for other geometries. Peak mechanical power >= ~235 W per leg (3.24 W/kg x 71.7 kg) [derived]. Enforce |v_spool| <= v_max and F*v <= P_max. Placeholders to sweep: F_max 270 N, v_max 2.5 m/s, P_max 300 W [argued].
  - *Dynamics and delay:* Controller at 50 Hz (SMAT) or 55 Hz (Molinaro). Force response as a first-order lag with ~20 Hz bandwidth (tau ~8 ms; Moon-group PEQDD abstract, position bandwidth), e.g. MuJoCo dyntype 'filter' with dynprm[0] = 0.008. Plus a transport delay in the env wrapper via a ring buffer, swept over {0, 20, 50, 100} ms: Molinaro's rigid exo needed a 50 ms minimum; Moon's delay is not reported [argued]. Admittance-like force loop for the spool: v_cmd = K_a*(F_d - F) + dL(q)/dt feedforward, clipped to v_max. This is a generic reconstruction; Lee 2017 details were not read [argued].
  - *Mass:* Add 4.40 kg (Moon's incremental mass [derived]): actuator 2.818 kg + vest 0.177 kg on the upper back (torso); batteries 0.72 kg on the front of the torso; waist belt 0.25 kg on the pelvis; per thigh a 0.094 kg brace + 0.123 kg sensor module (placement of the sensor module assumed) [argued]. Comparators: HMEDI_L1 4.50 kg [probe]; Hippo_L1 4.9 kg (sibling digest); the SMAT device 5.94 kg.
  - *Required conditions:* No device; suit worn with zero force (spool tracks slack); mass only (no cable); ideal torque (F*r(q), no dynamics); realistic cable. Moon had only the first and last.
  - *Strength invariance:* Muscle parameters must be identical across all conditions. Assistance may only add external cable forces and mass (programme rule).
- *E offline replay dataset recipe:*
  - *Dataset:* Molinaro 2024 GT dataset. DOI 10.35090/gatech/75759; handle hdl.handle.net/1853/75759; landing https://repository.gatech.edu/entities/publication/a9341fbd-33c4-43e8-878d-a44cf9e99c75; file API https://repository.gatech.edu/server/api/core/items/a9341fbd-33c4-43e8-878d-a44cf9e99c75/bundles. Licence: In Copyright plus a non-commercial research distribution licence, so research use is fine; do not re-host raw files in the handoff repo [argued].
  - *Files md5:* readme.txt 06ec590378c2e1b363586d08a945dd44 (bitstream 68b2d62a-d61a-4687-bcb3-475d3c1e5a73). Phase1And2_Parsed.zip 136d73fb6a2e6be9097aa97dfe6a1675 (bc3a3006-c97c-4e42-979f-062899b216cd). Phase3_Segmented.zip 9d16c4da4e726e7a9a0e20ff8615609b (034be8cc-1ee4-4c83-8aa6-74be32f9b633). Phase3_Parsed.zip 1e93712d9eadde58a1af84e3ce0f2199 (0d68a3a1-225b-4098-8856-91867b116fcf). Phase1And2_Complete.zip c99cd0a9c1178b85e5305d174065c6dc (e509a099-e704-4f7a-9aa6-34bb143b4b17). Phase3_Complete.zip 7ec55fc6920a4d21b1839e156bbeddf2 (e9e74581-2f56-4087-ad6a-02adb00a3b43). Download URL pattern: .../server/api/core/bitstreams/<id>/content (curl -L -C -).
  - *Which subset:* SMAT: '10 subjects walking at 0.6, 1.2, and 1.8 m/s', bio torque from the 'no-exo condition'. Candidate (i): phase-1 unpowered trials normal_walk_*_{0-6,1-2,1-8}_off (exo worn but off) in Phase1And2_Parsed.zip. The readme lists 11 phase-1 IDs; BT01's right-leg torques are NaN for these trials, and excluding BT01 leaves exactly 10 (BT02-04, BT06-12) [argued reconstruction]. Candidate (ii): the 10 phase-3 validation users (BT01, BT02, BT13, BT18-BT24), exo powered on all tasks with the final model, bio = net - interaction torque, in Phase3_Segmented.zip (3.44 GB). Run both. Keep the one that reproduces 11.6-12.3 N m peaks and 9-20% lag, and report the choice.
  - *Per trial files:* <subj>_<task>_angle.csv (hip_flexion_l/r, deg, 200 Hz); _angle_filt (6 Hz 5th-order Butterworth); _velocity.csv (deg/s, central difference of angle_filt); _moment_filt.csv (net, N m/kg); _moment_filt_bio.csv (net minus exo interaction torque); _exo.csv (encoder hip_angle and velocity in extension-positive exo convention; torque_desired/measured in N m; exo signals upsampled 55 -> 200 Hz); _activity_flag.csv. Segmented: <task>_parsing.mat (per-side cycle start/end times). Task names follow base_trial_desc_onoff, e.g. normal_walk_1_1-2_on.
  - *Steps:*
    - 1) Select normal_walk speeds 0-6, 1-2 and 1-8 (0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s; 'shuffle' is 0.4 m/s, excluded). Keep both legs; drop NaN spans (BT01 phase-1 list; loadcell-failure NaNs).
    - 2) Input source: OpenSim hip_flexion angles (default) and exo encoder angles (sensitivity), converted to rad and to the MyoAssist sign. Flip exo.csv signs, which are extension-positive.
    - 3) Resample 200 -> 50 Hz with an anti-alias FIR, decimating by 4. Compute velocities consistently with training (finite difference or the filtered values).
    - 4) Build the SMAT exo observation (18-D: 3-step bilateral kinematics plus the actor's own torque history). Feed back the actor's outputs; the kinematics stay open loop. Use deterministic mean actions and record this choice.
    - 5) Torque = action x 15 N m. Segment cycles by heel strike (parsing.mat, or a 20 N GRF threshold). Normalize to 0-100%.
    - 6) Compare against -M_bio_hip (extension-positive), amplitude-scaled to the exo peak. Lag = argmax of circular cross-correlation, per subject and speed, in % and ms. Also report peak torque and peak/mean positive power (tau x omega from the dataset velocity).
    - 7) Run baselines A-C through the same pipeline: A and B need MHF from hip or thigh angle; C needs M_bio or an estimate. This tests whether a 9-20% lag is specific to SMAT (sibling digest idea).
  - *Pitfalls:* The exo is worn in every GT trial (no true no-exo). Phase-1 '_on' trials used heuristic spline or impedance assistance, so do not use them as 'no exo'. The readme does not say which mass normalises N m/kg. All data are treadmill level walking; the dataset maximum is 2.5 m/s (run), so the replay cannot validate sprint timing.
  - *Alternative:* Scherpereel 2023 dataset (DOI 10.35090/gatech/70296, CC BY 4.0; landing https://repository.gatech.edu/entities/publication/20860ffb-71fd-4049-a033-cd0ff308339e). True no-exo data, but: AB01-AB13 without AB04; AB07's treadmill trials were removed, leaving 11 subjects for level walking at 0.6/1.2/1.8 m/s; moments are in N m (divide by Subject_masses.csv); 'treadmill trials are missing one force plate (right leg ... except for declines)', so right-leg level-walk moments are NaN and only the left leg is usable; velocities use backward differences. ProcessedData.zip is 13.38 GB (md5 3d8e5dbd208da9c0030c6d117e2e33a1).
- *F first checks before learning:*
  - Run A at 25.8% BW on the unassisted-competent simulated sprinter. Compare per-leg peak moment (target band 0.36-0.41 N m/kg in Moon convention), positive power (0.45-0.52 W/kg), timing, and hip extension velocity (~12 rad/s) with Moon Table S3. A large mismatch means sprint kinematics or cable geometry need attention before any RL claim [argued].
  - Delay-cadence map: 200 m time vs transport delay (0-150 ms) x F_pk for A and B [argued].
  - Arm-length map: fixed 0.15 m vs geometric r(q) at equal F_pk [argued].

**Throughput.**
- [derived] A 200 m episode lasts 27.4-39.0 s in Moon's cohort (Table S1). At 50 Hz control that is 1,370-1,950 policy steps; at the myolegs26 physics dt of 0.002 s [probe log] it is 13,700-19,500 physics steps per episode.
- Baseline budgets [argued]: A = 3 peak forces x 2 timing variants x >= 3 seeds (18 runs). B and C = CMA-ES with a fixed recorded evaluation budget, matched to the RL evaluation protocol.
- Replay data [metadata]: the minimal SMAT-compatible download is Phase3_Segmented.zip (3.44 GB); candidate (i) needs Phase1And2_Parsed.zip (20.15 GB); the whole dataset is 72.99 GB. The Scherpereel alternative is 13.38 GB (ProcessedData) + 1.15 GB (Segmentation). Replay inference (10 subjects x 3 speeds, MLP at 50 Hz) is trivial on CPU.
- For scale: Uchida ran 660 CMC simulations; Molinaro's release has >22 million moment labels per joint.
- Access notes (2026-09-23): science.org, journals.biologists.com, mdpi.com and projects.iq.harvard.edu returned 403 to curl, and the latter three also to WebFetch. Zenodo's API rate-limited curl (403 'unusual traffic') but served WebFetch. repository.gatech.edu, api.crossref.org, api.datacite.org, api.openalex.org and journals.plos.org served normally.

**Rival explanations a reviewer would offer**
- Moon: chance plus multiplicity. p = 0.047 uncorrected, Holm-Sidak 0.091, n=9, and 2 of 9 were slower [derived].
- Moon: expectation or motivation without blinding, sham or zero-force control; assisted-only familiarization [argued].
- Moon: start and timing artefacts. The start procedure is unreported; the gate-triggered start and the largest gain in the segment containing the start leave room for start-technique differences [argued].
- Moon: order, learning or fatigue imbalance. Double reversal balances only linear drift; sequence allocation is unreported; rest is described inconsistently [argued].
- Moon: environment. Per-session wind of 1-1.5 m/s with unknown direction; the two sessions had different weather [argued].
- Moon, if real: a passive elastic or damping extension moment from the tensioned suit, or a cable-shortened swing (the force starts just after MHF in terminal swing, raising cadence), rather than the hypothesised 'energy efficiency' pathway [argued].
- Programme simulations: an undertrained or strength-altered simulated sprinter lets assistance compensate for controller deficits (KB doc 01). Ideal-actuator artefacts (no delay, no slack, fixed 0.15 m arm, no mass) inflate benefit; Uchida predicted 25-33% vs an experimental 8-10% (p15). A baseline can fail from walking-tuned mistiming (125 ms = 23-25% of a sprint stride) rather than from a lack of learning [argued/derived].

**Ideas this work makes possible**
- [idea] Use Moon Table S3 as a sim-realism gate. Only then compare controllers.
- [idea] Report 'what the device delivered' twice: in Moon's convention (F x 0.15 m) and geometrically (F x r(q)). The HMEDI-like geometry gives ~0.04-0.06 m at 75-90 deg flexion, so early-window torque after MHF may be much smaller than Moon's convention implies [probe/argued].
- [idea] Timing prior from Dorn plus Uchida: Moon's window (terminal swing to early stance) overlaps Dorn's GMAX and HAMS terminal-swing demand and Uchida's early-stance extension peak. A learned policy that moves force into mid-swing would be a testable, distinct hypothesis [argued].
- [idea] Add the two arms Moon lacked (zero-force suit worn; mass only). Pre-register 200 m time as primary and the 0-100 m split as the more comparable secondary. The simulated human has no fatigue, so the 100-200 m pattern cannot be reproduced without a fatigue model [argued].
- [idea] Data gap: both GT datasets stop at 2.5 m/s. Sprint-speed replay needs another dataset (none identified in these sources).
